# Code sweep, 2026-11-12: status

Where each finding of [the sweep report](code_sweep_2026-11-12.md) stands. Every finding was re-read against the
code by one engineer and checked by a second, then classified: **SMALL** fixes (local, about 60 lines or fewer,
no open design question) were fixed and reviewed area by area; **CHALLENGING** ones are listed below with the
shape of a fix for whoever takes them on next; **NEEDS_DECISION** items were decided by the owner on 2026-11-13
([decisions](code_sweep_2026-11-12_decisions.md)), and the small ones are implemented. Ids are `F000` to
`F791`, the order of `build/sweep_saved/confirmed.json`, which holds each finding's full evidence.

| Status | All findings | Distinct defects |
|---|---|---|
| FIXED | 431 | 411 |
| CHALLENGING | 254 | 224 |
| NEEDS_DECISION (decided; 32 implemented, the rest to do) | 99 | 96 |
| NOT_A_BUG | 8 | 8 |

A finding that repeats another one points at it with *same as*; only the primary carries the plan.

## Follow-ups raised while fixing

- **F032** (fixed: the editor now shadow-copies native game modules into `<project>/.conjure/module_cache`). Not done: a per-instance cache folder, so two editors on one project cannot overwrite each other's copies; 07_modules.md D9 fixes the current path, so this needs a design decision.
- **F502** (fixed on Windows): the reviewer asked for the same change in `engine/src/editor/core/net/socket_posix.cpp` (around line 134), which cannot be compiled on the Windows build host; it needs a Linux or macOS build to land.
- **F081** was escalated by its fixer: covering the export pipeline's tool steps, shader copy and scripted or native projects needs a stub tool executable, CMake wiring and about 250 lines of cases. It is listed under Challenging.

## Challenging: for the next pass

### multi (146)

- **F027** BLOCKER Windowed conjure_editor and conjure_runtime cannot start from a fresh build tree: shaders are built into <build>/shaders but looked up in <exe dir>/shaders (`engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:12`). Still present: engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:12 writes to ${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/shaders, while renderer_subsystem.cpp:205 opens executable_directory()/shaders. Every build tree checked has shaders/ but no bin/shaders, except verify_perf where it was copied by hand. The production change is one line, but it must also correct docs/handbook (repository-layout.md:176, build-pipeline.md:209) and add an engine test, so it spans three owner areas. *Fix:* - engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:12: OUTPUT_DIR "${CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY}/shaders". It is set at root CMakeLists.txt:99 before add_subdirectory(engine) and lies inside the build directory, so the check at ConjureShaders.cmake:224-227 passes. Update the comment at :2-3. - Every test consumer reads the CONJURE_SHADER_OUTPUT_DIR property and follows automatically, and the export copies <distribution>/shaders (export_pipeline.cpp:352), which now exists. - repository-layout.md:176 and build-pipeline.md:209 should say build/<preset>/bin/shaders. - Depfiles will then sit in bin/shaders (F328), and multi-config trees remain broken (F329). *Test:* tests/engine/test_renderer_subsystem.cpp: 'renderer_subsystem - the default shader directory beside the executable holds the built index'. Assert core::executable_directory() / "shaders" / "index.json" exists and equals CONJURE_TEST_SHADER_DIR. Skip on multi-config generators via a CONJURE_TEST_MULTI_CONFIG define until F329 lands.
- **F044** BLOCKER Resizing the window kills the app on D3D12: back-buffer framebuffers are still cached when ResizeBuffers runs (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:1395`). Still present: frame_loop::apply_resize (frame_loop.cpp:394-417) calls swapchain->resize first and renderer->on_swapchain_resized() (renderer.cpp:1395-1398) only on success, while the framebuffer cache still holds back-buffer TextureHandles (including the ImGui framebuffer at renderer.cpp:1345-1347), so ResizeBuffers fails twice and the engine quits (reproduced by the sweep). The fix spans the engine loop and the renderer, changes the documented event order (06_engine_loop.md:489, 11_platform.md:227) and GPU resource lifetime around the swapchain. *Fix:* Add a renderer hook called before the resize (e.g. renderer::before_swapchain_resize(): wait_idle, framebuffers->clear(), bindingSets->clear(), device run_garbage_collection) and call it in apply_resize before swapchain->resize, keeping on_swapchain_resized after success (or fold both into one 'prepare' call and make on_swapchain_resized a no-op); also call it on the retry path. Fix the order in test_renderer.cpp:305-306 and the design/API docs. The related D3D12 swapchain queue-flush defect (sweep report 'framebuffer resize ... 0xC0000005') touches rhi and should be fixed alongside. Risk: other holders of back-buffer handles (transient graph imports, binding sets caching SRVs) must also be dropped; only a real D3D12/Vulkan swapchain can prove it. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: '[gpu] renderer - rendering, resizing the swapchain and rendering again succeeds' (WARP device, hidden window); tests/engine/test_frame_loop.cpp: 'frame_loop - a resize drops the renderer's back-buffer framebuffers before resizing the swapchain'
- **F072** BLOCKER Resizing or minimise/restoring the window crashes the process (D3D12, Debug) (`engine/src/engine/frame_loop.cpp:398`). Still present. frame_loop.cpp:398 calls swapchain->resize before renderer->on_swapchain_resized (:401-404), so the renderer still holds back-buffer framebuffers during ResizeBuffers. swapchain_d3d12.cpp:175-176 waits with device->waitForIdle() and then releases the back buffers, which the sweep observed racing DXGI's internal present command list. The fix touches GPU synchronisation, resource lifetime and frame ordering across rhi, renderer and engine. *Fix:* Shape: (1) in apply_resize, have the renderer drop every reference to swapchain textures (cached back-buffer framebuffers, ImGui targets) before swapchain->resize, with a new renderer hook such as on_swapchain_resizing(), keeping on_swapchain_resized() after; (2) in d3d12_swapchain::resize, flush the graphics queue with an explicit fence (signal on the present queue, wait on the event) before release_back_buffers(), then runGarbageCollection, then ResizeBuffers; (3) handle minimise (0x0) by skipping the resize and present until restored. Make the Vulkan path symmetric (swapchain_vulkan.cpp:284). Risks: NVRHI keeps internal references (its own command lists and garbage queue), and DXGI's present queue is distinct from NVRHI's queue tracking. This must be verified under the debug layer on a real window, which CI does not have (REAL_WINDOW precondition). *Test:* tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp: "swapchain - resize and minimise-restore on a real window keep the device alive" (REAL_WINDOW). Engine-level: "frame_loop - a resize drops the renderer's swapchain framebuffers before the swapchain resizes" (null device, ordering asserted through a recording renderer hook).
- **F084** BLOCKER script.compile always fails in the shipped editor: ConjureManagedDir is passed as the relative path 'managed' (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_script.cpp:296`). Still present: managed_directory() falls back to the relative path "managed" (commands_script.cpp:291-297), which MSBuild resolves against <project>/scripts, while the scripting subsystem uses mServices->engineDirectory / "managed" (scripting_subsystem.cpp:106-108). The engine directory is only available through the private engine/src/engine/executable_path.h and engine_services; engine_context (engine_context.h:82-108) does not expose it, so the fix needs a public engine API the editor then follows. *Fix:* Shape: expose the resolved directory from the engine module, e.g. add `std::filesystem::path engineDirectory` to engine_context filled from services.engineDirectory in application.cpp (or publish executable_directory() as a CONJURE_API header), then in the editor resolve exactly as the scripting subsystem does: config->scripting.managedDirectory when set (made absolute against the engine directory if relative), else engineDirectory / "managed". For a meaningful regression test the harness needs a way to point the editor at the build's managed SDK (an editor option or engine flag that sets scripting.managedDirectory), then the dotnet case in test_commands_script.cpp:166-183 must require success instead of accepting COMPILE_FAILED. Hard because it changes the engine's public context (other modules follow it) and adds a configuration path. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_script.cpp: "script.compile - with dotnet and the managed SDK present the build succeeds" (gated on dotnet and CONJURE_EDITOR_TEST_MANAGED_DIR; replaces the either-or case)
- **F104** BLOCKER The Viewport never shows the scene through the editor camera; orbit/pan/zoom, picking and gizmos are disconnected from the picture (`engine/src/editor/ui/panels/viewport_panel.cpp:39`). Still present: viewport_panel.cpp:39-44 treats the panel as a pure input surface over an opaque ImGui window. renderer_subsystem builds views only from scene camera_components and screenshot_service.cpp:40 states that no hook exists for an editor-camera view. So orbiting, picking (aspect of mRectSize, :59) and the ImGuizmo matrices all use a camera and rectangle that the picture does not use. The fix needs a new renderer/engine hook, an off-screen target, ImGui texture registration and frame ordering, across renderer, engine and editor. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: (1) renderer/engine: an API to append an extra render_view (view and projection matrices, viewport size) that renders into a caller-owned render target, exposed through engine_context or renderer_subsystem and consumed in EXTRACT/render before the ImGui pass. (2) editor: the viewport panel owns a render target sized to mRectSize, recreates it on resize with deferred destruction after the frames in flight, registers it with imgui_renderer::register_texture and draws ImGui::Image where the InvisibleButton now sits. Picking and gizmos already use the editor camera and panel rectangle. (3) screenshot_service uses the same view. (4) Until it lands, amend 09 section 4 item 15(d) to say that the windowed editor viewport also does not show the editor camera. Risks: GPU resource lifetime on resize, the barrier or state transition for sampling a texture written earlier in the frame (NVRHI), the headless and off-screen path (item 15(d)), and the Scene/Game tab split of D18. Hard because it crosses renderer, engine and editor and touches frame ordering and GPU synchronisation. *Test:* tests/engine (renderer_subsystem): 'renderer_subsystem - an extra view renders into its own target' (null device: one extra view submitted and counted). A GPU-gated tests/editor process or integration case: 'viewport_panel - the viewport image shows the scene through the editor camera' (render a sprite, read back the target, pixel under the projected centre is the sprite colour).
- **F137** BLOCKER Every .gltf/.glb is imported as a physics collision mesh, never as a render model (`engine/src/assets/asset_importer.cpp:253`). Still present. collision_mesh_importer claims .gltf/.glb/.obj (collision_mesh_importer.cpp:29) and the renderer importer claims .gltf/.glb. importer_registry::find_for_path keeps the first importer on equal match length (asset_importer.cpp:253), and physics registers first, so every fresh glTF gets the collision importer pinned in its .meta. 14_physics.md:836-838 intends the .glb to be a render model, with collision geometry selected through the .meta importer. But 05_assets.md:826 and 14_physics.md:324 still list .gltf/.glb as collision extensions, so the fix crosses physics, assets, the design docs and an application-level test, and existing metas already pinned to the wrong importer need a migration decision. *Fix:* Recommended shape: (1) physics: drop '.gltf' and '.glb' from collision_mesh_importer's by-extension list (keep '.obj'; import() still branches on the extension at :531-532, so a .glb whose meta names conjure.physics.collision_mesh_importer keeps working). (2) assets: make extension conflicts visible. Have importer_registry::register_importer log a WARN naming both importers when an extension is already claimed, or reject it, or add an explicit priority. A WARN is the least invasive. (3) docs: update 05_assets.md:826, 14_physics.md:324, docs/api/physics/bodies-and-colliders.md:124 and the collision_mesh_asset.h:17 comment to say collision meshes from glTF are selected by naming the importer in the .meta. (4) A real-application test: a fresh .gltf scanned through the engine's full importer set resolves to conjure.renderer.gltf. Hard parts: projects that already have .meta files pinning conjure.physics.collision_mesh_importer for render models stay wrong (they need an editor 'reimport as' or a one-off migration warning), and registration order across subsystems is fragile, so the test must go through the real application rather than a private pipeline (tests/integration/test_assets_renderer_headless.cpp registers the renderer importer alone). *Test:* tests/engine/test_renderer_subsystem.cpp (or an editor command test): "regression - a fresh .gltf imports as a renderer model when physics is registered too" (the full subsystem set; asset type conjure.renderer.model; the meta names conjure.renderer.gltf). tests/physics/test_collision_mesh_asset.cpp: "collision_mesh_importer - claims only .obj by extension and imports a .glb named in its meta". tests/assets/test_asset_importer.cpp: "importer_registry - a second importer for a claimed extension is reported".
- **F179** BLOCKER .gltf/.glb are imported by the physics collision-mesh importer, never as a renderer model, in the real engine (`engine/src/assets/asset_importer.cpp:220`): same as F137.
- **F008** MAJOR Module-registered components lose their C++ default values (component_type_desc carries no default bytes) (`engine/include/conjure/ecs/component_type_desc.h:24`). Still present: component_type_desc (component_type_desc.h:24-34) has no default-value field, describe_component (47-59) never captures default_component_bytes<T>(), and register_described (component_registry.cpp:204-210) leaves info.defaultValue empty. Trivially copyable types with member initialisers therefore get no constructDefault (component.h:406-415), and world_structural.cpp:480-491 zero-fills them, unlike register_component (component_registry.h:109). The fix changes a trivially copyable module-ABI record whose exact shape the design records fix (01_ecs.md:711-720, 07_modules.md:792-793). The way to carry the defaults across a module unload is also a design choice, so this is not SMALL. *Fix:* Shape: add `const std::byte *defaultValue = nullptr; u32 defaultValueSize = 0;` to component_type_desc. It stays trivially copyable, and the generated ABI header hash already forces plugins to rebuild. In describe_component, point them at a function-local `static const std::vector<std::byte>` initialised from default_component_bytes<plain_type>(). This allocates during static init inside a noexcept function, so either drop noexcept or accept terminate on OOM. register_described copies the bytes into info.defaultValue, and validate_layout already rejects a size mismatch. Update the design records and the API/handbook pages. The rejected alternative is setting ops.constructDefault for these types: it needs no record change but leaves function pointers into an unloaded module. Risks: (1) a re-registration of the same name keeps the old defaults, because same_layout ignores defaultValue; (2) scripting fills the record by hand (script_component.cpp:357) and must keep working with the new zero defaults; (3) the module-boundary contract changes and needs the owner's sign-off on the design-record edit. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_component_type_desc.cpp: "component_type_desc - member initialisers survive registration". Use a struct with `f32 speed = 1.0f; f32 scale = 2.0f;`, registered through register_described(describe_component<T>()). Assert that info.defaultValue equals default_component_bytes<T>(), and that world.create_entity(mask) and world.add_raw(e, id) both yield speed 1 and scale 2.
- **F014** MAJOR macOS cannot create any swapchain and the error message points at a seam that does not exist (`engine/src/rhi/backends/vulkan/swapchain_vulkan.cpp:145`). Still present. swapchain_vulkan.cpp:145-150 rejects COCOA and points at platform's create_vulkan_surface (window.h:242), but rhi::swapchain::create takes only a native_window and the rhi exposes no VkInstance, so nothing can pass a platform-created surface. rhi_subsystem.cpp:94-101 never calls it. A windowed macOS start therefore always fails. The fix needs a new API seam across rhi, engine and platform, or a MoltenVK/VK_EXT_metal_surface path in the rhi, and the docs do not choose between them. *Fix:* Two shapes. (A) Surface factory: add an optional callback such as `std::function<core::result<void>(void *instance, void **surface)> createSurface` to native_window or swapchain::create. Then rhi_subsystem::to_native_window binds window->create_vulkan_surface, the Vulkan swapchain uses it for COCOA (and optionally for all kinds), and the device adds window->required_vulkan_instance_extensions() plus VK_KHR_portability_enumeration. (B) The rhi creates the surface itself through VK_EXT_metal_surface from a CAMetalLayer that platform supplies in native_window.window, which needs platform to create and attach the layer. Risks: surface ownership and destruction order (the surface must outlive the swapchain), MoltenVK portability flags on instance creation, and no macOS CI to verify on. Interim, docs only: add the gap to docs/design/09_cross_area_resolutions.md section 4 and reword the error to 'windowed macOS is not supported yet: Cocoa windows need a MoltenVK surface path'. Recommendation: (A), because the platform already implements create_vulkan_surface. *Test:* tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp (or a new tests/rhi/test_swapchain_vulkan.cpp): 'swapchain - [gpu] a Vulkan swapchain is created from a platform-supplied surface'. It is macOS/lavapipe gated; until a runner exists, add a null-device test that the surface callback is invoked and its result propagated.
- **F028** MAJOR Export with the documented distributionDirectory ships no shaders and still reports success (`engine/src/engine/export_pipeline.cpp:352`). Still present. engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:12 builds shaders into ${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/shaders, not bin/. Of the build trees on disk only two hand-made ones have bin/shaders. export_pipeline.cpp:352-356 turns a missing source/shaders into a warning and returns success. The documented distributionDirectory (docs/api/engine/export-and-diagnostics.md:41) is .../bin. Every export test sets copyShaders=false (tests/integration/test_export_roundtrip.cpp:83, tests/runtime/runtime_test_support.cpp:165). A real fix needs the shaders to exist where the distribution directory points, which is a change to the build structure, before the export can sensibly fail. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Build: make the shader blobs and index land next to the executables. Either set OUTPUT_DIR to the runtime output directory's shaders/ (taking care of multi-config generators, which append a config subfolder to bin/), or add a POST_BUILD copy of the conjure_shaders output into $<TARGET_FILE_DIR:conjure_runtime>/shaders. This also lets renderer_subsystem::shader_directory's <exe>/shaders default (renderer_subsystem.cpp:198-206) work for the editor and runtime, which is the related build finding. (2) Export: when settings.copyShaders && config.rendering.enabled and source/shaders/index.json is missing, return export_error(STEP_NAMES[4], "the distribution directory has no shaders/index.json") instead of a warning, and keep the warning when rendering is disabled. (3) Combine with F658's blob filter. Risks: the tests read CONJURE_SHADER_OUTPUT_DIR and must keep working; multi-config paths; the editor's project.export defaults copyShaders to copyRuntime (commands_project.cpp:193), so exports that 'succeeded' before will now fail loudly until the build change lands. *Test:* tests/engine/test_export.cpp: "export_pipeline - copyShaders with no shader index in the distribution directory is EXPORT_FAILED" (fake distribution directory without shaders/, rendering enabled). tests/integration/test_export_roundtrip.cpp: "export - the exported folder carries shaders/index.json from the distribution directory" (copyShaders=true against integration_test::runtime_bin_directory()).
- **F031** MAJOR The macOS legs run on macos-14 without selecting Xcode 16, but the build's own configure check demands it (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:57`). Still present: ci.yml:56-58 and nightly.yml:53-55 run macos-debug on macos-14 with only `brew install ninja`, while engine/src/core/jobs/CMakeLists.txt:13-29 fails the configure without std::jthread. The suggested 'Xcode 16.2' may not be enough: Apple's Xcode 16.0-16.2 libc++ is LLVM-17 based, where jthread/stop_token still need -fexperimental-library, so the right toolchain (Xcode 16.3+ / macos-15) is uncertain, can only be proven by a CI run, and the first macOS run is expected to surface other issues (09_cross_area_resolutions item 15(h)). *Fix:* Move both workflows to `os: macos-15` (default Xcode 16.4, Apple clang 17, libc++ with stable jthread) and add an explicit `sudo xcode-select -s /Applications/Xcode_16.4.app` step (if: runner.os == 'macOS') before Configure so an image default change cannot break it; print `clang++ --version` in the log. Correct the configure message in engine/src/core/jobs/CMakeLists.txt to 'libc++ 18+ (Xcode 16.3+)' after confirming on the runner, and update docs/handbook/ci.md:42 and :119 (macos-14 -> macos-15, AppleClang version). Risk: unverifiable locally; requires iterating on a CI run and may expose further macOS failures (visibility, @loader_path) that are separate work. *Test:* none: CI configuration; verified by a green macos-debug leg in ci.yml and nightly.yml
- **F033** MAJOR No install rules and no ConjureConfig.cmake: a game project outside the engine tree cannot build a native module (`cmake/ConjureModule.cmake:244`). Still present: no install() call exists in any CMakeLists.txt or *.cmake, and no ConjureConfig.cmake exists. docs/design/07_modules.md:727-729 and :869 and docs/handbook/module-system/creating-a-module.md:99 promise find_package(conjure CONFIG), and export_pipeline.cpp runs `cmake --build --preset` in the project directory. This is a large packaging feature across build and docs. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: - install(TARGETS conjure_shared EXPORT ConjureTargets ...) plus install(DIRECTORY) of engine/include and generated/include (abi_identity.h, build_info.h). - A configure_package_config_file ConjureConfig.cmake that recreates conjure::shared, conjure::platform_defines, the ABI runtime facts and a relocatable conjure_add_game_module. What makes it hard: - conjure_shared's INTERFACE compile definitions are $<TARGET_PROPERTY:lib,...> expressions over unexported static libraries (ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:341-343). They must be flattened. - glm, which conjure_shared links PUBLIC, and the ImGui interface for EDITOR_UI modules come from FetchContent and must be installed or re-provided. - conjure_add_game_module uses in-tree variables (CONJURE_HOST_*, output directories). - Multi-config trees. Interim: record the gap in 09 section 4, and say in writing-a-module.md and creating-a-module.md that native modules build only inside the engine tree. *Test:* tests/integration (slow, label modules): 'modules - a game module project outside the engine tree configures against the installed package, builds and loads'.
- **F034** MAJOR Export drops a module's content directory while the exported manifest still lists it (`engine/src/modules/module_manager.cpp:456`). Still present: export_layout (module_manager.cpp:456-458) copies the manifest with contentDirectories intact but never copies or cooks those directories. export_pipeline.cpp:215-240 cooks only assets:// roots, and the asset database does not index modules://. The runtime then mounts a missing directory (assets_module_consumer.cpp:143). The fix spans engine, assets and modules. *Fix:* Proper fix (07 3.7 and 9): have the asset database index modules://<name>/ content. Make the export pipeline add each exported module's content assets to the cook (or an always-include set). Write the exported manifest with an empty content list, or have the runtime resolve modules:// through the cooked bundle. Stopgap: copy each content directory raw in export_layout and record the deviation from 07 in the docs. Risks: cooked asset paths for module content, mounting in a cooked runtime, and existing tests that pin contentDirectories == {"assets"} without checking the directory. *Test:* tests/engine/test_export.cpp: "export_pipeline - a module's content assets are present in the exported game"; tests/modules/test_module_export_layout.cpp: "module_manager - export_layout leaves no content entry pointing at a missing directory".
- **F038** MAJOR -DCONJURE_WITH_RENDERER=OFF silently removes the engine, the runtime, the editor and the bindings (`engine/src/engine/CMakeLists.txt:8`). Still present: engine/src/rhi/CMakeLists.txt:10-12 returns when CONJURE_WITH_RENDERER is OFF, engine/src/engine/CMakeLists.txt:8-11 then skips conjure_engine with only a STATUS line, and runtime/CMakeLists.txt:15-18 and the editor skip in turn, so a renderer-off configure with CONJURE_BUILD_RUNTIME=ON builds no executable; CI (ci.yml:54) only varies CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING. The fix changes build-system structure across rhi, root CMake and CI. *Fix:* Preferred shape: build conjure_rhi with only the null backend when CONJURE_WITH_RENDERER is OFF (no NVRHI D3D12/Vulkan sources, no shader build), so engine/runtime/editor keep linking and rendering degrades to the null device, matching 06's 'renderer is an optional private dependency'; the root CMakeLists.txt:55 check for at least one RHI backend must then apply only when the renderer is ON. Alternative: a root FATAL_ERROR when CONJURE_BUILD_RUNTIME or CONJURE_BUILD_EDITOR is ON with CONJURE_WITH_RENDERER OFF. Either way add a CI leg configured with -DCONJURE_WITH_RENDERER=OFF -DCONJURE_WITH_PHYSICS=OFF -DCONJURE_WITH_AUDIO=OFF that builds and runs the tests. Hard parts: NVRHI is linked privately by rhi and the null backend may still include NVRHI types; the editor needs ImGui rendering through the renderer; the bindings CMake (engine/src/bindings/CMakeLists.txt:53) and the shader target must tolerate the absence. *Test:* CI: a new ci.yml matrix leg 'renderer-off' (configure with CONJURE_WITH_RENDERER/PHYSICS/AUDIO=OFF, build, ctest) that must produce conjure_runtime; tests/engine "application_headless - boots without the renderer module" compiled in that leg.
- **F040** MAJOR Nested prefabs: mark_instance overwrites inner links and a fresh outer instance never diffs clean (`engine/src/scene/prefab.cpp:108`). Still present. The editor runs build_document first (commands_prefab.cpp:158) and mark_instance afterwards (:207). mark_instance (prefab.cpp:106-114) relinks every subtree entity to its own guid, nested instances included. instantiate_many (prefab.cpp:164) keeps the archive's inner links for nested entries, so their link (inner guid) never equals the outer entry guid. One prefab_link cannot serve both the outer diff and the nested diff. The existing test 'nested prefab baked and inner marker preserved' uses the opposite order and never diffs. *Fix:* Shape of a fix: make the outer link the only per-entity link and resolve nested matching through the outer asset. Outer documents keep guid = outer entry identity and prefab_link = the inner link (as build_document writes today). instantiate_many always sets prefab_link = entry guid (it currently skips entries that already have one). diff/revert/apply of a nested instance root then map entity -> outer entry -> that entry's stored prefab_link -> inner asset entry, which requires locating the enclosing outer instance and its asset. The alternative is a second link field or component for nested entities, which changes a component layout, the file format and possibly the C# mirrors. Risks: mark_instance/build_document ordering, unpack/apply/revert semantics for nested instances, interaction with F039's duplicate links, and existing prefab files written under the current scheme. Design 13 3.12 must be rewritten. *Test:* tests/scene/test_prefab.cpp: add 'prefab - nested prefab created in editor order diffs clean for source and fresh instances'. In editor order (build_document, publish, mark_instance), CHECK that diff of the source's inner instance, diff of the outer source and diff of a fresh outer instance and its nested instance are all empty.
- **F041** MAJOR refresh_instances(onlyUnmodified) skips every instance that was placed or renamed (`engine/src/scene/prefab.cpp:406`). Still present, and worse than reported. prefab.cpp:404-408 gates on diff().empty(). diff excludes only guid, link and instance (lines 260-263), so a placed or renamed root counts as modified (test_prefab.cpp:453-463 asserts this). diff also compares against the payload currently published, and asset_manager.h:237-241 says reload swaps it in place. After any asset edit, every untouched instance therefore diffs MODIFIED or REMOVED against the new asset and is skipped, so onlyUnmodified refreshes almost nothing. The code matches the design text in 13 3.12 ('whose diff is empty'), so a correct fix needs a redesign of what 'unmodified' means. There is no production caller yet. *Fix:* Shape of a correct fix: define overrides against the asset version the instance was made from, not the current one. Keep that base per prefab id (for example a scene-side map from asset id to the std::shared_ptr<const entity_archive> captured at instantiate/revert time, or a content hash on prefab_instance; a hash is a component layout and file change). refresh_instances(onlyUnmodified) then reverts the instances whose diff against their base is empty, ignoring the root's conjure.scene.name and conjure.scene.local_transform, which revert carries over. A quick partial step is to ignore a root-only MODIFIED entry whose components are a subset of {name, local_transform} in the refresh gate. That fixes placed instances only while the asset is unchanged. Risks: memory for kept base archives, the base's lifetime across reload and unload, nested instances (F040), and keeping diff's override display unchanged for the editor. *Test:* tests/scene/test_prefab.cpp: add 'prefab - refresh_instances updates placed unmodified instances after the asset changes'. Instantiate three instances with distinct rootTransform and rootName and modify one bulb. Edit the asset (bulb speed), republish and reload it. CHECK that refresh_instances(target, id) returns 2, that those two instances carry the new speed with their placement kept, and that the modified instance is untouched.
- **F042** MAJOR Scene mutators on a parentless raw entity leave it outside the root list (`engine/src/scene/scene.cpp:487`). The defect is still there, but it is not a closed local fix. - These all add a scene component to a raw entity without insert_root: add_transform (scene.cpp:487-502), ensure_guid (591), set_guid (613), set_name, set_tag and set_layer (661, 674, 690), and set_enabled(false) (707). validate (scene.cpp:844) then flags the entity. - The behaviour reaches other modules. C# Entity.Create makes a bare world entity (scripting/native_api_core.cpp:230-252). Scripts then name, tag, layer or enable it through bindings (entries_entity.cpp:74, 149, 205, 249). With mutator adoption, every script-spawned entity that gets named joins mRoots, the hierarchy panel and save_scene. The editor's entity_ref::guid_of (editor/core/entity_ref.cpp:122) calls ensure_guid, so reporting a raw entity's guid would also turn it into a scene root. - The design does not settle whether raw script-created entities belong to the scene tree. 13_scene.md S7 covers only parent edits, and README:143-145 states only validate's rule. - The bindings' locked branch adds `name` through the structural guard's command buffer (entries_entity.cpp:66-72) and never reaches scene::set_name. After a mutator-only fix, the same C# call would leave a loose entity inside a query scope and adopt it outside one. A consistent fix needs either observer-based adoption in scene or a bindings change. - Adopting script spawns as roots also makes every root operation slower while root bookkeeping is linear (F349). *Fix:* Options: (a) Mutator adoption, the first reader's plan. Add a private `adopt_if_loose(entity)`: if the entity has no parent and root_index_of == NO_ROOT, call insert_root(APPEND_CHILD, SCENE_ID_AUTHORED) and bump_structure. Call it only on the branches that add a component: add_transform when local_transform was missing, ensure_guid and set_guid after their world.add, set_name, set_tag and set_layer after their add, and set_enabled(false) when it adds `disabled`. Document this on the seven mutators and in docs/api/scene/scenes-and-hierarchy.md. (b) Option (a) plus a catch-all for the raw and deferred routes. Observers on the ADDED event of the attribute and identity components (or one destroyed/added hook) queue an adopt entry for a parentless, non-root target into the repair queue. apply_hierarchy_changes inserts it as an authored root, which also covers entries_entity.cpp:72 and raw world.add. (c) Relax validate instead: name, tag, layer, guid and disabled stop implying tree membership, and only hierarchy and transform components require a root listing. Document this in the README and scene.h:564-569. (d) Make C# Entity.Create create through scene::create_entity (bindings or scripting change) so script entities are scene entities from the start. Recommendation: (a) now plus (b) for the locked bindings path, with the owner confirming that named script spawns belong in the saved hierarchy. Coordinate with F349 because mRoots grows with spawns. *Test:* tests/scene/test_scene.cpp: "regression - scene mutators adopt a raw parentless entity as an authored root". Use one SUBCASE per mutator (add_transform, ensure_guid, set_guid, set_name, set_tag, set_layer, set_enabled false). In each: raw = world.create_entity(), call the mutator, then check root_owner(raw) == SCENE_ID_AUTHORED, roots().back().entity == raw, and that validate() passes; a second call adds no second root. In 'scene - guid helpers', add CONJURE_CHECK_OK(target.validate()) before the raw destroy at line 772. tests/bindings/test_entity_bindings.cpp: a raw entity named through entity_set_name both outside and inside a query scope ends up the same way after the flush and step (listed root, or ignored under option (c)), and validate() passes.
- **F043** MAJOR restore() leaves scene_manager records stale when scenes were loaded or unloaded since capture (`engine/src/scene/scene_snapshot.cpp:45`). The defect is still there. restore() (scene_snapshot.cpp:44-85) clears the scene and reassigns root owners but never touches scene_manager records, and play_controller.cpp:74 only calls scene::restore. 13_scene.md:438-439 assumes the editor restores the same loaded set, which scripts loading or unloading during play break. A fix crosses scene and editor and touches asset residency and scene_id generations. *Fix:* Give scene_manager a reconcile step that play_controller::stop calls after scene::restore. Either capture the manager's records next to the snapshot (ids, assets, paths, names, load order, batches) and restore them, or add `reconcile(const scene_snapshot &)`: - unload records created after capture (their roots are already gone); - re-create records whose owners appear in snapshot.rootOwners but died during play, with the same scene_id slot and generation so restored owners match, re-acquiring their asset batch with load_blocking or load_batch. What makes it hard: re-acquiring batches changes residency during stop; generation reuse must not collide with ids handed out during play; it is an editor and scene change; and 13_scene.md 3.14 must be updated. *Test:* tests/editor/test_play_controller.cpp: "play_controller - stop reconciles scenes loaded and unloaded during play". It covers scenario A (additive load in play; after stop, loaded_scenes() no longer lists it and its assets are collected) and scenario B (unload in play; after stop, is_loaded(id) is true and unload(id) succeeds). Add a matching scene_manager-level case in tests/scene/test_scene_manager.cpp.
- **F065** MAJOR Entity.Find / Entity.Name never see names authored in the scene (`engine/src/scripting/native_api_core.cpp:289`). Still present: entity_get_name/entity_find_by_name (native_api_core.cpp:276-295) read only script_runtime::mEntityNames (script_runtime.cpp:680-695). That table is fed only by entity_create (native_api_core.cpp:251) and bindings entity_set_name (entries_entity.cpp:82), so names authored in a scene (scene::name) are invisible to C#, and Entity.Create(name) adds no scene::name. Fixing it needs bindings to answer from scene::name_of/find_by_name. fill_core_native_entries (native_api_core.cpp:546-557) unconditionally overwrites those slots after bindings fill the table (script_runtime.cpp:110), so the fix crosses the scripting and bindings ownership split (15_bindings B2, R64). *Fix:* Shape: make the scene the source of truth when one exists. (1) scripting: let fill_core_native_entries leave entity_create/get_name/find_by_name alone when already filled, or let bindings re-fill after initialize. Keep the runtime table only as the ecs-only fallback. (2) bindings: implement entity_get_name from scene::name_of (string_id -> interned text, which needs a reverse lookup of the string_id table), entity_find_by_name from scene::find_by_name(string_id::from_hash(fnv1a64(name))) with a defined winner, and entity_create(name) adding scene::name (through the deferred buffer while locked). (3) Update the docs (Entity.cs:41/82 promises 'or by the scene'). Risks: the fill order across two modules and the ABI ownership table, string_id reverse lookup cost, allocation-free steady state, and the interplay with F430/F604 (the fallback table's lifetime and winner). Decide F604's 'first' rule at the same time. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_entity_bindings.cpp: 'entity bindings - Entity.Find and Entity.Name see a name authored in a loaded scene' (load a scene with an entity named Player; entity_find_by_name("Player") returns it and entity_get_name returns "Player"). Plus 'entity bindings - Entity.Create(name) gives the entity a scene::name'. Keep tests/scripting/test_native_entries.cpp's ecs-only case as the fallback test.
- **F073** MAJOR A subsystem added by a module entry is destroyed after its library was unloaded (`engine/src/engine/application.cpp:531`). Still present: application.cpp:273-275 accepts module-entry subsystems during step 15, stop_subsystems (application.cpp:521-525) shuts down in reverse so modules_subsystem::shutdown (modules_subsystem.cpp:257-260, unload_all) unloads every library before mSubsystems.clear() at application.cpp:531 runs their destructors from unmapped code; a hot reload keeps the old object in mSubsystems too. The fix touches shutdown ordering and needs module_manager to know which module owns a subsystem (reload/unload refusal), plus a new shared-library plugin fixture. *Fix:* Shape: record, for each subsystem added while mAcceptingModuleSubsystems, which module is currently running its entry (module_manager would have to expose the module id being loaded, modules area) or at least the index range [modulesIndex + 1, mUserSubsystemBegin); in stop_subsystems shut down and destroy (reset the unique_ptr) every module-added subsystem before calling the modules subsystem's shutdown, then erase their slots; refuse unload/reload of a module that registered a subsystem (or tear its subsystem down before the reload and re-add after, which needs a new lifecycle hook). Hard parts: shutdown order versus the assets/ecs swap at application.cpp:514-520, reload semantics, and a test plugin DLL built by tests/engine CMake. *Test:* tests/engine/test_startup_order.cpp: "startup_order - a subsystem added by a module entry is destroyed before its library unloads" using a new shared-library fixture module whose entry calls add_subsystem and whose subsystem destructor records into a host-owned flag checked before unload.
- **F078** MAJOR A failed system registration at step 18 only logs; the engine starts without transform, physics or script systems (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/scene_subsystem.cpp:61`). Still present. engine_subsystem::register_systems is a public void virtual (engine_subsystem.h:50). application::start_subsystems ignores per-subsystem failures (application.cpp:474-476) and only checks scheduler->finalise. The adapters log an ERROR and continue (scene_subsystem.cpp ~:59-62, audio_subsystem.cpp:116-138, scripting_subsystem.cpp:169-177, physics, renderer). The proper fix changes a public virtual that tests/support, the test probes and the docs override or describe. An engine-internal variant exists but leaves user subsystems uncovered, so the choice is a design one. *Fix:* Shape A, public: change the virtual to `[[nodiscard]] virtual core::result<void> register_systems(...)`. Have the built-in adapters return the scene/physics/scripting/audio/renderer result. In application step 18, return make_error(SCHEDULER_CONFLICT, "<subsystem>: ...") on the first failure. Update every override (tests/support headless_engine.cpp, engine_test_support probes) and 06 3.4. Shape B, engine-only: add `std::optional<core::error> registrationFailure` to engine_services, have the adapters set it, and check it after the loop. Risks: the editor may prefer to start degraded rather than refuse to start; partial registrations left in the scheduler when startup unwinds. *Test:* tests/engine/test_startup_order.cpp: "startup_order - a built-in system whose name is already taken fails initialise with SCHEDULER_CONFLICT" (a user subsystem pre-registers a system named like scene's propagation system before step 18)
- **F080** MAJOR An editor export is not a runnable game: no runtime, no managed code, and the response names a runtime that does not exist (`engine/src/engine/export_pipeline.cpp:365`). Still present. commands_project.cpp:190-193 defaults copyRuntime to !distribution.empty(), so the pipeline's 'no distribution directory' warning (export_pipeline.cpp:336-339) is skipped. bootstrapProject and scriptsProject are never set anywhere in engine/src/editor, so export_pipeline.cpp:269-272 always skips managed publishing. `config` is read and discarded (:202-203). Step 6 (export_pipeline.cpp:365-367) rewrites only cooked/cooked_root and leaves scripting.assembly pointing at the development path. report.steps records skipped steps as done (:212, :310, :360). The fix spans the editor command, the export pipeline and the protocol schema, and needs layout knowledge (where Conjure.Bootstrap and <project>/Scripts live). *Fix:* Shape: (a) in project.export, default distributionDirectory to the editor's own executable directory (engine_services::engineDirectory) when the args omit it, or fail with BAD_ARGUMENTS when no runtime can be found; (b) fill bootstrapProject from the engine layout (managed/Conjure.Bootstrap/*.csproj relative to the engine root) and scriptsProject from <project>/Scripts/*.csproj when it exists; (c) honour or remove the `config` argument; (d) in step 6, rewrite scripting.assembly to the published location under managed/ when report.managedPublished, and set scripting.enabled/required consistently when nothing was published; (e) record a step in report.steps only when it did work, and list skipped steps separately (e.g. report.skippedSteps). This changes export.h's report shape and the protocol response, so editor, engine and generated protocol docs must move together. Risk: CI and editor tests that rely on today's cheap export. The managed publish needs dotnet on PATH in the test environment. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: "project.export - without a distribution directory reports the runtime as not copied and returns no runtimePath". tests/engine/test_export.cpp: "export_pipeline - the exported project.json points scripting.assembly at the published managed folder".
- **F082** MAJOR No test runs an executable with a real window or exercises a swapchain resize, so three blockers went unseen (`tests/engine/test_render_failure.cpp:151`). Still present: test_render_failure.cpp:151 is the only resize case and needs no swapchain. No test in tests/runtime, tests/engine or tests/integration uses precondition::REAL_WINDOW (it is used only in tests/platform and tests/support). The retry-then-quit rule in frame_loop::apply_resize (frame_loop.cpp:394-417) has no test with a swapchain. Covering it needs a new null-backend fault (rhi::null_fault has only WAIT_IDLE and PRESENT, device.h:32-36), engine tests and REAL_WINDOW runtime tests. The windowed tests also cannot pass until F071 is fixed. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In rhi, add null_fault::RESIZE (the next null swapchain resize fails with SWAPCHAIN_CREATION_FAILED, optionally a count), documented in device.h and tested in tests/rhi. (2) In tests/engine/test_render_failure.cpp, boot with a null swapchain and inject RESIZE. One failure must log a WARN and retry on the next frame; two must request_quit(RUNTIME_FAILURE). (3) In tests/runtime, add REAL_WINDOW-gated cases that run conjure_runtime windowed for --frames 3 (exit 0, and the 'stopped with' line reaches the log file), that resize the window and that minimise to 0x0. Risks: real-window tests on CI agents without a desktop session (precondition skip); dependence on the F071 shutdown fix and on the shader-directory finding; timing-sensitive resize events. *Test:* tests/engine/test_render_failure.cpp: "render_failure - a swapchain resize that fails twice quits with RUNTIME_FAILURE and once retries"; tests/runtime/test_runtime_windowed.cpp: "runtime - a windowed run of three frames exits 0" (REAL_WINDOW)
- **F088** MAJOR scene.save / scene.save_as (and prefab.apply) during play write the play-time state to disk (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_scene.cpp:372`). Still present: scene.save and scene.save_as are registered MUTATES \| NO_UNDO \| REQUIRES_SCENE (commands_scene.cpp:372, :383) and prefab.apply MUTATES \| REQUIRES_PROJECT \| REQUIRES_SCENE (commands_prefab.cpp:487), so during play they serialise the live play world and clear the dirty flag. 04_editor.md 4.4 lists scene.save as M (not E), so refusing versus saving the edit snapshot is a behaviour choice the design does not settle, and the fix spans editor, the design doc and the agent skill. *Fix:* Recommended: add command_flags::EDITING_ONLY to scene.save, scene.save_as, prefab.apply and prefab.create (it writes the play-time subtree too); make editor.quit {saveChanges:true} stop play first (context.play().stop(), as prepare_for_detach in commands_modules.cpp:161-178 does) before dispatching scene.save, otherwise the new flag makes it fail during play. Update 04_editor.md 4.4 flag letters, regenerate docs/generated/editor_protocol.json, and change the skill's 'Stop play before saving' advice into the rule. Alternative (heavier): save the play_controller's snapshot instead of the live world (restore it into a scratch scene and serialise that). Check the UI's Ctrl+S / File > Save during play shows the WRONG_PLAY_STATE refusal. *Test:* tests/editor/test_play_controller.cpp: "play - scene.save, scene.save_as and prefab.apply are refused while playing and the file keeps the editing state"
- **F098** MAJOR physics.raycast and view.pick mode:"physics" never hit anything while EDITING (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_physics.cpp:20`). Still present: bodies are created by the physics push system in PHYSICS_STEP (physics_systems.cpp:61-65), which runs only in the fixed loop while playing, so queries while EDITING see an empty world, contradicting the comment at commands_physics.cpp:19-20 and design 04 3.8/D10 (physics.raycast flagged S only, 04_editor.md:890); test_picker.cpp:154 asserts the miss. Making the world reflect the scene while editing touches physics, the engine frame and the editor. *Fix:* Shape of the design-conforming fix: give physics_world a sync-without-step entry (create/update/destroy bodies from the ECS, no integration) and run it while the fixed loop is paused (from the physics subsystem or lazily from the editor query commands and view.pick mode physics before querying). Risks: per-frame body sync cost on large scenes, play.start must not inherit editing-time body state (velocities, sleeping), determinism of play sessions, and the ordering between editor command dispatch and physics systems. The fallback (flag the commands WRONG_PLAY_STATE or add simulated:false while editing and fix the comment/docs) contradicts the design and would need the owner's decision. *Test:* tests/editor/test_picker.cpp change "pick - physics mode delegates to the physics world and misses without bodies" into "pick - physics mode hits a static collider while editing"; add tests/editor/test_command_catalogue fixture or test_commands_* case "physics.raycast - hits a static box while editing"
- **F099** MAJOR view.screenshot on a real device succeeds with an image of the clear colour; camera and scene are never rendered (`engine/src/editor/core/screenshot_service.cpp:41`). Still present. renderer_screenshot_backend creates a target and reads it back without rendering any view into it (the comment at screenshot_service.cpp:39-43, and capture() ignores the request at :73), so a windowed or offscreen editor answers ok with a clear-colour PNG. camera is ignored and jpg is refused despite 04_editor.md:378. The real fix needs a renderer hook to append a camera view targeting the render target during EXTRACT, which is cross-module frame-ordering work. *Fix:* Shape: the renderer exposes a way for the editor to submit an extra render_view (camera matrices, layer mask, target id) for the coming frame, as designed in R12 (09:33). screenshot_service submits it for waitFrames+1 frames, then calls read_back. The camera argument resolves editor/game/guid through editor_camera and scene cameras. Interim (owner decision): answer NOT_AVAILABLE on a real device until the hook exists, and record the gap in 09 section 4. This would break any --offscreen golden that relies on the blank image. Also either drop jpg from 3.10 or implement stbi_write_jpg. *Test:* tests/editor/test_screenshot_service.cpp "screenshot_service - the renderer backend renders the chosen camera into the target" (offscreen/real-device precondition; checks the image is not uniform) and, for the interim, "screenshot_service - a backend without a view hook answers NOT_AVAILABLE".
- **F103** MAJOR scene.new, scene.open, project.open and editor.quit discard unsaved scene changes silently (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_scene.cpp:53`). Still present: scene_new unloads at commands_scene.cpp:53 and scene_open replaces at :75-80 with no dirty check, project_open (commands_project.cpp:84-109) restarts without one, and editor_quit (commands_editor.cpp:277) silently skips the save when the dirty scene has no path and still quits 0; only scene_close (:143-146) guards. The API fix follows scene.close's precedent, but the UI callers (File > New Scene at editor_workspace.cpp:57, asset browser double-click/Open Scene at asset_browser_panel.cpp:97/113) would start failing on a dirty scene and need a Save/Discard/Cancel prompt, and the change spans editor, design docs, the generated catalogue and the agent skill. *Fix:* Shape: add a helper in commands_scene.cpp (exposed through command_support.h for commands_project.cpp) `core::result<void> require_clean_or_discard(editor_context &, const core::json::value &args)` that returns INVALID_ARGS "the scene has unsaved changes: save it or pass discard: true" when has_scene() && scene_dirty() && !bool_or(args, "discard", false); call it first in scene_close (replacing :143-146), scene_new, scene_open and project_open, and add `"discard":{"type":"boolean"}` to the three schemas. editor_quit: when saveChanges && dirty && scene_path().empty() return INVALID_ARGS "the scene has no path: scene.save_as first, or quit without saveChanges" and do not request_quit. UI: the New Scene menu item and the asset browser's scene open must open a modal (Save / Discard / Cancel) when the scene is dirty and only then fire the command with discard:true (or scene.save first). Then fix every test/fixture that replaces a dirty scene (test_commands_scene_project.cpp project.open case creates an entity first; check build_grid_sweeper.json flows), regenerate docs/generated/editor_protocol.json (--conjure-update-golden), and update 04_editor.md 4.4 rows, ai-protocol.md and the skill. Risks: silently breaks existing agent command files that relied on replace; the modal is new UI design work; project.open is deferred to the next frame so the guard must run at request time. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: "scene.new / scene.open / project.open - refuse a dirty scene unless discard is true" and "editor.quit - saveChanges on a dirty scene without a path is refused and the editor keeps running"
- **F105** MAJOR No way to open or create a project from the UI; started without --project the editor is inert (`engine/src/editor/ui/editor_workspace.cpp:249`). Still present. The File menu (editor_workspace.cpp:249-269) has New Scene, Save Scene, Save Project, Export and Quit only. project.open, project.create and project.list_scenes are reachable only from the AI Session panel. Without --project the workspace shows hints only. The design settles the remedy as a start screen with recent projects, Open and New (04_editor.md 3.14, step 21), and recent projects live in the per-user configuration folder (3.15, step 22, platform::user_config_directory), so the fix spans editor and platform. *Fix:* Shape: - When !context.has_project(), the workspace draws a start screen instead of the dockspace. It lists recent projects (read from editor_user_settings in the user configuration folder) and offers Open (path prompt -> project.open) and New (directory + name -> project.create, then project.open). - Add File > New Project..., Open Project... and Open Scene... (a list from project.list_scenes -> scene.open). - Record each successfully opened project in the recent list. Hard parts: - project.open restarts the engine and recreates the UI layer (editor_application::apply_pending_project_open), so the start screen must survive the swap and any modal state must not dangle. - The recent list needs the new platform user-config API and a per-user settings file format. - An unsaved-scene guard is needed before project switches. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_editor_workspace.cpp: "editor_workspace - without a project the start screen offers Open and New and project.create from it opens the project". Drive the start screen's helper methods against a headless editor created withProject = false.
- **F117** MAJOR Export dialog: the Configuration field does nothing, the result has no runtime, warnings are hidden and the UI blocks (`engine/src/editor/ui/panels/export_dialog.cpp:127`). Still present. The dialog sends {directory, config} (export_dialog.cpp:127), but project_export discards config (commands_project.cpp:202-203). Without distributionDirectory, copyRuntime and copyShaders are false (:190-193). The dialog ignores the warnings array (export_dialog.cpp:60-66), and pipeline.run executes synchronously inside the handler (commands_project.cpp:209-223), so the UI freezes. Making the export truly asynchronous means running engine::export_pipeline off the main thread (threading, engine module), and the Configuration field needs a product decision. *Fix:* Shape: (a) Decide whether Configuration is removed or maps to something real; recommendation: remove it until a build-configuration concept exists. (b) Add a Distribution directory field to the dialog, defaulting to the editor executable's directory when it contains conjure_runtime, so exports are runnable. (c) Show the result's warnings array in the status. (d) Run the pipeline as a job with a frame task that marshals progress events and the final reply to the main thread, keeping the async_reply contract. This needs export_pipeline's asset-database and VFS access audited for off-main-thread use, or the pipeline split into resumable per-frame steps. Risks: concurrent scene or asset edits during export; cancellation on project.open (cancel_all). *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: "project.export - a distribution directory copies the runtime and the reply lists warnings". tests/editor/ui/panels/test_export_dialog.cpp: "export_dialog - the status shows the export's warnings and the dialog stays responsive while the export runs".
- **F120** MAJOR The committed asset lock is empty and CI never runs --locked, so downloaded test assets are never verified against a reviewed hash (`tools/test_assets.lock:1`). Still present: - tools/test_assets.lock is `{}`. - .github/workflows/ci.yml:106 and nightly.yml:88 call the downloader without --locked. - The cache key at ci.yml:103 hashes test_assets.json only. - URLs track glTF-Sample-Assets `main`. So every cache miss pins whatever upstream serves into a throw-away runner lock, contrary to docs/handbook/ci.md:67 and the script docstring. The fix needs a network download and a reviewed pin, and spans tools and CI. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: 1. After F554 trims the manifest, run `python tools/download_test_assets.py` on a trusted machine, review the five digests and commit tools/test_assets.lock. 2. Pin the glTF-Sample-Assets URLs to a commit SHA instead of main. Poly Haven URLs cannot be pinned, so the hash is their guard. 3. Switch ci.yml:106 and nightly.yml:88 to `--locked`. 4. Key the cache on hashFiles('tools/test_assets.json', 'tools/test_assets.lock'). 5. Update docs/handbook/ci.md:67. Risks: an upstream change now fails CI until it is re-pinned (intended), and this must land after F554 because the lock is keyed by asset name. *Test:* tools/tests/test_download_test_assets.py: 'test_assets - every enabled manifest entry is pinned in tools/test_assets.lock'. CI's Download test assets step with --locked is the end-to-end check.
- **F128** MAJOR One case in the default unit run takes 84 s alone in Debug (93-99 s under ctest-like load), a timeout risk on hosted runners (`tests/editor/test_protocol_robustness.cpp:47`). Still present: the corpus case (test_protocol_robustness.cpp:47-92) runs all 62 corpus entries (1 MiB and 0.5 MiB payloads among them) in the default editor;unit run, and the sweep measured 84 s alone. About 1.4 s per entry over roughly 5 headless frames each points to an unexplained slowness (likely quadratic buffering of large payloads in the protocol server or loopback transport), not just a missing label. The finding also covers physics label mismatches, so the fix needs investigation and spans editor and physics. *Fix:* (1) Profile the case per corpus entry. If the time is in protocol_server line framing or the loopback transport, fix the algorithm (consume the buffer by offset rather than erasing its front) in the editor protocol code, which may remove most of the 84 s. (2) Move what remains slow, following tests/physics/CMakeLists.txt:42-52, into a conjure_editor_slow_tests executable labelled editor;slow (full corpus sweep, entity.create_many 10,000, possibly the catalogue). Keep a small-entry corpus subset in the default run. (3) Physics: give physics.determinism's inline 8 s case the [slow] tag or move it, and move physics_stack (tagged [slow] but in the default executable) into conjure_physics_slow_tests. Risk: hosted-runner timeouts until it lands, and nightly coverage must keep the full corpus. *Test:* tests/editor/test_protocol_robustness.cpp: 'protocol_robustness - small corpus entries are answered or ignored' (default run) plus the full 'protocol_robustness - every corpus entry is answered or ignored, never fatal' in the editor slow executable; tests/editor: 'protocol_server - a 1 MiB line is rejected in linear time' if the cause is the server.
- **F129** MAJOR Windowed Debug editor and runtime die at exit with unhandled exception 0x0000087D (D3D12 debug layer) instead of exit code 0 (`engine/src/engine/engine_config.cpp:31`). Still present: engine_config.cpp:31 forces rendering.gpuValidation in every Debug build, rhi_subsystem.cpp:65-66 turns on the D3D12 debug layer and GPU-based validation from it, project.cpp ORs the project flag in (gpuValidation = project \|\| config), and nothing turns it off; no D3D12 info-queue callback logs the message behind the 0x87D exit. Diagnosing the actual validation error means GPU resource-lifetime work at device/swapchain destruction in rhi, plus engine config and an integration test. *Fix:* Shape: (1) rhi: register an ID3D12InfoQueue1 message callback (or drain ID3D12InfoQueue at shutdown and after each present in Debug) that forwards to LOG_RHI with severity, so the offending message is visible; (2) find and fix the live-object or invalid-state error at shutdown (likely an outstanding reference or a swapchain released while in use) in the rhi destruction order; (3) engine: a `--no-gpu-validation` flag and a project `rendering.gpu_validation: false` that can actually switch it off (project::apply_to currently ORs), documented in command_line help; (4) integration: a [gpu][process] test that runs conjure_runtime windowed for a few frames and asserts exit code 0. Hard parts: reproducing needs the D3D12 SDK layers on a real GPU, the break-on-error behaviour of the debug layer, and the fix lives in GPU resource lifetime. *Test:* tests/integration: "runtime_process - a windowed Debug run exits with code 0" ([gpu][process], skipped without a real adapter) plus a tests/rhi case "device_d3d12 - validation messages reach the log".
- **F134** MAJOR wait()/parallel_for helping runs any queued job, so the main thread can execute a whole `dotnet build` or file-watcher pass inside a frame (`engine/src/core/jobs/job_system.cpp:680`). Still present: wait() (job_system.cpp:679-683) pops from every lane including LOW, and long blocking work runs on the pool: managed_build.cpp:159 schedules the dotnet build at LOW, and file_watcher.cpp:100 schedules the stat pass. A main-thread wait(stageFence) from ecs/scheduler.cpp:212 can therefore run a whole build inline. The fix spans core scheduling policy plus scripting and assets. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In core, stop helpers from taking the LOW lane. Give pop_locked a 'helping' flag that skips LOW, used by wait() and parallel_for's caller loop. Workers, wait_idle() and stop()'s drain still take LOW, so LOW work cannot be starved forever. (2) Move genuinely blocking work off the pool: managed_build runs process::run on a core::jobs::dedicated_thread (or keep LOW once (1) lands), and the file_watcher pass likewise. (3) Document in jobs README and api/core/jobs.md that jobs must be short and that blocking I/O or processes belong on a dedicated_thread. Risks: wait_idle semantics (it must still drain LOW), the 0-worker inline mode, and shutdown ordering of the new dedicated threads against the job system. This touches three owner areas and the frame-time behaviour of every parallel stage. *Test:* tests/core/jobs/test_job_system.cpp: "job_system - wait does not help with LOW priority jobs" (occupy all workers, queue a LOW job that sets a flag and sleeps, wait on an unrelated NORMAL fence from the main thread, assert the LOW job did not run on the main thread).
- **F135** MAJOR AI TCP port accepts unauthenticated commands by default and tolerates garbage lines, so any web page can drive the editor (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/protocol_server.cpp:293`): same as F180.
- **F139** MAJOR project.export / --export can never produce the runnable folder the design promises (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_project.cpp:188`). Still present: project_export (commands_project.cpp:188-203) never sets bootstrapProject, scriptsProject, runtimeIdentifier or alwaysInclude, so step 3 always takes the 'no bootstrap project' warning (export_pipeline.cpp:269-272) and managed code is never published, contrary to 06_engine_loop.md 3.6 and export.h:27. Resolving those inputs needs layout knowledge (managed/Conjure.Bootstrap relative to the editor install, the project's Scripts csproj, host RID, an always_include list in project.json) spanning editor, engine and the project file format. *Fix:* Shape: an editor-side resolver that defaults bootstrapProject to the installed managed/Conjure.Bootstrap/Conjure.Bootstrap.csproj next to the editor (build tree and install layouts differ), scriptsProject to <project>/Scripts/*.csproj when present, runtimeIdentifier to the host RID, distributionDirectory to the editor's own directory unless given (see F029), and alwaysInclude from a new project.json member (file-format addition owned by the engine). Risks: install vs build-tree layouts, dotnet availability in CI, export time, and the project.json schema change. Cover with an editor-level export test that asserts managed/ exists when scripting is enabled (skipped without dotnet). *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp "project.export - with scripting enabled the managed assemblies are published" (precondition dotnet); tests/integration/test_export_roundtrip.cpp "export roundtrip - the exported folder boots with conjure_runtime"
- **F142** MAJOR model_prefab_converter is unreachable from production code and fails for multi-root models (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/model_prefab_converter.cpp:52`). Still present. model_prefab_converter::convert (model_prefab_converter.cpp:52-110) passes every parentless node to builder.add_node with INVALID_INDEX, so a model with two scene roots fails prefab_builder::build ('exactly one root', 13_scene.md:426). The converter is referenced only by its own files and tests/engine/test_renderer_subsystem.cpp:211: commands_asset.cpp never calls it, although 13_scene.md:430 says asset.import of a glTF writes <model>.prefab.json. Wiring it up needs a public engine API, because the converter header is private under engine/src, plus editor command changes, so it crosses areas. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In convert(), when the model has more than one parentless node, add a synthetic root node named prefabName (identity transform) and parent the model roots under it. Apply the 13_scene.md 3.13 rule that cameras are added only on request, which needs an options argument. (2) Expose a public engine entry point (for example conjure/engine/model_prefab.h: convert_model_to_prefab(registry, model, name, options)). (3) In the editor's asset.import, after a glTF import, write <model>.prefab.json next to the source and import it. Decide what happens when a prefab with that name already exists and was edited by the user (overwrite, skip or suffix). Risks: hot reload re-triggering on the written file; stable sub-asset uuids across re-imports; deciding the overwrite policy. *Test:* tests/engine/test_renderer_subsystem.cpp: "model_prefab_converter - a model with two root nodes gets one synthetic root named after the model"; tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp: "asset.import - a glTF import writes the model's prefab next to the source"
- **F143** MAJOR A headless editor serving the AI protocol free-runs at about 20,000 frames per second (`engine/src/engine/frame_pacer.cpp:57`): same as F726.
- **F145** MAJOR Entity.Find / Entity.Name only see names set through scripts, not scene-authored names (`engine/src/scripting/script_runtime.cpp:688`): same as F065.
- **F148** MAJOR audio_sync re-applies every source every frame and never handles clip hot reload (design 12 4.4) (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/audio_subsystem.cpp:245`). Still present. audio_subsystem::sync (audio_subsystem.cpp:197-255) takes audio_source by mutable reference and calls engine.apply_source for every valid voice every frame (:244-245). handle.clipVersion is written at :315 and never read, there is no several-listeners WARN, and the listener has no velocity (:262-277). That is contrary to docs/design/12_audio.md:706-715. The fix spans the engine adapter (split the passes, restart on a version change, listener velocity and warning) and the audio engine (setters that skip unchanged values), and it interacts with change detection, because the sync system itself writes playbackRequest and isPlaying. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Keep the state pass (requests, isPlaying, finished handles) but read-only where possible. Write isPlaying and playbackRequest only when they actually change, so the column is not stamped every frame. (2) Run apply_source only for changed<audio_source> chunks, which needs ecs change filters in the view. (3) When clip.version() != handle.clipVersion, stop (release) the voice and replay it. (4) In audio_engine, make set_volume / set_pitch / set_pan / set_loop / set_group / set_attenuation / set_spatial early-out when the clamped value equals slot.params, so no ring command is pushed. (5) Listener: WARN once when several are active, and derive velocity from the previous frame's position. Risks: change ticks stamped by this very system (the self-write problem); the editor's paused mode (RUNS_WHILE_PAUSED); interaction with the F144 voice release. *Test:* tests/engine/test_audio_subsystem.cpp: "audio_subsystem - an unchanged source pushes no mixer command after its first frame" and "audio_subsystem - a hot-reloaded clip restarts the source on the new version"; tests/audio/test_audio_engine.cpp: "audio_engine - setting an unchanged volume pushes no command"
- **F153** MAJOR Undo of entity.create_many (entity.destroy_many of roots) is quadratic: 16,000 entities take 23 s, 100,000 never finish (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_entity.cpp:282`). Still present: entity_destroy_many sorts with a comparator calling tree_position (commands_entity.cpp:282-284), which scans scene.roots() linearly for roots (command_support.cpp:285-290); restore_inverse calls tree_position again per root (:304), and destroy_now -> erase_root -> root_index_of is another front-to-back linear scan (scene.cpp:904-935). Removing the quadratic cost needs a scene-side change to root bookkeeping as well as the editor change, plus a benchmark. *Fix:* Shape: (1) editor: compute each root's (position) key once - one pass over scene.roots() into a vector indexed by entity.index (children use sibling_index) - sort (key, entity) pairs, and add restore_inverse(scene, root, index) so the per-root capture does not rescan; (2) scene: make root_index_of O(1) (an entity.index -> root slot map maintained by insert_root/erase_root/reorder, or at least scan from the back so trailing roots are found first) so destroying N trailing roots is linear; (3) check the undo path's inverse (editor.batch of N entity.restore with insert_root vector inserts and per-child schema validation) and redo; (4) add a bench_editor case for create_many 20,000 + undo + redo. Risks: root order is shared with additive scenes/scene_manager owner ids, and the sibling-order restore semantics guarded by test_commands_entity.cpp:147 must stay exact. *Test:* tests/benchmarks/bench_editor.cpp "bench_editor - undo and redo of create_many 20,000"; tests/editor/test_commands_entity.cpp "regression - undo of create_many 20,000 finishes within a time box and restores the scene"
- **F154** MAJOR scene.open, scene.new and project.open silently discard unsaved changes while scene.close refuses (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_scene.cpp:65`): same as F103.
- **F159** MAJOR Enumeration fields accept any number that fits the storage: projection 99 is stored, saved and reloaded (`engine/src/editor/core/component_access.cpp:1`). Still present: core::reflect read_enum accepts any integer that fits the underlying size (json_conversion.cpp:232-251) and the editor passes values straight to component_from_json (component_access.cpp:241-246); there is no semantic validation of camera planes or transform rotation/scale. Tightening enum reads in core affects every scene/prefab load (and flag-style enums whose valid values are combinations), and the value-range checks belong to the owning modules. *Fix:* Shape: decide whether enum strictness lives in core (a strict option on read_enum, or an enum_info flag distinguishing flag enums) or only in the editor's write path (walk the reflection and check ENUM fields against info's enumerators before component_from_json, INVALID_ARGS listing the names); recommended is the editor-side check plus a core option so loaders can warn. Zero quaternion / zero scale / negative near plane need per-component validators (scene transform setter normalises or rejects a zero-length quaternion; renderer camera validates near < far, near > 0). Risks: rejecting values existing scene files contain; flag enums. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_component.cpp: "component.add - an enum number that is not an enumerator is INVALID_ARGS" and "component.set - a zero quaternion rotation is refused"
- **F160** MAJOR Writing an AudioSourceData built from Default over a playing source orphans its voice and starts a second one (`managed/Conjure.Managed/Api/Components/Audio/AudioMirrors.cs:97`). Still present: AudioSourceData exposes the engine-owned PlaybackRequest, IsPlaying and Handle as public fields (AudioMirrors.cs:88-92), Default zeroes them (:97-107), and both the AudioSource.Data setter (AudioSource.cs:118-122) and the generic Entity.SetComponent (Entity.cs:170-176) write all 88 bytes. audio_subsystem.cpp:199-242 then sees no handle and no started-once flag, starts a second voice, and the retained first voice is orphaned. A correct fix must also cover the generic SetComponent path and other mirrors with runtime fields, which is a cross-module design question. *Fix:* Shape: write paths must not clobber engine-owned runtime fields. Options: (1) natively, have component_set keep the fields that reflection marks as runtime/hidden (copy them from the live component before the memcpy). This is generic and also covers editor and C++ writers, but it needs a field flag in core::reflect and touches scripting's native_api_core.cpp. (2) In managed, add a per-mirror write hook used by both Entity.SetComponent/AddComponent and AudioSource.Data that copies Handle/IsPlaying/PlaybackRequest from the current component; this is cheaper but covers only scripts. (3) Make the audio sync detect and stop a retained voice that no source refers to any more; this contradicts B8, where the component is the voice map. Risks: a script that sets PlaybackRequest through Data on purpose; RigidBodyData.IsSleeping and other mirrors with the same problem; the extra component_get on every write. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_bindings_managed.cpp: 'regression - writing AudioSourceData.Default over a playing looping source keeps one voice [dotnet]': start a looping source, have a probe write Default with the same clip and Loop = true, step frames, then check the audio engine's live voice count stays 1 and AudioSource.Stop() silences it
- **F164** MAJOR Deferred prefab instantiate copies the archive's guids verbatim: every second instance has duplicate scene guids (`engine/src/bindings/entries_prefab.cpp:121`). Still present: instantiate_deferred records `commands.instantiate(prefab.asset->archive)` verbatim (entries_prefab.cpp:121), so the archive's scene.guid values are copied while the unlocked path regenerates them (scene/prefab.cpp REGENERATE_ALL). A second deferred instance collides in the guid index at flush (scene_observers.cpp:113-119). Fixing it needs a scene-owned adoption or guid-regeneration path for command-buffer instances, which the handbook already lists as an open item (bindings.md:151-152). *Fix:* Shape: give scene a routine that adopts an instance created through a command buffer: regenerate every guid of the subtree (REGENERATE_ALL), index them and add the prefab_link markers, called from the ATTACH fixup of an instantiate root (a new scene_fixup kind, e.g. ADOPT_INSTANCE carrying the prefab id). The hard part: the guid observer has already rejected the duplicates at flush time, so either the archive copy must get fresh guids before the instantiate command is recorded (a per-instance archive copy, allocation and an ecs/scene archive-rewrite API) or the observer must defer indexing for command-buffer instances. Deferred entity_create_many (entries_entity.cpp:390-403) also creates entities without guids and should go through the same path. Risks: guid index consistency, scene save, and expected-repair accounting. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_span_scope_routing.cpp: "span routing - two deferred instances of one prefab carry distinct guids" (two prefab_instantiate calls in one scope; after query_end every entity of both instances has a unique guid, find_by_guid resolves each, no WARN in log_capture); tests/scene/test_prefab.cpp: a case for the new adoption routine.
- **F177** MAJOR scene.open and scene.new silently discard unsaved scene edits and the undo history, while scene.close refuses without discard:true (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_scene.cpp:65`): same as F103.
- **F178** MAJOR The editor never collects unused asset payloads (no 'unload unused'), and a slot costs about 400 B per uuid, not the documented 128 B (`docs/design/05_assets.md:277`). Still present. asset_manager::collect() is called only from scene_manager.cpp:136 and application.cpp:240 (shutdown), and no editor command or path calls it, contrary to 05_assets.md:277. D3 (05_assets.md:26) states 128 B per uuid against a measured ~400 B. A fix needs an editor residency policy: in-session assets such as prefab.create results may be held only through non-owning asset_refs, so collecting on play.stop or asset.delete can unload assets the edit scene still shows. It also spans the editor and the assets design record. *Fix:* Shape: (1) add an `asset.unload_unused` editor command (M-less, NOT_IN_BATCH) that calls asset_manager::collect() and returns `{unloaded}`. (2) Decide whether play.stop and asset.delete also collect, which is only safe once the editor scene holds strong handles for everything it displays (audit scene_manager/editor residency first). (3) Correct D3's figure in 05_assets.md to the measured slot + slot_data + index cost (~400 B per touched uuid) or shrink slot_data for retired slots (assets module). Risks: unloading assets referenced only by asset_refs (get() returns null until reload, causing flicker or missing meshes); RETIRE_FRAMES keeps GPU retirement safe but a reload costs a frame; and an assets-side change if slot_data is shrunk. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp: 'asset.unload_unused - retires payloads with no strong refs and keeps pinned and scene-held ones'
- **F180** MAJOR AI port accepts commands wrapped in an HTTP request: any web page can drive the editor (cross-protocol / DNS-rebinding class) (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/protocol_server.cpp:128`). Still present. A parse failure only answers PARSE_ERROR and keeps the client (protocol_server.cpp:129-139), and without --ai-token every client is authenticated on connect (:293). A browser POST's header lines are therefore rejected one by one and its JSON body line executes.
