The design explicitly says a malformed line keeps the client (04_editor.md:942) and makes the token optional (:403), so the full fix changes the documented contract and the agent workflow (skill, wiki). *Fix:* Step 1 (mitigation inside the editor, no workflow change): for TCP clients that are not yet authenticated or have not sent a valid request, disconnect on a first line that is not a JSON object, or on any line matching an HTTP request line (`^[A-Z]{3,7} \S+ HTTP/\d`). Browsers always send the request line first, so this ends cross-protocol and DNS-rebinding drive-bys. Amend the design table row 'Malformed JSON line' for TCP pre-handshake. Step 2 (owner decision): generate a random per-session token by default, write it next to the port in .conjure/ai_port with owner-only permissions, and require session.hello with it. stdio and loopback stay exempt. Risks: Step 2 breaks every agent that skips hello or does not read the file, and changes the ai_port format (see F741). The skill and design must change in lockstep. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_protocol_server.cpp "protocol_server - a TCP client whose first line is an HTTP request line is disconnected and its body never runs" (use a real TCP transport and send the full POST bytes) and "protocol_server - without a token a garbage first line disconnects a TCP client".
- **F182** MAJOR NUL byte in a JSON path bypasses the script path checks: script.write overwrites scripts/Game.csproj (and any non-.cs file) (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_script.cpp:135`). Still present: script_file builds std::filesystem::path(std::string(text)) (commands_script.cpp:123) and validates its text (:129-135), but write_text opens the path with std::ofstream (:159), which stops at the embedded NUL, so "Game.csproj\u0000.cs" overwrites the project file. The finding (and the fix it needs) covers every path-taking command, core VFS normalisation and the project/meta readers, so it spans editor, core, engine and assets. *Fix:* Shape: (1) editor: a `require_path_string(args, key)` helper in command_support that refuses strings containing '\0' or other C0 controls with INVALID_ARGS "args.<key>: a path cannot contain control characters"; use it for every path argument (script.*, scene.*, asset.*, prefab.*, project.*, view.screenshot, editor.exec_file). The script_file slice (reject text.find('\0') != npos) closes the concrete csproj overwrite and can land first. (2) core: core::vfs path normalisation rejects embedded NUL. (3) engine/assets: project.json, .meta and manifest readers reject NUL in path members (cooked_root etc.). Hard because it is a cross-module audit of every std::string-to-path conversion with tests per command. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_script.cpp: "script.write - a path with an embedded NUL is refused and Game.csproj is untouched"; tests/editor/test_command_support.cpp: "require_path_string - refuses NUL and C0 controls"; matching cases in the core vfs and engine project tests
- **F186** MAJOR An idle headless editor server spins at ~25,000 frames/s and burns more than one CPU core (`engine/src/engine/frame_pacer.cpp:18`): same as F726.
- **F190** MAJOR A project whose directory is longer than ~248 characters cannot be opened by the editor or the runtime (project.json is read outside the VFS) (`engine/src/engine/project.cpp:348`). Still present. project::load (project.cpp:345-366) uses plain std::filesystem::is_regular_file and std::ifstream on directory/project.json. The extended-length handling exists only privately in core/vfs (native_platform.h / native_platform_windows.cpp, extend_native_path). The same pattern recurs in export, modules, editor commands, json document file IO, rhi shader_library and runtime main. A fix needs a new public core helper adopted across many modules, plus manifest changes. *Fix:* Shape: expose a public core helper, e.g. `core::vfs::native_path(const std::filesystem::path &)`, that applies the \\?\ extended-length form on Windows (absolute and normalised first) and is the identity elsewhere. Route every direct std::filesystem/fstream user above core through it: project load/save, json document load/write_file, export copy_into, module shadow copies, editor script/project commands, shader_library, and runtime project_next_to_executable. Add <longPathAware>true</longPathAware> to the runtime and editor manifests. Risks: the \\?\ form disables normalisation, so relative paths and '..' must be resolved first. Paths shown to users and logs must stay un-prefixed. Each module owner must adopt the helper, and Windows-only behaviour is hard to test on POSIX CI. *Test:* tests/engine/test_project.cpp: "project - a project in a directory longer than MAX_PATH loads" (Windows only; temp_directory nested past 260 characters, created through the core helper). Plus tests/core/vfs/test_native_file_system.cpp: "native_path - extends an absolute path past MAX_PATH and leaves short paths alone".
- **F191** MAJOR scene.save_as with a different letter case on Windows renames the file under its .meta and the project no longer starts (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_scene.cpp:126`). Still present: scene_save_as (commands_scene.cpp:123-136) writes through the caller's spelling and register_scene_file (:24-38) looks it up with the exact-string find_by_path, then only logs the DUPLICATE_UUID failure; scene_open checks filesystem().exists (:69) instead of the database. A correct fix needs a case-folding lookup in the assets module plus a cross-platform path-case policy, so it crosses editor and assets. *Fix:* Shape: add to asset_database a case-insensitive lookup (ASCII fold, e.g. find_by_path_folded(path) -> optional<asset_id> with the record's canonical spelling) and decide the policy: recommended is to reject with INVALID_ARGS naming the existing spelling when a user path matches a record case-insensitively but not exactly (portable: a command file that works on Windows then works on Linux), or alternatively rewrite to the record's spelling. Apply it in scene.save_as, scene.open (resolve through the database, not filesystem().exists), asset.import (commands_asset.cpp:228-231) and asset.info. Risks: the database index is keyed by exact path, Linux volumes may legitimately hold two files differing only in case, and the .meta/rename interplay on Windows (file replaced under its old .meta) must be tested on a case-insensitive volume. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: "scene.save_as - a path that differs from an existing scene only in letter case is refused"; tests/assets/test_asset_database.cpp: "asset_database - find_by_path_folded matches a record regardless of ASCII case"
- **F192** MAJOR The windowed (D3D12) Debug editor dies with exception 0x87D at exit after drawing UI frames: exit code 2173, no shutdown log, truncated log file (`engine/src/editor/core/editor_application.cpp:191`). Still present as far as the code shows: shutdown() (editor_application.cpp:180-200) tears the UI layer and its ImGui/D3D12 objects down inside mEngine->shutdown() with no explicit GPU idle wait in the editor, the rhi has no ID3D12InfoQueue routing, and commit 9101599 only added the icon to editor_layer::on_attach. Root cause is unisolated, involves GPU resource lifetime and shutdown ordering across editor, engine and rhi, and needs a debugger with D3D12 debug output. *Fix:* Shape: (1) rhi D3D12 backend: route ID3D12InfoQueue messages into core::log (and optionally break-on-severity in Debug) so the debug-layer message that precedes 0x87D is visible; (2) reproduce under a debugger with --exec running editor.wait_frames 5; most likely fix is to wait for the GPU (flush the frame fence / device wait_idle) before the ImGui renderer and editor layer release their resources and before the swapchain goes away, in the order engine layers detach; (3) add a windowed process test. Risks: shutdown order across layers/subsystems, only reproducible on a D3D12 debug-layer machine. *Test:* tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp: "conjure_editor - a windowed --exec run that draws frames exits 0 with the shutdown log" (gated on a real GPU/window, like CONJURE_TEST_WINDOW)
- **F193** MAJOR No test opens a project under a non-ASCII or long path with the editor, engine::project, modules or the export pipeline (`tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp:111`). Still present: only tests/runtime/test_runtime_boot.cpp and tests/core vfs use non-ASCII paths (grep for u8" in tests finds no editor, engine, modules, scripting or integration case), and no project or asset path exceeds 260 characters. The fix spans tests/support and several test areas, and the new cases will fail until the separately reported path defects are fixed in engine, editor and modules. *Fix:* Add `unicode()` and `deep()` variants to conjure::testing::temp_directory in tests/support (a u8 name such as projekt_ü_λ_日本, and a nested path pushing the project root past 260 characters). Then parametrise test_editor_process (open, scene.save, asset.import, project.export, script.create), test_engine_project_boot and test_export_roundtrip over {plain, unicode, deep}. Sequence: land after, or together with, the path defect fixes in engine::project, the export pipeline and module discovery, otherwise the new cases go in red. Risk: Windows long-path support (manifest longPathAware, \\?\ prefixes) differs between the test executables and the editor. *Test:* tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp: 'conjure_editor - opens, saves, imports and exports a project under a non-ASCII path' and '... under a path longer than 260 characters'; tests/integration/test_export_roundtrip.cpp: 'export_roundtrip - non-ASCII project root'.
- **F199** MAJOR Managed output directory is not next to the executables on multi-config generators (`managed/CMakeLists.txt:13`). Still present: managed/CMakeLists.txt:12-13 writes the SDK to ${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/bin/managed. On multi-config generators CMakeLists.txt:99 puts executables in bin/<Config> (engine/src/scripting/CMakeLists.txt:57-62 already allows for this with nethost.dll), and scripting_subsystem.cpp:106-108 defaults to <exe>/managed. The fix changes the build output layout, contradicts the shared-OUTPUT design written in ConjureManaged.cmake:25-28, and cannot be checked by CI, which never configures windows-vs. *Fix:* Shape: in managed/CMakeLists.txt set CONJURE_MANAGED_OUTPUT_ROOT to "${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/bin/$<CONFIG>/managed" when CMAKE_CONFIGURATION_TYPES is set, and keep bin/managed otherwise. Then check every consumer of the genex path. COMMAND and COMMENT of the custom command are fine on CMake 3.28. The CONJURE_MANAGED_OUTPUT_DIR target property and the CONJURE_MANAGED_SDK_DIR / CONJURE_MANAGED_TEST_SCRIPTS_DIR cache variables are only used inside $<PATH:...> compile definitions today, but no configure-time use (if(EXISTS), file()) may appear. Risks: every configuration now builds its own SDK copy; the editor's ConjureManagedDir default (commands_script.cpp:296 uses a relative 'managed') must still resolve; a manual windows-vs-debug build and editor run are needed. Related but separate: shaders are built into ${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/shaders (engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:12) while renderer_subsystem.cpp:205 defaults to <exe>/shaders, even with single-config generators. That belongs to a renderer/build fix, not to this one. *Test:* managed/CMakeLists.txt: CTest entry 'conjure_managed_layout.beside_executables' running a small cmake -P check that $<TARGET_FILE_DIR:conjure_scripting_tests>/managed and the SDK OUTPUT of the configuration under test are the same directory, plus a manual windows-vs-debug run of the editor with scripting on

Only docs/handbook/getting-started.md:29 states the real floor. The fix spans the ci and docs areas, and the macOS CI effect can only be verified on a macOS runner. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Docs: state the floor as 'GCC 13+, Clang 16+ with libstdc++, libc++ 18+ (AppleClang from Xcode 16+)' in README.md:20-21, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:370 and docs/handbook/architecture.md:200, consistent with getting-started.md:29. (2) CI: in ci.yml and nightly.yml, either move macos-debug to `macos-15`, or add a macOS-only step `sudo xcode-select -s /Applications/Xcode_16.x.app` (pin the exact version present on the image). - **F329** MAJOR Multi-config generators share one shader and one managed output directory, and neither is where the executables look (`cmake/ConjureShaders.cmake:216`). Still present. Cheap alternative, which is an owner decision: mark the windows-vs preset as build-only (not runnable) in its displayName and the docs. Related to F027, the single-config half of the same location mismatch. *Test:* tests/engine/test_renderer_subsystem.cpp: 'renderer_subsystem - the default shader and managed directories beside the executable hold index.json and Conjure.Managed.dll', run under the windows-vs presets.

An exported manifest keeps a non-optional dependency on a skipped editorOnly module. The fix spans engine and modules and needs a warning-versus-error policy that 07 3.7 ("for each LOADED module") does not settle. *Fix:* In export_pipeline step 4, compare registry.all() with load_order(). Add a report warning, or an EXPORT error for the project's required or game module, for every non-engine, non-editorOnly, non-static module that is registered but not LOADED, naming its state and last_error. In export_layout, before writing anything, fail with EXPORT_FAILED when an exported module has a non-optional dependency on an editorOnly module (optional ones are dropped from the exported manifest). - **F433** MAJOR Script asset residency is not cleared on PLAY -> EDIT as design B9 requires (`engine/src/bindings/bindings_subsystem.cpp:110`). Still present: bindings_subsystem.cpp:110-118 subscribes only to scene_manager::on_before_unload; nothing clears script_residency when the editor's play_controller (engine/src/editor/core/play_controller.cpp:214) calls script_runtime::set_mode(EDIT), although 15_bindings.md B9 requires it, and script_runtime exposes no mode-change notification (script_runtime.h:224). A correct fix needs a scripting-side notification (or a polling design with a known gap) and also touches the frame ordering of stop-play, so it is not a one-area change. *Fix:* Shape: add a `core::event<script_mode, script_mode>` (old, new) `on_mode_changed()` to script_runtime, emitted at the end of set_mode after the OnDestroy flush; the bindings subsystem subscribes in startup next to the unload subscription and calls mResidency.clear() on PLAY -> EDIT, releasing the subscription in shutdown. A bindings-only alternative (poll `context.scripting->mode()` in begin_frame and clear on a PLAY -> EDIT transition) misses a stop and restart inside one frame (scripted play.stop + play.start), so it is the weaker option. Open point to settle with it: the unload subscription clears ALL residency when any additive scene unloads (README/B9 say 'ends with the scene that asked for it'); per-scene residency would need the requesting scene recorded per handle. Risks: ordering against OnDestroy scripts that call asset_release during set_mode, and the editor snapshot restore. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_bindings_subsystem.cpp: "bindings_subsystem - PLAY to EDIT clears the script residency" (load an asset through asset_load in PLAY, set_mode(EDIT), residency.size() == 0 and assets.collect() frees it); tests/scripting/test_script_runtime.cpp: "script_runtime - set_mode notifies the mode change once per transition".
- **F459** MAJOR Time.FixedStepIndex (and the other frame-block time fields) do not advance between fixed steps of one frame (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/scripting_subsystem.cpp:213`). Still present: the frame block is written once per frame in scripting_subsystem::begin_frame (scripting_subsystem.cpp:180-188, fixed_step_index at :213). frame_loop::run_fixed_steps (frame_loop.cpp:319-329) advances engine_time per step without rewriting the block, and ecs::frame_info (system_phase.h:44-51) has no fixedStepIndex that the scripting FIXED_UPDATE system could use. Time.cs:20 also claims that DeltaTime is the fixed step inside OnFixedUpdate, which the block does not do. Fixing it means choosing per-step semantics and either adding a frame-loop hook or extending an ecs public struct. That crosses engine, ecs or scripting, and the managed docs. *Fix:* Shape: decide that the fields seen inside OnFixedUpdate describe the current fixed step: fixed_step_index of that step, and delta_time = fixed_delta_time if Time.cs is kept. Then either (1) add `u64 fixedStepIndex` to ecs::frame_info, fill it in make_frame_info, and have the scripting FIXED_UPDATE system (script_system.cpp:53-55) write frame_block().fixed_step_index (and delta_time) before run_phase and restore after it; or (2) let frame_loop::run_fixed_steps call a scripting_subsystem hook before each step's phases. UPDATE and LATE_UPDATE should see the post-steps value. Otherwise, document the field as a per-frame snapshot and fix the Time.cs comments. Risk: the editor's play_controller fixed-step stepping relies on engine_time.fixedStepIndex. *Test:* tests/engine/test_scripting_subsystem.cpp: "scripting_subsystem - each fixed step of a frame sees its own fixed_step_index" (a frame of two fixed steps; the null host records the block's fixed_step_index at each FIXED_UPDATE run_phase)
- **F504** MAJOR Windows: UTF-8 paths from the protocol and argv are decoded with the ANSI code page (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_project.cpp:52`). Still present: protocol paths are built with std::filesystem::path(std::string(...)) (e.g. commands_project.cpp:52, :87, :189), editor_options stores argv as path(string_view) (editor_options.cpp:75, :82, :109) and results return generic_string(); on Windows these use the ANSI code page. The only UTF-8 path helper is core-internal (vfs_internal.h:52), the sites are dozens across the editor in both directions, and main.cpp needs wmain/CommandLineToArgvW. *Fix:* Shape: promote core's path_from_utf8 and add path_to_utf8 (generic_u8string -> std::string) as a public core helper; route every protocol/argv boundary in the editor through them (inputs: project.create/open/export, editor.exec_file, asset.import, script and module paths, view.screenshot, editor_options; outputs: every generic_string() put into JSON); on Windows take UTF-16 argv in editor/src/main.cpp and convert to UTF-8 before parse_editor_options. Hard because of the number of sites (easy to miss one), the core API addition, and needing a non-ASCII path test that runs on Windows CI. *Test:* tests/core/system/test_path_utf8.cpp "path_utf8 - a non-ASCII UTF-8 path round-trips"; tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp "project.create / info - a non-ASCII project path round-trips through the protocol"
- **F509** MAJOR Transactions interact badly with clear(), undo and other clients (`engine/src/editor/core/undo_stack.cpp:201`). Still present: - clear() drops open transactions (undo_stack.cpp:199-204), so the owner's end_transaction later fails INVALID_REQUEST (:121-126). - apply_history never checks has_transaction (commands_history.cpp:13-66). - end_transaction appends at the cursor at end time (:127-131, push_entry :206-217), after entries other clients recorded meanwhile. Fixing it means choosing multi-client transaction semantics (refuse, auto-commit, or reserve a stack slot) that the design does not settle, across the undo stack, history, scene and editor commands. *Fix:* Decide and document in 04_editor.md: (a) history.undo, history.redo and history.clear, and scene.open/new/close, answer INVALID_REQUEST or WRONG_STATE while any transaction is open (or commit the caller's own transaction first). (b) Reserve the entry's position at begin_transaction. Insert a placeholder entry at the cursor, fill it as records arrive and drop it if it stays empty, so undo order matches execution order. (c) clear() closes transactions in a way the owner can observe, for example a warning event, and end_transaction on a cleared transaction succeeds as a no-op. Risks: the placeholder interacts with coalescing, eviction and redo-tail truncation, and other clients' pushes discard the redo tail above a placeholder. *Test:* tests/editor/test_undo_stack.cpp "undo_stack - a transaction keeps its stack position when another client records meanwhile" and tests/editor/test_batch_command.cpp "editor.end_transaction - history.undo while a transaction is open is refused".
- **F519** MAJOR The Grid / Bounds / Physics checkboxes (and view.debug_draw) change flags nobody reads (`engine/src/editor/ui/panels/viewport_panel.cpp:147`). Still present: the Grid, Bounds and Physics checkboxes (viewport_panel.cpp:146-151) and view.debug_draw (commands_view.cpp:225-243) write editor_context::debug_draw_flags. The only reader is the 'no scene open' overlay condition at viewport_panel.cpp:77. The design routes debug drawing through renderer::debug_draw (04_editor.md:982), and 09 section 4 item 15(e) records exposing it as a request not yet applied, so the real fix crosses engine, renderer and editor and depends on the editor-camera view of F104. *Fix:* Correct fix: expose renderer::debug_draw through engine_context (item 15(e)) and have the editor submit each frame, for the editor view: a grid in the theme's grid and gridMajor colours, the bounds of the selection (or of all entities), and the physics shapes. This needs F104's editor view to draw into, and the checkboxes should dispatch view.debug_draw instead of writing the flags directly. Interim mitigation, editor-only, can land alone: wrap the three checkboxes in ImGui::BeginDisabled(true)/EndDisabled with a tooltip 'not drawn yet (09 section 4 item 15(e))', and amend item 15(e) to record that the flags currently have no consumer. Risk: the debug-draw submission order relative to extraction, and the physics module's debug query API. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/panels/test_viewport_panel.cpp: 'viewport_panel - debug draw toggles reach the renderer debug draw' (with the hook: grid on gives non-zero debug_draw line count for the frame). For the interim: 'viewport_panel - debug draw boxes are disabled until a consumer exists'.
- **F539** MAJOR Paths cross between ImGui/JSON (UTF-8) and std::filesystem through narrow strings, which is the ANSI code page on Windows (`engine/src/editor/ui/panels/export_dialog.cpp:86`). Still present. Narrow path conversions remain at export_dialog.cpp:86, commands_project.cpp:52/87/189/191, editor_options.cpp:75-109, commands_asset.cpp:222/233, commands_script.cpp:123/596, commands_modules.cpp:206, commands_editor.cpp:254 and screenshot_service.cpp:149. The fix spans core, editor core and UI and the executable, and needs a decision between a process-wide manifest and explicit u8 conversions (or both, since the test executables would still run in the ANSI code page). *Fix:* Shape: (1) Give conjure_editor the same activeCodePage=UTF-8 manifest as the runtime, plus UTF-8 argv on Windows (CommandLineToArgvW, as runtime::utf8_arguments does). (2) Add core helpers `std::filesystem::path core::str::path_from_utf8(std::string_view)` and `std::string core::str::path_to_utf8(const std::filesystem::path &)`, implemented with char8_t/u8string. Use them at every JSON/ImGui/argv <-> path boundary listed above, so library code is correct in any host, the test executables included. Risks: - Many call sites. - generic_string() is used for JSON results in many commands. - Tests need a non-ASCII temp directory on Windows CI. - The engine's project and VFS code may have the same issue outside the editor. *Test:* tests/core/strings/test_string_utils.cpp: "str - path_from_utf8 and path_to_utf8 round-trip a non-ASCII path". tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: "regression - project.create and project.open accept a non-ASCII directory" (a temp dir named with U+00E9).
- **F726** MAJOR Headless editor server is never paced: about 1.1 cores when idle, --target-hz silently ignored, and play mode runs hundreds of times faster than real time (`engine/src/engine/frame_pacer.cpp:57`). Still present. frame_pacer.cpp:57-59 `if (mForcedDelta) { return 0; }`. Headless defaults set forcedFrameDeltaSeconds = 1/60 (engine_config.cpp:45), and apply_to keeps it under headless (project.cpp:607-611). --target-hz (command_line.cpp:155-158, 235-236) is therefore ignored without a word. Design 06 3.3 says pacing is inactive with a forced delta, yet also says 'servers tick at simulation rate by default'. Resolving that contradiction changes frame timing semantics and needs an editor-side notion of 'server mode', which spans editor, engine, skill docs and the design record. *Fix:* Shape: define server mode in the editor as headless with the AI protocol enabled (TCP or stdio) and no --exec/--export. In server mode, either (a) let the pacer honour an explicit target under a forced delta: effective_target_hz returns mPacing.targetHz when mForcedDelta and targetHz != 0, else 0, and the editor sets pacing.targetHz = round(1/fixedDeltaSeconds) by default in server mode, so play time tracks wall time; or (b) keep the forced delta unpaced but block in the protocol poll (WSAPoll/select on the sockets, a condition variable for stdio) with a ~1-5 ms timeout when no request, frame task or play session is pending. (a) is simpler and fixes both the CPU burn and the play-time mismatch. Reject or warn on --target-hz combined with a forced delta outside server mode. Update design 06 3.3 and the editor-ai-control skill ('play time is simulation time; in server mode it tracks wall time at the fixed rate'). Risks: --exec batch runs and editor protocol tests rely on unpaced speed and must stay unpaced; reply latency grows by up to one frame; existing test 'frame_pacer - inactive with a forced frame delta' changes meaning. *Test:* tests/engine/test_frame_pacer.cpp: "frame_pacer - an explicit target paces even with a forced frame delta" (manual clock). tests/editor/test_editor_options.cpp: "editor_options - a headless protocol server paces at the fixed rate and --exec stays unpaced".

- **F126** MINOR On Windows the tool sees imports and exports only: editor or ImGui code linked statically into the runtime or kept un-exported inside conjure_shared is invisible (`tools/check_distribution_symbols.py:209`). Still present: tool_commands (tools/check_distribution_symbols.py:204-209) runs `dumpbin /SYMBOLS /IMPORTS /EXPORTS`. /SYMBOLS prints no COFF table for a linked image, so on MSVC only imports and exports are scanned, while the docstring (:7-9) says the tool proves the stripping from the runtime's own symbol table. Statically linked editor or ImGui code that is not exported would pass. Closing the gap needs a different evidence source (link map, PDB publics or the link inputs), which means link changes in runtime/ and cmake/ as well as tool support. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: - On MSVC, scan the public symbols of each binary from one of: - a link map (target_link_options /MAP for the Distribution conjure_runtime and conjure_shared, read from its 'Publics by Value' section); - the PDB (llvm-pdbutil dump -publics). Fail with a usage error when neither is available, instead of passing on imports and exports alone. - On ELF and Mach-O, make the static nm pass fatal when the binary still has a symbol table. Otherwise say plainly that only dynamic symbols are covered. - As an independent belt, assert from the build graph that no conjure_editor* or imgui library is a link input of the Distribution runtime. - Rewrite the docstring to say exactly what each platform covers, and add self-test fixtures for the new source. Risks: map and PDB format parsing, tool availability on CI runners, and keeping the imgui_layer / set_imgui_draw_data bridge exemption working. Note that today the build already prevents editor targets in Distribution, so this hardens a guarantee rather than fixing a live leak. *Test:* check_distribution_symbols --self-test (CTest runtime.check_distribution_symbols_tool): 'a link map naming an ImGui public is a finding' and 'a clean link map is clean', using embedded .map fixtures.

Risks: unknown volume of findings to fix first, slower tests, validation-layer behaviour on a device that never executes. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer.cpp: 'renderer - the renderer test device runs under the NVRHI validation layer' (device desc reports enableDebugLayer and a full null frame logs no ERROR)

The atomic helper (vfs internal::replace_file, native_platform.h:102) lives in the vfs area, and docs/design/10_core.md C2 forbids json -> vfs, so a correct fix needs either a dependency change or edits to callers in other areas. *Fix:* Shape, three ways. (1) Route the three callers through a core::vfs::native_file_system / write_file_atomic with to_string(true) bytes (edits engine and modules, json unchanged). - **F278** MINOR Binding design text has drifted from the implementation in several places (`docs/design/01_ecs.md:293`). All six drifts are still present. 01_ecs.md:293 has the counter-allocation parenthesis, but scheduler.cpp:177 uses a stack job_fence. :63/:266 describe RESERVED, but world.h:125-128 has only FREE/ALIVE. :226-227/:322-323 describe chunk-wise create and mask grouping, but world_structural.cpp:87-118 does place_rows per entity span. world.cpp:516 has a wrong comment, since archetype_for at :489-501 never touches edges. command_buffer.h:45 documents 'skipped' too narrowly (world_structural.cpp:555, 604, 623, 651-671, 756 also count failed instantiations, unregistered ids and failed placements). The fix spans docs/design and the ecs module. Item (3), the 'never a false negative' change-detection contract at :249-254, should stay: the code is what the separate change-detection findings must fix, so that part cannot simply be reworded. *Fix:* docs (01_ecs.md): (1) :291-293 drop the parenthesis about `jobs.schedule` allocating a counter; say stages join on a stack-held `core::jobs::job_fence`, so a warmed phase allocates nothing. (2) :63 `entity_state` (`FREE`, `ALIVE`): a reserved id is a FREE record whose generation the allocator handed out (`is_reserved`); :266 'not alive and not reserved (allocator-issued generation)'. (4) :226-227 create_entities resolves the archetype once, reserves all ids, then places rows (`place_rows`); :322-323 instantiate_batch resolves one archetype per archive entry. Do not claim mask grouping. (3) Keep the contract text and cross-reference the change-detection findings; if the owner decides to weaken the contract instead, state the false-negative cases explicitly. ecs: world.cpp:516 comment -> '// archetype_for may push a new archetype but never touches edges; re-fetch only because edge() may have grown mEdges.' (or drop the re-lookup comment entirely). command_buffer.h:45: 'commands not applied: entity dead or stale at flush, component id not registered, archive instantiation failed, or the entity's placement or move failed (all of its commands)'. Risk: item 3 must be sequenced after the change-detection fixes. *Test:* none: docs and comments only (item 3 is covered by the change-detection findings' own tests in tests/ecs/test_change_detection.cpp)
- **F279** MINOR Monitor work area origin is clamped to 0: wrong for monitors left of or above the primary (`engine/src/platform/glfw/glfw_window.cpp:749`). Still present. glfw_window.cpp:789-790 clamps workX and workY to 0 through clamp_dimension because monitor_info::workArea is a math::urect (monitor.h:28), so a monitor left of or above the primary reports the wrong work-area origin. Fixing it changes the type of a public struct field that design record 11_platform.md:200 specifies, and there is no signed rect in core/math (rect.h has only rect and urect). The fix therefore crosses into the design docs and needs a representation choice. *Fix:* Replace `math::urect workArea` with `math::ivec2 workAreaPosition` plus `math::uvec2 workAreaSize`. That stays inside platform; the alternative is adding a core math::irect, which would also touch core. In rebuild_monitors, store workX/workY unclamped and clamp only the width and height. Set the null monitor's work area to position (0,0) and size 1920x1080. Update the design 2.4 struct, the API page and the handbook. No engine caller reads workArea today (only tests/platform/test_monitor.cpp:38-39 and 60-63), so the break is contained, but it is still a public API change to agree on. Another option keeps urect with a monitor-relative origin (workX - positionX >= 0), which changes the field's documented meaning instead of its type. *Test:* tests/platform/test_monitor.cpp: update the defaults and null-monitor cases for the new fields, and add "monitor_info - a work area left of the primary keeps its negative origin" (construct monitor_info with workAreaPosition (-1920, 0) and check it round-trips). The glfw path is covered only manually on a multi-monitor desk.
- **F284** MINOR Every GLFW error is logged at ERROR, including the expected ones the callers already turn into results (`engine/src/platform/glfw/glfw_context.cpp:32`). Still present. glfw_context.cpp:32 logs every GLFW error at ERROR. Win32 raises GLFW_FORMAT_UNAVAILABLE for a clipboard without text (glfw win32_window.c:2441), so the handled CLIPBOARD_UNAVAILABLE path in glfw_window.cpp:622-633 still prints an ERROR line. The conformance suite already has to call logs.allow_errors() for this (test_backend_conformance.cpp:234 and 281). Fixing it contradicts design rule P7 ('routes every GLFW error to CONJURE_LOG_ERROR'), so the design record changes too, and a process-global callback is involved. *Fix:* Add an 'expected errors' scope to glfw_context: a RAII guard `expected_glfw_errors` that sets a static flag (main thread only, consistent with P8) and restores the previous value in its destructor. glfw_error_callback still records code, text and summary, but logs at DEBUG when the flag is set and at ERROR otherwise. Arm the guard around the glfwGetClipboardString call in clipboard_text() and the glfwSetInputMode(GLFW_CURSOR) call in set_cursor_mode(). Callers that see the recorded error and do not handle it should then log it themselves; set_cursor_mode already WARNs once through feature_unavailable, and its BACKEND_FAILURE result carries the summary. Update P7 to say that errors a caller expects and turns into a result are logged at DEBUG, consistent with P15's rationale, and update the handbook error-callback paragraph. Risks: one mis-scoped guard can hide a real failure. Nesting and early returns need the RAII restore. The level choice (DEBUG versus WARN) is the owner's call. *Test:* tests/platform/test_backend_conformance.cpp: remove logs.allow_errors() from "backend_conformance - a clipboard without text is CLIPBOARD_UNAVAILABLE and an empty copy is text" and from the cursor-mode round-trip case, so that no ERROR may be logged (glfw only under CONJURE_TEST_WINDOW=1). Add "backend_conformance - an expected clipboard error is not logged at ERROR".
- **F307** MINOR finalise_pass re-sorts and reallocates every update while anything waits on a dependency; load_blocking busy-waits (`engine/src/assets/asset_manager.cpp:404`). Still present. While anything waits to finalise, finalise_pass runs std::stable_sort, which allocates a buffer, and builds a fresh `remaining` vector on every update (asset_manager.cpp:404-416). load_blocking spins on std::this_thread::yield() for up to blockingTimeout (:702-715). The allocation half is a local fix, but the busy-wait cannot simply become jobs->wait(data.job): job_system offers only untimed wait(handle) (job_system.h:163), and load_blocking's documented TIMEOUT contract (asset_manager.h:233, 05_assets.md section 6) needs a deadline. That requires a timed or help-one wait in core::jobs. *Fix:* Assets part (could ship alone): keep `finalising` ordered on insertion. accept_stage inserts with std::upper_bound on (priority desc, sequence asc), since priorities cannot change while a slot is in the list. Drop the per-update stable_sort, and compact in place (a write index over the same vector) instead of allocating `remaining`. Core part: add `bool job_system::wait_until(const job_handle &, std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point deadline)` or `bool try_help_one()`, which helps with queued jobs and then blocks on the counter with a bounded wait. load_blocking then uses it in place of the yield loop and keeps returning TIMEOUT at the deadline. Risks: the main thread helping run arbitrary jobs inside load_blocking (re-entrancy into the asset manager from a helped job), and TSan coverage for the new wait. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_manager_load.cpp: "regression - finalise ordering stays HIGH first, FIFO within a level, without re-sorting" (an allocation counter or tracked allocator around update() while dependents wait). "regression - load_blocking behind a slow job waits without spinning and still times out". tests/core/jobs/test_job_system.cpp: "job_system - wait_until returns false at the deadline and true when the job completes".
- **F314** MINOR Source edits made while the startup import runs are never noticed (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/assets_subsystem.cpp:180`). Still present: the watcher baseline is taken at assets_subsystem.cpp:181-186, after scan() (:159) and import_stale() (:163), and polling_file_watcher::snapshot (file_watcher.cpp:62-85) records the current stat without reporting anything. The sweep's one-line fix does not work as is. scan() creates .meta files (asset_database.cpp:352, metasCreated) and import writes metas (:874). Taken before scan, the snapshot would report each of those as ADDED. hot_reload_controller::collect_dirty (hot_reload.cpp:133-167) maps a .meta to its owner, and asset_database::import (:580ff) re-runs the importer unconditionally, so every new source would be imported twice on first boot. *Fix:* Take the watcher baseline before scan(). After import, re-baseline only the paths the database itself wrote (the .meta files and side files it created or rewrote), so they do not surface as changes. Real source edits made during the import then come through on the first poll. This needs a new watcher entry point (for example file_watcher::rebaseline(std::span<const std::string> paths)) and a way to get the written meta paths out of scan_report / import_report, which are assets API changes. An alternative is to make hot reload skip a dirty source whose content hash equals the record's (no importer run). That is cheaper, but it changes import semantics for importer and settings edits. Risk: double imports on first boot if the meta filtering misses a path. *Test:* tests/assets/test_file_watcher.cpp: "file_watcher - rebaselined paths are not reported but other changes since the snapshot are"; tests/engine/test_application_headless.cpp: "application_headless - a source modified during the startup import is re-imported by hot reload"
- **F320** MINOR Group and master gain changes are applied as an instant step (clicks / zipper noise) (`engine/src/audio/mixer.cpp:110`). Still present. SET_GROUP_GAIN writes mGroupGain directly (mixer.cpp:108-112), and the voice loops multiply it as a constant per block (:312, :327, :389). Every set_group_gain or set_master_gain is therefore a step. The current behaviour is what design A11 and 3.3 specify ('groupGain[] is a plain array written only by commands applied at the top of the same block', 12_audio.md:312), and the test 'mixer - group gain, group pause and master pause apply at the top of the block' pins it. Fixing it changes the real-time mixer inner loop and the design record. *Fix:* Shape: per group, keep gainCurrent, gainTarget and a step/remaining-frames ramp in the mixer. SET_GROUP_GAIN sets the target and starts a ramp over command.fadeFrames; the engine sends DEFAULT_RAMP_SECONDS, but zero frames from create_group and remove_group so a new or reparented group starts at its value. At the top of render_block, compute each group's block start and end gain. In render_pcm_voice and render_stream_voice, interpolate the group gain per frame, or per block if that is accepted as enough. Advance the group ramps once per block, not once per voice. Update A11/3.3 in 12_audio.md and the handbook. Risks: the per-frame cost of 256 voices against the A16 budget; the existing exact-value test of the top-of-block application must change; master gain and listener gain interplay. Hard because it is real-time DSP in the device-thread loop and it contradicts the current design text. *Test:* tests/audio/test_mixer.cpp: "mixer - a group gain change ramps over the fade without a step larger than the ramp slope" (mirror "mixer - gain ramps are bounded"). Update "mixer - group gain, group pause and master pause apply at the top of the block" to check the value after the ramp.
- **F328** MINOR Compiler depfiles (*.dxil.d / *.spv.d) are written into the shader output directory and get exported with the game (`cmake/ConjureShaders.cmake:311`). Still present: ConjureShaders.cmake:311/353/373 write *.dxil.d and *.spv.d next to the blobs, and export_pipeline.cpp:353 copies shaders/ recursively. Moving the depfiles is about 10 lines in build, but rhi.md:204-206 and 02_renderer.md:388-394 document `DEPFILE <out>.d` and must change too, so the fix spans build, rhi and the design docs. *Fix:* In conjure_add_shaders: - Set depfileDirectory "${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/${ARG_TARGET}_depfiles", call file(MAKE_DIRECTORY) on it, and compute its path relative to PROJECT_BINARY_DIR (the WORKING_DIRECTORY). - Use -MF "<depRel>/${blobStem}.dxil.d", -MF "<depRel>/${blobStem}.spv.d" and --depfile "<depRel>/${blobStem}.spv.d". - Use DEPFILE "${depfileDirectory}/${blobStem}.dxil.d" / ".spv.d". The -Fo / -o targets stay unchanged, so the target named inside each depfile still matches its OUTPUT for Ninja. Update rhi.md and 02_renderer.md to say where depfiles live. Existing trees keep stale .d files in shaders/ until cleaned. The engine-side export filter from the separate sweep finding at export_pipeline.cpp:335 is the robust fix for the shipped-game symptom; coordinate with it. *Test:* tests/rhi/test_shader_library.cpp: 'shader_library - the shader output directory holds index.json and the listed blobs only' (iterate CONJURE_TEST_SHADER_DIR and fail on any *.d). Stale trees need a clean shaders/ once.
- **F330** MINOR Two hosts sharing one shadow-copy cache collide on index 1 (latent until the editor enables shadow copies) (`engine/src/modules/shadow_copy.cpp:56`). Still present but latent: shadow_copy.cpp:56 names copies <cache>/<name>/<loadCount+1>, and the constructor wipes the whole cache (module_manager.cpp:57-59). Two processes sharing a cache therefore collide. No host sets shadowCopyBinaries today (grep finds it only in module_manager.h). A safe fix needs per-process ownership and liveness detection across processes, which core does not offer for the current process (core::process exposes only a child's process_id). *Fix:* Shape: put copies under <cache>/<owner>/<name>/<n>/, where owner is the current process id or a random token. Each manager holds a lock file open in its owner directory. The constructor removes only owner directories whose lock can be taken (owner gone), never the whole cache. As a minimal hardening, retry with the next index when copy_file fails because the target is in use. Risks: needs a current-process id or file-lock primitive from core/platform, cross-platform lock semantics (Windows share modes versus POSIX advisory locks), and stale directories after crashes. *Test:* tests/modules/test_module_shadow_copy.cpp: "module_manager - two managers sharing one cache directory do not overwrite or remove each other's copies".
- **F331** MINOR A CI machine without the .NET host pack silently configures with scripting off and still goes green (`CMakeLists.txt:80`). Still present: CMakeLists.txt:75-83 downgrades a missing NetHost to a WARNING and turns scripting and the managed build off (the cache keeps ON because a normal variable shadows it), and ci.yml:108-109 / nightly.yml:89-90 do not assert the result, so a broken setup-dotnet removes the [dotnet] tests silently despite D12 ('a [dotnet] skip is never allowed'). The fix needs a build option plus flags on every CI leg and docs, i.e. build, ci and docs areas. *Fix:* Add `option(CONJURE_REQUIRE_SCRIPTING "Fail the configure instead of disabling scripting when the .NET host pack is missing" OFF)` next to CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING (CMakeLists.txt:28) and in the not-found branch at :78 emit `message(FATAL_ERROR ...)` when it is ON (keep the WARNING path otherwise so a fresh local clone without .NET still configures). Pass `-DCONJURE_REQUIRE_SCRIPTING=ON` on every CI configure that does not set -DCONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING=OFF: ci.yml windows-msvc-debug, windows-msvc-dev, linux-gcc-debug, macos-debug (via a matrix configure-args entry), nightly.yml full-matrix (all but linux-clang-sanitize), distribution-export (both presets), golden-* and benchmarks-ci-class. Document the option in AGENTS.md section 5 and docs/handbook/ci.md. Risk: macOS / Distribution legs whose host pack layout differs would turn red at configure, which is the intent but needs one CI round to confirm. *Test:* none: build option; verify locally by configuring with DOTNET_ROOT pointing at an empty directory and -DCONJURE_REQUIRE_SCRIPTING=ON (configure must fail) and without it (warning, scripting off)

- **F336** MINOR discover() returns either a count or an error, so a partial success loses the count: module.discover replies "added": 0 after adding modules (`engine/src/modules/module_manager.cpp:163`). Still present: module_manager.cpp:163-176 returns only the combined error when any manifest is rejected, although valid ones were registered. commands_modules.cpp:217-225 then reports "added": 0, and modules_subsystem.cpp:186-213 does not count them. Fixing it changes discover()'s public return type, which the engine and editor must follow, and the DUPLICATE_NAME rule is a behaviour choice. *Fix:* Change discover() to return `core::result<discover_report>` with `struct discover_report { u32 added = 0; std::vector<core::error> rejected; }`, or keep result<u32> and add an out-parameter `std::vector<core::error> *rejected`. Return an error only when the directory cannot be listed. Treat DUPLICATE_NAME as "already registered", not a rejection, when the registered record's rootDirectory equals the manifest's parent, so a second discover of the same directory is quiet. Update modules_subsystem (count added, warn per rejection) and module.discover (report "added" plus a "rejected" array). Risk: an API change across modules, engine and editor, and the editor protocol output changes. *Test:* tests/modules/test_module_manager.cpp: "module_manager - discover reports the added count alongside rejected manifests" and "module_manager - rediscovering the same directory adds the new module and rejects nothing"; plus an editor command case for module.discover.
- **F345** MINOR The ABI signature reports the engine build's compiler major, CRT and ASan facts for every module, and TSan is not part of it (`engine/include/conjure/modules/module_abi.h:26`). Still present: module_abi.h:39-53 falls back to the frozen abi_identity.h values because no file defines CONJURE_COMPILER_VERSION_MAJOR, CONJURE_RUNTIME_DYNAMIC_CRT or CONJURE_SANITIZER_ADDRESS (grep finds only module_abi.h and the modules README). runtimeFlags (module_abi.h:77, 113-115) has no TSan bit, and conjure_abi_runtime_facts (ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:110-127) computes only CRT, ASan and UBSan. A fix needs edits in core (platform_detect.h), build (cmake) and modules, and changes the module ABI signature, so it is not SMALL. *Fix:* In core/platform_detect.h define CONJURE_COMPILER_VERSION_MAJOR (_MSC_VER/100, __clang_major__, __GNUC__), CONJURE_RUNTIME_DYNAMIC_CRT (MSVC: defined(_DLL); elsewhere 1) and CONJURE_SANITIZER_ADDRESS (__SANITIZE_ADDRESS__ or __has_feature(address_sanitizer)), plus CONJURE_SANITIZER_THREAD from __SANITIZE_THREAD__ / __has_feature(thread_sanitizer). In module_abi.h add ABI_RUNTIME_TSAN = 1u << 3 and fold it into ABI_SIGNATURE_RUNTIME_FLAGS. In ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake add a TSan fact to conjure_abi_runtime_facts and CONJURE_ABI_RUNTIME_TSAN to the generated header. Make compare_abi name the new bit. Until this lands, add the open item to 09 section 4. Risks: the host and every in-tree fixture must agree on the values, which needs matching compile flags. On MSVC, _DLL must match CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY. Changing the signature content invalidates already-built third-party modules. Bump CONJURE_MODULE_ABI_VERSION if the bit meaning counts as an ABI change. *Test:* tests/modules/test_module_abi.cpp: "module_abi - the signature reports the compiler major and runtime flags of the compiling translation unit" (compare ABI_SIGNATURE_COMPILER_MAJOR and ABI_SIGNATURE_RUNTIME_FLAGS with the platform_detect.h macros, TSan bit included).

Reword :1-2 to say every listed module must exist, and that each module's CMakeLists honours its CONJURE_WITH_* option. - engine/src/core/CMakeLists.txt: same change at :46-48 for areas, and drop the "not present yet" sentence at :8-9. - tests/CMakeLists.txt: add an else(), message(FATAL_ERROR "tests: '${testDir}' is missing") to the loop at :35-40, and fix the header comment at :1-3. - **F349** MINOR Root bookkeeping is linear per operation: flat scenes with many roots go quadratic (`engine/src/scene/scene.cpp:904`). The cost is still linear per operation. - root_index_of (scene.cpp:904-911) is a linear scan, and erase_root (930-935) does a vector erase. - on_entity_destroyed calls erase_root for every raw destroy (scene_observers.cpp:72). - load_scene nests document.roots x rootEntries (scene_serializer.cpp:221-239) and calls root_owner twice per root (245, 253). - set_root_order calls root_index_of plus std::find per entry (scene.cpp:427-436), and snapshot restore calls root_owner and set_root_owner per root (scene_snapshot.cpp:76-81). The fix is a data-structure redesign of root bookkeeping, and the benchmark rows live in the integration area. *Fix:* Index the root list while keeping roots() an ordered span. Options: (a) A flat_hash_map entity -> owner for membership: O(1) root_owner, set_root_owner, 'is a root' checks, and the non-root case of on_entity_destroyed. Linear search stays only for erase and reorder. (b) An order key per root_entry with swap-remove and lazy re-sort. (c) An entity -> position map rebuilt lazily after bulk changes. In load_scene, build a guid -> root-slot map once from rootEntries, and give set_root_order a position map built once. For the sibling half (F607): renumber_children in unlink_child runs per destroy and stamps through get<parent>. Batch the renumbering once per parent in destroy_pending, or read without stamping via get_raw(..., false). What makes it hard: every mRoots mutation (insert_root, erase_root, set_sibling_index, set_root_order, clear, restore, repair) must keep the index consistent. Add bench rows for 10k flat roots (load, unload, destroy all) with thresholds. *Test:* tests/benchmarks/bench_scene.cpp: rows scene.flat_roots_10k_load, scene.flat_roots_10k_unload and scene.flat_roots_10k_destroy, plus thresholds.json entries. tests/scene/test_scene.cpp: "scene - root index stays consistent across insert erase reorder", which runs mixed set_parent, set_sibling_index, set_root_order and raw destroy, then validate() and root_owner checks.

Still present: capture_subtree (scene_serializer.cpp:306-316) deep-clones the whole captured archive through clone_archive just to drop `parent` from entry 0, and prefab::apply clones again (prefab.cpp:377). entity_archive exposes only `entries() const` (entity_archive.h:94), so avoiding the copy needs an ECS API change that scene then has to follow. *Fix:* Add an ECS way to shape the root entry without a copy. Either an archive_options field that excludes a component mask from the first captured entry, or an owner-only `remove_component(entryIndex, id)` or mutable-entry accessor that keeps the mask and component list consistent. Then capture_subtree passes the parent id instead of cloning, and prefab::apply's clone uses the same accessor. What makes it hard: it is a public ecs API change across two owner areas; entity_archive::validate invariants must hold after in-place edits; and archives are published as shared_ptr<const>, so edits must happen before they are shared. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_entity_archive.cpp: "entity_archive - capture can exclude a component from the root entry". tests/scene/test_scene_serializer.cpp: "scene_serializer - capture_subtree of a child has a parentless root entry", which checks that entry 0 has no parent and the rest of the archive is unchanged.

Apply the same wording to gpu_resource_cache.h:34 and :184, and to 02_renderer.md:784 and :922. (b) 02_renderer.md:774: '`find_*`: takes the cache mutex (render thread by contract)'. Keep renderer.h:155 'Render thread' as the conservative contract. (c) 02_renderer.md:155: '(`maxVersions = MAX_VIEWS * VIEW_CONSTANT_WRITES_PER_VIEW * MAX_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT`, 8 * 1 * 4)'. (d) render_settings.h:81-82: 'invalid = no sky; ambient then comes from the grey fallback at intensity 1 (intensity applies only to a valid environment; nothing turns ambient off)'. Risks: the flush cost per frame must stay out of the allocation-free headless frame and the benchmarks; a crash hook must be async-signal-safe on POSIX (flushing an ofstream is not), so on POSIX prefer flush-on-write for the file sink or an unbuffered mode; it interacts with test harness crash handling. *Test:* tests/core/test_log.cpp: "file_log_sink - a WARN line reaches the file without an explicit flush"; tests/engine/test_application.cpp: "application - the log file holds every frame's lines before shutdown" (read the file between step_frame calls).

- **F555** MINOR temp_directory removal failures are silent, and the self-contained publish test leaks about 71 MB per run into <build>/test_tmp (`tests/support/src/temp_directory.cpp:54`). Still present: temp_directory::~temp_directory (temp_directory.cpp:54-60) reports a failed remove_all through WARN_MESSAGE, which a passing CTest entry never shows, and nothing prunes <build>/test_tmp. tests/scripting/slow/test_dotnet_publish.cpp:52-98 publishes a self-contained runtime into a temp_directory and hosts CoreCLR from it in-process. The CLR cannot be unloaded, so on Windows the 71 MB layout can never be removed. A real fix needs changes in scripting's test (where to publish) and in support (stale-directory pruning or louder removal failures). Pruning has to tolerate parallel CTest processes of the same executable sharing one temp root. *Fix:* Shape: (1) scripting: publish into a fixed per-executable directory, environment().temporaryRoot / "dotnet_publish_self_contained", which the case removes with remove_all at its start. The previous process has exited, so its DLLs are no longer mapped, and at most one layout persists per build tree. Alternatively, a CTest FIXTURES_CLEANUP entry in tests/scripting removes it after the run. (2) support: at test_main start-up (not in listing mode), delete entries under environment().temporaryRoot whose last_write_time is older than an age limit (for example 24 h), ignoring errors. The age limit is what keeps concurrently running case processes of the same executable safe. Decide whether temp_directory removal failures stay WARN, since they cannot become failures while (1) is missing. Risks: deleting a live directory of a parallel process if the age limit is too short; filesystem timestamp granularity; and a start-up cost in every one-case-per-process run, which is cheap only while the root stays small. *Test:* tests/support/tests/test_temp_directory.cpp: "temp_directory - start-up pruning removes directories older than the age limit". However, docs/handbook/getting-started.md:170-171 documents the bare ctest form as intended, and docs/handbook/testing.md:369 depends on it (`--build-dir build/bench ... --test -L benchmark`). The fix therefore changes documented behaviour and needs a handbook (docs area) edit alongside tools. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: 1. Reuse F334's CMakePresets.json loader. Resolve the test preset of the same name through `inherits`, falling back to the hidden base-test. 2. On the private-dir path, append `--output-on-failure`, `-j <execution.jobs>`, `--no-tests=error` (when noTestsAction is error) and `-LE <filter.exclude.label>`. Omit -LE when the user passes -L, so the documented `-L benchmark` / `-L process` runs keep working. 3. Call parser.error() when -R or -L is given without --test. 4. Refuse --clean unless the resolved directory lies inside ROOT / "build". 5. Use shutil.rmtree(path, onexc=handler) on Python 3.12 and later, and onerror before that (the handler ignores its third argument). 6. Rewrite the getting-started.md section 6 sentence: the private-dir test run mirrors the preset's label exclusion, jobs and no-tests error, and -L replaces the exclusion. Risks: - Existing agent runs of `--build-dir ... --test` stop running benchmark/slow/process entries and become 4-way parallel. - The preset path still cannot run `-L benchmark`, so the two paths stay asymmetric for -L by design. What makes it hard: changing a documented interface and choosing the -L semantics. Land after F334, which touches the same file. *Test:* tools/tests/test_build.py (factor a test_command(args, presets, private_dir) helper): 'build.py - a private build directory runs the preset's test filter' (-LE benchmark\|golden\|downloaded\|slow\|process, -j 4, --no-tests=error), 'build.py - -L on a private build directory replaces the label exclusion', 'build.py - -R without --test is a usage error', 'build.py - --clean outside build/ is refused'.

Path rule in check_docs. - **F582** MINOR stop_core clears the application's task queue but leaves the process-wide core main-thread queue populated (`engine/src/engine/application.cpp:405`). Still present: stop_core clears only the application's queue (application.cpp:404-405) while core's process-wide queue (main_thread.cpp:19-31) has no clear function, so tasks posted by scripting/managed_build.cpp:147 or editor/core/screenshot_service.cpp:274 survive shutdown and run on the next application's first frame (frame_loop.cpp:99-101). The fix needs a new core API used by engine, and managed builds post from their own thread, so the shutdown race is a threading question. *Fix:* Shape: add `CONJURE_API usize clear_main_thread_queue();` to core/jobs/main_thread.h (drops queued tasks under the mutex, destroying them outside the lock, returns the count) and call it in application::stop_core after services.jobs.stop() next to mMainThreadQueue->clear(), logging a DEBUG line with the dropped count. Hard parts: a managed build thread may still post after stop_core, so either the scripting subsystem must join/cancel its build before shutdown (scripting area) or drop late tasks by stamping each post with an engine generation; destroying a std::function whose captures hold shared_ptrs to subsystem state must not run their destructors on a dead owner; core tests share the process-wide queue and drain it in a guard (test_main_thread.cpp:23). *Test:* tests/core/jobs/test_main_thread.cpp: "main_thread - clear_main_thread_queue drops queued tasks without running them"; tests/engine/test_application.cpp: "application - a task posted to the core queue before shutdown does not run after a second initialise".
- **F584** MINOR load_blocking busy-yields on a job it could help with, and can time out while the job sits queued (`engine/src/assets/asset_manager.cpp:714`). Still present: asset_manager.cpp:702-715 spins on std::this_thread::yield() until the owning job completes or the deadline passes, and never helps run queued jobs. job_system.h:159-178 only offers an unbounded wait(handle) and has no wait with a deadline or help-one-job call. A correct fix therefore either drops the documented TIMEOUT guarantee (design 05 table line 766, plus the existing timeout test) or adds a core job API, and it touches threading. *Fix:* Shape: add a bounded helping wait to core, for example `bool job_system::wait_until(const job_handle &, std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point deadline)`, which runs queued jobs and parks between them, and returns false at the deadline. Then replace the yield loop in load_blocking_slot with it and keep the TIMEOUT path when it returns false. An alternative is to steal the slot's job when it has not started (mark it cancelled and run the stage inline), but that needs a cancel/claim primitive on job_handle. Risks: a helping wait can start a long unrelated job and overrun the deadline, so the deadline becomes best effort; core API and threading changes need TSan coverage. *Test:* tests/core/jobs/test_job_system.cpp "job_system - wait_until helps with queued jobs and returns false at the deadline"; tests/assets/test_asset_manager_load.cpp "load_blocking - a job queued behind busy workers is helped instead of timing out [parallel]".
- **F589** MINOR An empty or null string_id prints as a hash, so empty entity names and tags show as '#cbf29ce484222325' / '#0000000000000000' (`engine/src/core/strings/string_id.cpp:99`). A fix spans core and editor. *Fix:* Shape: (1) core: in to_string, return an empty string when mHash == hash::FNV1A64_OFFSET_BASIS (the interned "" has no other spelling), and keep '#0000000000000000' for null per the pinned contract (update the to_string summary). (2) editor: add a helper `std::string display_name(core::string_id id)` returning "" for is_null() and to_string() otherwise. Use it in entity_summary, entity.get's name/tag output and entity_rename's 'previous'. Make entity_rename map an empty 'name' to core::string_id::null() (matching bindings) or reject it with INVALID_ARGUMENT. Pick the bindings-consistent null mapping, and make sure the undo inverse restores null. (3) check that scene path resolution ('a/b') treats a null-named entity consistently. Risks: other consumers that print null ids and expect '#000...' in logs. It crosses core and editor. *Test:* tests/core/strings/test_string_id.cpp: "string_id - an interned empty name prints as an empty string". tests/editor/test_commands_entity.cpp: "entity.rename - an empty name clears the name and undo restores the previous one" and "entity.get - an untagged entity reports an empty tag".

(2) gltf_importer ignores or rejects a COLOR_0 accessor with fewer than 3 components; test expects white vertex colours or a clean error. In the threaded case at test_document.cpp:1008, count a false result as a failure (`if (!doc.set_pointer(...)) { ++failures; continue; }`), because doctest assertions are not used inside those threads. 4. Leave platform::input::apply alone: its false is informational (a window event), not a failure. 5. Risk: low, but it changes editor command behaviour, so the editor tests must be run. *Test:* tests/core/json/test_document.cpp: change line 189 to CHECK(copy.set_pointer("/a/0", 99)) and check the result at line 1008. tests/core/json/test_json.cpp:111-113: CHECK each set_pointer. tests/editor (component access tests): new case "component_access - a write through a scalar parent reports an error instead of dropping the edit".

- **F652** MINOR script.create accepts C# keywords; script entries serialise with an empty class name; script component reports kind UNKNOWN (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_script.cpp:1`). Persisting the class name needs a scripting-module API and changes scene file content, so it is not editor-only. *Fix:* Shape: (1) editor: reject C# reserved keywords (abstract, as, base, bool, break, byte, case, catch, char, checked, class, const, continue, decimal, default, delegate, do, double, else, enum, event, explicit, extern, false, finally, fixed, float, for, foreach, goto, if, implicit, in, int, interface, internal, is, lock, long, namespace, new, null, object, operator, out, override, params, private, protected, public, readonly, ref, return, sbyte, sealed, short, sizeof, stackalloc, static, string, struct, switch, this, throw, true, try, typeof, uint, ulong, unchecked, unsafe, ushort, using, virtual, void, volatile, while) for args.name and each namespace segment with INVALID_ARGS; this slice is small and could land on its own. (2) scripting: store the class name in script_entry (new add_script overload taking the name, kept through clone_set and read_set_json) and write it when the registry does not know the class, so saved scenes stay readable; script_attach passes the name. (6) docs/design/15_bindings.md 4.3: rename the record in the Interop record list. Add `public sealed class BindingRaycastHitNameProbe : ScriptBehaviour { public string HitType = ""; protected override void OnUpdate(float deltaTime) { RaycastHit hit = default; HitType = hit.GetType().FullName ?? ""; } }`. It compiles only when the name is unambiguous. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_bindings_managed.cpp: 'bindings_managed - RaycastHit is unambiguous in a script importing Conjure.Interop [dotnet]': HitType == "Conjure.RaycastHit" and no ERR.
