- **F717** MINOR The leak check's interned-name budget hides real leaks (`tests/support/engine/src/headless_engine.cpp:559`). Still present: headless_engine.cpp:149 budgets each new name at max(shortCost, longCost) and :559-560 tolerates newNames * that. Each short name interned therefore hides one leaked block (MSVC Debug: 2 vs 3; release: 1 vs 2), which the README already admits (README.md:44-45). The fixture cannot tell short names from long ones: core::string_id exposes only interned_count() (string_id.h:116) with no enumeration. An exact budget therefore needs a new core API, and the fix crosses core and support. *Fix:* Shape: core adds a count of interned names whose spelling does not fit std::string's small-string buffer. string_id.cpp (detail::register_string_id_name) increments it under the table mutex when a newly inserted name has size() > std::string().capacity(). It is exposed as `[[nodiscard]] static usize interned_heap_name_count() noexcept` next to interned_count(). headless_engine then records both counts at the baseline and computes budget = newShort * shortCost + newLong * longCost, where newLong is the heap-name delta and newShort = newNames - newLong. It keeps both measured costs instead of their max, and the README sentence about the remaining imprecision goes away. Risks: this changes core's public API (CONJURE_API export count, docs/api/core page, test_string_id). The SSO capacity differs between standard libraries, so the count must use the same std::string the table stores. Rehash allocations of the unordered_map must stay net zero live (old buckets freed); confirm this on MSVC Debug, where container proxies add blocks. The measured costs must stay stable per category, otherwise the tolerance becomes flaky. *Test:* tests/core/strings/test_string_id.cpp: "string_id - interned_heap_name_count counts only names beyond the small-string buffer". tests/support/tests/engine: a child case "support_engine_child - a one-block leak beside short interned names is reported", which interns three short names and leaks one block, checked by "headless_engine - a leak hidden by short-name slack is reported", which expects exactly one failure naming the budget.
- **F718** MINOR world_builder helpers swallow failures and return results as if they had succeeded (`tests/support/engine/src/world_builder.cpp:279`). Still present: world_builder.cpp:137-140 and 155-163 log a WARN and return a partial tree when create_entity fails. world_builder.cpp:279 and world_builder.h:63 discard the flush_report, and spawn_many silently stops when the command buffer returns a null id. Today's failure modes are narrow: STRUCTURE_LOCKED and WRONG_THREAD assert first, so only ENTITY_LIMIT_REACHED (ecs world.cpp:477) or a captured assert reaches them silently. The real choice is unsettled. FAIL_CHECK inside would tie world_builder to doctest, while 08_testing.md 2.3/849 name it a shared basis for benchmark code too, and bench_support.h:43-44 already avoids temp_directory because it reports through doctest. A core::result return changes the 08_testing.md 4.5 signatures and the integration caller. *Fix:* Recommended shape: return core::result from the three bulk helpers, matching spawn_box_body and spawn_script_entity: `core::result<std::vector<ecs::entity>> spawn_many(...)`, `spawn_hierarchy(...)` and `mark_dirty_fraction(...)`. Each fails with the scene's or ecs error on a failed create, with ecs_error / INVALID_STATE when the flush report shows skipped commands or nothing applied (report.skipped != 0, or !tagged.empty() && report.applied == 0), and with INVALID_ARGUMENT for an unusable hierarchy shape, replacing today's WARN plus empty vector. hierarchy_entity_count stays as is. Update tests/integration/test_hierarchy_propagation.cpp:103 to CONJURE_REQUIRE_OK the result, update every test_world_builder case, amend 08_testing.md 4.5 and the README. Alternative: keep the vector returns and FAIL_CHECK inside. This is support-only, but it makes world_builder unusable from the doctest-less benchmark harness, and a FAIL_CHECK under a live headless_engine adds a second memory_guard failure. Risk: an API change across support, integration and the design record; it is the owner's call between the two. *Test:* tests/support/tests/engine/test_world_builder.cpp: "spawn_hierarchy - a failed entity creation is an error, not a short tree". On a locked world (ecs::iteration_scope plus an expect_assert guard), spawn_hierarchy returns an error and creates nothing silently. Add the same pattern for spawn_many and mark_dirty_fraction ("mark_dirty_fraction - a refused flush is an error").
- **F719** MINOR REQUIRE inside callbacks that engine code invokes throws a doctest exception through engine frames (`tests/core/vfs/test_file_system_conformance.cpp:405`). Still present. REQUIRE is used inside callbacks invoked by engine code: the enumerate callback (test_file_system_conformance.cpp:405), the script phase hook with the world locked (test_bindings_subsystem.cpp:175-176), and register_determinism_systems passed as registerSystems (test_determinism.cpp:104-115, three CONJURE_REQUIRE_OK). A failure then throws through non-throwing engine frames. Each edit is mechanical, but the fix spans three owner areas (core, bindings, integration), so by the rules it is not SMALL. It can be split into three per-area SMALL fixes. *Fix:* In each callback, replace REQUIRE with CHECK, record the outcome in a flag, and REQUIRE the flag after the engine call returns. Conformance case: `bool wroteDuringEnumeration = true;` updated in the callback with the write_file result, then CHECK it after REQUIRE(outcome). Bindings phase hook: `const bool begun = api.query_begin(...) == 0; CHECK(begun); CHECK(world->is_locked()); if (!begun) { return; }`, so no query_end runs without a scope. Determinism: register_determinism_systems is a plain function pointer, so record failure in an anonymous-namespace flag reset in run_sequence, use CHECK for each registration, and REQUIRE the flag once the engine has started. Also grep tests/ for other REQUIRE or CONJURE_REQUIRE_OK inside lambdas passed to engine APIs and convert them the same way. *Test:* No new case. The changes themselves are to the existing "file_system_conformance - enumerate callbacks may call back into the file system" case, the bindings phase-hook regression case in test_bindings_subsystem.cpp, and the determinism cases in tests/integration/test_determinism.cpp.
- **F721** MINOR Log sinks flush only at ERR and above, so a crash loses the tail of the console and file log (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:265`). Still present: file_log_sink::write flushes only at ERR and above (log.cpp:265-268), and so does the console sink (log.cpp:215-217). There is no crash hook (no SetUnhandledExceptionFilter, signal handler or set_terminate anywhere in engine/editor/runtime). Only the assert path flushes (assert.cpp:42, 78). A real fix needs a flush policy that trades performance against durability, which no design doc settles, and a crash-time flush hook outside core with signal-safety and deadlock hazards, because the file sink holds a mutex. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In core, flush the file and console sinks at WARN and above. Add a time-based flush with no thread: remember the last flush time in the sink state and flush when more than about 250 ms have passed since. Pipe-redirected stdout is fully buffered, so the console sink needs the same rule. (2) In engine (or platform), install crash hooks at application start: std::set_terminate and, on Windows, SetUnhandledExceptionFilter, POSIX SIGSEGV/SIGABRT/SIGBUS via sigaction. Each calls a best-effort flush that uses try_lock on the sink mutexes so a crash inside a sink cannot deadlock, then chains to the previous handler. Hard parts: async-signal safety (iostream flush is not signal-safe), ownership (core cannot know about the engine's lifetime, and the hooks are process-global), and testing needs a child process that crashes. *Test:* tests/core/foundation/test_log.cpp: "log - file sink makes a warning visible on disk before the sink is flushed or destroyed" (write a WARN, read the file while the sink is still alive). An integration process test that crashes a child after INFO logging and checks the tail is in --log-file, for example tests/integration "crash_flush - an abnormal exit keeps the last log lines".
- **F725** MINOR Entity and component lookups by name intern every queried string in the never-freed string_id table (`engine/src/editor/core/entity_ref.cpp:24`). Still present. The editor interns runtime query strings at entity_ref.cpp:24, component_access.cpp:100/106, commands_modules.cpp:95 and commands_entity.cpp:586-587; the scene does so at scene.cpp:457. The bindings avoid this with string_id::from_hash. The fix spans the editor and scene modules, and the unbounded growth from unique entity names is a scene design question (name storage lifetime). *Fix:* Shape: switch every pure lookup to `core::string_id::from_hash(core::hash::fnv1a64(text))`, as bindings/entries_entity.cpp:310-313 does (editor lookups and scene::find_path). Audit the other query-only sites (commands_entity.cpp entity.find name/tag, commands_physics.cpp:66, commands_component.cpp:41). For names that are set (entity.create/rename with unique names), the scene owner decides between a reference-counted or scene-scoped name store and a documented bound. Risk: any code that later calls .str() on a from_hash id gets no text, so only comparisons may use it. *Test:* tests/editor/test_entity_ref.cpp: "regression - resolving an unknown entity path does not grow the string_id table". tests/scene/test_scene.cpp: "scene::find_path - a miss does not intern the queried segments" (string_id table entry count unchanged).
- **F732** MINOR Startup imports every stale source synchronously; a 224 MB scene peaks at 1.84 GB and leaves 776 MB resident before it is opened (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/assets_subsystem.cpp:163`). Still present: assets_subsystem::post_startup (assets_subsystem.cpp:159-179) runs scan() and then a synchronous import_stale(jobs) before startup finishes, and asset_database::import (asset_database.cpp:580ff) keeps every job's bytes and output alive until the batch merges. Fixing it means splitting startup import into a priority closure (the startup scene and its dependencies) and a background remainder with progress events, plus ASSET_NOT_READY handling in the editor and bounding the importer's intermediate memory. That touches the assets module, the engine startup contract (06 step 17) and the editor. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In post_startup, import only the stale records in the dependency closure of the startup scene, using asset_database::import(ids, jobs) with collect_dependencies. (2) Queue the remaining stale ids on a background import driven from begin_frame, in batches with a byte budget, and publish progress through the event bus. (3) In asset_database::import, release each job's source bytes and import_output as soon as its blob has been written, not at the end of the batch. Also look at the scene importer's re-serialisation, which peaks at about 8x the file size. Risks: hot reload and the background import racing on the same record; the editor and runtime seeing assets that are not imported yet (load returns CACHE_MISS today); the startup-scene closure itself still being large. Measure before and after with the 224 MB scene from build/sweep_dyn/scale_big.txt. *Test:* tests/engine/test_application_headless.cpp: "application_headless - startup imports only the startup scene closure and the rest arrives over later frames"; a benchmark row assets.startup_import_100k in tests/benchmarks for peak and resident memory
- **F734** MINOR delete_asset rebuilds every index and graph and rewrites the snapshot for each deleted asset (`engine/src/assets/asset_database.cpp:1218`). Still present. Every delete_asset call erases its entries, then runs rebuild_indexes_locked, rebuild_graphs_locked, detect_cycles_locked, recompute_stale (which reads every blob header) and save_snapshot (asset_database.cpp:1207-1233). Deleting k assets therefore costs k full rebuilds and k snapshot writes. A fix needs a new public batched API and editor call sites using it (commands_asset.cpp, commands_prefab.cpp), so it crosses into the editor area. Much of the per-call cost is the recompute_stale problem in F301. *Fix:* Add `core::result<void> delete_assets(std::span<const asset_id> ids)` to asset_database. It validates every id first (unknown -> NOT_FOUND, sub-asset -> INVALID_ARGUMENT, before touching anything), removes each source and meta (on an I/O error, stop and report which assets were already removed), erases every entry and its sub-assets in one erase_if, rebuilds indexes, graphs and cycles once, runs recompute_stale once (or the incremental form from F301), removes the blobs, and saves the snapshot once. delete_asset(id) becomes delete_assets({id}). The editor's folder delete and multi-select delete (and their undo/redo) switch to the batched call. Risks: partial failure semantics mid-batch, the editor undo payload for many assets, and the hot reload controller seeing many REMOVED events at once. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_database.cpp: "asset_database - delete_assets removes many assets with one rebuild and one snapshot write" (100 assets, count snapshot writes through a counting file system or a timing bound). An editor test: "asset.delete - deleting a folder removes every asset in one database call".
- **F739** MINOR No test measures growth over a long run or cost against root count, so every item of this sweep passes the suite (`docs/design/08_testing.md:32`). Still present. 08_testing.md:32 (D6) memory_guard compares live allocation counts at fixture teardown, which containers that only grow and are freed at shutdown always pass. No test or label in tests/ is a soak, and tests/editor/test_console_buffer.cpp wraps only equal-sized records. Closing the gap adds a new test category (soak with a plateau criterion, scale-ratio timing) across integration, editor and possibly support, and needs care to stay deterministic and non-flaky. *Fix:* Shape: (1) a `slow`-labelled integration executable that runs headless_editor (or headless_engine plus the editor core) through 10,000 play frames and 1,000 create/destroy/unique-name cycles, sampling live bytes (core::memory tracked counters; add a live-bytes accessor to the support snapshot if missing) every N iterations and asserting a plateau after warm-up (for example the last quarter grows less than a small fixed budget), not a zero delta. (2) A scene-load scale case of 20,000 roots against 20,000 children of one root, asserting a time ratio bound (relative, so runner speed cancels out). (3) An unequal-size wrap case in test_console_buffer.cpp. Risks: flakiness of timing ratios on shared CI runners (keep them nightly/slow), choosing plateau thresholds that catch the sweep's leaks without false failures, and runtime cost in Debug. *Test:* tests/integration/test_editor_soak.cpp: 'editor soak - [slow] 10,000 play frames reach an allocated-bytes plateau'; tests/integration/test_scene_scale.cpp: 'scene load - [slow] 20,000 roots cost within a fixed ratio of 20,000 children'; tests/editor/test_console_buffer.cpp: 'console_buffer - wrapping with unequal record sizes keeps the newest records intact'
- **F741** MINOR .conjure/ai_port is stale after a crash/kill and is clobbered and deleted by a second editor on the same project (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/protocol_server.cpp:50`). Still present. The file is truncated and rewritten unconditionally (protocol_server.cpp:50-52) and removed unconditionally in stop() (:210-214), so a killed editor leaves a stale port and two editors on one project clobber and delete each other's file. A real fix changes the ai_port file format that agents parse (the skill at SKILL.md:36/179, the wiki and design D12/3.12) and needs a platform-specific process-liveness check that core::process does not offer. *Fix:* Shape of the fix: - Write `{pid, port}` (or the line `port pid`) atomically (temp file plus rename). - On start, if the recorded pid is alive and is not this process, warn and pick a policy: keep the other editor's file, or write a per-pid file. - On stop, remove the file only if it still names this pid and port. The liveness check needs a backend pair (OpenProcess/kill(pid,0)) in core or the editor's platform files. A cheap partial step that stays inside the editor: in stop(), re-read the file and remove it only when it still contains this server's port. That fixes the 'second editor deletes the file' half. Risks: every agent and the skill parse the current one-number format. The format change must be coordinated with the skill (ci owner) and the design record. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_protocol_server.cpp "protocol_server - stop leaves a port file another server rewrote" (two servers on one project root) and "protocol_server - a port file naming a dead process is replaced on start".

- **F751** MINOR No test feeds hostile/corrupt input through the real application: glTF, cooked clip, db.bin, protocol handshake, NUL paths (`tests/integration/test_assets_renderer_headless.cpp:177`). Still present: tests/integration/test_assets_renderer_headless.cpp:174-176 registers only the renderer's asset types and importers in a private pipeline. No test drives corrupt glTF, cooked clips, snapshots, pre-auth protocol traffic, NUL paths or a corrupted content.cpak through the real application. The finding is a coverage umbrella over defects reported separately in other areas (assets/renderer, audio, scene, editor protocol, physics importer selection, runtime). Each regression test belongs with its own fix, and the fuzz smoke adds CI work, so it spans many owners. *Fix:* Shape: land one regression test per reproduced case together with that case's fix, in its own area. glTF accessor bounds -> tests/assets or tests/renderer. Clip frameCount overflow -> tests/audio. Snapshot count bombs -> tests/scene. Importer selection with every subsystem registered -> an integration case building the engine's full importer set and asserting .gltf imports through renderer::GLTF_IMPORTER_ID rather than the physics collision-mesh importer. Pre-auth events, seat exhaustion, HTTP-wrapped requests and NUL in path arguments -> tests/editor/test_protocol_robustness.cpp and tests/editor/data/commands. asset.import onto an existing path plus undo -> a command fixture. Non-ASCII editor project path -> covered by F783's test. Then add a corpus-driven smoke (truncate, bit-flip, re-hash content.cpak) run through conjure_runtime under the process label in CI. Hard: it depends on fixes owned by several areas, the importer-precedence behaviour needs an owner decision, and the fuzz job needs CI time and a deterministic corpus. *Test:* tests/integration/test_assets_renderer_headless.cpp 'assets_renderer_headless - with every engine importer registered a .gltf imports through the renderer importer', tests/runtime/test_runtime_boot.cpp 'conjure_runtime - a corrupted content.cpak exits with a named error and never crashes', plus one case per protocol and parser defect in tests/editor, tests/assets, tests/audio and tests/scene.
- **F752** MINOR std::error_code::message() text (ANSI, localised on Windows) is embedded in UTF-8 error strings (`engine/src/core/vfs/vfs_internal.cpp:84`). Still present. vfs_internal.cpp:84 embeds std::error_code::message(), which MSVC formats with FormatMessageA in the user's language and ANSI code page. The same pattern appears in core/system image.cpp, engine/export_pipeline.cpp:116,171,176, modules/shadow_copy.cpp and module_manager.cpp, and editor tcp_transport.cpp:63. The runtime embeds an activeCodePage UTF-8 manifest; the editor does not. A real fix needs a new shared foundation helper with a Windows-specific implementation and edits in core, engine, modules and editor. *Fix:* Shape: add a public foundation helper, for example core::error_code_text(const std::error_code &). On Windows it formats code.value() for system_category through FormatMessageW and converts to UTF-8 (the logic of system/win32_utils win32_error_message, duplicated in a foundation backend file because foundation cannot depend on system); otherwise it returns message(). Replace every message() call on std::error_code across the modules listed. Cheaper partial alternative for the owner to weigh: embed the runtime's activeCodePage UTF-8 manifest in conjure_editor as well (editor area only), which makes FormatMessageA return UTF-8 there, but test executables and other hosts stay affected. Hard parts: the cross-module public API, a new platform backend in foundation, and auditing every site. *Test:* tests/core/foundation/test_error.cpp: "error - error_code_text of a system error is valid UTF-8 and matches the wide system message". Check ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED / EACCES for valid UTF-8, and on Windows compare against the FormatMessageW text.
- **F759** MINOR f32 values are written as widened doubles: 0.1f becomes 0.10000000149011612 in project.json and every other JSON the engine writes (`engine/src/core/json/writer.cpp:130`). Still present: writer::value(f32) (writer.cpp:130-132) widens to f64, and document stores f32 the same way. project.cpp:325-328 even claims the opposite ('f64 so 0.1 reads back as 0.1'). Every reflected float in scenes and prefabs goes through write_f32 -> writer::value(f32) (json_conversion.cpp:80-86), so the change rewrites the text of all saved data and of canonical hashes, and must keep writer and document byte-identical (10_core.md 2.8). *Fix:* Shape: in writer::value(f32), format with std::to_chars(first, last, item) (shortest float round-trip). In document set/push_back/set_pointer/init_value(f32), store the double parsed back from that shortest float text (std::from_chars to f64), so to_string and to_canonical_string match the writer. Fix the project.cpp comment. Risks: (a) all saved scene, prefab and project text changes on next save, and any content hash built from canonical JSON (asset settings, import caches) changes, forcing reimports; (b) the double-rounding safety of shortest-f32 -> f64 -> f32 must be proven or tested exhaustively over all finite floats; (c) goldens and fixtures holding 17-digit values must be regenerated. Spans core and engine. *Test:* tests/core/json/test_writer.cpp: "writer - f32 values use the shortest f32 spelling" (0.1f -> "0.1", -9.81f -> "-9.81"). tests/core/json/test_document.cpp: "document - set(f32) and writer produce identical text", plus an exhaustive or sampled round-trip test reading every written f32 back as the same float.
- **F763** MINOR Options table says a missing .NET host pack fails the configure; the same page and the code say it warns and turns scripting off (`docs/handbook/build-pipeline.md:84`). Still present: build-pipeline.md:84 lists the .NET host pack as a configure error under 'Error'. The root CMakeLists.txt:75-82 uses find_package(NetHost QUIET), prints a WARNING and turns CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING and CONJURE_BUILD_MANAGED off, so FindNetHost.cmake:109's FAIL_MESSAGE never prints. .claude/skills/build-and-test/SKILL.md:73-74 still tells agents to look for 'Could not find the .NET host pack'. Both edits are trivial; it is CHALLENGING only because the fix spans the docs and ci owner areas. *Fix:* build-pipeline.md: remove the row at :84 from the 'Error' table and add a sentence below the table: 'One rule is a warning, not an error: when `CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING` is on and `find_package(NetHost QUIET)` finds no .NET host pack, the root file warns and turns `CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING` and `CONJURE_BUILD_MANAGED` off for the tree (section 2), so every `[dotnet]` case is absent; `-DCONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING=OFF` makes it explicit and silent.' SKILL.md:73-74: replace the bullet with '- The CMake warning `CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING is ON but no .NET 8+ host pack was found` means the .NET 8+ SDK is missing. The tree configures with scripting and the managed build off, and the summary shows `Scripting ... OFF`. Install the SDK (or set `DOTNET_ROOT`) and reconfigure, or pass `-DCONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING=OFF` to make it explicit.' *Test:* none: docs only
- **F771** MINOR NVRHI fetches DirectX-Headers (a preview tag) and Vulkan-Headers behind the 'declared exactly once' rule (`third_party/CMakeLists.txt:186`). Still present: NVRHI's own CMakeLists fetches DirectX-Headers v1.717.0-preview and Vulkan-Headers v1.4.352 by default (NVRHI_FETCH_*_HEADERS ON; build/verify_perf/_deps/directx_headers-src exists). third_party/CMakeLists.txt:168-186 neither declares nor pins them, against the header's "declared here exactly once" rule, and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:377 does not list them. The fix needs network lookups of the commit hashes, a choice about the preview tag, and a docs update, so it spans build and docs. *Fix:* Before FetchContent_MakeAvailable(nvrhi), do one of: - set NVRHI_DIRECTX_HEADERS_GIT_TAG and NVRHI_VULKAN_HEADERS_GIT_TAG with CACHE STRING "" FORCE to the full commit hashes of the tags NVRHI's pinned commit expects; - or declare DirectX-Headers and Vulkan-Headers with FetchContent_Declare at commit hashes and set NVRHI_FETCH_DIRECTX_HEADERS / NVRHI_FETCH_VULKAN_HEADERS OFF. Check nvrhi-src/CMakeLists.txt for the target names it then expects. Also decide whether the -preview DirectX-Headers tag is acceptable, given the features that need it are off. Update the file header and add both to the ARCHITECTURE third-party row. *Test:* Configure-time check in third_party/CMakeLists.txt: after MakeAvailable, compare `git -C ${directx_headers_SOURCE_DIR} rev-parse HEAD` with the pinned hash and FATAL_ERROR on a mismatch. This mirrors the Jolt pin check at :291-310.
- **F774** MINOR ABI header hash skips the two public .inl files (`cmake/ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:137`). Still present: conjure_add_abi_identity hashes only CONJURE_PUBLIC_HEADERS (ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:137). bindings_error.inl is listed nowhere, and native_api.inl is kept out of PUBLIC_HEADERS on purpose (engine/src/scripting/CMakeLists.txt:34-35). The fix needs a new conjure_add_module argument in build plus list changes in the bindings and scripting areas. *Fix:* Shape: - Add an ABI_INCLUDES multi-value argument to conjure_add_module and conjure_add_core_area. Store it in a CONJURE_ABI_EXTRA_HEADERS target property and add the files as sources. - conjure_add_abi_identity collects both properties. conjure_header_check (ConjureTesting.cmake:244) keeps reading only CONJURE_PUBLIC_HEADERS. - bindings lists engine/include/conjure/bindings/bindings_error.inl; scripting lists ${CONJURE_SCRIPTING_TABLE} (native_api.inl). Effect: the hash value changes once, so every game module must be rebuilt. *Test:* tests/modules/CMakeLists.txt: CTest modules.abi_header_list_complete. A cmake -P script globs engine/include/conjure/**/*.h and *.inl and compares the result with generated/abi_identity/headers.txt.
- **F775** MINOR ABI runtime facts omit ThreadSanitizer (`cmake/ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:109`). Still present: conjure_abi_runtime_facts (ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:109-127) and the generated header (:74-76) cover the dynamic CRT, ASan and UBSan only. module_abi.h:77/114-130 defines runtimeFlags bits 0-2 with no TSan bit. Adding one changes the native module ABI identity across build, modules and 07_modules.md 2.2 (:251). *Fix:* Shape: - Add a tsan fact (CONJURE_ENABLE_TSAN) to conjure_abi_runtime_facts, pass -DCONJURE_ABI_IDENTITY_TSAN, and emit `#define CONJURE_ABI_RUNTIME_TSAN`. - In module_abi.h add ABI_RUNTIME_TSAN = 1u << 3 and CONJURE_ABI_SIGNATURE_TSAN, preferring the compiler's __SANITIZE_THREAD__ / __has_feature(thread_sanitizer) as the ASan path does at :47-52. OR it into the signature's runtimeFlags. - Update the bit comments, 07_modules.md 2.2 and modules/README.md:101-112. - Decide whether CONJURE_MODULE_ABI_VERSION must bump: the layout is unchanged and non-TSan builds keep bit 3 at 0, so probably not. Risk: any module built against the old header reports bit 3 = 0, so it is still rejected by a TSan engine, which is the desired outcome. *Test:* tests/modules/test_module_abi.cpp: 'module_abi - the runtime flags carry the ThreadSanitizer bit of the build'.
- **F780** MINOR Each TestScripts variant recompiles Conjure.Managed and drops a second copy of it beside the scripts (`managed/CMakeLists.txt:142`). Still present: each variant at managed/CMakeLists.txt:140-153 builds Conjure.TestScripts.csproj, whose ProjectReference (csproj:29-33) recompiles Conjure.Managed under its own --artifacts-path (ConjureManaged.cmake:164,182-184), and -o sends that copy into test_scripts/vN. Loading is harmless because GameAssemblyLoadContext.cs:45-48 always resolves Conjure.Managed to the default context. The fix changes how the managed projects reference each other, which is build-system structure, and it must keep the slow managed_build_runner case (test_managed_build_scripts.cpp:64-84) working, since that case builds the project without ConjureManagedDir. *Fix:* Shape: in Conjure.TestScripts.csproj reference the built SDK like Game.csproj:27-30 does (<Reference Include="Conjure.Managed"><HintPath>$(ConjureManagedDir)/Conjure.Managed.dll</HintPath><Private>false</Private></Reference>) when ConjureManagedDir is set, and keep the ProjectReference under Condition="'$(ConjureManagedDir)' == ''" as a fallback for standalone builds. Add "ConjureManagedDir=${CONJURE_MANAGED_OUTPUT_ROOT}" to the PROPERTIES of the three variants in managed/CMakeLists.txt; the DEPENDS chain from conjure_managed_sdk already orders the builds and rebuilds the variants when the SDK changes. Risks: deps.json no longer lists Conjure.Managed, which is fine because the load context resolves it by name; hot-reload tests that stage test_scripts/vN must not depend on the copied DLL; the change must stay compatible with F199's per-config output directory. *Test:* tests/scripting/test_dotnet_script_host.cpp: 'dotnet script host - test script variants carry no copy of Conjure.Managed [dotnet]', checking that scripting_test::test_scripts_assembly("v1").parent_path() / "Conjure.Managed.dll" does not exist. The existing slow case 'managed_build_runner - builds Conjure.TestScripts into a scratch directory' must keep passing.
- **F785** MINOR FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED is forced ON with a normal variable that -D cannot override (`third_party/CMakeLists.txt:16`). Still present. third_party/CMakeLists.txt:16 sets FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED as a normal variable. include(FetchContent) at :10 has already created the cache option as OFF: CMake 3.31 Modules/FetchContent.cmake:1925, confirmed in the local CMake 3.31.6 install. So the normal variable shadows any -D value. The first reader is right that the sweep's `if(NOT DEFINED CACHE{...})` guard would be wrong. The fix adds a new user-facing build option, though, and the repository documents its build options in handbook pages owned by the docs area. getting-started.md section 7 (:177) says options are declared in the root CMakeLists.txt and lists them in a table. architecture.md:202 also lists the options. AGENTS.md section 6 requires the handbook to be updated with the change. The point of the fix is to give users an override they can find, so an undocumented option leaves the fix incomplete. The fix therefore edits the build and docs areas: tiny, but not in one owner area. *Fix:* 1. Root CMakeLists.txt: next to the other options (lines 25-47, which keeps getting-started.md:177 true), add `option(CONJURE_FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED "Reuse fetched third-party sources without contacting their remotes on reconfigure; turn OFF for one configure after changing a pin" ON)`. 2. third_party/CMakeLists.txt:16 becomes `set(FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED ${CONJURE_FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED})`. Add a comment: include(FetchContent) already created FetchContent's own cache entry as OFF in every tree, so a -D on that name cannot carry the project default, and the project option does. 3. docs/handbook/getting-started.md: add a row to the section 7 table (`CONJURE_FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED` \| ON \| reuse `_deps` without contacting remotes; OFF for one configure after changing a pin). Extend the sentence at :65 with the same hint. 4. docs/handbook/build-pipeline.md:272: name the option and say that it is the switch to flip after a pin bump. 5. docs/handbook/architecture.md:202: add the option to the options list. 6. Run `python tools/docs.py check`. *Test:* Configure-time only, no CTest hook. In an already configured tree, change one GIT_TAG and run `cmake --preset <p> -DCONJURE_FETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED=OFF`. The <dep>-subbuild's git update step should fetch the new ref instead of failing. A second configure without the -D keeps the cached OFF until it is set back to ON.
- **F787** MINOR Shared test helper headers are in no source list (`tests/modules/CMakeLists.txt:142`). Still present: tests/modules/CMakeLists.txt:122-147 lists only .cpp files, and the fixtures pass only their .cpp, so fake_consumer.h, shared_fixtures.h, static_fixtures.h and fixtures/fixture_descriptors.h are in no source list. tests/core/vfs/CMakeLists.txt omits vfs_test_helpers.h, while other test directories list their helper headers. Each edit is trivial, but the finding spans two owner areas (core and modules), so by the triage rules it is not SMALL. It can be split into two one-line SMALL edits. *Fix:* tests/modules/CMakeLists.txt: add `fake_consumer.h`, `shared_fixtures.h`, `static_fixtures.h` and `fixtures/fixture_descriptors.h` to the SOURCES of `conjure_add_test_module(modules ...)`. In conjure_modules_add_fixture, change SOURCES to `"${fixtureSourceDir}/${name}/${name}.cpp" "${fixtureSourceDir}/fixture_descriptors.h"`; source_group(TREE CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR) in conjure_add_game_module already covers tests/modules/fixtures. tests/core/vfs/CMakeLists.txt: add `vfs_test_helpers.h` to the SOURCES of conjure_add_test_module(core_vfs ...). *Test:* none: build-list only; configure and build conjure_modules_tests and conjure_core_vfs_tests to confirm.

### editor (32)

- **F087** MAJOR component.set / component.remove can corrupt scene bookkeeping (guid index, hierarchy) (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_component.cpp:154`). Still present. The only guard is the transform check in component_remove (commands_component.cpp:154) and in add_component (component_access.cpp:273). write_component and remove_component (component_access.cpp:215-251, 293-312) write guid, parent, name, disabled and the prefab markers raw through ecs::component_from_json / remove_raw, bypassing the scene's guid index and hierarchy, and is_editable (158-160) reports parent as editable. Each scene-managed component needs its own policy (refuse or route through scene::set_guid, set_parent, set_enabled or the prefab API), and undo inverses currently rely on these paths. *Fix:* Shape: build a table of scene-managed component ids in component_access (guid, parent, children, prefab_instance, prefab_link, plus name and disabled if the scene keeps indexes or inherited state for them; check scene.cpp). For each, either refuse in add/write/remove_component with INVALID_ARGS naming the right command (entity.set_parent, entity.rename, entity.set_enabled, prefab.*), or route the write through the scene API. Make is_editable() and reflection.components report them as not editable without changing scene registration (marking them EDITOR_HIDDEN in scene/register.cpp would make the fix multi-area). Risks: entity.get and the inspector display, component.add inverses that restore these values, prefab override tooling that may write the markers, and the undo of an earlier corrupting edit. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_component.cpp "regression - component.set and component.remove refuse scene-managed components and leave the guid index and hierarchy intact".
- **F090** MAJOR asset.import (and asset.restore) overwrite an existing asset without capturing it; a failed import leaves it destroyed (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_asset.cpp:248`). Still present. asset_import writes the destination without an exists check (commands_asset.cpp:244-255) before import_now validates it (276), records asset.delete as the inverse (298), and never restores on failure. asset_restore does the same (592-606). Fixing it needs a protocol choice (refuse versus overwrite:true), capturing the previous bytes and .meta as the inverse, and rollback in two handlers whose redo payloads feed each other. It also absorbs F138's leftover-file cleanup. *Fix:* Shape: (1) asset.import: when the destination exists, refuse with INVALID_ARGS unless a new `overwrite: true` argument is given (schema and catalogue golden change). When overwriting, capture restore_inverse_for(existing id) before writing and use it as the inverse instead of asset.delete. (2) Both asset_import and asset_restore: if anything after the write fails (meta write, register_and_import), write the captured previous bytes and .meta back, or remove the new file and .meta when there was none. (3) Keep the redo replay (asset.restore of the new bytes) able to overwrite, because it runs after an undo restored the old file. Risks: uuid and .meta preservation when the old and new importer types differ, cooked blobs of the replaced asset, asset.restore being both undo-of-delete and redo-of-import, and the interaction with F756's payload storage. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp "regression - asset.import onto an existing asset is refused without overwrite, and a failed overwrite leaves the old bytes".
- **F091** MAJOR Requests pipelined behind project.open execute against the old project and are then silently lost (`engine/src/editor/core/editor_context.cpp:257`). Still present. editor_context::tick dispatches every polled request (editor_context.cpp:257-262) with no has_pending_project_open() check. The restart happens after step_frame in pump_frame (editor_application.cpp:168-170), and rebind clears mInbound (editor_context.cpp:147). apply_pending_project_open only logs a failed restart (259-265) after the client already got ok. Fixing it means carrying requests across an engine restart, i.e. a change in request ordering relative to the restart. *Fix:* Shape: in tick(), after each dispatch, if mHost != nullptr && mHost->has_pending_project_open(), move the rest of mInbound into a new mDeferredInbound that rebind() does not clear. At the start of the next tick, dispatch mDeferredInbound before newly polled requests, keep the per-frame cap, and drop entries whose client closed meanwhile. Apply the same pending-open check in exec_file_task::tick. When the restart fails, emit an editor_event::WARNING (code PROJECT_OPEN_FAILED, with the error) to every client. Risks: interaction with completed async replies and cancel_all in rebind, per-client ordering and fairness under the cap, a second project.open among the deferred requests, and the double-failure path of F540. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_protocol_server.cpp: "regression - requests pipelined behind project.open in one packet run on the new project" (loopback client sends project.open B, scene.new, entity.create, scene.save in one write; the scene file lands in B).
- **F093** MAJOR A client that half-closes after sending (printf ... \| nc) gets its requests dropped without any response (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/tcp_transport.cpp:204`). Still present. On recv()==0, receive_all sets closing and clears outbound (tcp_transport.cpp:203-206), and poll reports the close in the same call. protocol_server::poll queues the lines and then on_client_closed erases that client's backlog (protocol_server.cpp:318-320), so `printf ... \| nc` gets nothing back. A correct drain must know when everything the client sent has been answered, including async replies held by the dispatcher. That couples the transport, the server and the dispatcher through a new lifecycle state. *Fix:* Shape: - Treat a read-side close (CLOSED from recv) as 'input ended', not as 'connection gone'. Stop reading, keep outbound, and report the state through a new transport query or event. - The server keeps the client until its backlog is empty, command_dispatcher::pending_count(clientId) == 0 and the transport's pending_bytes == 0, then disconnects it. Async replies are bounded by the existing TIMEOUT. - FAILED (reset) or a send error still drops the client at once. stdio already defers the close by one poll (stdio_transport.cpp:101-111); loopback needs the same semantics. Risks: a seat is held until the drain finishes, the interaction with F743's keep-outbound change, and the protocol_transport interface change affects every transport. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_protocol_server.cpp "protocol_server - a client that half-closes after its request still receives the response" (real TCP: send session.ping, shutdown the write side, expect the reply and then EOF) and tests/editor/protocol/test_tcp_transport.cpp "tcp_transport - a read-side close keeps queued output until it is sent".
- **F100** MAJOR Two pending screenshots share one render target: the first gets the wrong image, the second fails (`engine/src/editor/core/screenshot_service.cpp:61`). Still present. The renderer backend keeps one mTarget, and begin() destroys any previous target (screenshot_service.cpp:60-64). Meanwhile request() accepts any number of overlapping pending entries (:215-219), so the first capture reads the second's target and the second fails. Fixing it means either holding one GPU target per pending request (up to 1 GiB each at 16384x16384) or serialising requests. That is a resource-lifetime and policy decision. *Fix:* Option A: begin() returns a ticket (or screenshot_request carries a service-assigned id), the backend keeps a map from ticket to render_target_id, and capture(ticket) reads and destroys that target. Option B (recommended: bounded GPU memory): the service begins only the head request, starting the next after the previous capture, so one target is in flight; waitFrames counts from each request's own begin. Either way the reply's width and height must come from the captured image. Risk: the change interacts with F510's release path and with F099's view hook. *Test:* tests/editor/test_screenshot_service.cpp "screenshot_service - two overlapping screenshots each capture their own size" (recording fake backend; both replies succeed with their own width and height).
- **F107** MAJOR No default dock layout: on first run all six panels are undocked, auto-sized and stacked at (60,60) (`engine/src/editor/ui/editor_workspace.cpp:219`). Still present. draw() only calls DockSpaceOverViewport (editor_workspace.cpp:219) and never builds a layout. load_layout_once (:342-356) loads the ini mid-frame, and editor_layer.cpp:48-50 sets a layout file only when a project is open. The remedy is design step 21 (04_editor.md D17, 3.14: build_default_layout with DockBuilder, Window > Reset layout, the editor.layout.reset command, the per-project ini), which is a feature build with ImGui frame-ordering constraints, not a local fix. *Fix:* Shape: add editor_workspace::build_default_layout(), which splits the dockspace node with ImGui::DockBuilderRemoveNode/AddNode/SplitNode/DockWindow/Finish (imgui_internal.h) into the 3.14 regions. Hierarchy goes left (262 px), Inspector right (340 px), Assets/Console/History/Profiler/AI Session at the bottom (236 px), and the Viewport in the central node. Run it on the first frame when no layout file was loaded or the loaded ini holds no dock nodes, before any panel window is submitted that frame. Hard parts: - The ini must be loaded before NewFrame. Today it is loaded inside draw(), so the load has to move to editor_layer before the frame opens, or use LoadIniSettingsFromMemory at attach. - The toolbar in the design is a fixed bar, not a dock node. - Without a project there is nowhere to persist the layout (a per-user file needs the 3.15 user configuration folder). - Window > Reset layout and the editor.layout.reset command (NOT_AVAILABLE headless) are protocol additions. - The design's real-window test tier is needed to verify the result. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_editor_workspace.cpp: "editor_workspace - the first frame without a layout file docks every default panel". Draw two frames, then check that ImGui::FindWindowByName("Hierarchy"/"Inspector"/"Assets"/"Console")->DockId != 0 and that each sits on the expected side of the central node.
- **F111** MAJOR The scale gizmo scales about the world origin in world axes: scaled entities move and rotated ones shear (`engine/src/editor/ui/gizmo_controller.cpp:141`). Still present. apply_delta does `dragged.worldMatrix = deltaWorld * dragged.worldMatrix` for every operation (gizmo_controller.cpp:141). ImGuizmo's scale delta is a bare diag(s) in the gizmo's local frame, while its translate and rotate deltas are world-to-world, so scaled entities slide relative to the world origin and rotated ones shear. A correct fix must pick multi-selection scale semantics (about the pivot vs. per-entity) and must tell delta kinds apart in UNIVERSAL mode, which the design does not settle. *Fix:* Shape: - Track which sub-operation produced the delta. In UNIVERSAL mode it can be translate, rotate or scale on any frame: use ImGuizmo::IsOver(ImGuizmo::SCALE) at drag start, or decompose the delta. - For a scale delta S with gizmo pivot P, apply world' = P * S * inverse(P) * world when every entity shares the pivot's orientation. For a single entity in LOCAL space (P carries its rotation) this equals world * S. - For WORLD-space or multi-selections with rotated entities, scaling in world axes cannot be represented by transform::from_matrix (shear is lost). Choose and document one rule: scale each entity's local scale by S and scale its offset from the pivot. Risks: - Non-uniform scale under rotated parents. - Coalesced undo of a whole drag. - The UNIVERSAL snap array, which today uses the translate step for every sub-operation (line 101). *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_gizmo_controller.cpp: "regression - the scale gizmo scales an off-origin entity in place". With the entity at (10,0,0), apply a scale delta diag(2,1,1) with operation SCALE and check the position stays (10,0,0) and the scale becomes (2,1,1). Add "gizmo_controller - scaling a 45-degree rotated entity keeps its rotation".
- **F116** MAJOR A module panel keeps the draw pointer of the unloaded image after a hot reload (`engine/src/editor/ui/editor_workspace.cpp:181`). Still present. sync_module_panels (editor_workspace.cpp:175-204) matches only on `owner + "." + title` (:179-181), so a detach and re-attach with the same title keeps the old descriptor and live module_panel. Both hold a by-value copy of the old panel_desc (editor_panel.cpp:91-93, :16/:28-29), and its draw pointer and userData point into the unloaded DLL image. Nothing subscribes to editor_module_consumer::on_panels_changed (editor_module_consumer.h:78). A correct fix has to tear down instances relative to DLL unload and the frame's draw loop. *Fix:* Shape: the workspace subscribes to context.module_consumer().on_panels_changed() and stores the subscription. It also compares entry.module, desc.draw and desc.userData against what each module descriptor captured, and recreates the descriptor and any open instance when they differ (this also removes the two string vectors built every frame, see F530). Hard parts: - The instance must stop calling into the old image before FreeLibrary. If the detach event fires while the workspace is iterating mInstances (a module panel's draw can fire module.reload through support::fire_command), instances cannot be erased in place. They need a 'dead' flag on module_panel (null the copied draw pointer) plus deferred erase after the loop. - The subscription must not outlive the workspace across project.open, where the context is rebound and the layer recreated. - Panel open/closed state and ImGui window identity should survive a reload. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_editor_workspace.cpp: "regression - a module panel re-attached under the same title draws through the new callback". Attach a fake module whose panel_desc draw increments counter A, sync and draw, then detach and attach a desc with the same title whose draw increments counter B. Draw again and check that A did not move and B did.
- **F118** MAJOR Asset and entity references are raw uuid text boxes although the wiki promises pickers (`engine/src/editor/ui/property_drawer.cpp:634`). Still present: ASSET_REF uses draw_uuid (property_drawer.cpp:634) and ENTITY is an InputTextWithHint guid box (:384-406), while docs/api/editor/workspace-ui.md:84 promises 'entity and asset pickers'. A mistyped uuid is silently dropped (draw_uuid :187-190). Real pickers need data the drawers do not get today: property_draw_context (property_drawer.h:23-27) carries no editor_context, so the drawer API changes and three panels are involved. *Fix:* Extend property_draw_context with an optional reference provider (a pointer to editor_context, or a small interface that lists asset records by asset type and scene entities by name). The ASSET_REF drawer becomes a combo or popup with a filter listing database records of the field's asset type, showing the name and keeping the uuid as value. The ENTITY drawer lists scene entities by path. Both accept drag-and-drop: the hierarchy's existing CONJURE_ENTITY payload, plus a new CONJURE_ASSET payload that asset_browser rows become sources for. An invalid typed uuid shows an error tint instead of being ignored. inspector_panel fills the provider. If this is deferred, correct workspace-ui.md:84 to say uuid text fields, a small interim fix. Risks: the field's asset type must be known (field_info has no asset-type member today), and the per-frame database enumeration cost (cache per frame). *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_property_drawer.cpp: 'property_drawer_registry - an asset field lists the imported assets of its type and writes the chosen uuid', and 'property_drawer_registry - an invalid typed uuid is reported, not silently dropped'.
- **F510** MAJOR A screenshot pending at project.open or quit destroys its render target on a renderer that was already shut down (`engine/src/editor/core/screenshot_service.cpp:55`). Still present. While a screenshot is pending, renderer_screenshot_backend holds mTarget, and its destructor calls destroy_render_target (screenshot_service.cpp:53-57). Both destroy paths run after the engine has shut down: editor_application::shutdown runs mEngine->shutdown() and then mContext.reset() (editor_application.cpp:192-196), and apply_pending_project_open shuts the old engine down (:256) before rebind replaces the backend (editor_context.cpp:372). The renderer's services are gone by then. This is GPU resource lifetime ordering across engine teardown and restart. *Fix:* Shape: - Add `virtual void release() {}` to screenshot_backend; the renderer backend's override destroys mTarget. - Add editor_context::release_engine_resources(), which cancels pending screenshots, calls mBackend->release() and resets the backend. - editor_application calls it before `mEngine->shutdown()` in both shutdown() and apply_pending_project_open(); bind_engine recreates the backend for the new engine. Audit other editor objects that hold renderer resources across the same boundary (the viewport target in the UI layer, ImGui textures). Risk: ordering mistakes become use-after-free on real devices only, and null-device tests cannot see them. *Test:* tests/editor/test_screenshot_service.cpp "screenshot_service - releasing engine resources frees a pending screenshot's backend target before shutdown". Use a recording fake backend that asserts release() precedes destruction and that the reply answers CANCELLED.
- **F181** MINOR Eight idle unauthenticated connections lock every legitimate client out of the editor (no handshake/idle timeout) (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/tcp_transport.cpp:178`). Still present. Every accepted socket takes one of the MAX_CLIENTS seats (tcp_transport.cpp:178-181) and nothing ever times out a silent connection. Without --ai-token every connection counts as authenticated, so idle-timing out sockets would also drop legitimate idle agents. Choosing the deadline and quota policy, plus moving seat accounting between transport and server, is more than a local fix. *Fix:* Shape: - Give each connection an accepted-at timestamp and enforce a handshake deadline (for example 5 s) for connections that have not sent a valid first request (with a token, a valid session.hello). - Count only established clients against MAX_CLIENTS and keep a small separate pre-handshake quota (for example 4), evicting the oldest pre-handshake socket when it is full. The transport lacks the authentication state, so the server must tell it which clients are established, or own the seat check itself. Decision needed: whether a token-less editor also requires a first request within the deadline. Recommended: yes, any valid request counts. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_tcp_transport.cpp "tcp_transport - silent connections do not lock out a client that completes the handshake" (8 idle sockets, then a 9th that sends hello is served) and "protocol_server - a connection without a first request is closed after the handshake deadline".
- **F473** MINOR Every undoable command marks the scene dirty, even when it does not touch the scene; play edits leave it dirty after the restore (`engine/src/editor/core/command_dispatcher.cpp:457`). Still present in three places. The dispatcher dirties the scene for any undoable MUTATES (command_dispatcher.cpp:428-433, 453-458), and history.undo/redo force it as well (commands_history.cpp:40, 46). play_controller::stop (play_controller.cpp:63-98) restores the snapshot but not the dirty flag. Module commands are forced to NO_UNDO (editor_module_consumer.cpp:31-35), so they never set it. The fix spans four call sites and leaves open choices: whether REQUIRES_SCENE may serve as the 'edits the scene' signal or the module-ABI command_flags needs a new bit, and what dirty means after a scene.save during play. *Fix:* Shape: 1) Dirty only for scene edits. In today's catalogue every undoable scene command carries REQUIRES_SCENE and no undoable non-scene command does (asset.*, project.set_settings, script.create/write/delete), so set dirty when MUTATES && REQUIRES_SCENE && !NO_UNDO. Alternatively add an explicit flag, but command_flags crosses the module ABI (command_desc.h:25-60). 2) Remove the unconditional set_scene_dirty(true) on success in commands_history.cpp:46, because each dispatched inverse or replay now sets it through the dispatcher; keep the failure path. 3) play_controller: save scene_dirty() in start() and restore it in stop(). Set it true when the restore failed (line 81), and decide what a scene.save during play means (scene.save is not EDITING_ONLY, so the file can then hold play state). 4) Module commands: in make_descriptor's handler wrapper, call invocation.context.set_scene_dirty(true) after success when desc.flags has MUTATES and REQUIRES_SCENE, or use the new flag from 1. Risks: REQUIRES_SCENE as a proxy for future commands, transactions or batches that mix scene and asset edits, and auto-saves that read the flag (commands_modules.cpp:165, commands_editor.cpp:277). *Test:* tests/editor/test_command_dispatcher.cpp "regression - project.set_settings and asset.create_folder leave a saved scene clean"; tests/editor/test_play_controller.cpp "play_controller - play.stop restores the dirty flag the scene had before play"; tests/editor/test_editor_module_consumer.cpp "editor_module_consumer - a module MUTATES and REQUIRES_SCENE command marks the scene dirty".
- **F479** MINOR A headless protocol server spins a full CPU core while idle (`engine/src/editor/core/editor_application.cpp:148`). Still present: run() pumps frames back to back (editor_application.cpp:148-155) and the headless engine config forces a 1/60 frame delta (engine_config.cpp:45), which turns the frame pacer off (frame_pacer.cpp:57-58), so an idle headless server spins a core. The fix needs a pacing or idle-wait policy that does not slow play, play.step, wait_frames or asynchronous replies, and choosing between an editor idle wait and changing the engine pacer rule is a frame-loop timing decision. *Fix:* Shape (recommended, editor-local): after each pump_frame in run(), when headless and the frame was idle (no inbound requests, no pending async replies or frame tasks, play state EDITING), sleep briefly (about 1-2 ms) or block on the transports with a short timeout (TCP select/poll, stdio queue condition variable); never sleep while anything is pending so command latency and test speed stay the same. editor_context needs an 'idle last tick' query. Alternative: let the engine pacer pace headless servers even with a forced delta (engine module, 06_engine_loop.md 3.3). Risks: added latency per request, test durations, timeout accounting in frames. *Test:* tests/editor/test_editor_application.cpp: "editor_application - an idle headless server sleeps between frames" (count frames over ~200 ms of idle wall time and CHECK it stays below a few hundred)
- **F495** MINOR Tests that cannot fail or miss the failing path (`tests/editor/test_commands_script.cpp:166`). Still present: 'script.compile - with dotnet present…' accepts ok or COMPILE_FAILED (test_commands_script.cpp:166-183), and the other five weak cases cited remain. It bundles six independent tightenings. Two of them cannot pass until other open defects are fixed: script.compile success needs the managed-dir and template fixes, and an --ai-stdio process case would expose the stdout-pollution defect. The undo-time case needs a budget figure that 08_testing.md does not give. *Fix:* Split into separate changes: (1) script.compile: define CONJURE_EDITOR_TEST_MANAGED_DIR for the test, then require ok and succeeded == true when the SDK is present (land after the managed-dir and template fixes). (2) test_console_buffer: a multi-lap wrap with unequal chunk sizes. (3) test_batch_command: atomic rollback during play. (4) test_editor_process: an --ai-stdio case without --log-level warn that asserts every stdout line parses as protocol JSON (land after the stdout-pollution fix). (5) test_picker: an edit-mode physics hit. (6) test_commands_entity: undo of 10,000 entities within a budget measured on the reference machine and recorded in 08_testing.md. Items 2, 3 and 5 are test-only and can go first. *Test:* tests/editor/test_console_buffer.cpp: 'console_buffer - text ring wraps several laps with uneven chunks'; tests/editor/test_batch_command.cpp: 'editor.batch - atomic rollback while playing'; tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp: 'conjure_editor - --ai-stdio writes only protocol lines to stdout'; tests/editor/test_picker.cpp: 'picker - edit-mode physics hit'; tests/editor/test_commands_script.cpp: 'script.compile - with dotnet and the SDK present the build succeeds'.
- **F497** MINOR Undo bookkeeping serialises and copies whole payloads (`engine/src/editor/core/undo_stack.cpp:33`). Still present: - measure() serialises every inverse and replay to count bytes (undo_stack.cpp:30-39), again on every coalesced drag frame (:79-82). - pop_for_undo and pop_for_redo deep-copy the entry (:149, :156). - asset.import builds the base64 restore payload before the !copiedIn early return (commands_asset.cpp:281 vs :292). Returning references changes entry lifetime during dispatch_inverses (an inverse could clear or evict the deque). Measuring without a string needs a size model or a core JSON API. Capping large asset payloads is a policy choice. Together that is beyond SMALL. *Fix:* (1) Move restore_inverse_for after the copiedIn check in asset.import. This is trivial and could ship first. (2) Measure each entry once at record time, and on coalescing subtract only the replaced replay's cached size, or add a byte-size estimate to core::json::document (core API). (3) Make pop_for_undo/pop_for_redo return `const undo_entry *`. Before doing so, prove that no inverse or replay dispatched by commands_history.cpp can call undo.clear(), evict() or discard while the pointer is live (recording is suppressed, but scene-level inverses may clear the stack). Otherwise move the entry out and back. (4) Decide whether one entry above the byte budget may stay (currently intentional at :227-235) or large asset payloads are externalised to temp files. Risk: subtle undo corruption if lifetimes are wrong. *Test:* tests/editor/test_undo_stack.cpp "undo_stack - undo and redo do not copy the entry's argument documents" (count allocations or compare document identity) and tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp "asset.import - importing a file already under assets records nothing and reads no restore payload".
- **F498** MINOR Avoidable deep copies and re-serialisation on the request/response path (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/protocol_message.cpp:150`). All five hot spots are still present: protocol_message.cpp:150/160/178/192 (from_value copies), commands_editor.cpp:170 (serialise then parse), reflection_path.cpp:324 (copies the whole component), commands_scene.cpp:258 (read_component inside the per-field loop) and schema_validator.cpp:269/292 (formats a path for every element). Together they span five files, the read_at fix needs a value-level JSON pointer lookup that core::json::value lacks (only document::at_pointer exists, document.h:227), and the validator change rewrites the recursion signature. That is well over the SMALL budget for a MINOR performance item. *Fix:* (1) Serialise with core::json::to_string(value). F188's fix already does this. (2) response_to_json: build the document from the struct fields (id copy, ok, result or error{code,message,details}) instead of serialise followed by parse. (3) read_at: resolve the pointer on the value without copying the component. Either add `value::at_pointer` to core (a core API change), or walk the field_ref segments in reflection_path.cpp. (4) scene.query: read each (entity, component) document once per row and reuse it across fields. (5) schema_validator: pass the parent path plus a segment (name or index) down the recursion and format the full path only when instance_error is produced. Every error message must stay byte-identical. Risks: the validator runs on every request and its messages are asserted by tests and agents, so behaviour must not drift. A benchmark is needed to show the gain. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp "scene.query - several fields of one component read the same values as component.get". tests/editor/test_schema_validator.cpp "schema_validator - error paths for nested arrays and members are unchanged" pins the messages. Optionally add a tests/benchmarks case (integration owner) for a large set_many.
- **F500** MINOR The final MESSAGE_TOO_LARGE / UNAUTHORISED response can be lost, and partial sends are quadratic (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/tcp_transport.cpp:120`): same as F743.
- **F501** MINOR Inbound backlog is unbounded and the per-frame cap is global, so one flooding client starves the others (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/protocol_server.cpp:151`). Still present. mBacklog.push_back at protocol_server.cpp:145 has no limit, and :151-155 drain a single FIFO shared by all clients, so one flooding client delays everyone else. The design's failure table covers the write backlog but not the inbound one. Choosing between BUSY answers and backpressure (stop reading the socket, which needs a transport API) is a behaviour decision on top of reworking the scheduling. *Fix:* Keep per-client ordering but drain round-robin across clients, up to maxRequests per frame. Cap each client's queued requests (for example 4096 or a byte budget). Over the cap, either answer BUSY and drop the line (simple, lossy) or pause reading that client's socket until it drains (lossless, needs `protocol_transport::pause_reading(clientId)` in tcp_transport and a no-op in the others). Recommendation: backpressure for TCP, BUSY for stdio and loopback. Record the policy in 04_editor.md section 6. Risks: the existing test 'the per-frame cap leaves a backlog that drains in order' must keep passing, and changing the fairness order changes which requests run in a frame. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_protocol_server.cpp "protocol_server - a flooding client does not starve another client's request" and "protocol_server - a client over the inbound backlog cap is throttled".
- **F507** MINOR asset.set writes the file before the import validates it, and its path syntax differs from component paths (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_asset.cpp:566`). Still present. asset_set writes the file atomically (commands_asset.cpp:566-572) before import_now (573), with no rollback, so a rejected value stays on disk without an undo entry. The path is split on '.' into raw JSON-pointer segments (540-543), with no [i] and JSON sources only, while docs/design/04_editor.md:865 promises a 'reflection path on any asset type'. The rollback is small, but the path half is an open design gap: implement reflection paths for every asset type, or narrow the design to JSON assets. *Fix:* Shape: (1) rollback, which is small and independent. When import_now fails after the write, decode the already captured restore.args contentBase64 (and metaBase64), write it back with write_file_atomic, re-import, and return the original error. (2) Path syntax: either parse args.path with editor::reflection_path::parse (supports [i]) and map the segments to a JSON pointer for JSON assets, keeping the JSON-only limit documented in ai-protocol.md and 04_editor.md; or implement reflection-based editing of non-JSON asset types as the design says, which needs per-type reflected settings. The design choice is the hard part. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp "regression - asset.set whose reimport fails leaves the file byte-identical and answers the import error" plus "asset.set - an indexed path [i] edits an array element".
- **F513** MINOR stdio transport on Windows: CRLF line ends, and quick_exit skips buffered log output (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/stdio_transport.cpp:119`). The CRLF half is real: stdio_transport::send writes '\n' through std::cout (stdio_transport.cpp:118-120), and std::cout is in text mode on Windows. The quick_exit half is not a bug. main.cpp:45-50 calls application.shutdown() first, which runs engine::application::shutdown: it removes and destroys the file sink and calls core::log::flush() (application.cpp:406-410). Then cout and cerr are flushed. Switching stdout to binary needs Win32 code, AGENTS.md rule 10 keeps that in backend files, and design 3.12 names the socket pair as the editor's only platform-specific files, so where this seam lives is a design choice. *Fix:* Shape: add `void set_binary_stdout() noexcept` behind a backend pair. Either reuse the socket pair (net/socket.h, `_setmode(_fileno(stdout), _O_BINARY)` in socket_win32.cpp, a no-op in socket_posix.cpp), or add a new stdio_win32/stdio_posix pair and update 3.12. Call it from main.cpp whenever stdout carries protocol data (the condition from F083: --ai-stdio, --exec without --out, --export). Leave stdin in text mode: CRLF from clients is harmless because \r is JSON whitespace. Risk: std::cout is synchronised with stdio, so the mode must be set before anything is written. *Test:* tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp "editor_process - --exec without --out writes LF-only lines [process]" (standardOutput contains no '\r'). --ai-stdio cannot be driven by core::process because the child's stdin is the null device and the editor keeps running after EOF.
- **F525** MINOR Text edits commit only on Enter and are dropped silently when focus leaves; the entity-name edit can land on another entity (`engine/src/editor/ui/panels/inspector_panel.cpp:86`). Still present. The name field and the string, uuid and entity drawers commit only on Enter (inspector_panel.cpp:82-96; property_drawer.cpp:158-170, 172-189 and the entity drawer). The drawers rebuild `current` from the component every frame, so the typed text is gone by the time the field deactivates. Commit-on-deactivate needs a persistent per-active-field edit buffer and a captured target, which the stateless drawer registry does not have yet. *Fix:* Shape: - Give property_drawer_registry (or the inspector) a single active-edit slot `{ImGuiID id; std::string buffer; core::uuid target; std::string component; }`. Fill it on ImGui::IsItemActivated, let the text-like drawers edit the slot's buffer while their id is active, and commit on IsItemDeactivatedAfterEdit (Escape cancels) through component.set addressed to the captured guid and component, not the current selection. - The entity-name field likewise captures the guid when activated and renames that entity. Risks: - draw_object returns a whole-component 'changed', so a deactivation commit has to flow back through draw_component for the captured entity even if the selection changed. - Nested arrays and structs need unique ids. - Coalescing keys. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/panels/test_inspector_panel.cpp: "regression - a string field edit is committed when the field loses focus". Also "regression - an entity-name edit renames the entity that was selected when the edit began" (change the selection via selection.set mid-edit, then deactivate).
- **F527** MINOR Array fields can be edited element by element but not resized, and tag components cannot be added from the inspector (`engine/src/editor/ui/property_drawer.cpp:471`). Still present: the ARRAY branch of draw_object (property_drawer.cpp:471-498) only edits existing elements. draw_add_component skips tag types (inspector_panel.cpp:195), field_attributes::category is unused, and the filter is a case-sensitive `name.find` (:202). It is four features rather than one bug: resizing needs a default element per field_kind and a struct default from reflection, and tag listing is a behaviour choice because the zero-size `conjure.scene.disabled` tag duplicates the Enabled checkbox. *Fix:* Split into: (a) case-insensitive Add Component filter, a lowercase compare as in the hierarchy filter (trivial); (b) array add and remove: after the element loop draw `+`, and per element an `x` (skipped when readOnly). A new element is the struct type's default JSON (reflection type_info default) or a per-kind default table (0, "", [0,0,0], identity quat, null entity, empty uuid). Write the whole array through the existing changed path so component.set records one undo entry; fixed-size reflected arrays, if any, must not resize; (c) tags in Add Component: list zero-size tags except the ones owned by dedicated widgets (conjure.scene.disabled); (d) grouping by attributes.category with collapsing headers. Risks: undo coalescing of structural edits, and reflection defaults for nested structs. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_property_drawer.cpp: 'property_drawer_registry - array fields grow and shrink through the add and remove buttons'. tests/editor/ui/panels/test_inspector_panel.cpp: 'inspector_panel - Add Component filter is case-insensitive and lists tag components'.
- **F536** MINOR The `<scene>.editor.json` sidecar of D12 is not implemented; editor camera, tree expansion and open panels are lost between sessions (`docs/design/04_editor.md:47`). Still present. 04_editor.md:52 (D12, re-affirmed by the owner in 38bc350) lists `<scene>.editor.json`, but nothing in engine/ or editor/ reads or writes it. editor_workspace.cpp:131-135 opens panels only from descriptor.openByDefault, so the open-panel set is not persisted. Implementing it means a new editor-only file format plus scene-lifecycle hooks (open, save, rename, unsaved scenes) and layout persistence that overlaps the pending D17 default-layout work. *Fix:* Shape: an editor_scene_sidecar (JSON via core::json) holding editor camera pose and mode, view mode (2D/3D), hierarchy expanded guids and the gizmo mode. Read it after scene.open succeeds and write it on scene.save / scene.close / editor shutdown, next to the scene as `<scene>.editor.json`; ignore it silently when missing or invalid. Persist the open-panel set in editor_layout.ini through an ImGui settings handler ([Conjure][Panels]) or a sibling file, and set the OS window title to 'Conjure - <project> - <scene>[*]'. Risks: the D17 default-layout and io.IniFilename work (04 3.14) touches the same code and should land first or together; sidecars of unsaved, renamed or deleted scenes; keeping 'scene files never learn the editor exists'; and tests need temp project directories. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_scene_project.cpp: 'scene.open - restores the editor camera and expanded rows from the scene sidecar'; tests/editor/ui/test_editor_workspace.cpp: 'editor_workspace - the open panel set survives save_layout and a new workspace'
- **F537** MINOR The UI tests are draw-only smoke tests: no test clicks, types or presses a key, and none runs under the real imgui_layer (`tests/editor/ui/panels/test_toolbar_panel.cpp:23`). Still present: every panel test only draws frames and checks counters (for example test_toolbar_panel.cpp:23-43), and imgui_frame_scope offers only set_mouse(position, leftDown) (imgui_frame_scope.h:43-46), used only by its own test. The windowed path (imgui_layer, editor_layer, a real device) has no test. The fix is a test-infrastructure feature plus one interaction test per panel and a GPU-gated process test: large, and it needs a CI decision on GPU availability. *Fix:* (1) Extend imgui_frame_scope with input injection for the next frame: add_key(ImGuiKey, bool down) (modifiers included), add_text(std::string_view utf8), set_mouse_button(int button, bool down), set_mouse_wheel(f32), and focus_next_item() wrapping SetKeyboardFocusHere. Add tests in test_imgui_frame_scope.cpp. (2) Write one interaction test per panel for the defects named in the sweep: hierarchy Delete during rename, stale names, toolbar shortcut gating (F115), gizmo scale delta, console limit, asset drop target. (3) Add a test_editor_process case that runs the windowed editor for N frames with --frames N where a GPU exists (skip through a precondition probe otherwise) and checks exit code 0. Risks: ImGui input trickling across frames makes tests order-sensitive, and CI runners without a GPU need the precondition skip. *Test:* tests/editor/ui/test_imgui_frame_scope.cpp: 'imgui_frame_scope - injected keys and text reach the next frame'; one '<panel> - <interaction>' case per panel (for example 'hierarchy_panel - Delete while renaming edits the text, not the selection'); tests/editor/test_editor_process.cpp: 'conjure_editor - the windowed editor runs a few frames and exits 0'.
- **F540** MINOR If project.open fails and the previous project cannot be restarted either, the context is left bound to a destroyed engine and the exit code becomes RUNTIME_FAILURE (`engine/src/editor/core/editor_application.cpp:266`). Still present. apply_pending_project_open (editor_application.cpp:251-285) shuts the old engine down at 256. If start_engine(previous) also fails, it returns at 269 before `old.reset()`, so `old` is destroyed at scope exit while mContext still points at it (rebind only runs on success, start_engine:239). run() then overwrites INIT_FAILURE with RUNTIME_FAILURE at 152, and ~editor_context, the screenshot backend and the headless runner's epilogue all touch the dead engine. The fix is about engine and context teardown order across a restart, and about every caller assuming context() stays bound, so it is not local. *Fix:* Shape: on a double failure, tear down in the order shutdown() uses while `old` is still alive: mServer->stop(), mContext->set_server(nullptr), mContext.reset(), then old.reset(). Then make run(), run_export(), headless_tick(), the epilogue of headless_runner::run_requests and editor_application::context() callers tolerate a null context, or leave the context in an inert state. In run(), do not overwrite mExitCode when mQuitRequested is already set, and use the startup-failure code 2 (04_editor.md 3.11), not INIT_FAILURE (1). Optionally validate the target project more deeply (mounts, engine config) before shutting down the old engine, and refuse or save a dirty scene before the restart. Risks: the module consumer must outlive the module manager's shutdown; protocol_server is built over mContext->events(); the UI layer holds the context; the restart order is subtle, and the double failure needs fault injection to test. *Test:* tests/editor/test_editor_application.cpp: "regression - project.open whose new and previous projects both fail to start quits with the startup failure code and leaves no dangling context" (target project.json loads but its engine config fails initialise; the previous project directory is removed before the restart frame).
- **F596** MINOR Final responses can be truncated on disconnect, and a client that half-closes after writing gets nothing (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/tcp_transport.cpp:119`): same as F093.
- **F641** MINOR Every mutating command marks the scene dirty, including project.set_settings, asset.* and script.* (`engine/src/editor/core/command_dispatcher.cpp:428`): same as F473.
- **F642** MINOR Result schemas omit most of what commands actually return (`docs/generated/editor_protocol.json:1`). Still present. In docs/generated/editor_protocol.json, entity.get, scene.info, editor.state and physics.raycast have the result schema {type: object, additionalProperties: true} with no properties. history.undo declares only applied and labels, and view.pick omits candidates, mode and ray. Fixing it means rewriting the result schemas of about 100 commands across 15+ files, regenerating the catalogue (which changes the schemaHash clients see in session.hello) and adding a validation test, so it is too large and too contract-bearing to be SMALL. *Fix:* Shape: in each registration, write the result schema the reply actually produces: properties plus required, nested objects for settings and components, and additionalProperties false where the reply is closed. Regenerate docs/generated/editor_protocol.json through the existing dump test. Then add a catalogue test that sends every command fixture through the dispatcher and validates each reply against the declared result schema with the editor's own schema validator, in strict mode. What makes it hard: the 04_editor.md 2.1 schema subset (shallow oneOf, no $ref) may not express replies such as entity.get components or physics hit lists; module-registered commands (command_desc::resultSchema) must keep loading; closing schemas changes the protocol contract and the schemaHash; .claude/skills/editor-ai-control/SKILL.md may need its summary refreshed. *Test:* tests/editor/test_command_catalogue.cpp: "command_catalogue - every fixture reply validates against its command's result schema"
- **F649** MINOR Unknown fields in a whole-component value are silently dropped (`engine/src/editor/core/component_access.cpp:1`). Still present: write_component hands the whole value to ecs::component_from_json (component_access.cpp:241-246), whose reflection reader ignores members that are not fields, so component.add / entity.create {components} accept misspelt names while component.set on the same path fails with PATH_NOT_FOUND.
