The check must mirror core reflection's JSON shapes (nested structs, arrays, asset and entity references, serializer-hook components) and must not break the undo round trips that feed read_component output back through write_component. *Fix:* Shape: before component_from_json in write_component, when info.reflection != nullptr and the component has no custom readJson hook, walk the value object against the type_info: every member key must name a field (else INVALID_ARGS "args.value.<path>: '<key>' is not a field of '<type>'"), recursing only into fields whose type is a reflected struct (objects) and arrays of reflected structs, never into asset/entity reference or opaque fields. Verify against every registered component that read_component output passes the walk (a catalogue-wide round-trip test). The script.attach fields part cannot be validated before the class is loaded; leave it or validate only when runtime->classes().find(className) is non-null. Alternative: a strict mode in core::reflect (multi-area). *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_component.cpp: "component.add - an unknown member in the value is INVALID_ARGS and nothing is added" plus "component.set - every registered component's own read_component output is accepted back"
- **F729** MINOR Undo deep-copies the whole entry and re-serialises its arguments to measure it; one large entry bypasses the 256 MiB budget (`engine/src/editor/core/undo_stack.cpp:149`): same as F497.
- **F743** MINOR Outbound queue is drained with std::string::erase(0, sent): quadratic for large responses; disconnect() drops whatever one non-blocking send did not take (`engine/src/editor/core/protocol/tcp_transport.cpp:236`). Still present. disconnect() flushes once and then clears outbound (tcp_transport.cpp:119-121), truncating any final response larger than the socket buffer, and flush_connection memmoves the whole remainder on every partial send (:236). Keeping the bytes is not enough on its own: protocol_server::flush disconnects clients precisely because they stopped reading above 64 MiB, so a kept buffer needs a deadline or cap. Closing while the peer is still sending resets the connection unless the socket layer gains a shutdown(SD_SEND) and drain, which means changes in both platform socket backends. *Fix:* (1) Replace erase(0, sent) with a `usize outboundOffset` per connection. Advance it on each send and compact (erase the consumed prefix) once it passes half the buffer; pending_bytes returns size - offset. This part is trivial. (2) In disconnect(), keep the outbound bytes and set closing with a deadline (for example 2 s or N polls). poll() erases a closing connection when outbound is drained or the deadline passes. (3) Add net::shutdown_send() to socket.h and both backends. Call it once outbound is drained, then close after the peer's EOF or the deadline, so the final line is not lost to an RST. Risks: connection lifetime now spans frames, seat accounting must ignore closing connections (it already does at :177), and the timing must not slow tests. *Test:* tests/editor/protocol/test_tcp_transport.cpp "tcp_transport - a final response larger than the socket buffer arrives whole before the close": queue a multi-megabyte line, disconnect, then read until EOF and check the length.
- **F756** MINOR asset.import holds the imported file four times in memory and keeps a base64 copy in the undo stack (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_asset.cpp:241`). Still present. asset_import reads the source into a stringstream and a string (commands_asset.cpp:241-243), writes it, reads it back through the VFS and base64-encodes it into the replay (restore_inverse_for 176-182, stored at 298-302), so the undo history pins about 1.33x every imported file. Removing the in-memory payload means designing undo payload storage that every asset.* inverse shares (asset.delete, asset.set, asset.create_material), including lifetime and cleanup. *Fix:* Shape: (1) the cheap part: copy with std::filesystem::copy_file, or a chunked VFS copy, instead of a stringstream and a string; (2) the hard part: keep large payloads out of the undo stack. Either write them to .conjure/undo/<entry>.bin, referenced from asset.restore args (a new `contentFile` argument) and deleted when undo_stack evicts or clears the entry, which needs an eviction callback; or cap inline payloads (for example 16 MiB) and make larger imports non-undoable with a `warning` event. Risks: file lifetime across project close and crashes, redo after eviction, protocol schema change for asset.restore, every restore_inverse_for caller. *Test:* tests/editor/test_commands_asset_prefab.cpp "asset.import - a copy-in above the inline limit keeps no base64 payload in the undo entry and redo still restores it".

### core (14)

- **F005** MAJOR wait() helps with any queued job, so fork-join jobs nest without bound and can overflow the stack (`engine/src/core/jobs/job_system.cpp:680`). Still present: job_system::state::wait (job_system.cpp:676-693) pops any queued job via try_pop/pop_locked (939-954, FIFO from the lane head) and runs it on the waiting thread's stack, with no depth bound (execution_frame at 1003-1017 only chains frames). The fix changes the scheduler's helping policy, which is threading design. *Fix:* Shape: bound nesting while keeping wait() deadlock-free. Option 1: keep a thread_local help depth (the number of execution_frame entries with helping=true on sInnermostFrame). Past a limit (e.g. 32), wait() may only pop jobs whose counter is the awaited counter, or jobs scheduled by this thread (block->scheduler == this_thread), searched LIFO from the lane tails. Otherwise it spins or blocks. Option 2: make helping LIFO for the helper, so a waiter finds its own just-pushed children first. Risk: a naive "stop helping past N" deadlocks when every worker sits at the limit waiting on children only they could run. That is why the waiter must still be allowed to run its own descendants, which needs a way to find them in the lanes (scan, or a per-job owner tag). Interacts with wait_idle's mIdleWaiterDepth accounting and parallel_for's caller participation, so all [parallel] tests with 0 and 8 workers must be rerun. *Test:* tests/core/jobs/test_job_system.cpp: "job_system - thousands of schedule-and-wait jobs complete without unbounded nesting" (8000 top-level jobs, each schedule(child)+wait(child), 7 workers; assert completion and a recorded max help depth <= the limit through a test hook or stats field).
- **F202** MINOR Every rehash and backward shift deep-copies the keys (pair<const K, V> cannot move its key); move-only keys do not compile (`engine/include/conjure/core/containers/flat_hash_map.h:466`). Still present: elements are std::pair<const K, V> and rehash (flat_hash_map.h:465-467) and relocate (493-500) construct from std::move(*source), which copy-constructs the const key, so every growth step copies every key and move-only keys fail to compile. A correct fix changes how elements are stored or relocated and has a UB question attached, so it is not a mechanical patch. *Fix:* Shape: relocate the key by move without modifying a const object. Option 1 (suggested by the sweep): const_cast the source key and move it in piecewise construction; simple but formally UB because pair::first of an object created as pair<const K, V> is a const object. Option 2 (what libc++ unordered_map and Abseil do): store slots as a union of std::pair<const K, V> and std::pair<K, V> (or construct the slot as pair<K,V> and expose it through std::launder'ed pair<const K,V> pointers), so rehash/relocate/erase-shift can move the mutable view. Risks: strict aliasing/launder correctness, iterator value_type and reference types must not change, the trivially-copyable memcpy path must stay, clone_from and the set (Value = K) must keep working; requires a move-only-key test and a copy-counting key test across rehash and backward-shift erase. *Test:* tests/core/containers/test_flat_hash_map.cpp: "flat_hash_map - rehash and erase move keys instead of copying them" (counting key: 100 try_emplace(K&&) perform 0 key copies) and "flat_hash_map - move-only keys are supported" (flat_hash_map<std::unique_ptr<int>, int, ptr_hash> with insert, rehash, erase and find).
- **F207** MINOR The built-in console sink always emits ANSI colour escapes, also into pipes, files and consoles without VT processing (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:90`). Still present: snapshot_sinks creates the built-in console with `std::make_shared<console_log_sink>()` (log.cpp:90, so useColour = true), and console_log_sink::write wraps every line in escapes (log.cpp:209-210). Nothing checks whether the stream is a terminal, NO_COLOR, or VT processing. The fix needs new platform backend code in core (isatty/_isatty, GetConsoleMode/SetConsoleMode) and per-stream decisions (stdout may be piped while stderr is a terminal). It also leaves policy open: whether to honour NO_COLOR, and whether to change the process console mode on Windows. It is also hard to test headless. *Fix:* Shape: add an internal foundation backend (console.h plus console_windows.cpp / console_posix.cpp, selected in foundation/CMakeLists.txt with if(WIN32) like core/system) exposing `bool stream_supports_colour(std::FILE *)`. POSIX: isatty(fileno(stream)). Windows: _isatty(_fileno(stream)) plus GetConsoleMode on the handle, enabling ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_PROCESSING and returning false if that fails. Both return false when NO_COLOR is set. The default console sink then decides colour once per stream (two bools: stdout and stderr). The explicit console_log_sink(bool) constructor keeps forcing colour on or off for tests. This could share a backend unit with F221's local_time_of. Risks: SetConsoleMode changes a console shared with the parent process; legacy conhost; keeping the public constructor's meaning; per-stream state. *Test:* tests/core/foundation/test_log.cpp: "log - the default console sink writes no colour codes when stdout is not a terminal". Under CTest stdout is a pipe, so assert the internal stream_supports_colour(stdout) is false there, or expose a query on the sink. Replace the cannot-fail "log - console sink writes without colour and with colour" with checks of the bytes written to a temporary file stream if the sink gains a stream parameter.
- **F208** MINOR Every log message copies the sink vector under the global mutex (one heap allocation plus shared_ptr ref-count traffic per line) (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:85`): same as F581.
- **F230** MINOR steady_clock::sleep_until relies on a 2 ms OS sleep accuracy that Windows only gives when the timer resolution is raised (`engine/src/core/time/clock.cpp:91`). Still present. clock.cpp:89-93 relies on std::this_thread::sleep_for overshooting by less than SPIN_WINDOW_SECONDS, but nothing in the repository raises the Windows timer resolution (no timeBeginPeriod or CREATE_WAITABLE_TIMER_HIGH_RESOLUTION anywhere). The frame pacer (frame_pacer.cpp:47) can overshoot by a whole 15.6 ms tick. The fix needs a choice between mechanisms with different side effects (process-global timer resolution and power, versus a high-resolution waitable timer on Windows 10 1803+), a new platform backend in core/time, and timing behaviour that can only be verified per machine. *Fix:* Shape: move the sleep step behind a platform function in core/time (for example a sleep_windows.cpp / sleep_posix.cpp pair selected in time/CMakeLists.txt). On Windows, use a thread_local CreateWaitableTimerExW(..., CREATE_WAITABLE_TIMER_HIGH_RESOLUTION, TIMER_ALL_ACCESS) with SetWaitableTimerEx (relative 100 ns units) and WaitForSingleObject. Fall back to Sleep, or to timeBeginPeriod(1)/timeEndPeriod(1) around the wait, when the flag is unsupported. Keep the spin window, possibly re-tuned. Risks: process-global timer resolution costs power if timeBeginPeriod is chosen; on older Windows the waitable-timer path silently degrades. An overshoot test is flaky on loaded CI unless it uses a median and a generous bound. *Test:* tests/core/time/test_clock.cpp: "clock - steady sleep_until overshoots the deadline by less than a scheduler tick". Run 20 sleep_until calls of 5 ms through the sleeping path and require the median overshoot to be below 3 ms. This also covers F254.
- **F235** MINOR Without a job object the timeout / destructor can still hang forever on a grandchild holding the pipe (`engine/src/core/system/process_windows.cpp:479`). Still present. When job creation or assignment fails (process_windows.cpp:664-686), terminate() only kills the direct child (497-509). wait() still calls join_readers() unconditionally after a timeout (471-479), and so does ~state() (412-420). The readers block in ReadFile while a surviving grandchild holds the pipe, which breaks the documented timeout bound and process.h:119-120 ("never blocks on a grandchild"). The fix changes reader-thread cancellation and synchronisation, so it is not SMALL. *Fix:* Shape: when the readers must be abandoned (after the post-deadline terminate() in wait(), and in ~state() when no job exists), cancel the blocked synchronous reads before joining. Either loop on CancelIoEx(pipeHandle, nullptr) for each read end until mReadersDone completes, or use CancelSynchronousIo on the reader threads' native handles. drain_pipe must treat ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED as the end of the stream. Risks and hard parts: CancelSynchronousIo is a no-op if it lands before the thread enters ReadFile, so it needs a retry loop against the latch; cancelling must not race with handle closure in unique_handle; output still buffered in the pipe is lost, which must be documented together with timedOut. The no-timeout wait() without a job is documented to block, so only the deadline and destructor paths change. Testing needs a test-only seam to suppress job assignment, because a real job-creation failure cannot be produced on demand. *Test:* tests/core/system/test_process.cpp: "process - without a job object the timeout still returns while a grandchild holds the output". Use spawn-sleeper with timeoutSeconds 0.5 through a test seam that skips job assignment, and check that wait() returns timedOut within a few seconds. Add a destructor variant: "process - without a job object destroying a process whose grandchild holds the output returns promptly".
- **F247** MINOR value::operator== is quadratic for objects whose keys are in a different order (`engine/src/core/json/value.cpp:236`). Still present: on the non-lockstep path structurally_equal (value.cpp:231-237) calls nth_member(rhs, key, earlier_occurrences(lhs, key, position)), and both helpers (113-142) rescan the object, so the comparison is O(n^2). The README (lines 57-62) documents the 'nothing is sorted' choice. Replacing it means rewriting the duplicate-key pairing algorithm with side storage kept in sync with the explicit stack, which is beyond a small, low-risk edit. *Fix:* Shape: when enter() sees two objects without the same key sequence, collect their key nodes into two vectors and stable_sort each by key bytes. Compare the key sequences pairwise (return false on any mismatch), then push the (left value, right value) pairs into a flat side buffer. The frame records [begin, end) of its pairs and truncates the buffer when it pops (LIFO matches the stack). Remove earlier_occurrences/nth_member and update README lines 60-62. Risks: keeping the n-th occurrence pairing for duplicate keys identical (the stable sort gives exactly that), invalidating 'top' references when the side buffer grows, and allocation behaviour of operator== (one allocation per non-lockstep object). *Test:* tests/core/json/test_value.cpp: "value - objects with 20000 members in reverse order compare equal quickly" plus "value - duplicate keys still pair by occurrence when member order differs".
- **F249** MINOR Reflection JSON builds an error-path string for every field and array element on the success path (`engine/src/core/reflect/json_conversion.cpp:659`). Still present: write_struct builds `join_path(path, field.name)` for every field (json_conversion.cpp:659), the read side does the same at 690, and the array loops format `std::format("{}[{}]", path, index)` per element (474, 625), all on the success path. Removing this means re-plumbing the path parameter through about ten recursive functions (write_value, read_value, write_struct, read_struct, read_transform, with_path, the unknown-member list and the field_ref entry points at 944-953): a broad refactor of a 950-line file, not a local edit. *Fix:* Shape: introduce `struct path_node { const path_node *parent; std::string_view name; usize index; bool isIndex; }` on the stack. Pass `const path_node *` down instead of `const std::string &`, and materialise the dotted string only in with_path() and when pushing to 'unknown' (a walk to the root, then a reverse join). The public write_field/read_field paths (944-953) create a root node from ref.path or ref.field->name. Risks: keeping error messages byte-identical (tests pin 'field 'parts[1].weight': ...'), and lifetime of string_views into member keys while recursing. Benefit: zero per-field allocations on save and snapshot. *Test:* tests/core/reflect/test_json_conversion.cpp: "json_conversion - a successful to_json allocates nothing per field" (allocation_scope around to_json of a struct with N fields vs one with 2N fields into a pre-reserved writer; equal allocation counts), plus the existing error-path message tests unchanged.
- **F251** MINOR Every log line copies the sink vector under the logger mutex (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:94`): same as F581.
- **F254** MINOR steady_clock::sleep_until is only tested for 'not early', never for 'not late' (`tests/core/time/test_clock.cpp:239`): same as F230.
- **F581** MINOR Every log line copies the sink vector (heap allocation plus shared_ptr refcount traffic) under the global logger mutex (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:357`). Still present: snapshot_sinks (log.cpp:85-96) copies std::vector<std::shared_ptr<log_sink>> under logger.mutex for every log::write (line 357) and log::flush (line 367). That is one heap allocation plus a ref-count round trip per sink per message, on every thread. The fix changes how the logger shares its sink list across threads (a synchronisation design change), and the reentrancy guarantee (sinks may log or add/remove sinks inside write, README:31-32) must survive it, so it is not SMALL under the strict rules. *Fix:* Shape: hold the sinks as `std::shared_ptr<const std::vector<std::shared_ptr<log_sink>>>` in logger_state. add_sink/remove_sink/clear_sinks build a new vector from the current one and swap the pointer under the mutex (copy-on-write). snapshot_sinks copies one shared_ptr (and the console shared_ptr) under the mutex, with no allocation. Alternatively use std::atomic<std::shared_ptr<...>> to drop the lock from the hot path, but check its lock-freedom and availability on libc++ first. Risks: keep sinks alive while a snapshot is iterating (shared ownership handles that); the lazy console creation in snapshot_sinks (log.cpp:89-91) allocates on first use, so create it eagerly or keep it out of the measured path; per-message std::format and format_log_line still allocate, so the gain is limited to the sink copy and the README should say so. *Test:* tests/core/foundation/test_log.cpp: "log - delivering a message to registered sinks allocates nothing". Disable the console, register a sink whose write only increments a counter, then wrap a `log::write(LOG_TEST, log_level::INFO, __FILE__, __LINE__, "fixed")` call in CONJURE_CHECK_NO_ALLOCATIONS. Keep "log - a sink may log and change the sink list from inside write" passing.
- **F602** MINOR ANSI colour escapes are written to redirected output (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:174`): same as F207.
- **F722** MINOR ANSI colour escapes are written when stdout/stderr is redirected to a file or pipe (`engine/src/core/foundation/log.cpp:210`): same as F207.
- **F749** MINOR Tools are started by bare name through CreateProcessW(nullptr, ...): the editor's directory and current directory are searched before PATH (`engine/src/core/system/process_windows.cpp:635`). Still present. process_windows.cpp:635-646 calls CreateProcessW(nullptr, commandLine) with the bare name as the first token (197-199), so Windows searches the application directory and then the current directory before PATH. The editor's "dotnet"/"cmake" defaults can therefore run an executable shipped inside a project opened as CWD. A correct fix re-implements the Windows executable search for every tool launch (scripting, modules, export). That is security-sensitive and involves several search-order choices the header only partly settles (process.h:34-36 promises PATH plus the parent's directory), so it is not SMALL. *Fix:* Shape: when desc.executable has no separator and no drive prefix, resolve it explicitly in process_windows.cpp. Candidates are CreateProcessW's order minus the current directory: the directory of GetModuleFileNameW(nullptr), then GetSystemDirectoryW, the Windows directory's System folder and GetWindowsDirectoryW (or only PATH, if the owner wants the header taken literally), then each absolute entry of the parent's PATH. Strip quotes, and skip empty and relative entries such as '.'. Append ".exe" when the name has no extension; the first regular file wins. Pass it as lpApplicationName and keep argv[0] as the caller wrote it. Nothing found returns NOT_FOUND. Hard parts: matching extension and trailing-dot rules exactly, deciding whether the system directories stay, keeping error codes stable for every caller, and interaction with F234's .bat/.cmd refusal (a resolved shim must also be refused). Document the search order in process.h and the README. *Test:* tests/core/system/test_process.cpp: "process - a bare name is not resolved against the current directory on Windows". Copy conjure_process_child into a temp directory as conjure_cwd_only_tool.exe, use scoped_current_path into it, and expect NOT_FOUND from start("conjure_cwd_only_tool"). Add "process - a bare name is still found next to the running executable", which starts conjure_process_child by bare name.

### renderer (12)

- **F047** MAJOR A view with a sub-viewport gets wrong light culling, SSAO and screen sprites (NDC is computed against the target, not the viewport) (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:1108`). Still present: renderer.cpp:1108-1113 writes the target size into constants.viewportSize while the projection aspect uses stateOfView.viewport (renderer.cpp:869-871), and light_cull.hlsl:21/47, ssao_gtao.hlsl:51/68, ssao_blur.hlsl:19, sprite.hlsl:27 and forward_pbr.hlsl:190 derive NDC/uv from the target size. Fixing it changes the view constant buffer layout and several shaders, plus tile-count and dispatch sizing, and can only be verified on a real device. *Fix:* Add viewport origin and size to gpu::view_constants (keep the 1024 B budget and the HLSL mirror in gpu_types.hlsli in sync, update the layout static_asserts), write them from stateOfView.viewport, and compute NDC as (pixel - origin) / viewportSize in light_cull, ssao_gtao, ssao_blur and sprite.hlsl; size light-cull tiles and the SSAO half-resolution dispatch from the viewport rather than the target (renderer.cpp:1094-1098), and offset the dispatch/thread ids by the viewport origin (or render compute outputs in viewport-local space and sample with the origin offset in forward_pbr). Risks: transient textures are target-sized, so every reader must agree on local vs target pixel space; the null device cannot catch mistakes. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: '[gpu] renderer - split-screen views cull lights, run SSAO and place screen sprites relative to their own viewport' (two half-width views on one target, a point light and a HUD sprite at pixel (10, 10) checked by read_back in each half)
- **F048** MAJOR Tiled light culling (and the GTAO radius) assume a perspective camera: orthographic views lose local lights (`engine/shaders/light_cull.hlsl:28`). Still present: light_cull.hlsl:26-31 builds side planes through the view origin (w = 0) from far-plane corners only (:66-74), which is a tile frustum only under perspective. ssao_gtao.hlsl:68 divides the projected radius by view depth. The fix is compute-shader plane math with reverse-Z subtleties in two shaders and needs an orthographic GPU test, so it is not a quick edit. *Fix:* Light culling: for each side, take the tile edge's corner pair at the near depth (1.0 under reverse-Z) and one corner at a finite far depth, build the plane from the two edge directions, and set w = -dot(normal, nearCorner) so the plane is correct for both projections. Avoid depth 0.0 under an infinite reverse-Z perspective: view.w goes to 0 there (view_position clamps to 1e-6), which ruins precision. Keep the normals pointing inward, matching sphere_inside at :33-35. GTAO: when gView.clipFromView[3][3] == 1 (orthographic), use projectedScale = clipFromView[1][1] * viewportSize.y * 0.5 with no depth divide. Verify that the perspective results are unchanged with the existing [gpu] cases. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: 'regression - a point light near an orthographic camera lights the floor under it'. Setup: orthographic camera (height 20, far 100) and a range-1 point light at view (5, 0, -2) over a floor; the floor pixel under the light must be brighter than an unlit floor pixel.
- **F049** MAJOR Mirrored (negative-scale) instances are drawn inside-out (`engine/src/renderer/pipeline_cache.cpp:420`). Still present: pipeline_cache.cpp:420-421 sets frontCounterClockwise = true with back culling for every mesh pipeline, only a doubleSided variant exists (pipeline_cache.cpp:522-533), and gpu_types.cpp:33-37 uses the determinant only for the singular fallback, so a negative-determinant world matrix is culled inside-out. The fix needs a new pipeline variant set, a bit in a 64-bit draw key that is already fully used ([variant:8][material:22][mesh:22][submesh:4][depth:8]), an instance flag and a shader change for the tangent sign, across CPU and GPU code. *Fix:* Per instance compute det(upper3x3) < 0 in build_region (renderer.cpp:600-625) and store a 'mirrored' flag on draw_item/draw_batch next to doubleSided; make draw_list_builder refuse to merge mirrored with unmirrored items and fold the bit into the key (steal one bit, e.g. shrink depthBucket or the variant field, and update MAX_DRAW_KEY_* and the README key description). In pipeline_cache create a third mesh pipeline per variant with frontCounterClockwise = false (or cullMode Front) and pick it in pass_shared.cpp:170 for mirrored batches, including the shadow depth variants. Pass INSTANCE_FLAG_MIRRORED through gpu::make_instance flags and negate the bitangent sign in forward_pbr.hlsl when set. Risks: key layout change touches sorting/batching invariants and the 'overflow can never merge' guarantee; pipeline count grows by a third (startup time, test_pipeline_cache counts); must verify on a real device because the null device does not rasterise. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_draw_list_builder.cpp: 'draw_list_builder - mirrored items never batch with unmirrored ones'; tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: '[gpu] renderer - an instance scaled by (-1, 1, 1) renders its front faces'
- **F051** MAJOR A mesh with more than 16 submeshes asserts when drawn; more than 8 glTF primitives silently share the last material (`engine/src/renderer/draw_list_builder.cpp:59`). Still present. draw_list_builder.cpp:59 asserts submesh < 16 (the 4-bit key field, draw_list_builder.h:28). mesh_asset accepts up to 65536 submeshes (mesh_asset.cpp:22, :224), and the glTF importer emits one submesh per primitive with no limit. Primitives beyond the 8th are clamped to slot 7 (gltf_importer.cpp:452-454), and node materials record only 8 (:696-699). The fix needs a choice between widening the sort key, splitting meshes, or raising MAX_MATERIAL_SLOTS (a .cmodel/model_node format change). *Fix:* Pick one end-to-end limit. (1) Widen the submesh field in the opaque key by taking bits from the 8-bit depth field or the 22-bit indices; this changes sort precision and README:115-118. Or (2) make the glTF importer split a mesh with more than MAX_DRAW_KEY_SUBMESH primitives, or more than MAX_MATERIAL_SLOTS distinct materials, into several mesh_assets and sibling model nodes, each within both limits; this keeps the key and file formats but changes importer output and sub-asset numbering, so GLTF_IMPORTER_VERSION must be bumped. In either case, make mesh_asset::from_geometry and parse_blob reject submesh counts above the renderer's limit with a clear error, so no blob reaches the assert, and drop or keep the assert as the last line of defence. Risks: sort-key regressions, stable sub-asset ids, and model/prefab conversion in the engine. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_gltf_importer.cpp "regression - a glTF mesh with 17 primitives imports within the draw-key and material-slot limits" (built with gltf_test_builder: every primitive keeps its own material and the result draws without asserting). Change test_draw_list_builder.cpp:243 to pin the import-time limit.
- **F054** MAJOR Opaque (SOLID) materials write the base colour alpha into the frame, so render targets and read_back get translucent pixels (`engine/shaders/forward_pbr.hlsl:281`). Still present: forward_pbr.hlsl:213-218 forces alpha to 1 only under ALPHA_MASK, :281 returns baseColour.a for the SOLID/BLEND permutation, and tonemap.hlsl:38 copies hdr.a. The fix needs either a new shader permutation or per-pipeline colour write masks. It also needs a decision on what LDR alpha means (coverage for a transparent background, or always opaque), which 02_renderer.md does not settle; the editor viewport shows the target through ImGui's premultiplied pipeline. *Fix:* 1. Make opaque output alpha 1, by either: - an ALPHA_BLEND define axis for forward_pbr ps_main. The lists in engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt:32, shader_asset.cpp and pipeline_cache.cpp must stay in step. - or an RGB-only write mask on the SOLID/MASK forward pipelines with the HDR alpha cleared to 1. 2. Decide the background and blended alpha, and make tonemap write 1 when the view is not composited (or always). Risks: - pipeline cache keys and permutation counts; - sprites, skybox and blended meshes must agree on the alpha convention; - existing [gpu] checks such as centre.a == 255. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: 'regression - an opaque material whose base colour alpha is 0.5 reads back with alpha 255'. Also assert the empty background alpha according to the chosen convention.
- **F374** MAJOR The back buffer is never cleared: zero views or a partial viewport present undefined/stale pixels (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:1337`). Still present: render() (renderer.cpp:1337-1340) never clears the acquired back buffer or a view's target; tonemap/blit only cover the view viewport, and test_renderer_null_frame.cpp:488 pins 'zero views is a no-op frame'. Adding clears changes frame ordering and resource states of the swapchain image and conflicts with a pinned test, and needs a choice of what a partial viewport's remainder shows. *Fix:* After acquire, when a swapchain exists, record a clear of the back buffer to opaque black (commandList->clearTextureFloat with the back buffer in RenderTarget state, before any view graph) so zero-view frames and letterbox bars are defined; for render targets, clear the target on its first use in the frame when the view's viewport does not cover it (track per-target 'cleared this frame'). Keep ImGui after the clear. Risks: the clear must happen before the graph imports the back buffer (state tracking, keepInitialState Present), costs bandwidth every frame, and changes the no-op contract and null_device_stats clear counts in several tests. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_null_frame.cpp: 'null_frame - a frame without views clears the back buffer once' (null_device_stats clears == 1, replacing the no-op assertion); tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: '[gpu] renderer - pixels outside a sub-viewport read back black'
- **F373** MINOR release_* read frameIndex from any thread while render() increments it (data race) (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:366`). Still present: renderer.cpp:366-380 read the plain `u64 frameIndex` (renderer_impl.h:48) from any thread (renderer.h:31-33, 146-147 'Safe from any thread') while render() does `++state.frameIndex` at renderer.cpp:1299, a formal data race exercised by 'regression - uploads and releases from jobs while render() culls' (test_renderer_null_frame.cpp:656). It is a concurrency change with two materially different fixes (atomic counter vs burial stamp taken under the cache mutex), so by the triage rules it is not SMALL. *Fix:* Preferred: stop passing the frame index into release_*: gpu_resource_cache keeps `u64 mCurrentFrame` written under mMutex at the top of flush_uploads (which already receives frameIndex) and read under the same lock in release_mesh/texture/material/environment (gpu_resource_cache.cpp:528-584), so a release after frame N's flush is buried at N and one before it at N-1, both ordered against resolve_* by the mutex; renderer::release_* then no longer touch mImpl->frameIndex. Alternative: make renderer_state::frameIndex std::atomic<u64> (relaxed load in release_*, fetch_add in render, .load() where it is formatted in logs and in frame_count()). Risk: the graveyard delay semantics (collect expires when frameIndex - burial > releaseDelay) must stay conservative relative to frames still in flight; frame_count() is also read without synchronisation and needs the same treatment or a 'render thread only' doc. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_gpu_resource_cache.cpp: 'gpu_resource_cache - a release is buried at the frame of the last flush'; keep 'regression - uploads and releases from jobs while render() culls on the same job system [parallel]' and run it under a clang TSan build
- **F376** MINOR A demoted shadow tile is never promoted again, and settled lights outrank more important newcomers (`engine/src/renderer/shadows/shadow_atlas_allocator.cpp:199`). Still present: pass 1 (shadow_atlas_allocator.cpp:194-231) keeps every unchanged request at its previous grantedSize before pass 2 serves the rest by importance, so a demoted light is never promoted and a more important newcomer can be dropped while less important settled lights keep their tiles, contradicting 02_renderer.md:282-285 ('granted while space remains ... least important render unshadowed', :800). Fixing it means rebalancing tile stability against importance with an eviction policy in the allocator. *Fix:* Pass 1: only keep a previous allocation when previous->grantedSize == std::clamp<u32>(clamp_shadow_resolution(request.size), mMinTile, mMaxTile), so demoted lights retry their full size in pass 2. Pass 2: when a request (requests are in importance order, so its index is its rank) cannot be granted even at 256, evict the settled allocation with the highest request index greater than the current one (release its nodes in the quadtree, mark it unsettled, decrement mReused) and retry; evicted lights are then served later in pass 2 at whatever fits. Risks: tile churn when importance oscillates (shadow coordinates move frame to frame), quadtree node release must undo splits correctly, the zero-allocation steady-state test (test_shadow_atlas_allocator.cpp:279) must keep passing. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_shadow_atlas_allocator.cpp: 'shadow_atlas_allocator - a demoted light regains its requested size once space frees up' and 'shadow_atlas_allocator - a more important newcomer evicts the least important settled light when the atlas is full'
- **F388** MINOR Mip generation averages RGB without alpha weighting (dark fringes on cut-out sprites) and ignores the last row/column of odd sizes (`engine/src/renderer/import/mip_generator.cpp:67`). Still present: downsample_rgba8 averages RGB and alpha independently (mip_generator.cpp:63-79), and for odd sizes the 2x2 footprint at x*2 and x*2+1 never reads the last column or row (:50-51, :64-66). Fixing it changes every cooked texture. It also needs decisions the design does not make: which textures get alpha-weighted (alpha can be data in packed linear textures), and which odd-size kernel to use. The importer versions must be bumped to re-cook. *Fix:* Recommended shape: for sRGB colour textures only, weight the linear RGB by alpha and divide by the alpha sum, falling back to the plain average when the alpha sum is 0; linear/data textures keep plain averaging. For an odd source dimension, use a 3-tap footprint per output texel with weights proportional to box coverage (for output n of N from a source of 2N+1: (N-n)/(2N+1), N/(2N+1), (n+1)/(2N+1)) and apply it separably. Bump TEXTURE_IMPORTER_VERSION and GLTF_IMPORTER_VERSION. Risks: changes all cooked bytes and pinned expectations ('box filter of a checker is mid-grey', 02_renderer.md:913), CPU cost, and the alpha-semantics decision. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_texture_asset.cpp "texture_asset - mips of a cut-out sprite keep their colour at transparent edges" (white opaque texels next to transparent black give a white, partially transparent mip 1, not grey) and "texture_asset - odd sizes sample the last row and column" (a 3x3 image whose last column is red contributes red to mip 1).
- **F390** MINOR One unsupported texture fails the whole glTF import (`engine/src/renderer/import/gltf_importer.cpp:824`). Still present: a texture without a plain image fails the whole import (gltf_importer.cpp:824-826), and so does a decode failure (:829-837). The texture list is index-aligned with glTF texture indices, the material slot indices (import_material :562) and the stable sub-asset paths "texture/N" (:1010-1018). Dropping a texture therefore needs a choice between a placeholder texture and unbound slots, which the design does not settle. *Fix:* Recommended shape: on a missing image (including KHR_texture_basisu / EXT_texture_webp sources) or a decode failure, push a warning naming the texture and the reason. Push a 1x1 placeholder (texture_asset::solid_colour, white, in the texture's colour space) into result.textures so "texture/N" ids stay stable, and record the index in a failed-texture set passed to import_material. There, slots referencing a failed texture stay INVALID_INDEX, so the material falls back to its factors; a white placeholder would be wrong for normal maps. Risks: index alignment across textures, materials and sub-asset ids, a possible new field on gltf_import_result, and whether to bump GLTF_IMPORTER_VERSION so previously failing sources re-import. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_gltf_importer.cpp "gltf_importer - an unsupported or undecodable texture imports with a warning and an unbound slot": a model whose texture 0 has only an EXT_texture_webp source and whose texture 1 is corrupt PNG bytes imports successfully. It has two warnings, textures.size() == 2, and the materials using them have INVALID_INDEX in those slots.
- **F406** MINOR GPU coverage misses every path where this sweep found a defect (`tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp:91`). Still present: test_renderer_gpu.cpp has five [gpu] cases (lines 91, 167, 235, 291, 353), all one full-target perspective view into a render target; none drives a swapchain, a sub-viewport, mirrored instances, sprites, an orthographic camera with local lights or the BRDF LUT. It is a large test effort, needs a hidden-window WARP swapchain fixture, and most new cases would fail until the corresponding defects (F044, F047, F049, F374 and the shader findings) are fixed. *Fix:* Add a [gpu] fixture that creates a hidden platform window and a WARP swapchain; then add one analytic read_back case per path, landing each with its fix: swapchain render/resize/render (F044), split-screen viewports (F047), mirrored instance (F049), cleared bars and zero views (F374), world and screen sprites, orthographic camera with a point light, blended-only scene over a sky, BRDF LUT texel values, alpha in the target, out-of-range HDR texels, a mesh with more than 16 submeshes. Risk: CI time and WARP/driver variance; keep tolerances analytic. *Test:* tests/renderer/test_renderer_gpu.cpp: '[gpu] renderer - rendering, resizing the swapchain and rendering again succeeds' first, then one '[gpu] renderer - <path> ...' case per listed path
- **F724** MINOR Compiler ifdefs and warning suppression in a test file (`tests/renderer/test_tonemap_reference.cpp:23`). Still present: test_tonemap_reference.cpp:23-49 uses raw _MSC_VER/__clang__/__GNUC__ blocks to disable conversion, double-promotion and missing-prototype warnings around the HLSL include, the only warning suppression in engine/tests (AGENTS.md rules 6 and 10). The proper fix edits the production shader (about a hundred unsuffixed literals in tonemap.hlsli) and must be proven warning-free on MSVC, clang and GCC, which cannot all be built on this Windows host. *Fix:* Make the shader compile cleanly natively instead of silencing it: add `f` suffixes to every literal in tonemap.hlsli (valid HLSL, same DXC output), give its functions forward declarations or wrap the include so clang's -Wmissing-prototypes does not fire (e.g. declare prototypes at the top of tonemap.hlsli, which HLSL accepts), adjust hlsl_shim.h overloads if any mixed float/double call remains, then delete the pragma blocks. Fallback if a warning cannot be fixed in the shader: CONJURE_WARNINGS_PUSH/POP macros in platform_detect.h (core, multi). Risks: shader output must stay bit-identical (check the golden tonemap [gpu] case), and clang/GCC builds need CI to verify. *Test:* none: the existing tonemap_reference cases must still compile warning-free under /W4 /WX, -Wall -Wextra -Werror on MSVC, clang and GCC

### assets (9)

- **F025** MAJOR register_type / unregister_type rebuild the lookup tables while load jobs read them (`engine/src/assets/asset_type_registry.cpp:130`). Still present: register_type and unregister_type call rebuild_index() (asset_type_registry.cpp:64-65, :90-91), which clears and refills mIndexById and mView (:130-137) on the main thread. Load jobs meanwhile call find_loader() with no lock on workers (asset_manager.cpp:254 -> load_pipeline.cpp:40). Module attach/detach can register or unregister types between frames while loads are in flight. This is a concurrency fix, so CHALLENGING by rule. *Fix:* Shape: guard mTypes, mIndexById and mView with a std::shared_mutex: shared in find()/find_loader()/types(), unique in register_type/unregister_type around the mutation and rebuild_index. Run the hooks outside the unique lock so isInUse/onRegistered can call find(). types() returns a span over mView, which a lock cannot protect after return. Either document it as main-thread only or return a copy. Alternative: have the asset_manager hooks drain in-flight jobs (wait_for_jobs + drain_completions) before the tables change, which avoids a lock on the hot find path but stalls module reloads. Risks: find_loader runs on every load job, so the lock must stay cheap; pointers returned by find() must outlive a concurrent unregister (heap-stable already, and unregister is refused while that type is in use). Needs a TSan test. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_manager_load.cpp: "asset_type_registry - registering and unregistering another type while loads are in flight is race-free [parallel]", run under the TSan preset.
- **F133** MAJOR Load jobs read asset_type_registry unlocked while the main thread registers/unregisters types (`engine/src/assets/load_pipeline.cpp:40`): same as F025.
- **F185** MAJOR Pack entries' typeVersion is never compared with the registered asset type version (and pack header flags/padding are unhashed and unchecked) (`engine/src/assets/asset_source.cpp:181`). Still present: pack_asset_source::read (asset_source.cpp:181-187) and load_cpu_stage (load_pipeline.cpp:27-53) never compare the blob's typeVersion with the registered type's version, although D8 makes type version part of validity. Only asset_cache::is_valid (asset_cache.cpp:132) checks it. parse_tables also reads header flags and ignores the 8 padding bytes (asset_pack.cpp:463-478) without validating them. The fix needs choices the design does not settle: fail the entry or reject the whole pack, a new error code, and where to check. Putting the check in the shared load path also affects blobs hand-built in other modules' tests (scene, renderer, audio, bindings fixtures set typeVersion=1). *Fix:* Shape: in detail::load_cpu_stage, after find_loader, look up `types.find(stage.header.type)->version`. When it differs, fail with a new asset_error_code (for example TYPE_VERSION_MISMATCH, "cooked with type version X, runtime has Y") added to assets_error.h/.cpp. This covers pack and memory sources; cache blobs are already validated. Alternatively, check only in the pack path by giving pack_asset_source access to the registry, which is a constructor change for assets_subsystem.cpp. In parse_tables, reject `mHeader.flags != 0` and non-zero padding bytes 56..63 as PACK_CORRUPT. Risks: hand-built blobs in other modules' tests may carry versions that differ from what they register (audit tests/scene, tests/renderer, tests/audio, tests/physics, tests/bindings); also add a row to the design failure table. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_pack.cpp: "pack_reader - non-zero header flags or padding are PACK_CORRUPT". tests/assets/test_asset_manager_load.cpp: "load - a blob cooked with another type version fails with TYPE_VERSION_MISMATCH".
- **F299** MAJOR A source moved outside the editor (with its .meta) is not picked up until the next full scan (`engine/src/assets/hot_reload.cpp:179`). Still present: when a source is moved outside the editor, the ADDED path calls add_file (hot_reload.cpp:168-191). register_source then refuses a meta whose uuid already has a record with DUPLICATE_UUID (asset_database.cpp:1061-1069), and the later REMOVED of the old path marks the record MISSING_SOURCE (hot_reload.cpp:140-152). The fix changes record identity handling in the database: re-pointing a record, its sub-asset records, the side-file owners and the indexes. It also depends on the watcher's within-pass ordering (changes are sorted by path, so ADDED and REMOVED can come in either order). That is more than a local fix. *Fix:* Shape: in register_source, when meta.id already has a record whose source file no longer exists (mFileSystem->exists(file_path_for(old)) is false) or whose status is MISSING_SOURCE, treat the add as a move. Update the record's mountIndex and relativePath (and those of its sub-asset children), reset the status to OK, rebuild the indexes and graphs, save the snapshot, and return the existing id instead of DUPLICATE_UUID; reuse the move-by-uuid logic scan() already has. hot_reload then sees no record at the old path on REMOVED, or should skip mark_source_missing when the record's path no longer matches the removed path. Risks: a true duplicate (a copied file with its meta while the original still exists) must stay DUPLICATE_UUID; moves across mounts; side files of the moved source; sub-asset paths. *Test:* tests/assets/test_hot_reload.cpp: "hot_reload - a source moved with its meta outside the editor keeps its uuid and stays loadable" (rename textures/a.ta and its .meta to art/a.ta, tick(), CHECK the record path is updated, the status is OK and a fresh load succeeds). tests/assets/test_asset_database.cpp: "asset_database - add_file of a copied source with an existing uuid is still DUPLICATE_UUID".
- **F301** MINOR recompute_stale() reads a blob header for every asset on every single-record edit, and import() always rewrites db.bin and import_log.txt (`engine/src/assets/asset_database.cpp:1894`). Still present. recompute_stale() probes the cache header of every importable record (asset_database.cpp:1956-1968) and runs after every single-record edit (register_source :1093, delete_asset :1222, write_meta :1317, set_source_status :1689, scan, import). import() writes import_log.txt and db.bin unconditionally (:752-759). Making staleness incremental is a redesign of a state the editor, cooker and hot reload all rely on, and it has non-local inputs: importer versions and type versions change when importers or types are registered, and today the full recompute picks that up implicitly. *Fix:* Keep mStale as a set and add `recompute_stale(std::span<const asset_id> primaries)`, which re-probes only the given primaries (with their children) and patches the set. Call it from register_source, write_meta, set_source_status and import (for the imported primaries). delete_asset just erases from the set. Keep the full recompute for scan(), load_snapshot() and any change of importer or type registrations: this needs a hook from importer_registry and asset_type_registry to the database, or a cheap version counter compared before each operation. In import(), skip write_import_log and save_snapshot when sourceJobs is empty. Risks: the stale set drifting from the cache, the lock-free header probing (comment at :1895-1898), and the cook and hot reload paths depending on stale_ids(). *Test:* tests/assets/test_import_cache.cpp: "regression - write_meta re-checks only the edited source's blobs" (a counting file system or cache wrapper sees one header read, not N). "regression - import of nothing writes neither the log nor the snapshot". "regression - registering a newer importer version still marks its sources stale".
- **F315** MINOR Opening a pack reads and hashes the whole file; packing holds about four copies of the content in memory (`engine/src/assets/asset_pack.cpp:490`). Still present: parse_tables reads and hashes the entire file into one vector (asset_pack.cpp:488-498). build() keeps blobs, payloadArea, body and pack alive together (:176-241). add() and dedup are quadratic (:119-126, :182-188), and find_by_name is a linear string scan (:412-419). Fixing it means restructuring the writer into a streaming writer and changing the reader's open path; the 'hash only tables' variant would change what the format's content hash covers, so this is large and partly a format decision. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Reader: keep the format and hash the body in fixed chunks (for example 1 MiB) with an incremental FNV-1a over read_range. Only the tables (TOC, dependency table, string table) are kept in memory. Payloads are already re-verified per entry in read(). Dropping the full-body hash entirely would change the format's guarantee and needs a design call. (2) Reader: build a flat_hash_map<std::string, u32> from name to TOC index at open, for find_by_name. (3) Writer: keep a flat_hash_set<asset_id> for the duplicate check and a flat_hash_map<u64 hash, vector<placement>> for dedup. Stream the output: write a header placeholder, the tables and the padding, then each payload straight to a temporary file while hashing incrementally, then patch the header and rename. This needs a streaming write API on core::vfs (open_write/append), which may cross into core. Risks: byte-identical determinism of packs (export roundtrip tests), the atomic tmp+rename semantics, and memory_file_system support. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_pack.cpp: "pack_reader - open verifies the content hash in chunks and still rejects a flipped payload byte", "pack_writer - many entries build in linear time and the bytes equal the previous layout" and "pack_reader - find_by_name uses the name index". Also keep test_export_roundtrip byte-identical.
- **F327** MINOR Missing asset tests for behaviour that matters; every test runs on memory_file_system only (`tests/assets/test_hot_reload.cpp:369`). Still true: test_hot_reload.cpp:369-387 covers only the healing CACHE_MISS case, and no test exercises a dependency TIMEOUT followed by flush, unsubscribe during delivery, external moves, includeAll with NO_IMPORTER/MISSING_SOURCE records, closure membership changes, or native_file_system. The gap spans eight scenarios plus a new native-filesystem smoke suite. Several of those scenarios are expected to fail until defects in this and other batches are fixed, so it is large and cannot land on its own. *Fix:* Shape: add each listed case to its fixture. The tests for F016, F019, F304 and F299 come with those fixes. The remaining cases are stale_ids() after a rescan that drops a sub-asset (test_import_cache), a cook whose closure changes membership at constant size and includeAll with NO_IMPORTER/MISSING_SOURCE records (test_asset_cooker), and a file re-created or renamed onto a known path between scans (test_asset_database). Add tests/assets/test_assets_native_fs.cpp, which runs scan, import, hot reload (watcher scan_now) and cook over a conjure::testing temp_directory with native_file_system (case-insensitive paths, mtime granularity, rename over an existing file), register it in tests/assets/CMakeLists.txt, and update the README Testing section. Risks: these tests may expose defects owned by other findings; native-filesystem timing needs tolerance for mtime granularity. *Test:* tests/assets/test_assets_native_fs.cpp: "assets native fs - scan, import, hot reload and cook round trip over a temp directory", plus the per-fixture cases named above (for example "asset_cooker - includeAll skips NO_IMPORTER and MISSING_SOURCE records").
- **F611** MINOR Cook is quadratic in the asset count and holds about four copies of the pack in memory (`engine/src/assets/asset_cooker.cpp:129`). Still present: a linear std::find over `stale` for each closure member (asset_cooker.cpp:129), a linear duplicate-id scan on every pack_writer::add (asset_pack.cpp:119-126), and a linear payload de-duplication scan (asset_pack.cpp:182-188). build() also copies all payloads into payloadArea, then body, then pack (asset_pack.cpp:197, 216, 241), while mEntries still holds them. Removing the quadratics is easy; cutting peak memory means restructuring pack assembly, which must stay byte-identical, and true streaming needs a vfs streaming writer that core does not offer (file_system.h has only whole-buffer writes). *Fix:* (1) asset_cooker.cpp: build a core::flat_hash_set<asset_id> from stale_ids() once per pass and test membership there. (2) pack_writer: keep a private flat_hash_set<asset_id> of added ids for add(). (3) build(): de-duplicate through a flat_hash_map<u64 payloadHash, small list of placed indices>, comparing bytes only on a hash hit. (4) Compute the full layout first (TOC, dependency table, strings, aligned payload offsets), then write everything into one pack buffer reserved to its exact size: header placeholder, body written in place, contentHash computed over the body span, header patched last. That drops payloadArea and body and brings peak memory to about 2x the payload. Full streaming (about 1x) needs a writable-stream API in core::vfs and is a separate cross-area item. Risks: deterministic output is a contract (the 'two cooks ... byte-identical' and pack format tests), and the 16-byte alignment padding and dedup order must be reproduced exactly. *Test:* tests/assets/test_asset_pack.cpp: "pack_writer - a rebuilt pack is byte-identical to the reference layout with duplicated payloads" (golden bytes for a small set with duplicates). tests/assets/test_asset_cooker.cpp: "asset_cooker - a cook of 5,000 small assets finishes without quadratic passes" (time-bounded or a benchmark in tests/benchmarks).
- **F733** MINOR Pack writer holds about three copies of the pack in memory and is quadratic in entry count; the reader loads the whole pack to hash it on open (`engine/src/assets/asset_pack.cpp:204`): same as F315.

### physics (9)

- **F058** MAJOR Edits of collider and physics_material fields on a live body are never detected; collider-only (implicit static) bodies never follow their transform (`engine/src/physics/physics_world.cpp:287`). Still present: the only change queries are rigid_body+local_transform and character_controller+local_transform (physics_world.cpp:287-298). Collider and physics_material columns are observed only for ADDED/REMOVED (physics_world_bodies.cpp:103-109, 134-139), and implicit-static (collider-only) rows match neither query. Field edits therefore never reach Jolt, and a moved collider-only root never teleports. A fix needs new persistent change queries, per-row value diffs (change detection is per chunk column) and a teleport path for implicit statics, all within the P15 and physics.sync_10k budgets. *Fix:* Shape: build persistent changed-filter queries over the five collider ids and physics_material, plus one collider-only query (any collider, without rigid_body and character_controller) whose changed(local_transform) visits implicit-static rows for the existing teleport logic. For each visited row, compare against a per-row 64-bit hash or copy of the collider and material data stored in body_record. mark_rebuild(SHAPE) on a collider diff (isTrigger included, since it changes the sensor flag) and PROPERTIES on a material diff, so unrelated writes in the same chunk do not rebuild. Risks: query cost at 10k bodies, pending and asset-loading entities, interplay with hierarchy rows and the MIXED_TRIGGER rules, determinism. Document in docs/api anything that stays unsupported. *Test:* tests/physics/test_component_sync.cpp: 'component_sync - editing box_collider.halfExtents on a live body rebuilds its shape', 'component_sync - editing physics_material.friction on a live body reaches Jolt', 'component_sync - moving a collider-only static entity's local_transform teleports its body'
- **F059** MAJOR A single move_kinematic leaves the body flying with the MoveKinematic velocity forever (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_sync.cpp:446`). Still present: MOVE_KINEMATIC calls MoveKinematic once (physics_world_sync.cpp:446-455), which only sets the velocity that reaches the pose in one step. Nothing zeroes it later: the pull mirrors the 120 m/s into rigid_body (sync.cpp:532-535), so the push sees no diff and the body keeps flying. The fix needs state carried across pushes, a rule for which velocity wins when the same push also sets one (component edit or set_linear_velocity), and an allocation-free container. The docs half-describe move_kinematic as velocity driving (docs/api/physics/bodies-and-colliders.md:87-88). *Fix:* Shape: follow the header's promise ('towards the pose over the next fixed step') and stop at arrival. Keep a reserved std::vector<ecs::entity> of entities moved by MOVE_KINEMATIC in the previous push (reserve maxQueuedCommands) and a per-row flag set when a MOVE_KINEMATIC is applied this push. At the end of apply_commands, for each entity moved last push that got no MOVE_KINEMATIC, SET_LINEAR_VELOCITY or SET_ANGULAR_VELOCITY this push, and whose row is still KINEMATIC and not movesWithTransform: SetLinearAndAngularVelocity(zero) and zero row->mirror's velocities so the next component diff does not resurrect them. Then swap the vectors. Update physics_world.h and both API pages: the body stops at the pose unless move_kinematic is issued again. Risks: a velocity written into the component in the same push after a move (decide that a component edit wins, which means detecting it in visitBody), determinism, and the P15 allocation rule. *Test:* tests/physics/test_rigid_body.cpp: extend "rigid_body - move_kinematic reaches the pose over one step and is refused for dynamic bodies" with a second and third run_step after the move: CHECK position_of(plate).y stays Approx(2.0) and the pulled rigid_body.linearVelocity is zero. Add "rigid_body - move_kinematic issued every step drives a kinematic body smoothly" (10 consecutive moves of 0.1 m reach 1.0 m and stop).
- **F061** MAJOR An entity whose only collider can never be built is rebuilt and WARN-logged every fixed step, unthrottled (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_bodies.cpp:461`). Still present: create_body keeps the entity pending when built.shape == nullptr (physics_world_bodies.cpp:461-464). Every push re-runs build_shape, which logs unthrottled WARNs (shape_builder.cpp:242, 267, 324) and rebuilds a failed convex hull each step because failures are not cached (239-244). A fix needs a 'failed' state for pending entities distinct from 'waiting for an asset', retry triggers that do not exist today, and log-once bookkeeping across the shape builder and the pending list. *Fix:* Shape: keep a per-pending-entity failure record (a FAILED marker in rowOfEntity, or a side table keyed by entity) holding the reason and the collider/asset versions it failed with. Skip such entities in flush_pending_creates until a retry trigger fires: a body component ADDED/REMOVED (observers exist), an asset RELOADED (on_asset_reloaded must also walk pending failures, today it only walks rows), or a collider field edit (no observer today, see F058). Have build_shape report failures through a warned-once bit per entity instead of logging directly, and keep MIXED_TRIGGER_FLAGS while an asset is pending as a retry-each-step case without re-logging. Risks: interplay with F058's change detection, pending withdrawal, P15 (no per-step allocation). *Test:* tests/physics/test_colliders.cpp: 'colliders - an unbuildable mesh-only dynamic body warns once and is not rebuilt every step' (60 steps -> exactly one UNSUPPORTED_SHAPE_FOR_MOTION WARN; adding a sphere_collider afterwards still creates the body)
- **F063** MAJOR Contact budget overflow permanently corrupts the pair table: lost EXIT/ENTER, 'exactly once' no longer holds (`engine/src/physics/contact_collector.cpp:149`). Still present: contact_collector::record drops any kind once mRecorded reaches mCapacity (contact_collector.cpp:149-153), and collect_events refuses to track a new pair when activePairs is full (physics_world_contacts.cpp:249-253). A dropped REMOVED or ADDED therefore leaves activePairs permanently wrong (lost EXIT, STAY instead of ENTER, unreported pairs). The fix reworks the multi-threaded Jolt contact-listener budget and adds a pair-table resync under P11 (exactly once), P15 (allocation-free) and P16 (deterministic for any worker count). *Fix:* Shape: (1) Prioritise transitions. Give ADDED/REMOVED a reserved share of the budget (or separate per-thread buffers) so PERSISTED is dropped first. (2) After any step with drops, mark the table dirty and resync: for every tracked pair, ask Jolt whether the bodies are still in contact (PhysicsSystem::WereBodiesInContact) and synthesise the EXIT for stale pairs. Track pairs that produced ADDED/PERSISTED but were refused, so a later ENTER is still emitted once. Keep pairScratch-style preallocated buffers. Risks: the listener runs concurrently on Jolt workers (atomic budget accounting), which records get dropped depends on thread timing (so results after an overflow must still be sorted and deterministic), a resync costs O(pairs) on overflow steps, and sleeping pairs legitimately produce no callbacks and must not be expired. *Test:* tests/physics/test_contact_events.cpp: 'contact_events - after a contact budget overflow every pair still gets exactly one ENTER and one EXIT'. maxContactEventsPerStep = 8, a pile of 40 boxes that settles and is then cleared; per pair count ENTER == EXIT == 1 and activePairs drains to empty.
- **F412** MINOR A runtime motion-type change does not rebuild the shape, bypassing the mesh_collider/DYNAMIC rule (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_sync.cpp:184`). Still present: visitBody sets only MOTION \| PROPERTIES on a motion-type change and applies it immediately (physics_world_sync.cpp:184-200). The mesh_collider/DYNAMIC rule lives only in build_shape (shape_builder.cpp:265). Even a SHAPE rebuild would keep the stale mesh shape for a mesh-only entity, because apply_rebuilds does nothing when built.shape is null (physics_world_bodies.cpp:777). A correct fix has to order the motion change against the shape rebuild within one push, because apply_rebuilds already ran before push_changed_components. It also has to pick runtime behaviour for a mesh-only body made DYNAMIC (destroy and stay pending, as at creation) and avoid Jolt simulating a dynamic MeshShape body for even one step. *Fix:* Shape: in visitBody, when motionType changes and the entity has a mesh_collider (world->has_raw(target, ids.mesh)), do not call apply_motion_type inline. Instead mark_rebuild(target, MOTION \| PROPERTIES \| SHAPE) and either run apply_rebuilds a second time after push_changed_components, or handle the rebuild inline through a shared helper, so motion and shape change in the same push. In apply_rebuilds, when SHAPE is dirty, hasCollider is true and built.shape == nullptr (the mesh was dropped for DYNAMIC and nothing else remains), destroy the body (exits synthesised) and mark_pending_create, matching the creation rule (stays pending, bodiesWithoutShape). Apply SetShape before SetMotionType(Dynamic) so Jolt never sees a dynamic mesh body. Risks: push ordering inside push_transforms, Jolt assertions on mass properties of mesh shapes, contact EXIT/ENTER churn, and the interaction with F411's reordering. *Test:* tests/physics/test_colliders.cpp: "colliders - switching a mesh body to DYNAMIC at runtime drops the mesh with a WARN". Case 1: KINEMATIC rigid_body + mesh_collider (loaded ramp) + sphere_collider; set motionType = DYNAMIC; run_step. Expect a WARN UNSUPPORTED_SHAPE_FOR_MOTION, a ray at the mesh-only part misses, and the body falls. Case 2: mesh-only KINEMATIC switched to DYNAMIC -> has_body false and bodiesWithoutShape == 1. Case 3: DYNAMIC + mesh + sphere switched to STATIC -> the mesh becomes hittable.
- **F416** MINOR Destroying a body scans the whole contact pair table; mass destruction is O(bodies x table capacity) (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_contacts.cpp:70`). Still present: synthesise_exits (physics_world_contacts.cpp:70) and destroy_body's non-synthesising branch (physics_world_bodies.cpp:557-568) iterate activePairs for every destroyed body. core::flat_hash_map iteration walks every slot even when the map is empty (flat_hash_map.h:637-640 skip_empty). destroy_all_bodies destroys row by row and only clears the table afterwards (bodies.cpp:601-612). A real fix keeps a per-row pair count or adjacency in step with every insert and erase of activePairs, an invariant spread over about five sites in the contact code. Getting it wrong silently loses EXIT events, so it is more than a local edit. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Cheap part: in destroy_all_bodies call activePairs.clear() before the loop. In destroy_body and synthesise_exits, skip the scan when activePairs.empty(). (2) Real fix: add `u16 activePairCount` to body_record (reset by claim_row and destroy_body; kept by rebuild_character, which reuses the body id). Increment both rows when collect_events inserts a pair (contacts.cpp:249-253, only on a successful try_emplace). Decrement on every erase: contacts.cpp:198-200 (surviving row only), :243-246 (both rows), synthesise_exits (the other row) and destroy_body's non-synth branch (the other row). destroy_body / synthesise_exits then skip the scan when row.activePairCount == 0. Add a validate() check that the counts sum to 2 * activePairs.size(). Optionally batch destructions of one push into a single pass. Risks: a missed increment drops EXIT events, and a count that drifts must stay self-healing through the null-row erase path at contacts.cpp:196-203. The change is hard to prove with a unit test, so it needs a benchmark row (tests/benchmarks is owned by integration). *Test:* tests/physics/test_contact_events.cpp: "contact_events - destroying many bodies at rest reports one EXIT per contact and keeps the pair table consistent": 200 boxes resting on the ground, destroy them all in one frame, run_step, count EXIT == 200, validate() (with the new count invariant). A destroy-heavy benchmark row (physics.destroy_10k) belongs in tests/benchmarks/bench_physics.cpp as a follow-up.
- **F417** MINOR Four full scans of the record table per fixed step for rows that are usually absent (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_sync.cpp:272`). Still present: push_hierarchy_rows (physics_world_sync.cpp:272), step_characters (physics_world_characters.cpp:171), pull_parented_rows (sync.cpp:544) and pull_characters (characters.cpp:225) each walk the full records table every step. The comment at sync.cpp:295 calls the walk cheap. The step and pull of characters can early-out on characterCount == 0 trivially. Skipping the hierarchy walks needs counters or index vectors kept in step with hasParent / movesWithTransform / kind at about six mutation sites (observers, create_body, create_character, apply_motion_type, destroy_body, destroy_all_bodies). That is an invariant-maintenance change in the hot path, not a local edit. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Trivial part: `if (characterCount == 0) return;` at the top of step_characters and pull_characters. (2) Keep `std::vector<u32> hierarchyRows` (rows with movesWithTransform \|\| hasParent, BODY or CHARACTER) and `std::vector<u32> characterRows`, reserved to maxBodies / maxCharacters at construction (P15 allocation-free). Maintain them with swap-remove through one helper called whenever hasParent (on_component_added/removed for ids.parent, create_body, create_character), movesWithTransform (create_body, apply_motion_type) or the row's life (claim_row, destroy_body, destroy_all_bodies) changes. Iterate them in the four loops in row-index order so behaviour stays deterministic: sort, or accept insertion order and document it. Check membership in validate(). Risks: determinism of character update order (design 3.5 says table order), a stale index after destroy, and proving the win needs a benchmark. *Test:* tests/physics/test_component_sync.cpp: "component_sync - parented, kinematic-with-transform and character rows are still re-posed after rows are destroyed and reused": create 50 bodies, parent and unparent some, switch some to KINEMATIC_MOVES_WITH_TRANSFORM and back, destroy and recreate rows, move a parent and check every child follows (position_of) and validate() passes. Perf evidence through physics.sync_10k / step_10k_sleeping in tests/benchmarks.
- **F418** MINOR Validated settings can still abort the process: maxJobs >= 64 and tempAllocatorBytes >= 1 MiB (`engine/src/physics/physics_settings.cpp:94`). Still present: validate accepts maxJobs 64 and tempAllocatorBytes 1 MiB (physics_settings.cpp:90-96). But jolt_job_system::CreateJob CONJURE_FATAL_ASSERTs on pool exhaustion (jolt_job_system.cpp:49), and physics_world.cpp:71 builds a plain JPH::TempAllocatorImpl, which aborts when full. A correct fix changes the Jolt job-system adapter (threading, job dependency protocol) and the temp-allocator overflow policy, both against P3/P15. It is not a local bounds check. *Fix:* Shape: (1) At state construction (physics_world.cpp:69-71), raise maxJobs to the next power of two at or above a bound derived from (worker_count()+1) and collisionSteps (Jolt's Update creates about a dozen jobs plus several per thread), and WARN once when raised. Validate cannot do this because it does not know the worker count. (2) Make CreateJob degrade instead of a fatal assert, for example by running the job inline when the pool is empty. Barrier and dependency semantics make this delicate: a job created with dependencies must not run before they resolve. (3) Swap to JPH::TempAllocatorImplWithMallocFallback (or a subclass whose fallback goes through core::memory) and count fallback allocations in physics_stats with a rate-limited WARN, because P15 forbids silent allocation in step. Risks: Jolt barrier/job lifetime, the allocation-free benchmark gates, determinism. *Test:* tests/physics/test_physics_world.cpp: 'physics_world - maxJobs 64 on an 8-worker job system steps a 2000-body pile without a fatal assert' and 'physics_world - a 1 MiB temp allocator under load degrades with a WARN instead of aborting'
- **F608** MINOR Destroying a body scans every active contact pair (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_bodies.cpp:559`): same as F416.

### rhi (7)

- **F011** BLOCKER D3D12 swapchain is destroyed/resized with Present still in flight: Debug runtime crashes at exit (0x87D) (`engine/src/rhi/backends/d3d12/swapchain_d3d12.cpp:57`). Still present. ~d3d12_swapchain (swapchain_d3d12.cpp:57-59) and resize() (:174-177, then ResizeBuffers at :179) rely on nvrhi waitForIdle. In the pinned NVRHI that call (_deps/nvrhi-src/src/d3d12/d3d12-device.cpp:576-588) only waits for the fence of the last executeCommandLists. The Present queued after that fence is never waited on, so the buffers get their final release while the queue still references them. This is GPU synchronisation and resource lifetime on the present path, which makes it CHALLENGING by definition. *Fix:* Shape: give d3d12_device_backend a private ID3D12Fence, a u64 counter and an event, plus a `void flush_graphics_queue()` helper. The helper does graphics_queue()->Signal(fence, ++value) and, if GetCompletedValue() < value, SetEventOnCompletion and WaitForSingleObject. It skips the wait when messages().is_lost() or poll_device_removed() is true, because a removed device's fence reports UINT64_MAX. Call it in ~d3d12_swapchain before release_back_buffers() and before the ComPtr releases mSwapchain. In resize(), call it after device->waitForIdle() and before release_back_buffers()/ResizeBuffers. This is the Microsoft sample pattern (WaitForPreviousFrame before ResizeBuffers or teardown). Risks: the device-lost teardown path must not block; the destruction order in rhi_subsystem::shutdown (rhi_subsystem.cpp:123-134) and in swapchain::~swapchain (swapchain.cpp:41-50) must stay valid; the renderer's framebuffer cache must still drop back-buffer framebuffers on resize. Proof needs the D3D12 debug layer (Graphics Tools), so verify with conjure_rhi_tests run un-isolated and with conjure_runtime --rhi d3d12 in Debug. Also fix the misleading 'debug layer off' INFO line on later devices (device.cpp:126): the D3D12 layer is process-wide once enabled, so the backend should record and log that it is already active. Optional ci follow-up: run each test executable once un-isolated in CI. *Test:* tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp: new case 'swapchain - [gpu] a D3D12 swapchain on a debug-layer device presents, resizes and is destroyed without a debug-layer error'. It uses make_software_device(true), clears and presents 6 frames, calls resize(64, 48), presents 2 more frames, destroys the swapchain and then the device, all under log_capture with no ERROR. Also run the full conjure_rhi_tests executable without --conjure-isolated, which crashes today.
- **F071** BLOCKER Exit code 125 explained: every windowed D3D12 Debug run dies at shutdown with unhandled exception 0x87D (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/rhi_subsystem.cpp:128`). Still present. rhi_subsystem::shutdown (rhi_subsystem.cpp:123-134) calls device->wait_idle() and then swapchain.reset(). ~d3d12_swapchain (swapchain_d3d12.cpp:57-59) calls release_back_buffers() (:279-281) with no fence over the last Present. NVRHI's waitForIdle does not cover the DXGI present, so the debug layer raises OBJECT_DELETED_WHILE_STILL_IN_USE (0x87D). This is D3D12 GPU synchronisation and resource lifetime in the backend. *Fix:* Shape: in the D3D12 backend, before back buffers are released (destructor and resize), signal a fence on the graphics queue after the last Present and wait for it. Otherwise make device::wait_idle() flush the queue itself (ID3D12CommandQueue::Signal + SetEventOnCompletion), and also drop the DXGI frame-latency references, for example with a final Present(0, DXGI_PRESENT_TEST) or by waiting on the frame-latency waitable object. Release the swapchain before the device in every path. Check the Vulkan backend for the equivalent present-semaphore lifetime. Risks: a deadlock if the device is lost (bound the wait and honour is_lost); the order against NVRHI's own garbage collection; the fix can only be verified with the debug layer on a real adapter. *Test:* tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp (REAL_WINDOW, D3D12, enableDebugLayer): "swapchain - destroying a D3D12 swapchain right after a present raises no debug-layer corruption"; the end-to-end runtime check is covered by F082
- **F012** MAJOR conjure_rhi_tests fails when run directly; CTest per-case isolation hides the D3D12 debug-layer crash (`tests/rhi/test_device.cpp:1`): same as F011.
- **F013** MAJOR Vulkan swapchain requires the exact requested format, no fallback: the default RGBA8_SRGB is not offered on X11 (`engine/src/rhi/backends/vulkan/swapchain_vulkan.cpp:459`). Still present. create_swapchain accepts only `candidate.format == wanted && colorSpace == SRGB_NONLINEAR` (swapchain_vulkan.cpp:459-473), and the engine requests the default RGBA8_SRGB (swapchain.h:29, rhi_subsystem.cpp:96-101). Surfaces that offer only BGRA8 therefore fail. This exact-match rule is the documented contract (handbook rhi.md:168), so changing it means picking substitution rules. One rule is not expressible today, because texture_format has no BGRA8_UNORM. RGBA16_FLOAT also needs a different colour space, and the result cannot be verified while Vulkan is compile-only. *Fix:* Shape: when the wanted format and colour space pair is absent, pick a same-class substitute from the surface list: RGBA8_SRGB <-> BGRA8_SRGB, and RGBA8_UNORM <-> BGRA8_UNORM, which needs a new texture_format enumerator and its NVRHI mapping and name. Store the chosen format in mDesc.format before wrap_images so the wrappers and desc() agree; renderer.cpp:116 already reads swapchain->desc().format. Log a WARN with the substitution, and log the created format in swapchain.cpp:71-81 from the backend desc rather than the requested one. For RGBA16_FLOAT, either require VK_COLOR_SPACE_EXTENDED_SRGB_LINEAR_EXT (an instance extension) or reject it on Vulkan in validation. Update the docs contract ('format as created' may differ from the request). Risks: a public enum addition, a renderer pipeline/framebuffer format mismatch if any pipeline is built from the requested format, and no Vulkan runtime to verify on. *Test:* Unit-testable part: tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp 'swapchain - the Vulkan surface format chooser substitutes BGRA8 for RGBA8 in the same class', which needs the chooser factored into a pure function over a VkSurfaceFormatKHR list. A lavapipe [gpu] case can follow once CI runs Vulkan.
- **F172** MAJOR conjure_rhi_tests crashes when run as one process: the debug-layer case poisons every later D3D12 swapchain case (`tests/rhi/test_device.cpp:236`): same as F011.
- **F174** MAJOR No test exercises a D3D12 swapchain on a debug-layer device, which is exactly what every Debug editor and runtime start creates (`tests/rhi/test_swapchain.cpp:157`): same as F011.
- **F291** MINOR VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR is reported as OUT_OF_DATE 'resize the swapchain', but resize can never recover it (`engine/src/rhi/backends/vulkan/swapchain_vulkan.cpp:217`). Still present. acquire() (swapchain_vulkan.cpp:217-223) and present() (:262-268) report VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR as OUT_OF_DATE ('resize the swapchain'). But resize() starts with query_surface_capabilities() on the same mSurface (:291), so it fails again on every retry; the surface is only created in initialise() (:158). Recovery needs either a Vulkan WSI lifetime change (destroy the swapchain, then the surface, then recreate both from a stored native_window) or a new terminal error. That is a choice between materially different behaviours, and it cannot be verified (Vulkan is compile-only). *Fix:* Option 1: store the native_window in vulkan_swapchain.
