In resize(), when the capabilities query returns VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR: flush the pending acquire, wait idle, release images, destroy the swapchain (no oldSwapchain across surfaces), destroy and recreate the surface through create_surface(), re-check vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfaceSupportKHR, then create a fresh swapchain and semaphores. Option 2: map SURFACE_LOST to a terminal error (DEVICE_LOST semantics, or a new rhi_error::SURFACE_LOST) so the frame loop quits instead of retrying forever. Either way, separate SURFACE_LOST from OUT_OF_DATE in acquire/present and update the docs table at handbook rhi.md:169-170. Risks: the destruction order the spec requires (swapchain before surface) and an untestable path until lavapipe CI exists. Recommendation: option 1. *Test:* No runtime Vulkan tests exist yet; once lavapipe CI runs, add 'swapchain - [gpu] a Vulkan swapchain recreates its surface after VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR', using a test seam that forces the capabilities query to report SURFACE_LOST.

### ecs (5)

- **F258** MAJOR Nothing guards the phase's stage list against a rebuild or a self-removal while run_phase is iterating it (`engine/src/ecs/scheduler.cpp:181`). Still present: run_phase_filtered iterates state.stages by reference (scheduler.cpp:181) and indexes state.timings (:186, :225), while an EXCLUSIVE system runs on the owner thread with the world unlocked and may call back in. build_stages clears both vectors (scheduler_stages.cpp:137-138). remove_system does record->desc = {} (scheduler.cpp:113) on the record whose std::function may be executing, and puts it on the free list (:114), where a following add_system can overwrite it mid-call. Nothing tracks a running phase. Fixing this is re-entrancy and frame-ordering design inside the scheduler, not a local check. *Fix:* Add a per-phase running depth to phase_state, incremented and decremented around the stage loop of run_phase_filtered (RAII guard, so nested runs of other phases still work). While a phase's depth is above 0: build_stages for that phase only leaves it dirty and returns success (stages()/finalise() see the current list; the rebuild happens at the next run after the outermost run ends). remove_system marks the record dead (alive = false is enough for find_record) but defers clearing desc, the buffer shrink and the free-list push to a pending-release list drained when the outermost run of any phase ends, so add_system can never reuse a record whose run() is on the stack. Bound-check timingIndex against state.timings.size() defensively, and assert in Debug on a same-phase rebuild while running. Risks: same-phase nested run_phase semantics (a running phase must not rebuild under itself); the commands of a self-removed system flushed at its stage end must still be flushed or discarded consistently; ecs_module_consumer::attach (engine/src/engine/module_consumers/ecs_module_consumer.cpp:172-193) calls finalise() and must still surface SYSTEM_CYCLE, which add_system already detects. The zero-allocation gate in test_scheduler.cpp must stay green. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_scheduler.cpp: "scheduler - a running exclusive system may remove itself and add systems". A one-shot EXCLUSIVE system calls remove_system(self), add_system(another UPDATE system) and finalise() from its run. With for_each_job_mode, the frame completes (clean under ASan/Debug iterators), the new system runs from the next frame, and the removed one never runs again.
- **F257** MINOR redefine_dynamic overwrites a component_info in place under the mutex while info()/types() are documented lock-free; check-then-act on has_instances; old descriptions accumulate (`engine/src/ecs/component_registry.cpp:311`). Still present: redefine_dynamic checks has_instances at component_registry.cpp:289, outside the lock that replaces the entry at 307-313. It overwrites mTypes[id] in place while info()/types() are documented lock-free and address-stable (component_registry.h:61-63, 168-172; component.h:122-125). It never frees the superseded type_info, fires no event, and calls relayout_archetypes on worlds owned by other threads (world.cpp:526-548). Fixing it means defining the registry's concurrency contract for redefinition and the lifetime of descriptions that readers may still hold. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Define redefine_dynamic as an owner-thread, all-worlds-unlocked operation. Assert that every attached world is unlocked and owned by the calling thread, and say so in the header. (2) Re-check has_instances while holding mMutex, or under a world-side guard, so no add can land between the check and the swap. (3) Keep superseded type_info objects alive rather than erasing them: archives, inspector caches and C# mirrors may still point at them. Either bound the growth (replace only when no outstanding reader, needs a reader count) or accept and document it. (4) Add an on_redefined event, fired after the lock is released like on_registered, so editor and bindings caches can refresh. Hard parts: info() returns a reference that callers keep, so an in-place overwrite is unsafe whenever any thread can read it. A truly safe swap needs either an indirection (pointer-per-id with deferred reclamation) or an enforced quiescent point, which changes the registry's threading design and touches scripting hot reload ordering. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_dynamic_components.cpp: "dynamic_components - redefine refuses a world locked or owned elsewhere" (expect an assert, then COMPONENT_IN_USE or the new error), and tests/ecs/test_component_registry.cpp: "component_registry - redefine_dynamic fires on_redefined after the swap".
- **F267** MINOR create_entities / instantiate place rows one at a time although header, design and comments say chunk-wise (`engine/src/ecs/world_structural.cpp:225`). Still present: place_rows loops per entity, calling push_row, registry.info per column, construct_column_default(...,1) and stamp_all for each row (world_structural.cpp:225-253). That contradicts world.h:226 ('chunk by chunk'), world_archive.cpp:123 and 01_ecs.md:225-226 ('fills chunk by chunk (one loop per chunk)'). Implementing the designed behaviour is a 50-80 line rewrite of the structural core: it needs a new multi-row archetype reservation and must keep place_rows' undo semantics and the spare-chunk packing invariant. *Fix:* Add archetype::push_rows(chunk_pool &, u32 wanted) returning {chunkIndex, firstRow, rowCount} for up to min(wanted, free rows of the open chunk), reusing reserve_row/open_chunk so the spare-chunk rule (archetype.cpp:207-223) holds. In place_rows, loop per chunk: write the entity span, resolve each column's component_info once per call, run one construct_column_default(info, cell(column, firstRow), rowCount) per column, one stamp_all per chunk, then fill the records. On failure, undo by discarding the rows placed so far (discard_placed per id, or a chunk-wise undo) so the caller still sees an unchanged world. Risks: the swap-remove in discard_placed while rows of the same chunk are still partially initialised, mAliveCount/mEntityCount accounting, and the zero-allocation bulk-create guarantee (mPlacedScratch). The cheaper alternative is to correct the three texts instead (world.h, world_archive.cpp, and docs/design/01_ecs.md, which belongs to the docs area). *Test:* tests/ecs/test_world_entities.cpp: "world - create_entities constructs defaults once per chunk". A component registered through register_described has an ops.constructDefault that counts calls and the rows covered. create_entities of 3 chunks' worth gives calls == chunk count and rows == entity count, and world.validate() passes, including the failure-undo path on an exhausted chunk pool.
- **F269** MINOR component_from_json is not atomic and accepts archive-local integers into a live world (`engine/src/ecs/serialization.cpp:664`). Still present: component_from_json reads straight into get_raw(owner, id, true) (serialization.cpp:664), so a failure on a later field leaves earlier fields written and the column stamped. read_entity accepts a bare integer as entity::archive_local (:100-108), which write_entity later re-emits as a local index (:86-88). Rejecting integers only on the live path cannot be done locally: make_default_serializer's readJson hook has the fixed signature (void *, const json::value &), so the archive-versus-live distinction must reach read_entity through a new channel (a component_serializer signature change visible to scripting/physics, or an ecs-internal thread-local read mode). Atomicity also needs copy/destruct choreography over component_ops. *Fix:* Atomicity: in component_from_json, copy the current value into an aligned scratch image (ops.copy, or memcpy for trivial types), run read_component_json on the scratch, and only on success destruct the live cell and relocate the scratch into it via get_raw(owner, id, true), which stamps only then. On failure destroy the scratch. Patch semantics ('missing members keep their value') are kept because the scratch starts as a copy. Local indices: add an internal read mode (e.g. a thread_local flag set by component_from_json and cleared by an RAII guard) that read_entity consults to reject in.is_number() with 'entity reference must be {"external":"<index>:<generation>"} or null on a live entity'. Custom readJson hooks (script_component) keep their own handling, and the docs note that. Risks: non-trivial types without copy ops, the cost of the copy for large components on the inspector path, and the editor's add_component all-or-nothing path (editor/core/component_access.cpp:281-288), which must keep working. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_serialization.cpp: "serialization - component_from_json is all or nothing and refuses local entity indices". {"y":5,"z":"oops"} on a position fails, leaves the value unchanged and does not show it as changed. {"target":5} on a link fails with SERIALIZATION_FAILED, and the archive path still reads integer local indices.
- **F276** MINOR Change-detection tests cover only the serial, manually bumped cases (`tests/ecs/test_scheduler.cpp:439`). Still present: both change-detection cases use scheduler(world, nullptr) with a single system (test_scheduler.cpp:444, test_change_detection.cpp:168), and the second inserts a manual world.bump_version() (:195). The cases it asks for are the regression tests of other confirmed defects: the query stamping world.version() instead of the run version (query.cpp:124, another batch), a write without a bump, the tag in a view pack, the described default value, the stale deferred-instantiate root, register_dynamic with equal-size field changes, and F259/F258 here. Those tests fail until the defects are fixed, several of which are concurrency or contract fixes, so they cannot land as a standalone test change. *Fix:* Land each test with its fix rather than as one batch. The change-detection pair moves into for_each_job_mode with a second system in the same stage that makes a structural call before its view, and drops the manual bump_version at test_change_detection.cpp:195; this goes with the query run-version stamping fix. Duplicate-name and running-EXCLUSIVE-mutation cases go with F259 and F258. The view-pack tag, described default, failed deferred instantiate root and register_dynamic cases go next to their respective fixes in test_view.cpp, test_component_type_desc.cpp, test_entity_archive.cpp and test_dynamic_components.cpp. Risk: any test added before its fix is red or flaky in parallel mode. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_change_detection.cpp: "change_detection - system does not see own previous writes in a shared parallel stage" (for_each_job_mode, two systems in one stage, no manual bump), plus the per-fix cases listed in the plan.

### scene (5)

- **F348** MINOR Propagation and compute_world_transform disagree after local_transform is removed from a parent (`engine/src/scene/transform_propagation.cpp:65`). The disagreement is still there. lookup_parent (transform_propagation.cpp:57-76) takes the nearest ancestor with world_transform, while compute_world_transform (transform_system.cpp:80-97) multiplies only ancestors with local_transform. No observer handles local_transform REMOVED (scene_observers.cpp:15-54), so the companions and stale matrix remain and nothing marks the children. validate (scene.cpp:864-866) checks only local -> companions. The fix touches the propagation hot path and needs a way to dirty descendants. *Fix:* Keep the companions consistent with local_transform. Register a 7th observer (local_transform REMOVED, !mInOperation; mObservers grows from 6 to 7). It queues remove_raw of world_transform, render_world_transform and transform_state into the deferred buffer, and adds transform_dirty to the nearest transformed descendants, walking through transformless ones like mark_children_pending. Extend validate() to flag companions without local_transform. Optionally make lookup_parent also require local_transform; that adds one get_raw per parent lookup, so measure with the 10k propagation benchmarks. What makes it hard: signalling dirtiness to children (P's archetype move does not stamp them), the cost on the hot path, and the interplay with the editor's component.remove and with add_transform re-adding the component. *Test:* tests/scene/test_transform_propagation.cpp: "regression - removing a parent's local_transform keeps the pass and compute_world_transform in step". P is at (5,0,0) with child C at local (1,0,0). Call world.remove<local_transform>(P) and step(). Check that world_matrix(C) translation equals world_transform_of(C).position == (1,0,0) and that validate() passes.
- **F354** MINOR A leaked expected-repair count makes the next raw parent edit unrepaired, and its intent entry is requeued forever (`engine/src/scene/scene_observers.cpp:79`). The defect is still there. - mExpectedRepairs is a bare counter, incremented at scene.cpp:362-364 and consumed by any parent observer (scene_observers.cpp:79-82, 100-103). Only clear() resets it (scene.cpp:236). - An intent entry whose buffer is never flushed is pushed back on every pass (scene_link_pass.cpp:323-327), forever. The README (lines 63-66) understates the effect as 'unwarned'; in fact the later raw edit is never repaired. The fix redesigns the silent-repair bookkeeping and needs a timeout policy. *Fix:* 1. Replace the global counter with a per-entity expectation. Record on the intent entry which parent transition (ADDED or REMOVED) the flush will fire. The parent observers look the target up among hasIntent entries and stay silent only on a match; anything else is a raw edit and is queued with the WARN. 2. Drop stale intent entries: set_parent and destroy_now erase the entity's intent entry. apply_hierarchy_changes drops an intent entry that has waited more than N passes, or turns it into a plain repair with a WARN. What makes it hard: - The timeout is a policy choice. The existing test 'deferred_set_parent repair waits for the buffer flush' requires waiting at least one full frame, because a buffer recorded after TRANSFORM lands next frame. - The same-buffer multi-call net-change logic (scene.cpp:346-364) must be re-derived. - Observers run inside every structural flush, so the lookup must not allocate and should stay cheap; consider a small entity -> entry map. 3. Update the README paragraph. *Test:* tests/scene/test_scene.cpp: "regression - a dropped deferred_set_parent buffer does not swallow a raw parent edit". Call deferred_set_parent(cmds, rootChild, P), then cmds.clear(). Then world.add<parent>(x, {Q,0}) and step(). Check that x is linked under Q with its depth tag, the WARN fired, and validate() passes. Also check that the stale intent entry is gone after set_parent(rootChild, B) and pending_repair_count() == 0.
- **F355** MINOR deferred_set_parent computes keepWorld from the live tree, ignoring earlier commands in the same buffer (`engine/src/scene/scene.cpp:321`). The defect is still there. scene.cpp:321-326 computes childWorld and parentWorld with compute_world_transform over the live tree. The lookup at scene.cpp:312-319 only decides whether a parent is present, so an earlier unflushed reparent of the child or of an ancestor of newParent is ignored. A correct fix first has to settle what 'keep world' means once an ancestor was moved earlier in the same buffer, and 13_scene.md 3.7 does not settle it. *Fix:* keepWorld has to be computed against the tree as it will stand when this command lands. There are two ways to get there. (a) Simulate: store the recorded keepWorld local in repair_entry (std::optional<local_transform> intendedLocal). Add a private world_after_intents(entity) that walks ancestors, using each pending intent's intendedParent instead of mTree.parent_of and the intended local instead of the live one. Use it for both childWorld and parentWorld. (b) Refuse: return an INVALID_STATE error when keepWorld is requested and the child, newParent or any ancestor of either (live or intended) has a pending intent entry. Document this. What makes it hard: - For a child whose ancestor was reparented earlier in the buffer, it is undecided whether 'keep' means its live world or its world after the earlier commands. - Intent entries from different unflushed buffers cannot be told apart in mRepairQueue. - The intended-link walk must stay cycle-safe; check_reparent only checks the live tree, so A-under-B then B-under-A in one buffer already passes. deferred_set_parent has no production caller yet, so (b) plus a doc line is the cheap option if the owner prefers it. *Test:* tests/scene/test_scene.cpp: "regression - deferred_set_parent keepWorld follows an earlier reparent in the same buffer". P is a root at the origin, X is at (10,0,0). Call deferred_set_parent(cmds,P,X,false), then deferred_set_parent(cmds,C,P,true), flush, step. Check that world_transform_of(C) equals its pre-call world, or that the second call fails under option (b).
- **F371** MINOR Prefab tests avoid the cases where the prefab functions misbehave (`tests/scene/test_prefab.cpp:294`). The gaps are still there. The nested test calls mark_instance before build_document and never diffs (test_prefab.cpp:308-312). The refresh test instantiates with default options (line 530). 'apply writes links as guids' (468-515) has no duplicate-child case and no second diff. Each missing case exercises a prefab defect that is still open (prefab.cpp:108 nested relinking, :406 refresh_instances skipping placed instances, :380 duplicate entry guids in apply), so the tests fail until those fixes land and cannot go in alone. *Fix:* Add each case together with the fix of the prefab defect it covers: (1) Editor-order nested create (build_document, then mark_instance), instantiate the outer prefab, then CHECK(diff(freshOuter).empty()), with the nested-link fix in prefab.cpp around line 108. (2) refresh_instances(onlyUnmodified) over instances created with rootTransform and rootName that are otherwise untouched, which must be refreshed; fix in refresh_instances around line 406. (3) duplicate_subtree of a child inside an instance, apply, reload the asset, then diff(root).empty() and no duplicate entry guids; fix in apply around line 380. *Test:* tests/scene/test_prefab.cpp: "prefab - nested prefab created in editor order diffs clean", "prefab - refresh_instances refreshes placed unmodified instances", "prefab - apply after a duplicated child diffs clean after reload".
- **F591** MINOR Transform validation differs between commands and file load: a zero quaternion / zero scale loads from a scene file (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/command_support.cpp:128`). Still present. local_transform (scene/components.h:70-86) is a plain reflected struct with no read hook, so scene files, prefabs and the generic component.set path (component_access.cpp:215-251) all accept rotation [0,0,0,0]. Only entity.set_transform's read_rotation (command_support.cpp:125-130) refuses it. A zero scale is accepted by both paths (read_vec3), so only the quaternion is inconsistent. Fixing it means choosing reject or normalise on load in the scene module. *Fix:* Shape: validate local_transform once, in the scene module's load path (scene_serializer load/instantiate_subtree after the reflected read). The ecs reflection layer has no per-type post-read hook. The behaviour needs a choice: (a) replace a zero-length quaternion with identity and renormalise when \|len - 1\| > 1e-3, logging a WARN that names the entity (files keep loading); (b) fail the load with a serializer error. Recommend (a). Risks: renormalising slightly off quaternions changes byte-exact round-trip tests; the generic component.set, C# writes and prefab overrides bypass the serializer and need the same helper (or the editor's write_component should call it); zero scale probably stays legal because the command path allows it too. *Test:* tests/scene/test_scene_serializer.cpp "regression - a zero quaternion in a scene file loads as identity with a warning".

### engine (4)

- **F144** BLOCKER Component voices are retained and never released: voice table leaks, destroyed sources keep playing (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/audio_subsystem.cpp:298`). Still present. play_params_from sets retain = true (engine/src/audio/components.cpp:44). audio_engine::end_frame frees only non-retained terminal voices (audio_engine.cpp:1291), and on_voice_ended frees only stolen or release-requested ones (:603). The subsystem never calls release(): finish_one_shots just clears the handle (audio_subsystem.cpp:297-298), PLAY on a live voice only stop()s it (:208-211), and no REMOVED observer exists (no observe() call in engine/src/engine). Every component voice therefore leaks a table entry, and a destroyed entity's loop keeps playing. A correct fix adds an ECS observer to the world's structural callbacks, has to survive snapshot restore, world::clear and copied components with duplicated handles, and changes voice lifetime. It is not a one-liner, even though it stays in the engine adapter. *Fix:* Shape: (1) In finish_one_shots, call engine.release(source.handle.voice) before clearing the handle. (2) In sync, call release (not stop) on the old voice before restarting on PLAY. (3) In startup or register_systems, register mServices->world->observe(audio_source id, component_event::REMOVED, ...) plus observe_destroyed, so that a component with a valid handle is released. Unobserve at shutdown, as physics_world_bodies.cpp:107-121 does. (4) Deal with copies: a duplicated entity or snapshot that carries the same voice id must not release the original's voice. Either clear handle.voice on copy (the field is TRANSIENT) or verify that the voice still belongs to this entity. Risks: observers run inside structural calls, so release must not re-enter the world; the editor's play/stop restore path; worlds other than mServices->world. *Test:* tests/engine/test_audio_subsystem.cpp: "audio_subsystem - six hundred replayed component sounds never exhaust the voice table" and "audio_subsystem - destroying an entity with a looping source releases its voice"
- **F081** MAJOR Half of the export pipeline has no test: tool steps, shader copy and a scripted or native project (`tests/engine/test_export.cpp:76`). Still present. Every export test sets buildNativeModule = false and publishManaged = false (test_export.cpp:76-78, 142-144) and never turns copyShaders on together with copyRuntime. So run_tool (export_pipeline.cpp:63-99), step 1 (:186-213), step 3 (:266-310), the nethost copy (:348-350) and the shaders/ copy or warning (:351-357) are never executed by a test. export_settings already lets a test inject the tools and the source folder (cmakeExecutable, dotnetExecutable, processTimeoutSeconds, distributionDirectory in export.h:38-49), and core::process kills the child tree on timeout. The work is test-only and stays inside tests/engine: a helper executable following the tests/core/system/CMakeLists.txt:7-12 and 62-71 pattern, plus test cases. There is no production change. *Fix:* (1) Add tests/engine/export_tool_stub.cpp, a tiny program with no engine dependency. It appends its arguments, joined by single spaces, as one line to ./export_stub_calls.txt in its working directory, which run_tool sets to the project root. If ./export_stub_mode.txt exists, it reads 'exit <n>' (print 'stub failed' to stdout, return n) or 'sleep <ms>' (sleep, then return 0). Otherwise it returns 0. (2) In tests/engine/CMakeLists.txt, inside the existing `if(TARGET conjure::engine)` section, add conjure_add_executable(conjure_export_tool_stub SOURCES export_tool_stub.cpp FOLDER "Tests"), add_dependencies(conjure_engine_tests conjure_export_tool_stub) and target_compile_definitions(conjure_engine_tests PRIVATE CONJURE_EXPORT_TOOL_STUB_PATH="$<PATH:CMAKE_PATH,NORMALIZE,$<TARGET_FILE:conjure_export_tool_stub>>"). Mention the helper in the header comment of that file. (3) Cases in test_export.cpp, all using write_project and project_desc. Native build: create the project with extraJson `"extensions": {"build": "dist"}`, because the preset is read from the project description loaded at boot. Write project/module.json declaring a binary AFTER app.initialise(), not before: project::module_manifests (project.cpp:619-630) registers a root module.json as REQUIRED, so a manifest whose binary does not exist would fail the boot with MODULE_LOAD_FAILED. Step 1 re-reads module.json from disk at export time. Set cmakeExecutable = CONJURE_EXPORT_TOOL_STUB_PATH and expect report.nativeModuleBuilt plus a calls line '--build --preset dist --config Distribution'. Failure: mode 'exit 3' gives EXPORT_FAILED with a message containing "exited with 3" and "stub failed". Timeout: mode 'sleep 30000' with processTimeoutSeconds = 0.5 gives EXPORT_FAILED containing "timed out". Missing tool: cmakeExecutable = "no_such_tool_xyz" gives EXPORT_FAILED containing "could not be started". Missing preset: module.json with a binary but no extensions.build gives the 'no extensions.build preset' warning and nativeModuleBuilt false. Managed, guarded by CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING: after project_desc(), set desc.config.scripting.enabled = true and useNullHost = true. project::apply_to keeps scripting disabled for a headless config (project.cpp:597-607), so this must be set on the desc, not in project.json. Set dotnetExecutable = stub and bootstrapProject = "Boot.csproj". Expect managedPublished, "managed" in copiedFiles and a calls line starting 'publish Boot.csproj -c Release -o ' followed by (absolute output / "managed").string() and ' --self-contained false'. With scriptsProject set, expect a second 'build ...' line. Runtime: a temp distribution dir holding dummy files named conjure_runtime(.exe on Windows; the name helper is file-local in export_pipeline.cpp, so the test spells it out), core::dynamic_library::decorate_name("conjure_shared"), nethost.dll and shaders/a.bin, with copyRuntime and copyShaders on. Expect the files in the output and in report.copiedFiles ("nethost.dll", "shaders"). Without shaders/, expect the 'no shaders/ folder' warning. With an empty distributionDirectory, expect the 'no distribution directory given' warning. *Test:* tests/engine/test_export.cpp: "export_pipeline - a native module is built through the cmake tool and a failing, hanging or missing tool is EXPORT_FAILED", "export_pipeline - a scripted project publishes managed code through the dotnet tool", "export_pipeline - the runtime, nethost and shaders are copied from the distribution directory"
- **F146** MAJOR fixedTime is scaled twice by timeScale (`engine/src/engine/engine_time.cpp:35`). Still present: engine_time.cpp:35 adds fixedDeltaTime * timeScale per step although the step count already comes from the scaled delta (frame_loop.cpp:121 advance(app.mTime.deltaTime)), so fixedTime runs at timeScale squared, against 06_engine_loop.md:505 `time.fixedTime += fixedDeltaTime`. The one-line fix makes the editor step path inconsistent instead: step_fixed frames (frame_loop.cpp:69-80) advance time by N * fixedDelta * timeScale, which today matches the doubly scaled fixedTime, and what time should do for a forced step at timeScale != 1 (or 0) is not settled by R13. *Fix:* Shape: (1) engine_time.cpp:35 `time.fixedTime += static_cast<f64>(time.fixedDeltaTime);` and engine_time.h fixedTime comment 'scaled seconds: fixedDeltaTime per fixed step'. (2) Decide and implement the step_fixed frame: recommended reading of R13 is that the forced frame's scaled deltaTime equals fixedSteps * fixedDeltaTime whatever timeScale is (the fixed steps simulate exactly that much time), so in frame_loop's forced branch call begin_frame with a copy of timeSettings whose timeScale is 1 and then restore app.mTime.timeScale to the configured scale (begin_frame writes time.timeScale, which frame_loop.cpp:68 reads back next frame), keeping time and fixedTime together at any scale, including 0. Risk: the editor's play.step at timeScale 0 then advances time, a visible behaviour change to confirm with the editor owner. *Test:* tests/engine/test_engine_time.cpp: "engine_time - fixedTime tracks time at a time scale of one half" (60 frames of step_frame(1/60) at timeScale 0.5: \|time - fixedTime\| < fixedDeltaTime); tests/engine/test_application.cpp: "application - step_fixed keeps time and fixedTime together at a time scale of one half".
- **F461** MINOR Export is not all-or-nothing and leaves stale files from earlier exports (`engine/include/conjure/engine/export.h:95`). Still present: export.h:18 and :95-96 promise all-or-nothing steps, but export_pipeline.cpp:244 cooks straight into the output (`native_file_system destination(output, false)`), :346-353 copy the runtime file by file with CONJURE_TRY, and nothing removes cooked/, managed/, modules/<old> or shaders/ left by an earlier export, which the runtime then loads. A correct fix changes what export does to an existing output folder (staging plus swap, or deleting known subfolders), which is a behavioural choice with Windows rename and open-file risks. *Fix:* Shape: run all six steps into a sibling staging directory `<output>.export-<random>` (cooker destination, export_layout, copies and project.json all aimed at it); on success replace the output atomically enough: rename the existing output to `<output>.old-<random>`, rename staging to output, then remove the old one; on any failure remove the staging directory and leave the previous export untouched. Risks: std::filesystem::rename of a directory fails on Windows when a file inside is open (a running exported game) and across volumes, so fall back to an error that names the locked folder; user files placed in the output folder would be dropped by a swap, so decide whether to carry over unknown top-level files or to document that the folder is owned by the export. Until then, reword export.h:18/95-96 and the API page to state that earlier steps' output persists and stale files are not removed. Interacts with F152 (output-directory validation must run before staging). *Test:* tests/engine/test_export.cpp: "export_pipeline - a second export removes the files of the first" (export with a fake modules/old folder present in the output, then export again and check modules/old is gone) and "export_pipeline - a failed step leaves the previous export intact" (copyRuntime with a distributionDirectory lacking conjure_runtime, check the earlier output files are unchanged and no staging folder remains).

### audio (3)

- **F024** MAJOR A voice waiting for a stolen slot can lose that slot to another PRIMING voice and wait indefinitely (`engine/src/audio/audio_engine.cpp:711`). Still present. start_pending_voices walks the entries in index order (audio_engine.cpp:693-718). A non-awaiting PRIMING voice takes find_free_mixer_slot() (:711), and an awaiting voice accepts only its reserved slot (:706-709), so it can wait forever. The same table also lets a streamed voice that was not primed at play() (:1440 reserves nothing) starve behind voices played later. A correct fix needs a slot-reservation model across several lifecycle functions, not a reorder. *Fix:* Shape: let every admitted PRIMING voice hold a reservation. At play(), a streamed voice that is not primed yet reserves the free slot it found, and a stealing voice reserves the victim's slot as today. Record the reservation in slotOwner (or a separate slotReserved[]) and have find_free_mixer_slot skip reserved slots. start_pending_voices starts reserved voices on their own slot first and lets an awaiting voice fall back to any free slot if its slot was taken. Release reservations in free_entry, on_voice_ended and stop() on a PRIMING voice; today these clear slotOwner only when hasMixerSlot is true (:344-347, :597-599). choose_victim must ignore reserved but unstarted slots; reconcile and drain_events already skip owners without hasMixerSlot. The minimal patch (awaiting voices first plus fallback) fixes the stolen-slot case but leaves the unprimed-stream starvation. Risks: the slotOwner invariants used by six functions and the audio review 5 stolen-slot/seek tests. *Test:* tests/audio/test_audio_engine.cpp: "regression - code sweep F024 a voice waiting for a stolen slot is not overtaken by an unprimed streamed voice". Use a 2-worker job system. Play a streamed clip S (decode job pending, S has no slot), then fill the remaining slots with MAX_VOICES one-shot loops. Play P (retained, priority 200), which steals. Wait for S's job and step 2 frames. Check that P is PLAYING and that S is either PLAYING on its reserved slot or still primed and waiting, but not holding P's slot.
- **F324** MINOR Streamed voices are refilled with one 4096-frame chunk per update(), so a long frame or a low frame rate causes dropouts; seeks decode on the main thread (`engine/src/audio/audio_engine.cpp:497`). Still present. feed_stream schedules at most one STREAM_CHUNK_FRAMES decode per update() when a job system exists (audio_engine.cpp:497-499), and decode_chunk decodes one chunk (:452-470). The ring holds 16384 frames. Both seeks run streaming_decoder::seek on the main thread: streaming_pass at :765 and play() at :1400, although design 12_audio.md 3.7 says the seek happens in the next job. A fix reworks which thread owns the producer end of the SPSC ring and the decoder. *Fix:* Shape: (1) Make the decode job fill the ring: decode_chunk loops while `!ring.end_of_stream() && ring.free_frames() >= STREAM_CHUNK_FRAMES`, so it is bounded by the ring size. To survive a main-thread hitch, the job must also continue without update(): either it reschedules itself through the job system, or the ring and chunk sizes grow (memory cost: 32 slots times the ring). (2) Move the streamed seek into the job, as 3.7 specifies. In WAIT_RESET the main thread hands the job a seekFrame; the job does ring.reset + decoder.seek + refill and publishes the landed frame (decoder.position()) for the WAIT_PRIME SEEK. The play() startSeconds seek becomes the first job's work. Risks: the jobInFlight/releasing protocol when a stream slot is released or reused while a self-rescheduling job runs; the destructor's job waits (audio_engine.cpp:1195-1200); the deterministic jobs == nullptr path the tests rely on; the audio review 5 seek regression tests. Hard because it changes producer ownership of the ring and the seek state machine across threads. *Test:* tests/audio/test_audio_engine.cpp: "audio_engine - a streamed voice survives a 0.5 s main-thread stall without an underrun". Use a 2-worker job system and a long clip: update once, simulate a stall by rendering 0.5 s on the null backend without calling update(), then check stats().underruns == 0. Add "audio_engine - a streamed seek decodes on a worker, not in update()".
- **F580** MINOR voice() mirror seqlock copies a non-atomic struct while the main thread writes it (`engine/src/audio/audio_engine.cpp:303`). The race is still there. publish_mirror (audio_engine.cpp:272-281) writes the plain voice_mirror fields while read_mirror (:296-310) copies them on another thread with `copy = entry.mirror;` (:303). After MIRROR_READ_ATTEMPTS (64, :110) failed attempts, the possibly torn copy is returned. Fixing it changes the synchronisation scheme of the any-thread voice() path, which counts as concurrency design. *Fix:* Shape: make the seqlock payload race-free under the C++ memory model. Option A: keep voice_mirror trivially copyable and store it as an array of std::atomic<u64> words, sized by a static_assert on sizeof(voice_mirror). The writer memcpys into a local buffer and does relaxed stores of each word between the odd and even sequence stores. The reader does relaxed loads of the words into a local buffer, runs the existing acquire fence and sequence re-check, then memcpys into a voice_mirror. Option B: give each field its own std::atomic. On exhausted retries, never return a copy that failed the check. Either keep the last copy that passed the check (track a bool) or return the voice's FREE/default mirror so voice() reports nullopt. Risks: voice_info holds bools, enums and padding, so the copy must cover the whole object representation with the static_assert; the cost must stay negligible (256 voices per publish_mirrors); TSan coverage of the audio tests has to be added so the fix is actually checked. Hard because this is lock-free memory-model work on a path read from any thread. *Test:* tests/audio/test_audio_engine.cpp: "audio_engine - voice() read from a worker while the main thread publishes never returns a torn snapshot". A dedicated_thread loops voice(id) while the main thread calls set_volume and update(). Check every returned volume is one of the values written. Run it under the linux-clang-tsan preset (add the audio tests to the TSan label set).

### scripting (3)

- **F069** MAJOR Entity.AddScript inside a ComponentQuery scope hits a structural assert (and fails) (`engine/src/scripting/script_runtime.cpp:417`). Still present: add_script (script_runtime.cpp:411-423) requires is_alive and calls mWorld->add<script_component> directly, even while a query scope holds the world locked (state->lock, :760). world::check_structural asserts and returns STRUCTURE_LOCKED, so Entity.AddScript inside a ComponentQuery breaks in Debug and fails in Distribution. Every other structural request is deferred (design 03 3.9, R5). A correct deferral touches query-scope flush ordering and component lifetime in a command buffer. *Fix:* Shape: when mWorld->is_locked() (or the entity is only reserved by deferred_commands()), create the set now (mStore.create_set(entity)), append the entry, and record `deferred_commands().add_raw(entity, mScriptComponentId, bytes of script_component{handle})`. The ADDED observer at the flush then adopts the pre-made set and, in PLAY, queues its creates; run_phase already flushes deferred commands before flush_pending. Hard parts: a dropped or failed deferred add, or the entity being destroyed before the flush, leaks the pre-made set (needs cleanup, e.g. in a destroyed observer or by validating at flush); an entity that already has a component but gets one added again in the same scope; reserved ids (is_alive is false until the flush); the entry index returned to managed; and component ops/destruct on raw bytes held in a buffer that may be cleared unflushed. The alternative of returning a documented STRUCTURE_LOCKED without asserting contradicts the design's 'every structural request is routed' rule, so it needs the owner's approval. *Test:* tests/scripting/test_dod_query.cpp: 'dod query - AddScript inside a query scope adds the script at query_end' (an entity without a script_component and an id reserved by entity_create inside the scope both get their script created after the flush, with no assert). Plus 'dod query - a deferred AddScript on an entity destroyed in the same scope leaks no set'.
- **F431** MINOR find_entity_by_name is a linear scan with std::string compares and an unspecified 'first' match (`engine/src/scripting/script_runtime.cpp:688`): same as F604.
- **F604** MINOR mEntityNames: unordered iteration decides Find result, stale entries leak, lookup is O(n) (`engine/src/scripting/script_runtime.cpp:677`). Still present: mEntityNames is a std::unordered_map (script_runtime.h:149), and find_entity_by_name returns the first live match in hash iteration order (script_runtime.cpp:688-695). With duplicate names the winner differs between MSVC and libstdc++ and after a rehash, and every lookup is a full string-compare scan. The leak part is covered by F430. Fixing order and cost needs a new index and a definition of 'first' (Entity.cs:82 says 'The first live entity with this name') that must agree with whatever F065 decides about scene names. *Fix:* Decide together with F065. If the runtime table stays as the ecs-only fallback, store `name_record { std::string name; u64 order; }` per entity (order from a monotonically increasing counter at set_entity_name) plus a `name hash -> small_vector<entity>` index. Find returns the live match with the lowest order (document it as 'the earliest named live entity'). Keep both maps in step in set_entity_name and in F430's destroyed observer. If F065 moves lookups to scene::find_by_name, define the same rule there and drop this table's lookup role. Risks: allocations per named entity, rename and erase bookkeeping, cross-platform determinism tests. *Test:* tests/scripting/test_script_runtime.cpp: 'script_runtime - Entity.Find with duplicate names returns the earliest named live entity' (name three entities "enemy" in a known order, destroy the first; Find returns the second regardless of insertion count or rehash, e.g. after 1,000 other names are added).

### bindings (1)

- **F682** MINOR Transform and physics setters accept NaN/Inf and unnormalised quaternions while audio, input and diagnostics reject them (`engine/src/bindings/entries_transform.cpp:125`). Still present: transform_set_local memcpys unchecked (entries_transform.cpp:124), and transform_set_world, the velocity, force, kinematic, teleport, gravity, character and shape/overlap entries of entries_physics.cpp validate no float (overlap radius `!(radius >= 0.0f)` at :283 lets +inf through), while audio, input and diagnostics use std::isfinite. The fix spans about 15 entries including the hot transform_set_local path (pinned allocation-free and fast by test_transform_bindings.cpp:224), leaves open whether non-unit quaternions are normalised or rejected and with what tolerance, and C# span writes bypass the entries entirely. *Fix:* Shape: shared helpers in entry_support.h (`finite(const conjure_vec3 &)`, `finite(const conjure_transform &)`, `usable_rotation(const conjure_quat_xyzw &)`) and INVALID_ARGUMENT on failure in transform_set_local/world and every physics setter and query that takes vectors, rotations or radii. Decide: reject vs renormalise a non-unit quaternion (a tolerance such as \|len^2 - 1\| > 1e-3 is rejected, a zero quaternion is always rejected). Measure the cost on transform_set_local. Document that ComponentQuery span writes are not validated. Risk: scripts that currently pass slightly denormalised quaternions start throwing. *Test:* tests/bindings/test_transform_bindings.cpp: "transform - non-finite values and a zero rotation are INVALID_ARGUMENT"; tests/bindings/test_physics_bindings.cpp: "physics - non-finite vectors and degenerate rotations are INVALID_ARGUMENT"; keep "transform - 10k set_local calls are allocation free" green.

### build (1)

- **F779** MINOR Host pack lookup assumes the portable RID, so distro-packaged .NET is not found and scripting is switched off with only a warning (`cmake/FindNetHost.cmake:50`). Still present: FindNetHost.cmake:50/57/70 look only at packs/Microsoft.NETCore.App.Host.<os>-<arch>, and CMakeLists.txt:75-82 turns scripting and the managed build off with a warning. The glob fallback cannot be verified on this Windows machine or in CI (actions/setup-dotnet uses the portable RID). The finding's second half, a fatal error when scripting is requested, reverses a deliberate choice that conflicts with 03_scripting.md 3.10. *Fix:* Part 1: after the portable-RID loop finds nothing, glob ${root}/packs/Microsoft.NETCore.App.Host.*-${_nethost_arch} for each root. Skip musl RIDs unless on musl, derive the RID from the directory name, and use runtimes/<rid>/native. Set NetHost_RID to the RID found, and keep the portable RID first. Part 2 is an owner decision. Either make the downgrade a FATAL_ERROR when CONJURE_WITH_SCRIPTING was set explicitly or $ENV{CI} is set, or fix 03_scripting.md 3.10, which claims "SDK missing ... is a configure error". Root CMakeLists.txt:72-74 says the opposite. Risks: - The distro pack layout and the link-ability of its libnethost.a must be checked on Ubuntu 24.04 with apt dotnet-sdk-8.0. - The export's `dotnet publish -r <rid>` may also need the distro RID. *Test:* Configure-time and Linux-only, so no unit test fits. Add a CI or nightly leg on ubuntu-24.04 that installs apt dotnet-sdk-8.0 (no setup-dotnet) and asserts the configure log reports 'Found .NET host pack ... (ubuntu.24.04-x64)' and that ctest -L dotnet runs rather than skips.

### integration (1)

- **F369** MINOR Four of the six scene benchmarks the design lists do not exist (`tests/benchmarks/bench_scene.cpp:151`). Still present: tests/benchmarks/bench_scene.cpp registers only transform_propagation_10k(_sparse), snapshot_capture_restore_10k and load_10k (lines 151, 155, 159, 177). docs/design/13_scene.md:905 also lists propagate_10k_depth8, set_parent_subtree_1k, instantiate_prefab_100 and destroy_cascade_10k. The work is several new scenarios with fixtures (a prefab document, destructive operations repeated per iteration) and budgets the design never states, so it is larger than a small test addition. *Fix:* Add four scenarios. scene.transform_propagation_10k_depth8: hierarchy_shape depth 8 with childrenPerNode 2 and enough roots for 10k. scene.set_parent_subtree_1k: alternately reparent one 1,000-entity subtree between two parents each iteration, then step_transform so apply_hierarchy_changes and the archetype moves are inside the timing. scene.instantiate_prefab_100: build a prefab with prefab_builder into the memory asset source, instantiate_many 100 under a parent, then destroy them in the same body so the scene stays bounded. scene.destroy_cascade_10k: destroy a 10k tree and run destroy_pending, rebuilding inside the body or timing the destroy phase only through add_phase, because bench_context::measure has no per-iteration untimed setup. Register each in CONJURE_BENCHMARK_SCENARIOS and add thresholds.json entries (desktop measured on the reference machine with headroom, ci = 3x desktop per 08 D10, allocs only where the design states a gate). Update the README scenario list and deviations, including the name mapping propagate_10k_flat = transform_propagation_10k. Hard parts: destructive operations under a fixed-iteration harness, prefab fixture set-up, and calibrating budgets 13_scene.md does not give. *Test:* tests/benchmarks/bench_scene.cpp: new CTest entries benchmark.scene.transform_propagation_10k_depth8, benchmark.scene.set_parent_subtree_1k, benchmark.scene.instantiate_prefab_100 and benchmark.scene.destroy_cascade_10k (label benchmark), each with a thresholds.json row.

### modules (1)

- **F035** MAJOR An optional dependency on a module that sits in a cycle still fails the dependent (`engine/src/modules/module_resolver.cpp:399`). Still present: order_nodes (module_resolver.cpp:330-338) counts the optional edge to a not-yet-failed cycle member. report_cycles' trailing loop (:397-415) then fails the dependent with DEPENDENCY_FAILED without checking depends_optionally, although fail() (:76-81) never drags optional dependents down. A correct fix reorders the resolver and must also settle cycles closed by an optional edge, where today's outcome depends on which node find_cycle starts from. That rule is not in 07. *Fix:* Shape: record cycle membership (node.onCycle) in report_cycles. After the cycle failures, clear mResult.order and every node's ordered flag and run order_nodes() again. In-degrees already exclude failed nodes, so optional dependents of failed cycle members are released and the GAME-last tie-break still holds. Keep the trailing DEPENDENCY_FAILED loop as a safety net. Decide the rule for a cycle closed by an optional edge (A optional->B, B->A). Recommended: optional edges never form cycles. Ignore them in find_cycle and in the in-degree, so A loads first and B is linked later, matching "an optional dependency loaded later is linked to the module already using it". Risks: load order changes, failure codes asserted by the existing cycle test (2 CYCLE + 3 propagated), and the result must not depend on registration order. *Test:* tests/modules/test_module_resolver.cpp: "resolve_load_order - an optional dependency on a cycle member does not fail the dependent" (a optional->b, b->c, c->b: order contains a; b is DEPENDENCY_CYCLE, c is DEPENDENCY_FAILED) and "resolve_load_order - a cycle closed by an optional edge resolves the same whatever the registration order".

### platform (1)

- **F283** MINOR input_state::modifiers copies GLFW's per-event mods, which differ between Win32 and X11 for the modifier keys themselves (`engine/src/platform/input.cpp:177`). Still present. input.cpp:177 and 222 copy the event modifiers, and glfw_window.cpp:862 passes GLFW's mods through. GLFW X11 builds them from xkey.state, which is the state before the event (x11_window.c:1246, 1318), while Win32 uses GetKeyState, which reflects the event itself. A lone Shift tap therefore leaves SHIFT set on X11. The same stale mods also drive ImGui (imgui_layer.cpp:279-282). The right layer is debatable: normalising in input::apply_key changes inject() semantics and leaves ImGui wrong, while normalising in the glfw backend fixes both but cannot be exercised in CI and needs verification on X11 and Wayland hardware. *Fix:* Normalise in glfw_window::on_key, as Dear ImGui's GLFW backend does for X11. If the key is LEFT/RIGHT_SHIFT, CONTROL, ALT or SUPER, set the matching key_modifier_flags bit on PRESS and REPEAT. On RELEASE, clear it unless glfwGetKey(window, other-side key) == GLFW_PRESS; GLFW updates window->keys before the callback. Keep CAPS_LOCK and NUM_LOCK as reported. To make it testable headless, put the pure rule in a platform free function, e.g. `key_modifier_flags apply_own_modifier(key, button_action, key_modifier_flags, bool otherSideDown)` in key.h, and call it from on_key. That is a small public API addition. Risks: Wayland mods arrive in a separate wl_keyboard.modifiers event, so behaviour there needs checking. Do not also derive modifiers from keysDown in input, which would override authored modifiers in injected events. *Test:* tests/platform/test_key.cpp: "key - a modifier key's own press sets and its release clears its modifier flag". It covers LEFT_SHIFT PRESS with NONE giving SHIFT, LEFT_SHIFT RELEASE with SHIFT giving NONE, LEFT_SHIFT RELEASE with otherSideDown keeping SHIFT, and a non-modifier key passing its flags through unchanged. Add a manual X11 check: tap Shift alone, and snapshot().modifiers must be NONE.

## Decided

These findings needed the owner's call. All of them were decided on 2026-11-13; the decision for each, with its
effort, is in [the decisions page](code_sweep_2026-11-12_decisions.md). The findings themselves are listed below.

### multi (60)

- **F119** BLOCKER The [downloaded] decoder case fails (not skips) as soon as the documented asset download has run, which CI does on every leg (`tests/audio/test_decoder.cpp:404`): same as F194.
- **F194** BLOCKER [downloaded] decoder case fails (not skips) on every CI leg once the asset download succeeds (`tests/audio/test_decoder.cpp:416`). Still present, and a CI blocker. The case runs in the default ctest set: the labels are unit;audio from ConjureTesting.cmake:214, with no downloaded label. ci.yml:105-106 downloads before ctest, and download_test_assets.py:134-135 writes tests/.assets_ready when every required asset is present. The case then hits REQUIRE(exists("audio/tone.ogg")) (test_decoder.cpp:415-416). The docs disagree on the fix: 09_cross_area_resolutions.md:425 and audio.md:358-359 claim the case skips, while 08_testing.md:59-60 lists tone.ogg as an in-repo fixture under tests/assets. Making the case skip instead would also be flagged, because the nightly skip policy does not allow downloaded skips (ctest_check_skips.py:303). *Fix:* Option 1, recommended (matches 08_testing.md:60): generate short self-made tones (1 s, 440 Hz, mono, a few KB each) with an encoder such as ffmpeg and commit them as tests/assets/audio/tone.{ogg,mp3,flac}; being self-made, they carry no licence question. In test_decoder.cpp, read them from the repo asset root (environment().repoAssetRoot / the repo-asset helper in test_assets.h), drop CONJURE_TEST_REQUIRE(DOWNLOADED_ASSETS) and the [downloaded] tag, and rename the case to "decoder - ogg, mp3 and flac decode to the expected length within 1 %". The Vorbis path then runs on every leg. Remove the placeholder entry from tools/test_assets.json, and correct 09 item 13(d) and audio.md:328/358-359. Option 2: list CC0 tone clips from a stable source in tools/test_assets.json, pin them in tools/test_assets.lock and correct the same docs. This needs a source vetted for licence and stability, and keeps CI dependent on that host. Option 3, interim only: skip the case when the specific files are missing. This hides the Vorbis path and conflicts with the nightly skip policy, so it is not recommended. Whichever option is chosen, 08_testing D12's separate conjure_downloaded_tests executable (08_testing.md:65) does not exist; whether [downloaded] cases move there is a separate build-structure decision.
- **F015** MAJOR glfw content_scale() is the monitor DPI scale, not framebuffer/window as the contract says; the conformance case fails at DPI != 100 % (`engine/src/platform/glfw/glfw_window.cpp:364`). Still present. window.h:137-139, P9 and windows-and-events.md:142 define content_scale() as framebuffer_size()/window_size(), and the null backend implements that ratio. glfw_window.cpp:369-375 returns glfwGetWindowContentScale instead, which is DPI/96 on Win32 and X11, where the framebuffer equals the window size under SCALE_TO_MONITOR. At 150 % the conformance case at test_backend_conformance.cpp:206 fails. content_scale_changed_event (glfw_window.cpp:953-961) carries the DPI value too. Today no engine code reads either value (imgui_layer.cpp:238 computes its own ratio), so the owner must choose which meaning the name carries before the backends can be aligned. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): keep the documented ratio. glfw content_scale() returns framebuffer_size()/window_size() per axis, keeping the last non-degenerate ratio (initially 1) while minimised at 0 x 0. content_scale_changed_event is emitted from the pump when that ratio changes, not from glfwSetWindowContentScaleCallback. The OS UI scale stays available through monitors()[i].contentScale, plus a separate `ui_scale()` / `dpi_scale()` query added when DPI-aware editor fonts arrive. This change is confined to the glfw backend and the docs, and the null backend and conformance test stay as they are. Option B: redefine content_scale() as the OS UI-scale hint, as GLFW defines it. Rewrite window.h, P9, the API page and the handbook; make the null backend model the framebuffer ratio and the UI scale independently (the framebuffer from a new nullFramebufferScale); and change the conformance case to compare framebuffer/window with that ratio. Either way, P9's claim that SCALE_TO_MONITOR + SCALE_FRAMEBUFFER make high-DPI 'behave the same' on all OSes must be corrected.
- **F075** MAJOR A --headless run can never execute scripts, contradicting design 06 section 7 (servers) (`engine/src/engine/application_desc.cpp:43`). Still present: application_desc.cpp:43 copies headless_defaults' scripting.enabled = false, project.cpp:598-607 applies the project's scripting.enabled only when not headless, and the command line only has --no-scripting (command_line.cpp:131), so `--headless --project game` never runs C#, while 06_engine_loop.md:825/830 promise fully functional headless scripting and a `--headless` server mode. Which default a headless run should take is a product choice: enabling it makes every headless boot of a scripted project need .NET and the game assembly (scripting.required defaults to true). *Fix:* Option A (recommended): add a `--scripting` flag (options.scripting, applied last in apply_command_line as config.scripting.enabled = true) so a headless server or smoke test opts in explicitly, keep the dependency-free headless default, and update 06 section 7 'Servers' to `--headless --scripting --audio off --fixed-frame-delta 0`, runtime/README.md:38 and docs/api/runtime/index.md to use `--headless --scripting` for exports of scripted games. Option B: under headless take the project's scripting.enabled (project::apply_to outside the !headless block) and keep headless_defaults off only without a project; simpler for users but turns every headless test/CI boot of a scripted project into a .NET dependency and changes current behaviour of tests/runtime. Either way add a runtime test that boots a scripted project headless.
- **F089** MAJOR File-system commands run during play are permanent and un-undoable although the warning says play.stop discards them (`engine/src/editor/core/play_controller.cpp:191`). Still present. record_outcome drops every inverse while the undo stack is frozen (command_dispatcher.cpp:513-516), and the warning says 'play.stop discards it' (play_controller.cpp:191). play.stop restores only the scene snapshot, so disk-level effects of asset.*, script.*, prefab.* and project.set_settings persist and cannot be undone. D9 (04_editor.md:49) says mutations during play execute unrecorded and does not cover file effects. Choosing between refusing these commands, recording them, or rewording is a design call (live script.write during play is a real workflow). *Fix:* Options: (a) flag the file-mutating commands EDITING_ONLY so they answer WRONG_PLAY_STATE during play (simple, but blocks editing scripts while playing); (b) add a command flag (e.g. OUTSIDE_SCENE) for commands whose effects live on disk, have record_outcome record their inverses even while frozen since they do not reference scene state, and send a separate warning saying these persist after play.stop; (c) only correct the warning text, ai-protocol.md:81-83 and D9 to say file changes made during play persist and cannot be undone. Recommendation: (b) with the doc changes from (c); fall back to (c) if undo during play must stay untouched.
- **F166** MAJOR NOT_IN_PHASE is declared, mapped in C# and promised by the design but no bindings entry ever returns it (`engine/src/bindings/native_api_fill.cpp:372`). Still present: check_main_thread (native_api_fill.cpp:372-386) tests only core::jobs::is_main_thread(); no bindings entry returns NOT_IN_PHASE although 15_bindings.md (:401, :592, :621), 03_scripting.md:451/:647 and script_runtime.h:449 promise it. The literal rule (current_phase() != COUNT) would break legitimate calls: OnCreate/OnDestroy run by the flushes of frame_begin, frame_end and set_mode run with no phase (script_runtime.h:229-231). Managed installs no SynchronizationContext (no hit in managed/), so Task continuations land off the main thread and already get WRONG_THREAD. *Fix:* Options: (a) Implement the design literally (`current_phase() != COUNT`): breaks every OnCreate/OnDestroy that touches a Transform during the FRAME_BEGIN/FRAME_END/set_mode flushes. Not recommended. (b) Add a 'managed callback in progress' depth counter to script_runtime (raised around run_phase, the flush callbacks, collision dispatch and set_mode) and have the bindings guard return NOT_IN_PHASE when it is zero: catches stray main-thread calls without breaking the flushes, but spans scripting, bindings and docs. (c) Keep thread-only guards and amend the docs: 15_bindings.md 3.2/5/6 and the B11 note, 03_scripting.md:451/:647 and the script_runtime.h:449 comment say bindings check the thread only, and NOT_IN_PHASE stays reserved in bindings_error.inl (removing it would renumber the ABI codes) and in use by scripting's query_begin (script_runtime.cpp:719). Recommendation: (c), since without a main-thread SynchronizationContext there is no realistic main-thread call outside a callback; revisit (b) if one is ever added.
- **F187** MAJOR Several protocol commands are unconfined file/code primitives although module.build was deliberately hardened (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_editor.cpp:254`). Still present. commands_modules.cpp:512-525 hardens module.build because 'a localhost protocol client must not be able to run arbitrary programs'. Yet script.compile passes args.configuration unvalidated (commands_script.cpp:571), script.reload loads any assembly path (596), editor.exec_file and asset.import read any absolute path (commands_editor.cpp:254, commands_asset.cpp:233-234), project.export resolves relative paths against the process CWD, and module_manifest joins manifest.binary unvalidated (modules area). The protocol's trust model is not written down anywhere, and the fixes span editor and modules. *Fix:* Options: (a) a client is as trusted as the local user (localhost plus the optional --ai-token): document that in ai-protocol.md and fix only the inconsistencies; (b) honour the stated rule 'no arbitrary programs' and confine code-loading arguments to the project, while reads and exports may name any path; (c) confine every path argument to the project root unless the session presented the token or the path is on an allow-list. Recommendation: (b). Validate script.compile `configuration` with is_safe_tool_argument; confine script.reload `assembly` and module.scan directories to the project root; restrict manifest.binary to a plain file name with no separators or '..' (modules); resolve relative project.export and editor.exec_file paths against the project root; document the model and the token advice in ai-protocol.md. Reading files for asset.import and editor.exec_file stays allowed by design.
- **F200** MAJOR No LICENSE, NOTICE or third-party attribution file anywhere, and the export ships none (`README.md:40`). Still present: README.md:40-42 'Copyright (c) 2026. All rights reserved.' names no holder, no LICENSE/NOTICE/THIRD_PARTY file is tracked, and export_pipeline.cpp steps 1-6 (copies at :333-357) ship no notices, although MIT/zlib/Apache dependencies linked into conjure_shared require their notices in binary distributions; tonemap.hlsli:9 and :35 credit borrowed fits without licence text. Naming the holder and the project's own licence terms is the owner's decision, and the shipping part crosses root docs, engine export code and shaders. *Fix:* Options: (A) proprietary: LICENSE naming the holder with 'All rights reserved' terms; (B) an open-source licence of the owner's choice. In either case: add THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md built from each pinned dependency's licence file (GLFW zlib, glm MIT, yyjson MIT, stb MIT/PD, cgltf MIT, miniaudio+stb_vorbis PD/MIT-0, NVRHI MIT, DirectX-Headers MIT / Vulkan-Headers Apache-2.0 OR MIT, Jolt MIT, .NET runtime MIT plus its notices; ImGui/ImGuizmo MIT editor-only; doctest/glslang build-only); make export step 5 write THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt (runtime subset, no ImGui) into the output with a test in tests/engine/test_export.cpp ('export_pipeline - the output carries THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt'); add licence/source lines to tonemap.hlsli:9 (Hill ACES fit, MIT via BakingLab) and :35 (Wrensch AgX approximation). Recommendation: the owner names the holder and licence first; the notices file and export step can then land as an engine-owner change.
- **F136** MINOR Nightly TSan run excludes every module that actually has cross-thread code beyond the job system (`CMakePresets.json:128`). Still present: CMakePresets.json:127-128 restricts linux-clang-tsan to core\|ecs\|scene\|engine\|integration, so assets, audio, physics, renderer and editor thread code never runs under TSan. That scope is the documented design (08_testing.md D13 at :39 and :726, 'no suppression files'), so widening it, and possibly needing a suppression for the audio seqlock, is a design change. *Fix:* Options: (A) widen the include filter to core\|ecs\|scene\|engine\|integration\|assets\|audio\|physics\|renderer\|editor, update nightly.yml:2 and :146 comments, 08_testing.md D13 and :726, ci.md:99-101; fix the races it reports (the asset-type-registry and audio seqlock findings) rather than suppress, or amend the 'no suppression files' rule; (B) drop the include filter and exclude only labels that cannot run under TSan; (C) keep D13's scope. Recommendation: A, introduced module by module (assets and audio first, where races are already suspected), because a red nightly does not gate PRs and those modules have the most cross-thread code. Hard parts: Jolt's job system and miniaudio's device thread may produce reports that need upstream-aware triage.
- **F151** MINOR scene::layer does not drive render layer masks although design and wiki say it does (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/renderer_subsystem.cpp:306`). Still present: extraction copies item.layerMask = meshRenderer.layerMask (renderer_subsystem.cpp:306) and never reads scene::layer, and the sprite loop (:327-342) carries no mask at all. Yet 13_scene.md:67 and docs/api/scene/scenes-and-hierarchy.md:67 say bit 1 << layer matches the renderer's layer masks. The suggested 'OR the bit in' would not even fix the scenario, because the default mesh mask bit 0 stays set. Choosing what layer means for rendering, and whether sprites get masks, is a product decision. *Fix:* Options. (A) Correct the docs: 13_scene.md 2.1 and the wiki row say scene::layer is a grouping index for scripts, queries and the editor. Render visibility comes from mesh_renderer_component::layerMask against camera_component::layerMask; sprites are not masked. (B) Make scene::layer authoritative: the extracted mask becomes 1u << layer_of(entity) when the entity has a scene::layer, otherwise the mesh's own mask; add a layerMask to renderer::sprite and cull sprites by it. This is a renderer change. (C) Use both, mesh mask AND scene layer bit, which needs a defined rule for the default. Recommendation: (A) now, as a docs-only fix. Optionally rename sprite_component::layer to sortLayer later, which is a separate public API and file-format decision.
- **F171** MINOR The null command list tracks mOpen but never enforces it: recording on a closed or never-opened list, double open and draw without state all succeed (`engine/src/rhi/backends/null/null_command_list.cpp:34`). Accurate: null_command_list::open/close (null_command_list.cpp:34-49) only flip mOpen, and no recording method or draw checks it. But design 02_renderer.md R17 defines the null command list as a recorder of counters. The misuse checks the finding asks for already exist in nvrhi::validation (requireOpenState, the 'already open' check and the m_GraphicsStateSet check in validation-commandlist.cpp), and device.cpp:112-113 layers that over the null device whenever enableDebugLayer is set (tests/rhi/test_device.cpp:78). The gap is that the headless renderer tests create the null device without it (renderer_test_support.cpp:69-71). Whether to turn that on or to duplicate the checks is a test-strategy choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) Keep the null list a recorder and run the renderer and engine null-device tests through NVRHI validation by setting enableDebugLayer = true in renderer_support::null_device_desc(). The renderer.null_frame_10k allocation gate keeps the raw device. This may surface latent misuse in renderer tests and adds validation-layer overhead to those suites. (b) Duplicate the checks in null_command_list as the finding suggests. That needs about 40 recording methods to check mOpen, Errors for double open and double close, and draw*/dispatch* to require set*State since open(). It is rhi-only but re-implements nvrhi::validation. (c) Leave the code alone and document in the rhi README and handbook that misuse checks on the null device need enableDebugLayer. Recommendation: (a) plus the one-line documentation from (c).
- **F209** MINOR parse_log_level returns OFF for unknown names, so every caller has to re-detect the error (`engine/include/conjure/core/log.h:50`). The behaviour is real (log.cpp:53 returns OFF for unknown names) but documented as intended (log.h:49 and docs/api/core/logging-and-assertions.md:93), and all three callers already handle it correctly: command_line.cpp:174 accepts a real 'off', and both editor commands deliberately reject 'off' as a minimum filter level. Changing the return type is a public API redesign across core, engine and editor. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep as is; it is documented and every caller validates. (b) Change to `std::optional<log_level> parse_log_level(std::string_view)` (nullopt for unknown names), optionally also accept 'ERR' as an alias of ERROR, and migrate command_line.cpp:173-176, commands_console.cpp:16-19, commands_session.cpp:55-58 and test_log.cpp:160-165 plus the API page. (c) Add a `try_parse_log_level` returning optional next to the existing function. Recommendation: (b) at the next API-touching wave, since there are only three callers and it removes the footgun. Otherwise (a).
- **F212** MINOR non_copyable / non_movable are used nowhere and are untested; the style exception that excuses the missing test is factually wrong (`engine/include/conjure/core/non_copyable.h:10`). Partly wrong: non_copyable/non_movable are unused inside the engine, but they are documented public API for game code (docs/api/core/data-types.md:17, 106-116, 194), so 'dead code' is not established. The real defects are the missing test and the inaccurate style exception at tools/style_exceptions.json:4. Deleting versus keeping a documented user API is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the API: add tests/core/foundation/test_non_copyable.cpp with static_asserts (derived types: !is_copy_constructible, !is_copy_assignable, is_move_constructible/assignable for non_copyable; all four false for non_movable; is_empty for both) and remove the 'conjure/core/non_copyable.h' entry from tools/style_exceptions.json (check_style then finds the mirror test). This spans core and tools. (b) Delete the header and every reference: foundation CMakeLists.txt:11, the core and foundation READMEs, docs/api/core/data-types.md, docs/api/index.md, the handbook pages, 10_core.md:30, repository-layout.md:88 and the style exception. Recommendation: (a), since the API is documented for users and the change is tiny.
- **F213** MINOR same_rotation's epsilon is a cosine tolerance, about 2800 times looser than the component epsilon it shares a name and default with (`engine/include/conjure/core/math/glm_types.h:104`). same_rotation (glm_types.h:100-106) tests \|dot\| >= 1 - epsilon as its doc says, so the default 1e-6 accepts rotations about 0.16 degrees apart, much looser than nearly_equal's component epsilon. Physics passes one epsilon to both (physics_world_sync.cpp:35-40) and relies on the default in shape_builder.cpp:91. Changing the semantics changes physics sync and shape behaviour and every same_rotation tolerance in tests/core/math/test_transform.cpp. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the cosine semantics, rename the parameter to `dotTolerance`, and document that 1 - \|dot\| <= tolerance, i.e. an angle of about 2*sqrt(2*tolerance) (0.16 degrees for 1e-6). Let physics separately decide whether pose_differs / is-identity need a tighter rotation tolerance (core docs only, plus an optional physics follow-up); (b) redefine as component-wise after sign alignment (`nearly_equal(a, dot(a,b) < 0 ? -b : b, epsilon)`): consistent units, but it changes physics sync thresholds and requires recalibrating test_transform's TIGHT/LOOSE epsilons; (c) add an explicit `same_rotation_angle(a, b, maxRadians)` and move physics to it. Recommendation: (a) now and (c) for physics, so existing behaviour does not shift silently.
- **F214** MINOR 'Everything outside STL containers goes through tracked_allocate' is contradicted by core's own containers (`engine/include/conjure/core/memory/tracked_allocation.h:11`). Still present: tracked_allocation.h:11-15 and docs/design/10_core.md:170-172 say all engine heap memory outside STL containers goes through tracked_allocate. But small_vector.h:588, stable_vector and flat_hash_map.h:528-529 use std::allocator, the design note names aligned_alloc where the code uses posix_memalign, and containers/README.md 'Allocation' presents tracked allocation as not yet wired in. Whether containers should be tracked is an open design choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) reword: tracked_allocation.h line 11 to 'outside the standard library and the core containers (which allocate through std::allocator from isolated allocation points)', and in 10_core.md 2.5 the same plus 'aligned via _aligned_malloc / posix_memalign'. Docs only, but they span core and docs/design. (b) Route the three container allocation points through tracked_allocate/tracked_free with alignof(T): containers become visible to memory::counters() in shipping executables, but tests that count operator new through tracked_global_new.cpp and allocation-gate tests change behaviour, and per-allocation overhead needs a benchmark. Recommendation: (a) now, and record (b) as wave work if container memory is wanted in the stats.
- **F219** MINOR A missing or misspelled core area is silently skipped at configure time (`engine/src/core/CMakeLists.txt:46`). Still present: engine/src/core/CMakeLists.txt:43-48 skips a missing area with a STATUS line, and lines 8-9 keep the bring-up comment. However docs/handbook/build-pipeline.md:44-46 documents this as intended for both core areas and engine modules ('Missing directories print a status line and are skipped, so partial trees configure'), and engine/CMakeLists.txt:26-31 does the same for modules. The 09_cross_area_resolutions.md:148 'fail hard' decision is about listed source files, not areas. Dropping a documented behaviour is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: (a) Fail hard for core areas only: replace the else branch at core/CMakeLists.txt:46-47 with `message(FATAL_ERROR "core: area '${area}' is listed in CONJURE_CORE_AREAS but ${areaDir}/CMakeLists.txt does not exist")`, delete comment lines 8-9, and reword build-pipeline.md:45-46 so the skip applies to engine modules only. (b) The same, plus engine/CMakeLists.txt:29-30 for engine modules (all 14 exist), dropping the 'partial trees configure' sentence and the bring-up comment at engine/CMakeLists.txt:1-2. (c) Keep skipping and only refresh the stale comment. Recommendation: (b). Every area and module now exists, so a silent skip can only hide a typo or a lost file, which later shows up as unrelated link errors.
- **F233** MINOR Design doc, CMake comment and code disagree about who may create threads (`engine/src/core/system/process_windows.cpp:364`). Still present. docs/design/10_core.md:15 (C4, binding) says the pipe reader "is a jobs::dedicated_thread". process_windows.cpp:364-368 and process_posix.cpp:309-312 use plain std::thread and call that "the documented exception". engine/src/core/jobs/CMakeLists.txt:2 and jobs/README.md:4-5 still claim jobs is the only creator of threads. engine/src/core/system/README.md:118-122 already flags this as "a conflict for the architect to settle", so the owner has to choose. *Fix:* Option (a): amend C4 to "no std::thread outside jobs, except system::process's pipe readers, which are plain std::threads because C2 keeps system independent of jobs". Then name that exception in core/jobs CMakeLists.txt:2 and README.md:4-5, drop the "conflict for the architect" sentence from core/system README, and leave the code as is. Option (b): amend C2 so system may depend on jobs (no cycle: jobs depends only on foundation, containers and memory) and replace the reader std::threads with jobs::dedicated_thread. Readers then get debugger and profiler names, at the cost of a new dependency and the jthread toolchain requirement for system. Option (c): move thread naming (core/jobs/thread_name) into foundation and name the plain readers. Recommendation: (a). It is a wording-only change in docs/design and core comments, and it matches the reasoning already in the code; take (c) later only if unnamed reader threads hurt profiling.
- **F241** MINOR to_native() contradicts the binding design: it does not reject root escapes (`engine/include/conjure/core/vfs/native_file_system.h:42`). Still present: native_file_system.h:41-45 documents to_native as unchecked ('without checking that it stays inside the root'), and native_file_system.cpp:166-177 confirms it. docs/design/10_core.md:331 contradicts itself: it declares a plain `std::filesystem::path to_native(...)` but comments that it 'rejects ".." escapes with PERMISSION_DENIED', which a plain path cannot report. No code outside core calls to_native, so either reading can be adopted cheaply, but it has to be chosen. *Fix:* Options: (a) Make it checked. Public `[[nodiscard]] result<std::filesystem::path> to_native(std::string_view path) const` returns `resolve(path)`, so escapes and absolute paths give PERMISSION_DENIED. Rename the current body to a private `native_path_of(std::string_view relative) const`, used by resolve/resolve_writable and the internal calls at native_file_system.cpp:258, 312-313, 369, 392 and 409. Update tests lines 65-70, 94 and 123 to unwrap the result, add an escape case, and update the files.md:258 row and the 10_core.md:331 signature. This edits core and docs/design. (b) Keep it unchecked: fix only 10_core.md:331 to say to_native is an unchecked mapping and that the root-escape check lives in the file_system methods (docs owner). Recommendation: (a). The class advertises a sandbox (header lines 13-14, the vfs README, the handbook), an unchecked public mapper is a footgun for code that passes the result to process/dynamic_library, and nothing outside core has to adapt.
- **F259** MINOR add_system accepts duplicate system names; after/before then bind to whichever was registered first (`engine/src/ecs/scheduler.cpp:49`). Still present: add_system (scheduler.cpp:49-95) never looks for an existing name, and position_of in resolve_edges (scheduler_stages.cpp:48-55) binds an edge to the first match. Accepting duplicates is currently relied on: tests/scene/test_register.cpp:114-117 registers the scene systems twice and expects 8 systems ('the systems are simply added again'). The ECS design (01_ecs.md 3.5) does not say whether names are unique, so the owner has to pick the contract. *Fix:* Options: (a) Reject duplicates. In add_system, after the phase check, return make_error(ecs_error::NAME_CONFLICT, "system '<name>' already exists in phase <phase>") when desc.name is not null and an alive record of the same phase has the same name. Update the scheduler.h add_system summary and the system section of docs/api/ecs/queries-views-and-systems.md, and change tests/scene/test_register.cpp:114-117 to expect NAME_CONFLICT from the second register_systems with system_count() staying at 4. (b) Keep duplicates but make an after/before name bind to every system that carries it (position_of collects all matches), so the ordering is consistent and double registration still runs twice. (c) Keep first-match binding, log a WARN on a duplicate add, and document it. Recommendation: (a).
