The name is the system's identity for ordering, timings and profiling, a duplicate is almost always an accidental double registration, and NAME_CONFLICT already exists for the same rule on component names. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_scheduler.cpp: "scheduler - a duplicate name in one phase is NAME_CONFLICT". The same name in another phase and two unnamed systems are still accepted, and an after edge to the name orders against the surviving system.
- **F281** MINOR 'Windowed fullscreen' (P12) keeps GLFW_AUTO_ICONIFY on, so the fullscreen window minimises on focus loss (`engine/src/platform/glfw/glfw_window.cpp:169`). Still present. initialise() at glfw_window.cpp:174-187 never sets GLFW_AUTO_ICONIFY, and the GLFW default is TRUE. GLFW iconifies a monitor-attached window on WM_KILLFOCUS (win32_window.c:613) and on X11 FocusOut (x11_window.c:1779), so the window minimises when focus moves away. The simple fix is not obviously right, though: GLFW places monitor windows HWND_TOPMOST (win32_window.c:432, 1903). With auto-iconify off, an alt-tab on a single monitor would leave the game covering the app the user switched to. Choosing the fullscreen behaviour is a product decision that P12 does not settle. *Fix:* Option A: add glfwWindowHint(GLFW_AUTO_ICONIFY, GLFW_FALSE). This is one line, and the window stays visible when focus goes to another monitor, but on Win32 it stays topmost over alt-tabbed apps on the same monitor. Option B: real borderless fullscreen. Make the window undecorated, size it to the monitor rect and do not attach it via glfwSetWindowMonitor, then restore decoration and the windowed rect on exit. This matches the glossary ('a borderless window covering the monitor') and the comment at rhi swapchain_d3d12.cpp:114. It is a larger change and must be checked against DXGI flip-model fullscreen optimisations. Option C: keep the minimise-on-focus-loss behaviour and document it in P12 and the API page. I recommend B, with C (documenting the current behaviour) as the interim step. I advise against A alone because of the topmost behaviour.
- **F298** MINOR Debug builds always enable D3D12 GPU-based validation, not just the debug layer (`engine/src/engine/subsystems/rhi_subsystem.cpp:66`). Still present: rhi_subsystem.cpp:65-66 maps config.rendering.gpuValidation to both enableDebugLayer and enableGpuValidation. engine_config.cpp:31 forces it on in Debug, and project.cpp:591 ORs it with the project value, so a Debug run always gets GPU-based validation. 06_engine_loop.md:187 specifies only one gpuValidation flag 'forced on in Debug'. Splitting it adds a config field, a project.json key and a CLI flag, which is a public API and file-format choice the design does not settle. *Fix:* Options. (A) Keep gpuValidation = debug layer (and the Vulkan validation layers). Add rendering.gpuBasedValidation (default false, project key gpu_based_validation, CLI --gpu-based-validation) mapped to enableGpuValidation. Document in rhi/device.h that the D3D12 debug layer is process-wide once enabled. (B) Keep the status quo and document the startup cost. (C) Never request GPU-based validation from the engine (enableGpuValidation = false) and leave it to test code. Recommendation: (A). It keeps Debug runs usable and GBV available on demand. Update 06 2.3 and the wiki row with it.
- **F311** MINOR The built-in binary importer claims '.bin', so every glTF buffer becomes a second asset (`engine/src/assets/binary_asset.cpp:11`). Still present: binary_asset.cpp:11 claims ".bin", gltf_importer.cpp:996 reads external buffers as side files, and asset_database never skips files that are recorded side files. Every glTF .bin therefore also becomes a binary_asset with its own meta and blob. Fixing it is a product choice: the public docs use "assets://data/loot.bin" as the binary_asset example (docs/api/assets/index.md:112), and runtime, engine, bindings and integration tests all import .bin files as binary data. *Fix:* Options: (a) Drop ".bin" from binary_importer::EXTENSIONS and keep .dat/.bytes/.raw. Update the docs example and extension list, test_binary_asset (size 3, first ".dat"), and rename the .bin fixtures in the listed tests to .bytes. Simple and predictable, but projects already using .bin data files lose auto-import. (b) Keep .bin and have asset_database skip, or remove, records for files that owners_of_side_file reports. Side files are only known after the owner's first import, so this needs ordering and cleanup of already-created metas and records; more complex, but it also fixes other side-file types. (c) Keep it as is and exclude side-file records from includeAll packs only (a partial fix). Recommendation: (a) now, with a note in the docs that glTF buffers use .bin and raw data should use .bytes; consider (b) later if more side-file formats collide.
- **F332** MINOR The Distribution unit tests are never run by CI (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml:200`). Still present: nightly.yml:199-200 runs only `-L "process\|runtime"` on the Distribution tree and ci.yml has no Distribution leg, although CMakePresets.json defines windows-msvc-dist / linux-gcc-dist test presets. That scope is exactly what docs/design/08_testing.md:727 specifies for distribution-export, so running the full suite in Distribution is a change of the testing design, not a slip. *Fix:* Options: (A) add a step 'Distribution unit tests' to distribution-export after the build: `ctest --preset ${{ matrix.dist-preset }} --output-junit junit_dist.xml` followed by `python tools/ctest_check_skips.py --build-dir build/${{ matrix.dist-preset }} --junit build/${{ matrix.dist-preset }}/junit_dist.xml --allow-tag window --allow-tag audio_device --allow-tag downloaded` (plus --allow-tag gpu on Linux), and update 08_testing.md:727 and ci.md:162; expect a first red run because this suite has never run without asserts; (B) keep the design's process\|runtime scope and say in ci.md that the dist test presets are for local use. The other unexercised presets (windows-msvc-asan, windows-vs, macos-dev, macos-dist) should be documented as local-only. Recommendation: A, because code that relies on an assert side effect is only caught there and a red nightly does not gate pull requests.
- **F339** MINOR export_layout ships the whole bin/<triplet>/ directory, including import libraries, .exp, .ilk and .pdb files (`engine/src/modules/module_manager.cpp:483`). Still present: module_manager.cpp:481-485 copies the whole bin/<triplet>/ directory recursively, and ConjureModule.cmake:314-317 puts RUNTIME, LIBRARY, ARCHIVE and PDB output in that one directory. The handbook (importing-and-distributing.md, "copied as a whole (binary and companions)") and 07 3.7 describe the current behaviour. Which files ship, PDBs in particular, is a product choice. *Fix:* Option (a): copy only shared libraries (*.dll, *.so, *.so.N, *.dylib), which drops debug symbols and any data file a module keeps in bin. Option (b), recommended: copy everything except link and debug by-products (.lib, .exp, .ilk, .a, .pdb and *.dSYM directories). This keeps dependent DLLs and data files. Iterate the directory without throwing, use copy_file per entry and copy subdirectories except *.dSYM. Option (c): send ARCHIVE and PDB output to a sibling directory in conjure_add_game_module. Not recommended: shadow_copy.cpp:81 expects <stem>.pdb next to the DLL, and debuggers find symbols there. Whichever is chosen, update the handbook line and 07 3.7.
- **F353** MINOR deferred_set_parent is unused in production while four documents call it the bindings' route (`engine/src/scene/scene.cpp:301`). Still present. No code outside scene and tests calls deferred_set_parent; the bindings queue a scene_fixup and call scene.set_parent after the scope (native_api_fill.cpp:460-462). The scene README:60, 13_scene.md:314/839, 15_bindings.md:420/678 and 09 section 4 item 10 still describe it as the bindings' route. Choosing which route is the contract (and whether the API stays) is an owner decision across scene, bindings and docs. *Fix:* Option (a): switch bindings transform_set_parent* inside a query scope to scene::deferred_set_parent, keeping ordering consistent with the other scene_fixups. Option (b): delete deferred_set_parent, mExpectedRepairs and the hasIntent repair entries with their tests, and correct the four documents and risk item 10. Option (c): keep deferred_set_parent as the sanctioned route for C++ systems that reparent while the world is locked (they have a command buffer and no other way), and correct the documents to say the bindings use the scene_fixup route, so risk item 10 does not apply to them. Recommendation: (c). It removes the false documentation without throwing away the only locked-scope reparent for C++ systems.
- **F362** MINOR duplicate_subtree cannot place a copy at root level (`engine/src/scene/scene.cpp:191`). Present as documented: scene.cpp:191 maps a null parent to the source's own parent, and both scene.h:272-273 and 13_scene.md:556 state that on purpose. In-tree callers use the default (bindings entries_entity.cpp:353, editor commands_entity.cpp:361, tests/integration). Only tests/scene/test_scene.cpp:701 passes NULL_ENTITY explicitly to mean the same parent. Which meaning a null parent should carry is an API design choice. *Fix:* Option (a): keep the documented behaviour; a copy to the root costs one extra set_parent. Option (b): change the parameter to 'std::optional<ecs::entity> parent = std::nullopt', where nullopt means the same parent and NULL_ENTITY means the scene root. Defaulted callers are unaffected, but explicit NULL_ENTITY callers change meaning (only test_scene.cpp:701 in-tree). Option (c): add 'bool sameParent' or a separate duplicate_subtree_to(root, parent, index). Recommendation: (b), updating test 701 to pass std::nullopt, the header summary, docs/api/scene/scenes-and-hierarchy.md:157 and 13_scene.md:556.
- **F363** MINOR scene::layer and layer_table names have no consumer; file values are not range-checked (`engine/include/conjure/scene/components.h:172`). Still present. components.h:177 says the layer bit matches the renderer's layerMask, but renderer_subsystem.cpp:233/306 copies camera.layerMask and mesh_renderer.layerMask and nothing reads scene::layer. The .max = 31.0 in components.h:322 is reflection metadata only: nothing under engine/src/core/reflect enforces min/max, so a file index of 200 loads. Whether the entity layer should drive render visibility (Unity style) or stay a gameplay label next to mesh_renderer.layerMask (02_renderer.md:78, default 1) is a product choice the design does not settle. *Fix:* Option (a), label only: reword components.h:177 to 'Gameplay layer 0..31 (find_by_layer, layer_of); names live in layer_table. Render visibility is mesh_renderer.layerMask / camera_component.layerMask, which use the same bit numbering.' Make the same change in 13_scene.md:67 and the API page. Option (b), render-driving: the engine's renderer_subsystem extraction derives item.layerMask from (1u << layer.index) when the entity carries scene::layer. This is a cross-area change in engine and needs a precedence rule against mesh_renderer.layerMask. Either way, add range enforcement: in scene::instantiate_archive's pre-checks (scene_link_pass.cpp, next to the depth loop), reject any entry whose conjure.scene.layer index >= layer_table::LAYER_COUNT with scene_error::BAD_FORMAT naming the entry. That covers scene loads, prefab instantiation, snapshot restore and duplicate. Recommendation: (a) plus the range check, because (b) creates two competing masks.
- **F384** MINOR render_target's depth texture is allocated but never rendered to or read (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:439`). Still present: renderer.cpp:439-458 allocates a full-size D32 texture when withDepth (default true) is set, but every pass uses the graph's transient depth (depth_prepass.cpp:28, skybox/sprite/debug passes create their own) and targetState.depth is only referenced at renderer.cpp:451-452/489-490/510-511 and render_target.cpp:25 (has_depth). Whether to delete the public field and accessor or make the depth real is a product choice the design (02_renderer.md:564 lists withDepth = true without a consumer) does not settle. *Fix:* Options: (a) remove render_target_desc::withDepth, render_target::has_depth() and the allocation; update screenshot_service.cpp:67, test_render_target.cpp:100/134/139-145/184, 02_renderer.md:564 and rendering-a-frame.md:112 (public API change, editor follows). (b) keep the API but import the target's depth into the view graph instead of creating a transient when the view targets a render target with depth, so the scene depth survives the frame (useful later for editor gizmo depth testing or GPU picking); touches render graph setup and per-view frame ordering. (c) keep the field but stop allocating and document it as reserved (misleading). Recommendation: (a) - nothing reads target depth today, editor picking is CPU-side (R11), and it saves ~33 MB per 4K viewport; reintroduce depth as (b) when a consumer appears.
- **F391** MINOR gltf_import_options::generateTangents does nothing except add a warning (`engine/src/renderer/import/gltf_importer.cpp:873`). Still present: gltf_import_options::generateTangents is parsed (:934) and written (:953), but setting it to false only appends a warning to every import (:873-879); tangents are always generated by from_geometry. The design records list it as a real setting (02_renderer.md:709, 05_assets.md:139), and .meta files carry it, so removing it versus implementing it is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options. (A) Remove the option: drop the field and the per-import warning, stop writing the key in gltf_import_options_to_document, and keep accepting (ignoring) the key in gltf_import_options_from_json so old .meta settings still parse; the output is unchanged, so no version bump. Update renderer-assets.md, 02_renderer.md:709 and the 05_assets.md example. (B) Implement it: skip generation and pack a neutral tangent with a material or vertex flag that disables normal mapping; this needs shader and material work. (C) Keep it as reserved, delete the warning and document that it has no effect. Recommendation: A.
- **F408** MINOR A body falling asleep produces EXIT / TRIGGER_EXIT while it is still touching or still inside the trigger (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_contacts.cpp:233`). The behaviour is deliberate: design 14_physics.md:507-509 accepts EXIT at sleep and tests/physics/test_contact_events.cpp:404-428 pins it. Jolt fires OnContactRemoved at deactivation (ContactListener.h:88-89), and collect_events turns it into EXIT / TRIGGER_EXIT (physics_world_contacts.cpp:233-241). The design's justification '(Unity behaves the same)' is wrong, and for triggers (pressure plates) the behaviour is a gameplay trap. Changing it is a contract choice that the design currently settles the other way. *Fix:* Options: (A) keep the behaviour. Fix the design sentence (Unity sends no OnCollisionExit / OnTriggerExit on sleep) and document in docs/api/physics/physics-world-and-queries.md that a body falling asleep reports EXIT / TRIGGER_EXIT and wakes with ENTER, and that bodies which must stay 'inside' should clear ALLOW_SLEEPING. This is docs only. (B) Suppress sleep exits in collect_events: when a run takes a pair to 0 sub-shape pairs while either row is alive and asleep (awake flag 0), keep the pair in activePairs without an event, and treat the ADDED at wake as STAY or no event. This also needs a sweep at wake that synthesises the exit for pairs that were not re-added (a static trigger teleported away while the body slept), which makes it a challenging change across contacts and activation. (C) Triggers only: build trigger bodies as active kinematic sensors with mCollideKinematicVsNonDynamic (Jolt's documented way for a sensor to keep detecting sleeping and static bodies; the engine already sets it for kinematic triggers, bodies.cpp:495), so TRIGGER_EXIT at sleep disappears, and apply (A)'s doc fix for solid contacts. Recommendation: (C) plus the (A) doc fix now. It fixes the pressure-plate case, the one that breaks gameplay, at modest cost; (B) can follow if solid-contact EXIT at sleep also matters.
- **F410** MINOR Design says a destroyed body's EXIT never names a dangling entity; the test asserts that it does (`tests/physics/test_contact_events.cpp:199`). Present as described: synthesise_exits copies the dying row's entity (physics_world_contacts.cpp:81-87), test_contact_events.cpp:199 asserts the EXIT names a dead entity, and the design's table row (14_physics.md, test_contact_events.cpp row) says 'never a dangling entity'. contact_events.h and the API page say nothing about it. The script dispatch (script_runtime.cpp:320-350) skips the dead participant but passes the dead id as 'other' to the survivor. Which contract is right is a design choice. *Fix:* Options: (A) keep the destroyed entity's id in synthesised EXIT / TRIGGER_EXIT. Document in contact_events.h and docs/api/physics/physics-world-and-queries.md that an exit synthesised for a destroyed body may name an entity that is no longer alive (check is_alive before touching it). Reword the design row to '... produces EXIT next step naming the destroyed entity, and no later event references it'. (B) null the destroyed side (NULL_ENTITY) in synthesise_exits and flip the test. Scripts then cannot tell which body left (a trigger zone that counts occupants by entity breaks), and OnCollisionExit gets a null 'other'. Recommendation: (A). The id is the only way for consumers keyed on entities to clean up, it matches the current tests and dispatch, and the change is documentation only.
- **F413** MINOR Several commands return success and are then silently dropped (`engine/src/physics/physics_world_sync.cpp:384`). The behaviour is present: apply_commands counts droppedCommands for body commands on CHARACTER rows (physics_world_sync.cpp:384-386), MOVE_KINEMATIC on non-kinematic rows (:456-458) and CHARACTER_MOVE on bodies (:472-474), after queue() returned success. MOVE_KINEMATIC on a KINEMATIC_MOVES_WITH_TRANSFORM row is applied and then undone by the next push_hierarchy_rows. The queue-then-drop contract is what design 3.4 specifies and what tests pin (test_rigid_body.cpp:448, test_character_controller.cpp:313). Rejecting at queue time changes a public contract and needs a new physics_error code that bindings_error.cpp:136-142 would have to map. *Fix:* Options: (A) Keep queue-and-drop. Document in physics_world.h and the API page which command/kind combinations are dropped and counted in droppedCommands. Make MOVE_KINEMATIC on a KINEMATIC_MOVES_WITH_TRANSFORM row a counted drop instead of a one-step jump. Physics only. (B) Reject at queue time when the row already exists and the combination cannot apply: a new physics_error (for example UNSUPPORTED_FOR_BODY_KIND) for body commands on a character and for move_kinematic on a non-KINEMATIC or moves-with-transform row, like character_move already returns NOT_A_CHARACTER (physics_world.cpp:478-484). The late drop and counter stay only for entities that were pending at queue time. Map the new code in bindings_error.cpp. (C) Like (B) but make wake/sleep on a character a successful no-op, since characters never sleep. Recommendation: (B)+(C). Callers using CONJURE_TRY and the C# layer see misuse immediately, and the pending-entity path keeps its designed semantics.
- **F445** MINOR The ABI layout hash covers function signatures only, not record layouts or the managed table (`tools/generate_native_api.py:171`). Accurate as described: layout_hash (tools/generate_native_api.py:170-174) hashes only each entry's hashed_line (:110-112), and Records.cs:332-351 checks record sizes only. But this is the recorded design: docs/design/15_bindings.md:261 states 'the generator does not parse records (03 4.1), so this table is the contract', and 03_scripting.md D3 relies on size + version + hash. Widening the hash changes the native/managed ABI handshake, so it is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: - (a) Keep the design and document the residual risk (a same-size field reorder, or a conjure_managed_api signature change) in 15_bindings.md. - (b) Feed every conjure_* record of native_api.h (name and normalised field type/name lines in order, Pad* included) and the conjure_managed_api member signatures into CONJURE_NATIVE_API_HASH. Regenerate native_api_hash.h, NativeApi.g.cs and the manifest, update the C++ and C# hash tests, and bump CONJURE_NATIVE_API_VERSION. - (c) Additionally generate a field-offset table that Conjure.Managed.Tests checks with Marshal.OffsetOf, or generate the C# records outright. Recommendation: (b) plus the offset check from (c). (b) alone catches a stale Conjure.Managed.dll but not a hand-written C# record that was never updated. Either way it is a multi-area change across tools, scripting, managed and the bindings design.
- **F458** MINOR project.json cannot turn GPU validation off in a Debug build and there is no flag for it (`engine/src/engine/project.cpp:591`). Still present. project.cpp:591 ORs the project value with the config, engine_config.cpp:31 sets gpuValidation = CONJURE_DEBUG != 0, command_line.cpp has no validation flag, and rhi_subsystem.cpp:65-66 ties both enableDebugLayer and enableGpuValidation to the one switch. Design 06_engine_loop.md:187 explicitly says gpuValidation is '(forced on in Debug by defaults())', so the current behaviour matches the design record. Changing it is a design decision. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): split the switch. Debug builds keep the cheap debug layer on (rendering.gpuValidation keeps meaning 'debug layer', still forced on in Debug). A new rendering.gpuBasedValidation (default false, project key "gpu_based_validation", flag --gpu-based-validation) drives device_desc::enableGpuValidation in rhi_subsystem.cpp. Add --gpu-validation on\|off to command_line so a Debug run can also drop the debug layer. Amend design 06 2.x/table at :187. Option B: let an explicit project value win, which needs project_description to distinguish absent from false (std::optional<bool> or a 'gpuValidationSet' flag in project.h) plus the CLI flag. Option C: keep the design (Debug always validates) and document using the Development configuration to avoid it.
- **F546** MINOR Rule 'tests include no third-party headers' is unenforced and violated in 15 places (`docs/design/08_testing.md:530`). Still present. 08_testing.md:530-532 forbids third-party headers in tests, yet 15 test files include <nvrhi/nvrhi.h>, <imgui.h> or <stb_image_write.h> (for example tests/rhi/test_null_device.cpp:15, tests/editor/ui/test_gizmo_controller.cpp:16, tests/core/system/test_image.cpp:8). check_style.py's doctest-include rule (:24, :370) only covers doctest. The rhi/renderer/editor-UI tests cannot reasonably drop those headers, so either the rule or the enforcement must change, and that is the owner's call. *Fix:* Option A (docs only): amend 08 5.1 to 'third-party headers (test through the public API), except the library a test drives directly: nvrhi in tests/rhi and tests/renderer, Dear ImGui in tests/editor/ui, stb_image_write in tests/core/system/test_image.cpp'. Option B (recommended): Option A plus a check_style rule `third-party-include-in-test` over tests/** using THIRD_PARTY_INCLUDE_ROOTS (also matching bare <doctest.h>), with a per-file exception table 'third_party_in_tests' in tools/style_exceptions.json listing the 15 files with reasons, mirroring forbidden_in_tests. That keeps the rule honest and stops new violations. Option C: keep the rule strict and rewrite the 15 tests through engine-only APIs, which is impractical for the ImGui panel tests.
- **F606** MINOR Scene and prefab files write component keys in component-id (registration) order (`engine/src/ecs/serialization.cpp:525`). The code does write keys in component-id order (serialization.cpp:503-527 over entity_archive capture order). That order is what the scene design mandates: 13_scene.md:159-160 'component keys in component id order', repeated in docs/handbook/modules/scene.md:118 and engine/src/scene/README.md:106. Changing it is a file-output design change, not a bug fix. The cooked-hash part of the claim does not hold, because cooked scene and prefab payloads are re-emitted as canonical JSON (scene_loader.h:15, prefab_importer.h:13); only the source files churn. *Fix:* Options: (a) Keep id order as designed, and document in 13_scene.md that saved files reorder when module or registration order changes. (b) In write_archive_json, write each entry's non-transient components sorted by display name, with the preserved unresolved entries merged into the same order. Update 13_scene.md:160, docs/handbook/modules/scene.md:118, engine/src/scene/README.md:106 and the archives API page, and refresh any expected-text tests (tests/scene/test_scene_serialisation.cpp, tests/scripting/test_script_component.cpp:395 if multi-component). Every saved scene and prefab reorders once on its next save. Recommendation: (b). Files are meant to be diffable (13_scene.md S8), registration order is not stable across builds (CONJURE_WITH_PHYSICS, module load order), and asset_pack.cpp:270 and physics_layers.cpp:207 already sort keys for the same reason. The sort is a small per-entry buffer of indices, so the cost is small. *Test:* tests/ecs/test_serialization.cpp: "serialization - component keys are written in name order whatever the registration order". Two registries register the same types in opposite orders, the same entity is captured in each, and write_archive_json produces identical text.
- **F612** MINOR Change-triggered warnings log every frame when the overflowing count fluctuates; three render-path warnings are unthrottled (`engine/src/renderer/sprite_batcher.cpp:59`). Still present: sprite_batcher.cpp:59-70 and renderer.cpp:669-680 / 929-940 warn whenever the dropped count changes (so a fluctuating overflow warns every frame), and renderer.cpp:816, 1316 and 1369 warn every frame while their condition lasts. The once-per-change rule is the documented design (02_renderer.md:341) and pinned by test_sprite_batch.cpp:169-175, so changing the policy, and whether a rate limiter becomes a shared core facility, is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: (a) transition-only overflow warnings (WARN when the count goes from 0 to non-zero, INFO/DEBUG when it returns to 0; the live count stays in render_stats / dropped_count()), plus a renderer-local warn_limiter (copy of physics_world_internal.h:104-111: first 8 then every 1024th) on the three per-frame failure warnings. (b) as (a) but move warn_limiter into core logging and use it from physics and renderer (core + physics + renderer). (c) keep once-per-change and only throttle the three per-frame warnings. Recommendation: (a) now (renderer-only code, plus the one-line change to 02_renderer.md:341 and the test at test_sprite_batch.cpp:169-175), (b) later if more modules need a limiter.
- **F619** MINOR json::to_string(value, bool) and document::to_string(bool) give the bool opposite meanings (`engine/include/conjure/core/json/json.h:26`). Still present: json.h:26 `to_string(const value &, bool canonical = false)` vs document.h:268 `to_string(bool pretty = false)`. The free-function signature is named in design docs (ARCHITECTURE.md:193, design/05_assets.md:795) and used by assets (asset_meta.cpp:224, a hash of canonical settings), so renaming is a public API change across core, assets and docs. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the API and add a warning to both summaries that the bools mean different things (core only, no behaviour change); (b) mirror document: remove the bool from json::to_string(const value &) and add json::to_canonical_string(const value &); migrate asset_meta.cpp:224 and test_json.cpp:58-64 and update the docs. Removing the bool (rather than repurposing it to mean pretty) makes any leftover `to_string(x, true)` a compile error instead of silently changing the asset settings hash; (c) a shared enum json_format { MINIFIED, PRETTY, CANONICAL } for both functions. Recommendation: (b), which has one production caller and keeps asset hashes byte-identical.
- **F622** MINOR C# physics queries cannot include triggers or exclude static bodies (`engine/src/bindings/entries_physics.cpp:60`). Accurate but it is a missing feature, not a defect against the design: filter_of (entries_physics.cpp:60-65) passes only the layer mask and the ignore entity, and conjure_ray_query / conjure_raycast_hit (native_api.h:208-231) match the design records exactly (15_bindings.md:246-248), so triggers are always skipped and statics always included. Exposing them changes ABI records, the overlap signatures, the hash, the generator output and the C# facades. *Fix:* Options: (a) ABI revision: add `uint32_t flags` (INCLUDE_TRIGGERS, EXCLUDE_STATIC) to conjure_ray_query (the record grows from 40 to 48 bytes), a flags parameter on physics_overlap_sphere/box, and is_trigger on conjure_raycast_hit; regenerate with tools/generate_native_api.py; add optional C# parameters and RaycastHit.IsTrigger. (b) Keep the ABI and document in csharp-api.md and the Physics.cs summaries that queries skip trigger volumes and include static bodies. Recommendation: (b) now, and do (a) when a wave-6 demo needs trigger picking, batched with any other ABI revision so the handshake hash changes once.
- **F623** MINOR British and American spellings are mixed inside public identifiers (`engine/include/conjure/scripting/script_runtime.h:172`). Still present. scripting uses initialize/NOT_INITIALIZED/UNINITIALIZED (script_host.h:27, scripting_error.h:31, script_runtime.h:172). engine and audio use initialise/ALREADY_INITIALISED/NOT_INITIALISED (engine_error.h:44-45, audio_error.h:32, application.h:121-128). Math names are American (normalize, center). docs/handbook/documentation.md:124 sets British spelling for prose only, so no rule covers identifiers. A rename touches public API in many modules, the editor, bindings and possibly managed status names, so the owner must pick the rule. *Fix:* Options: (a) British everywhere (initialise/finalise/colour/centre/normalise): consistent with the docs, but heavy churn including math and graphics names that users expect in American spelling. (b) American everywhere for identifiers, British in prose: matches the math libraries, but renames the engine/application lifecycle API. (c) Unify only the lifecycle family (initialise/finalise, *_INITIALISED error codes, UNINITIALISED status) and leave math/graphics terms (normalize, center, color) as they are, then add the chosen word list to tools/check_style.py so new identifiers follow it. Recommendation: (c) in British, because those are the sibling APIs a user types side by side (application::initialise vs script_runtime::initialize). Do it as one coordinated rename commit across scripting, engine, editor, bindings, tests and docs, after checking that the scripting_error names are not mirrored in managed generated code.
- **F624** MINOR Validity and boolean getters use five different naming schemes (`engine/include/conjure/ecs/entity_handle.h:52`). Accurate: valid() exists on ecs::component_ref (entity_handle.h:52), core::jobs::job_handle (job_handle.h:56) and editor::async_reply (editor_command.h:83), while most types use is_valid(). docs/CONVENTIONS.md section 1 has no rule for boolean getter names, so nothing defines the right spelling. A rename touches public API in core, ecs, assets, editor, platform and engine plus every caller, and it needs a convention decision first. *Fix:* Options: (a) add a rule to CONVENTIONS.md section 1 (boolean getters take is_/has_/can_, validity is is_valid()) and rename every outlier with deprecated forwarding aliases for one wave; this is large and multi-area. (b) Rename only the three valid() methods to is_valid(), keeping valid() as a [[deprecated]] forwarder for one wave, and add the rule for new code; leave scene_dirty()/headless()/frozen()/current_*() alone. (c) Leave everything as is. Recommendation: (b). It fixes the one inconsistency that bites generic code (is_valid() on handle-like types) at low churn. Each rename is then a SMALL per-module item (ecs: component_ref; core: job_handle; editor: async_reply).
- **F625** MINOR Error enums, headers and factories are named differently per module (`engine/include/conjure/assets/assets_error.h:19`). Accurate: the names differ by module. assets uses asset_error_code/asset_error_code_name/make_asset_error (assets_error.h:19,46,51); editor uses protocol_error_code; ecs and engine declare their own make_error overloads; the design records themselves prescribe these differing names (05_assets.md:67, 04_editor.md:575, 07_modules.md:378, 01_ecs.md:386, 06_engine_loop.md:471). R63 (09_cross_area_resolutions.md:84) already says there is no per-module make_error, but enum and name-function naming was never settled. Unifying it is a cross-module public API rename, so the owner has to choose. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): do no rename now. Record the target pattern in docs/CONVENTIONS.md (`<module>_error` enum, `<module>_error_name`, built with core::make_error per R63). Apply it to new modules, and schedule a dedicated API-cleanup wave that renames assets, editor and scripting and drops the ecs/engine make_error overloads, keeping deprecated aliases (`using asset_error_code = asset_error;`, inline forwarding functions) for one release. Option B: do the full rename now with aliases. This touches every caller in engine/, editor/, runtime/ and the tests, the design records and the docs/api pages, and must check that no enum value or name crosses the managed ABI (bindings/scripting). Option C: accept the divergence and only document each module's names. The missing export of make_asset_error is a separate defect (F150) and does not wait on this decision.
- **F626** MINOR engine::audio_settings / engine::physics_settings shadow audio::audio_settings / physics::physics_settings with different fields (`engine/include/conjure/engine/engine_config.h:108`). Still present: engine_config.h:92 `struct physics_settings` and :108 `struct audio_settings` (field `device`, literal 343.0f) shadow physics::physics_settings and audio::audio_settings (`deviceName`). The design records name both pairs on purpose (06_engine_loop.md:188 table row `physics_settings` 'engine-side summary only', 12_audio.md:701 'map `engine::audio_settings` (... `device` ...) to `audio::audio_settings`'), and no .cpp today pulls in both namespaces with using-directives, so this is a naming choice, not a defect the code can settle. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): keep the names, which the design records fix and the namespaces keep apart; close the finding and at most add one sentence to engine_config.h's physics/audio summaries saying they are the engine-side sections that audio_subsystem / physics_subsystem map onto the module structs. Option B: rename the engine sections to `physics_config` / `audio_config` (types only; the `engine_config::physics` / `::audio` member names and the project.json keys stay) and rename `audio_config::device` to `deviceName` and use audio::DEFAULT_SPEED_OF_SOUND, updating project.cpp, application_desc.cpp, command_line.cpp, the two subsystems, the engine tests, docs/api/engine/running-the-engine.md and design records 06 2.3 and 12 section 'Offers to engine' in the same change. B is a public API rename touching engine and docs/design, so it needs the owner's go-ahead.
- **F627** MINOR scene_manager::load defaults to REPLACE, request_load defaults to ADDITIVE (`engine/include/conjure/scene/scene_manager.h:200`). Still present: scene_manager.h:185/194 default to REPLACE and :200-203 to ADDITIVE. That matches 13_scene.md:708-710 exactly, so changing it alters a designed default. Every in-tree caller of request_load already passes the mode explicitly: bindings entries_scene.cpp:79/97 and tests/scene/test_scene_manager.cpp. *Fix:* Option (a): keep ADDITIVE (async loads are usually streamed in) and document the asymmetry in the request_load summary. Option (b): make the request_load default REPLACE. Option (c): remove the default from request_load so the mode is always written. Recommendation: (c). No in-tree caller relies on the default, so nothing breaks, and the silent-mode switch in the finding's scenario becomes a compile error. Change scene_manager.h:200-203 to 'load_request_id request_load(const assets::asset_id &id, load_mode mode);' and update the signature at docs/api/scene/scene-files-and-prefabs.md:158 and 13_scene.md:710.
- **F631** MINOR C# Entity mixes properties, Set methods, bool returns and exceptions for the same kind of operation (`managed/Conjure.Managed/Api/Entity.Hierarchy.cs:17`). Still present. Name and Tag are get-only properties with SetName/SetTag methods (Entity.Hierarchy.cs:17, 88-111), although design 15_bindings.md:282 lists Name {set;} and Tag {get;set;}. Add/Set/RemoveComponent and Destroy return bool on a dead entity (Entity.cs:98-102, 162-182), while the hierarchy setters throw. Rigidbody is a deliberate rename in 15_bindings.md:271. Forward/Right/Up are documented as parent-space (Transform.cs:130-143), while LookAt works in world space (:222-225). Settling naming and failure conventions across the public SDK is an API policy decision. *Fix:* Options: (A) align with the design table: add `string Name { set; }` and a string Tag setter, keeping SetName/SetTag as [Obsolete] forwards; document in the Entity summary that component mutators return false for a dead entity, as B11-style predicates do, while identity and hierarchy setters throw; add WorldForward/WorldRight/WorldUp from the world matrix and keep Forward local; keep the Rigidbody name, which the design chose. (B) also unify failure handling, so every mutator throws EntityNotAliveException (breaking: callers that test the bool lose it). (C) document only. Recommendation: A. It is additive, honours 15_bindings.md:282, keeps game scripts compiling, and fixes the world-axis trap with new names instead of changing what Forward means.
- **F633** MINOR [[nodiscard]] on view::with/without/changed makes statement-style refinement a build error (`engine/include/conjure/ecs/view.h:130`). Accurate: with/without/changed mutate the view in place and are [[nodiscard]] (view.h:130, 146, 162), so `enemies.without<scene::disabled>();` on a named view fails under warnings-as-errors. However, the design record specifies `[[nodiscard]] view &with()` and `without()` (01_ecs.md:544-545), and the attribute also catches the real mistake of discarding a refined temporary (`world.view<A>().with<B>();`). Changing it overrides the design record. *Fix:* Options: (a) remove [[nodiscard]] from the three refiners and update 01_ecs.md:544-545. (b) Keep as designed and document the `(void)` / chaining idiom. (c) Ref-qualify: `view &with() &` without [[nodiscard]] for named views, plus `[[nodiscard]] view &&with() &&` returning std::move(*this) for temporaries; same for without and changed. Statement-style refinement then works on lvalues, while a discarded temporary still warns, and the chained renderer_subsystem.cpp:226-227 style keeps compiling. Recommendation: (c), with the design-record line updated to match.
- **F634** MINOR Adjacent and mode-selecting bool parameters in public signatures (`engine/include/conjure/editor/editor_context.h:174`). The signatures are as described: editor_context.h:174 (two adjacent bools), scene.h:319-324 set_parent(child, parent, bool keepWorld = true, u32 index) (where set_parent(c, p, 2) compiles as keepWorld = true), world.h:361 get_raw(..., bool forWrite), chunk_view.h:150, gizmo_controller.h:99 and prefab.h:149-159. No style rule in the repository forbids bool parameters, and the change edits public API in ecs, scene and editor. Whether to adopt the convention is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: (a) change nothing and document the bools; (b) targeted: an editor-local `enum class window_mode { WINDOWED, HEADLESS, OFFSCREEN }` for the editor_context constructor (one caller, editor_application.cpp:237), plus a `reparent_mode { KEEP_WORLD, KEEP_LOCAL }` for scene::set_parent / deferred_set_parent, the only signature where an integer literal silently binds to the bool; (c) the full sweep including ecs get_raw/column_data access enums, gizmo set_snap and the prefab refresh/unpack flags. Recommendation: (b). The editor_context part is a small editor-only change, and set_parent has a real silent miscompile. The ecs forWrite bools are low-level, mostly reached through the typed get/get_mut wrappers, and can stay.
- **F637** MINOR is_key_held exists only for keys, not for mouse or gamepad buttons, and not in C# (`engine/include/conjure/platform/input_state.h:195`). Still present. The `held` query exists only for keys: input_state.h:195 has is_key_held, but mouse buttons (input_state.h:202-218) and gamepad_state (115-131) have only down/pressed/released, and C# Input.cs:16-43 has no Held query at all. The API page docs/api/platform/input.md:59-61 says "The same four exist for mouse buttons ... and gamepad buttons" but then lists only three. Whether to widen the API across C++ and C# or shrink it is an API-shape choice, and design 11_platform.md:159 specifies is_key_held alone. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): add `is_mouse_button_held(mouse_button)` (= down && !pressed, with the same is_valid check) and `gamepad_state::is_held(gamepad_button)`, and add C# `Input.IsKeyHeld`, `IsMouseButtonHeld` and `IsGamepadButtonHeld`, each computed from the existing frame-block fields (Down && !Pressed), so there is no ABI change. Update design 2.3 and the API page tables, and fix the 'same four' sentence. Keep the gamepad names as they are (is_down etc.), because gamepad_state already supplies the noun. Option B: remove is_key_held. That edits the platform, support and bindings tests and the docs, and breaks the documented 'pressed for exactly one frame then held' story. Option C: keep the API as it is and only correct docs/api/platform/input.md:60 to say mouse and gamepad buttons have three queries. My recommendation is A.
- **F640** MINOR DEFAULT_WATCH_INTERVAL is std::chrono::milliseconds while every other duration in the API is f64/f32 seconds (`engine/include/conjure/assets/asset_types.h:129`). Part of the premise is wrong. DEFAULT_WATCH_INTERVAL (asset_types.h:129) is not the only chrono duration: asset_manager_desc::finaliseBudget and blockingTimeout are std::chrono too (asset_manager.h:36-37), so the assets module is internally consistent. What does differ is the convention between modules (scripting's hot_reload.h:76 takes f64 seconds), and docs/CONVENTIONS.md does not set one. Picking the convention is an API-style choice for the owner, not a defect. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): keep chrono in assets. It is type-safe: the finding's '500 to a seconds API' hazard comes from the f64 API, not from chrono. Record a duration convention in docs/CONVENTIONS.md (for example: chrono in C++ configuration structs, f64 seconds only where a value crosses into reflection, JSON or managed code) and fix the stale constant name `DEFAULT_WATCH_INTERVAL_MS = 500` in docs/design/05_assets.md:69 to `DEFAULT_WATCH_INTERVAL` (std::chrono::milliseconds(500)). Option B: convert every asset duration to f64 seconds (watch interval, finaliseBudget, blockingTimeout). This breaks public API in file_watcher.h and asset_manager.h, plus their tests and docs. Option C: adopt chrono everywhere, including scripting's set_watch_interval_seconds and the other *Seconds fields. That is a wide break, and a poor fit for reflected or JSON settings.
- **F650** MINOR project.export `config` argument is parsed and discarded (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_project.cpp:207`). Still present: `config` is read and discarded (commands_project.cpp:202-203). The design table lists `config?` (04_editor.md:840) without saying what it selects, the export dialog sends a free-text Configuration defaulting to "development" (export_dialog.h:19, export_dialog.cpp:127), while 06_engine_loop.md 3.6 always copies the Distribution runtime, so its meaning is a design choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) remove `config` from the project.export schema, the design table and the export dialog's Configuration field (export always targets Distribution); (b) implement it as an enum ["development","distribution"] that selects which build's runtime directory is copied (needs the distribution-layout resolution of F139); (c) keep it but validate the enum and echo it in the reply until (b) lands. Recommendation: (a) now - it removes a silently ignored input and the misleading "development" default - and reintroduce a validated enum with F139 if Development exports are wanted. The unused project.create `template` argument (commands_project.cpp:256) should get the same treatment.
- **F659** MINOR Engine boots fully before the --exec file is read; a corrupt startup scene still exits 0 (`engine/src/editor/core/headless_runner.cpp:73`). Still present. headless_runner::run reads and parses the file (headless_runner.cpp:72-81) only after initialise() has started the whole engine. application.cpp:570-572 deliberately turns a failed startup scene into a warning in editor mode ('editor continues with an empty world'). Whether a broken startup scene should fail an --exec run is a product choice: a command file may exist precisely to repair that scene. *Fix:* Options: (a) read and parse the --exec file before initialise() (move the file read and parse_command_file out of headless_runner::run into editor_application::initialise, or into main, keeping exit 3), so a bad file fails fast; (b) in --exec/--export mode treat a startup-scene failure as a startup failure (exit 2), which needs an engine_config switch beside application.cpp:570 and blocks repair scripts; (c) keep tolerating the failure but write a `warning` event (code STARTUP_SCENE_FAILED, with the importer's error) into the exec output, and have assets/scene report the import PARSE_FAILURE instead of the downstream CACHE_MISS. Recommendation: (a) plus the warning event from (c); not (b).
- **F673** MINOR Field meta 'assetType' carries the C# type name, not the registered asset type, and 'enumNames' has no values (`managed/Conjure.Managed/Runtime/ScriptClassInfo.cs:236`). Still present: ScriptClassInfo.cs:236 writes the CLR Name of the type argument, and :219-229 writes enum names without values. No engine code reads either key, and tests/scripting/test_dotnet_script_host.cpp:157 pins the current "TextureAsset" value. Design 03_scripting.md:152 lists the meta keys without defining what assetType holds, so the contract for the future inspector is an owner choice. *Fix:* Option A (recommended): assetType = the registered name AssetTypeId<TArg>.TypeName (e.g. conjure.renderer.texture), read through typeof(AssetTypeId<>).MakeGenericType(arg).GetField("TypeName"), with the key omitted when empty (untyped tags). Also add "enumValues":[...] parallel to enumNames, holding the 32-bit values the blob carries (unchecked((int)Convert.ToInt64(v))). Update test_dotnet_script_host.cpp:157: the untyped TestScripts.TextureAsset then has no assetType key; add a check for a typed AssetRef<AudioClipAsset> field reading "conjure.audio.clip". Add enumValues to the key lists at native_api.h:159, script_class_registry.h:29 and 03_scripting.md:152. Option B: send the numeric asset type id instead of the name. Option C: leave both keys until the inspector consumer is designed. Recommend A: the registered name is what asset_type_registry and the asset database key on, and values are required for sparse or [Flags] enums.
- **F674** MINOR Spans and copies of a ComponentQuery outlive the scope; the 'enforced at compile time' claim is too strong (`managed/Conjure.Managed/Api/Dod/ChunkView.cs:7`). Still present: ChunkView.cs:7-8, and 03_scripting.md:444-445 and D12, claim the ref-struct rule keeps spans inside the scope. It only keeps them inside the method. A Span taken before Dispose outlives it, and a copy of the mutable ComponentQuery (ComponentQuery.cs:12-18) keeps _open and _chunks after the original's Dispose (:112-126). D12 deliberately relies on the compile-time rule plus Debug poisoning of the chunk-view array (03_scripting.md:456-457), so adding runtime guards is a design choice. *Fix:* Options: (A) wording only: ChunkView.cs:6-9, the ComponentQuery summary and 03_scripting.md:444-445/D12 say spans and copies cannot leave the method, and using them after Dispose is undefined (Debug poisons the chunk views). (B) A plus a cheap managed guard: a main-thread list of open scope ids filled by Begin and emptied by Dispose, cleared by ScriptRuntime at every phase entry (to absorb native force-closes); NextChunk and ChunkView.Get check the scope id they carry and throw ObjectDisposedException for a stale copy. Spans already handed out stay unguardable. (C) a native query_is_open(scopeId) entry, an ABI change. Recommendation: A now, B as a follow-up if stale copies show up in practice. C is not worth an ABI bump.
- **F706** MINOR Default golden tolerances accept an image with 0.5 % of its pixels completely wrong (`tests/support/engine/include/conjure/testing/golden_image.h:32`). The arithmetic is right: golden_image.h:31-33 and golden_image.cpp:197-198 let 0.5 % of pixels be fully wrong, an MAE of about 0.956, which is under 1.0. But these exact numbers and the two-limit pass rule are fixed by the design record: 08_testing.md D11 (line 37) and 3.5 step 3 (lines 216-217). The code implements them faithfully, so changing them is a design change. No reference images exist yet either: render_headless returns UNSUPPORTED (golden_image.cpp:66-84; 09 item 15(d)), so no calibration data exists to set new limits from. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the D11 limits and document in D11, 3.5 and golden_image.h that they accept up to 0.5 % wholly wrong pixels (about 1,150 at 640x360, a 33x33 block), so reference review is the guard. (b) add a golden_options::maxPixelDelta cap (for example 64/255) that fails on any pixel beyond it. This is cheap, but WARP and lavapipe differ by large deltas on scattered edge pixels, the very noise the mismatch fraction exists to absorb, so it risks false failures. (c) lower maxMeanAbsoluteError below 0.956 (for example 0.5) so the two limits overlap. Simple, but it tightens tolerance for tonemapping noise too. (d) add a cluster criterion: fail when mismatching pixels form a connected region bigger than N pixels (for example 256), which tells a missing object apart from edge noise. Recommendation: (a) now, plus the 33x33-block self-test documenting today's verdict. Then choose (d), or (c) calibrated against the first real WARP and lavapipe references, once off-screen rendering lands (09 item 15(d)).
- **F723** MINOR GPU validation cannot be switched off in a Debug build (`engine/src/engine/project.cpp:591`): same as F458.
- **F736** MINOR Every prefab command logs an engine WARN about its own blocking load (`engine/src/editor/core/commands/commands_prefab.cpp:99`). Still present: load_prefab calls manager.load_blocking(id) (commands_prefab.cpp:99) and asset_manager.cpp:642-650 warns once per frame whenever it is called inside a frame, even for tool loads. The design calls the hitch correct and defers the fix to 'load_batch + async commands once asset residency UX is designed' (09_cross_area_resolutions.md:386-387; 04_editor.md:981 permits load_blocking in a frame), so how to silence it is an owner choice across assets and editor. *Fix:* Options: (a) assets API: a scoped guard (e.g. asset_manager::tool_load_scope) or a load_blocking overload with a reason flag that suppresses the WARN, used by the editor's prefab commands; (b) only warn when the call actually blocks (asset not already READY) - reduces but does not remove the noise after prefab.create's fresh import; (c) make the prefab commands asynchronous (load + frame_task) per the deferred design item - large, touches undo replays that must stay synchronous. Recommendation: (a), owner assets with the editor as the one caller, and keep the WARN for game code.
- **F776** MINOR Every managed build forces ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true, so PDBs map sources to /_/ and point at GitHub (`cmake/ConjureManaged.cmake:184`). Still present: cmake/ConjureManaged.cmake:184 and engine/src/scripting/managed_build.cpp:203 always pass -p:ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true, so PDBs get /_/ source paths and a Source Link URL. The flag is not an accident: docs/design/03_scripting.md:475 and managed_build.h:138 specify it, and it was there from the first design commit. Removing it reverses a documented choice across build, scripting and the design docs. *Fix:* Options: (a) Keep it everywhere for reproducible PDBs and document that local debugging needs source mapping. Game scripts built by the editor stay undebuggable at source level. (b) Pass it only for CI: in conjure_add_managed_project add it only when $ENV{CI} is set or under a new option CONJURE_MANAGED_CI_BUILD that defaults from $ENV{CI}. Drop it from managed_build_runner::command_line, which builds user game scripts. Update tests/scripting/test_managed_build.cpp:88/103 (expected argument lists), the managed_build.h:138 comment and 03_scripting.md 3.10. (c) Never pass it and rely on <Deterministic>true</Deterministic> (managed/Directory.Build.props:12); optionally also set <EnableSourceLink>false</EnableSourceLink> so no repository URL is embedded. Recommendation: (b) for the engine SDK build and never for editor-built game scripts, because users must be able to step through their own scripts.
- **F778** MINOR No global.json: the managed build uses whichever .NET SDK is newest on the machine (`managed/CMakeLists.txt:1`). Still present: there is no global.json, managed/Directory.Build.props:5,9 keeps net8.0 with TreatWarningsAsErrors, Game.csproj:9,13 repeats both, and ci.yml:68 / nightly.yml pin dotnet-version 8.0.x, so local and CI builds use different SDKs. Which SDK to pin and whether to leave the now out-of-support net8.0 target is a platform choice the design docs (00_brief.md:20 '.NET 8+') do not settle. *Fix:* Options: (A) stay on net8.0 for now: add <CheckEolTargetFramework>false</CheckEolTargetFramework> to managed/Directory.Build.props and Game.csproj so a newer SDK cannot turn NETSDK1138 into an error, and add a root global.json pinning an 8.0 feature band with rollForward latestFeature, which makes every machine build with SDK 8 the way CI does. (B) move to net10.0 (LTS): TargetFramework in Directory.Build.props and Game.csproj, CONJURE_DOTNET_MIN_MAJOR default 10 in FindNetHost.cmake, the 'install the .NET 8 SDK' message in ConjureManaged.cmake, dotnet-version 10.0.x in ci.yml/nightly.yml, the design docs that say .NET 8, and a global.json pinning 10.0.1xx with latestFeature, then a full [dotnet] test pass. Recommendation: land the CheckEolTargetFramework line from A right away (one line, no risk), then schedule B as its own change because it moves the host pack, the CI images and the docs together.
- **F784** MINOR No formatter is enforced: clang-format and dotnet format are advisory and incomplete (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:205`). Still present. ci.yml:203-213 runs clang-format and dotnet format with continue-on-error, the pathspec misses runtime/*.h, and dotnet format checks only Conjure.Managed.csproj. This advisory state is documented (.clang-format:1-3, ci.md:43, getting-started.md:203-205). But docs/design/08_testing.md:707 lists both formatters as style-and-headers checks without the advisory note, and :751 lists 'clang-format clean' as an exit criterion. Whether to reconcile and enforce, or to declare check_style.py the only gate, is an owner choice. Every option except C edits docs/ pages as well as .github, so the owner is multi, not ci. *Fix:* Options: (A) Reconcile the tree with .clang-format and make both steps blocking. This needs a whole-tree reformat, and .clang-format cannot express CODE_STYLE's two-space continuation indent, so it would fight the style guide. (B) Delete the advisory steps (ci.yml:203-213) and state that tools/check_style.py is the sole style gate and .clang-format only an optional local aid. Make the statements in docs/CONVENTIONS.md section 6 (the check_style.py bullet at :136; the file has no section 9), docs/handbook/ci.md:43 and docs/handbook/getting-started.md:203-205. Drop clang-format and dotnet format from the style-and-headers row of docs/design/08_testing.md:707, and drop 'clang-format clean' from :751. (C) Keep them advisory but complete them (ci-only): add 'runtime/*.h' to the clang-format pathspec, and run dotnet format --verify-no-changes over Conjure.Bootstrap, Conjure.TestScripts and Conjure.Managed. Recommendation: B plus the docs edits. A formatter that cannot express the house style can never become blocking, and an always-red advisory step is noise. If the owner wants to keep the hints, C is a small ci-only change, but 08_testing.md:751 should then still be reworded.
- **F790** MINOR Exported Windows game depends on the VC++ runtime and nothing ships or mentions it (`engine/src/engine/export_pipeline.cpp:344`). Still present. export_pipeline.cpp:341-350 copies only conjure_runtime, conjure_shared and nethost.dll. The build is /MD (cmake/ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake:110-113, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md), and nothing in the repository mentions the VC++ redistributable. Yet design 06 3.6 says 'The exported folder runs standalone'. Whether to ship the CRT next to the game or to declare a prerequisite is a distribution-policy choice. *Fix:* Option A (recommended, keeps the design's 'runs standalone' promise): app-local CRT deployment. A Distribution build of conjure_runtime copies the VC++ runtime DLLs it imports next to bin/: vcruntime140.dll, vcruntime140_1.dll, msvcp140.dll and msvcp140_atomic_wait.dll. They come from the toolchain's Redist\MSVC\<ver>\x64\Microsoft.VC14x.CRT folder, located with include(InstallRequiredSystemLibraries) / CMAKE_INSTALL_SYSTEM_RUNTIME_LIBS in a POST_BUILD copy. Export step 5 then copies every vcruntime140*.dll and msvcp140*.dll found in distributionDirectory. The UCRT is part of Windows 10+ and needs nothing. Option B (cheaper, engine only): on Windows, add an export_report warning ('the exported game needs the Microsoft Visual C++ 2015-2022 x64 redistributable') and a 'Prerequisites' paragraph in docs/api/engine/export-and-diagnostics.md, and amend design 06 3.6. Option C: switch Distribution to the static CRT (/MT). This conflicts with the /MD module ABI facts in ConjureSharedLibrary.cmake and is not recommended.

### core (8)

- **F223** MINOR arena::reset() keeps whichever block happened to be first, including a dedicated oversize block, and frees every regular one (`engine/src/core/memory/arena.cpp:143`). The behaviour is what the design says: docs/design/10_core.md:176 and arena.h:76-77 specify that reset() keeps the first block and frees the rest, and arena.cpp:137-146 does exactly that. The oversize-first-block retention (acquire_block 194-198) is a consequence of that rule. ecs::command_buffer::recycle_arena (command_buffer.cpp:344-359) already works around the per-reset churn, so changing retention is a policy choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) Keep it as designed and document in arena.h and api/core/memory.md that an oversize first allocation pins its block until release(). (b) Keep the first block only if it is regular-sized: in reset(), when mFirst->capacity > mBlockBytes, free the whole chain (subtract from mBytesReserved, decrement mBlockCount, set mFirst = mCurrent = nullptr), so the next allocate() acquires a regular block. This fixes scenario A and changes nothing for regular-first arenas or command_buffer. (c) Keep every regular block across reset() and free only oversize ones, with allocate() advancing to mCurrent->next before acquiring. This fixes scenario B but turns every arena into high-water retention and makes command_buffer's workaround redundant. Recommendation: (b), with a matching update to 10_core.md:176 and arena.h's reset() summary ("keeps the first block when it is a regular block").
- **F225** MINOR start() lets std::system_error escape when a worker thread cannot be created (`engine/src/core/jobs/job_system.cpp:466`). Still present: job_system::state::start creates workers with mWorkers.emplace_back at job_system.cpp:465-469 after setting mRunning (line 462), and nothing handles std::thread throwing std::system_error. But the sweep's fix (try/catch) contradicts docs/CONVENTIONS.md:44 ('Engine code never throws and never catches'). No core code catches anything today, and allocation failure (bad_alloc) is handled the same way. Whether thread-creation failure is recoverable is a policy choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) Treat it as fatal, like allocation failure, and document it: add to the job_system::start <summary> (job_system.h around line 101-107) that 'if the operating system refuses to create a worker thread, std::thread's std::system_error is not caught (engine code never catches) and ends the process like an allocation failure'. Docs only. (b) Approve one sanctioned catch boundary: wrap the emplace loop in try { } catch (const std::system_error &). On failure, under mQueueLock set mStopRequested = true and mAcceptingJobs = false, notify_all, join and clear the threads created so far, reset mStopRequested = false, mWorkerCount = 0 and mRunning = false, and return error(error_code::BACKEND_FAILURE or the closest existing code, message naming the index). Record the exception in CONVENTIONS.md section 2 and apply the same to dedicated_thread. Needs a test hook to inject the failure. (c) Create threads through a native backend that returns error codes (large). Recommendation: (a) now, matching the never-catch rule and the bad_alloc policy; choose (b) only if server or container deployments must degrade gracefully.
- **F232** MINOR delegate equality compares padding bytes of bound callables (`engine/include/conjure/core/delegate.h:176`). Still present: bind(Callable) memcpy's the whole closure including padding (delegate.h:121) and operator== compares raw storage (175-177), so equal captures with padding (e.g. bool + pointer) can compare unequal. There is no portable way to zero closure padding, so the fix is a choice between restricting what binds and narrowing the equality contract. *Fix:* Options: (a) add `(std::has_unique_object_representations_v<Callable> \|\| std::is_empty_v<Callable>)` to CAN_BIND: equality becomes exact, but lambdas capturing a bool next to a pointer, floats, or (depending on the compiler) by-reference captures no longer bind, which could break the documented `[&total]` example and existing callers in scripting tests; needs checking on MSVC/Clang/GCC first. (b) Keep binding as is and document in delegate.h operator==, the README and docs/api/core/time-and-events.md:207 that equality is exact for free-function, member and function-pointer bindings and stateless lambdas, and for capturing lambdas only when the closure has no padding (has_unique_object_representations). (c) Add a separate opt-in `bind_comparable` with the (a) constraint. Recommendation: (b) now, as it changes no behaviour and no engine code compares lambda-bound delegates; revisit (a) only if event unsubscription by delegate equality is ever added.
- **F243** MINOR write_file_atomic is atomic for readers but not durable: no flush to disk before the rename (`engine/src/core/vfs/native_file_system.cpp:492`). Present. write_bytes closes an ofstream (native_file_system.cpp:492-499) and write_file_atomic renames over the target (230-236) without FlushFileBuffers or fsync. The documented contract (file_system.h:121-124) only promises that readers never see a partial file, not durability across power loss. Whether to pay a flush on every scene, prefab and .meta write (asset_database, scene_serializer and editor commands all use write_file_atomic) is a product trade-off between durability and import throughput. *Fix:* Option (a): make native write_file_atomic durable. Add a platform function (internal::flush_to_disk(path) or write through a platform handle) that calls FlushFileBuffers on Windows and fsync on POSIX for the temporary before replace_file; leave out the directory fsync. Simple, but it costs a disk flush per .meta write during bulk imports. Option (b): add a durable flag or a separate write_file_durable on file_system, used only for user saves (scene, prefab, project). This is a public API change that the scene, editor and assets callers must adopt. Option (c): keep the behaviour and document write_file_atomic as reader-atomic but not crash-durable. Recommendation: (a), measured with the asset-database import benchmark; fall back to (b) if bulk meta writes regress noticeably.
- **F250** MINOR Infinite floats silently become NaN across a save/load (`engine/src/core/reflect/json_conversion.cpp:106`). Behaviour unchanged and intentional: write_f32/write_f64 (json_conversion.cpp:80-94) write null for any non-finite value, and read_f32/read_f64 (104-122) read null as quiet NaN. This is documented in docs/api/core/reflection.md:141 and pinned by test_json_conversion.cpp. NaN round-trips, but +inf and -inf silently become NaN. A fix changes the saved-data encoding (a file-format choice). *Fix:* Options: (a) Keep the encoding. Document prominently that infinities do not survive serialisation, and CONJURE_LOG_WARN once per write naming the field path when +/-inf is written. (b) Write +inf as the JSON string "inf" and -inf as "-inf" (NaN stays null). read_f32/read_f64 accept those strings alongside null and numbers, so old files still load. Also check the C# script-field and Python tool readers of scene JSON for number-only assumptions. (c) Reject non-finite floats at write time with an error. Recommendation: (b). It is backward compatible for reading and removes the silent data change for the common 'no limit' sentinel. Update reflection.md and README, and replace the pinned test with a round-trip test.
- **F586** MINOR job_system::start can throw std::system_error although it returns result<void> (`engine/src/core/jobs/job_system.cpp:466`). Still present: job_system.cpp:462-469 sets mRunning and emplaces std::thread workers with no failure path, and dedicated_thread.cpp:23 constructs a std::jthread the same way. The sweep's fix (catch std::system_error) breaks docs/CONVENTIONS.md:44 "Engine code never throws and never catches" (no try/catch exists anywhere in engine/), so the remedy depends on a policy choice, not a local fix. *Fix:* Options: (a) Treat thread-creation failure like std::bad_alloc: process-fatal resource exhaustion, outside the result<> contract. Document it in engine/src/core/jobs/README.md and docs/api/core/jobs.md (and in docs/design/10_core.md C4 as a docs-area follow-up). No code change. (b) Allow one narrow exception to the never-catch rule: in job_system::state::start, wrap the worker loop in a catch of std::system_error, set mStopRequested, notify, join the workers already created, clear mWorkers, reset mRunning and return error(error_code::BACKEND_FAILURE, ...). dedicated_thread would need a failure query. This needs a CONVENTIONS.md amendment. (c) Replace std::thread/std::jthread in jobs with a thin native wrapper (CreateThread/_beginthreadex, pthread_create) that returns an error code. That avoids exceptions entirely but brings platform code, stop_token plumbing and a dedicated_thread API change. Recommendation: (a) now, because the engine already treats every other allocation failure in std containers this way. Revisit (c) only if hosts with tight thread quotas become a target.
- **F615** MINOR string_id name table is never pruned and every view() takes a global mutex (`engine/src/core/strings/string_id.cpp:47`). Behaviour as designed: 10_core.md:141 specifies a global, mutex-guarded interning table, and string_id.cpp:21-23 relies on entries never being erased, because view() hands out string_views valid for the process (strings-and-identifiers.md:86-87). Pruning would break that contract. The unbounded growth comes from scene::name/tag being string_id (scene/components.h:161-174) and from bindings interning every Entity.Name (entries_entity.cpp:60), which is a scene and bindings design choice. *Fix:* Options: (a) Document that string_id is for bounded vocabularies, interned for the life of the process, and that names generated per spawn grow the table forever. Add this to string_id.h's header comment and strings-and-identifiers.md 'Using ids'. Optionally switch name_table to std::shared_mutex, with find_string_id_name/string_id_name_count under a shared lock and register_string_id_name doing a shared-lock find before an exclusive insert, to cut contention in hierarchy views. (b) Store entity names as a per-entity std::string (or a scene-owned refcounted name pool) instead of string_id. That is a scene, bindings, editor and serializer redesign. (c) A refcounted, prunable table with a different view lifetime contract (breaking). Recommendation: (a) now, documentation plus the shared_mutex read path. Leave (b) to a scene design discussion if games are expected to name thousands of spawned entities.
- **F753** MINOR VFS accepts Windows-special path segments: ':' creates an NTFS alternate data stream and leaves a 0-byte file, trailing dots are silently dropped (`engine/src/core/vfs/vfs_internal.cpp:13`). Present. resolve_backend_path (vfs_internal.cpp:13-42) only rejects a drive letter or UNC prefix. A later segment containing ':' (an NTFS alternate data stream) or ending in '.' or ' ' is passed through, and Win32 then writes somewhere other than the reported path. Which names the VFS should allow on which platforms is a portability policy no design document settles. Rejecting them everywhere also makes existing Linux files with such names unreachable. *Fix:* Option (a): reject segments containing : * ? " < > \| or control characters, or ending in '.' or ' ' (plus reserved device names CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM1-9, LPT1-9 with any extension), for every operation on every backend (INVALID_ARGUMENT). Projects stay portable, but files that already exist with such names on Linux or macOS can no longer be read or deleted through the VFS. Option (b): reject them only in the native backend on Windows. Behaviour then differs per OS and non-portable projects can still be authored on Linux. Option (c): apply the (a) rules on every platform only where a name is created (write_file, write_file_atomic, create_directories and the destination of rename), and keep read, stat, enumerate and remove lenient. Recommendation: (c). It keeps new content portable and avoids ADS and trailing-dot surprises, while existing odd names can still be read and cleaned up. Implement it as a shared internal::validate_new_name used by the native and memory backends, with conformance tests.

### renderer (7)

- **F377** MINOR The scene-wide shadow atlas is culled with the layer mask of whichever view renders it first (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:1052`). Still present: the scene-wide atlas is culled once, inside the first shadowing view, with that view's layerMask (renderer.cpp:1033-1053), so other views get shadows from that view's layers only; cascades by contrast use each view's own mask (renderer.cpp:1002-1005). The design gives no rule for how layer masks apply to shared local-light shadows, so the intended semantic must be chosen. *Fix:* Options: (a) cull atlas tiles with the union of the layer masks of every view that will render with shadows (computed in prepare_frame from scene.views, scene.shadows.enabled and SKIP_SHADOWS before the per-view loop, stored in renderer_state and used at renderer.cpp:1052) - ~10 lines, every view sees at least its own casters, a view may also see shadows of casters on layers it does not render. (b) always 0xFFFFFFFF - simplest, layer masks never filter local-light shadows. (c) per-view atlas caster lists (re-render the atlas per distinct mask) - exact per-view semantic matching the cascades, but more GPU work and a frame-structure change. Recommendation: (a), documented in rendering-a-frame.md ('local-light shadows use the union of the shadowing views' layer masks').
- **F385** MINOR resize_render_target silently changes colour_texture(); an ImGui handle registered before the resize shows the font atlas (`engine/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:492`). Still present: resize_render_target (renderer.cpp:468-498) unregisters the old colourId and adopts the replacement's, so colour_texture() changes; imgui_renderer resolves the registered gpu_texture_id each frame (imgui_renderer.cpp:83-91) and falls back to the font atlas for the stale id. The id change is currently a pinned contract (test_render_target.cpp:186 `CHECK(target->colour_texture() != before)`), undocumented in render_target.h:59-63 and renderer.h:168-171, so which behaviour is right is the owner's call. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the id stable: add gpu_resource_cache::replace_external_texture(gpu_texture_id, nvrhi::ITexture*, rhi::texture_format, u32 width, u32 height) that rewrites the mirror under mMutex; in resize_render_target keep targetState.colourId, call replace_external_texture with the new colour texture, unregister the replacement's own id instead of the old one, then move the rest of the state; resize already wait_idle()s first, and imgui_renderer rebuilds its binding set when the resolved pointer changes (imgui_renderer.cpp:95), so a registered ImGui handle follows automatically; flip test_render_target.cpp:186 to CHECK(==). (b) keep the change and document it: render_target.h colour_texture() and renderer.h resize_render_target say the id changes on resize and ImGui handles must be re-registered. Recommendation: (a) - the design's editor viewport (02_renderer.md:361, 04_editor.md:982) registers the target with ImGui and resizes with the panel, and a stable id removes a whole class of stale-handle bugs at ~25 lines.
- **F386** MINOR Exceeding MAX_SPRITES_PER_FRAME drops SCREEN-space (HUD) sprites first (`engine/src/renderer/sprite_batcher.cpp:49`). Still present: the sort key puts SCREEN after WORLD (sprite_batcher.cpp:48-49) and the cap keeps the first MAX_SPRITES_PER_FRAME sorted sprites (:57, :88), so HUD sprites are dropped first. This is documented (sprite_batch.h:71-73) and pinned by test_sprite_batch.cpp:155-166 ('The two highest sort keys are the screen sprites: those are the ones dropped'), so changing it is a behaviour choice for the owner. *Fix:* Options: (a) keep the current rule (documented and tested). (b) preserve SCREEN sprites first: emittedScreen = min(screenCount, cap), emittedWorld = min(worldCount, cap - emittedScreen), dropping the last WORLD sprites in sort order; emit the WORLD prefix then the SCREEN range of the sorted order. (c) separate per-space caps (e.g. a fixed SCREEN reserve). Recommendation: (b) - UI should not vanish under a particle burst, it is ~10 lines in build(), and the WARN should say how many of each space were dropped; update sprite_batch.h:71-73 and replace the test expectation with 'sprite_batcher - sprites beyond MAX_SPRITES_PER_FRAME drop world sprites before screen sprites'.
- **F392** MINOR glTF lights without a range are imported with range 10 m, and wrapT is ignored (`engine/src/renderer/import/gltf_importer.cpp:641`). Still present: import_light keeps light::range's default of 10 m when the glTF has no range (gltf_importer.cpp:641), and apply_sampler reads only wrap_s (:477-483). The renderer needs a finite range: its attenuation is windowed by saturate(1 - (d/range)^4)^2 (02_renderer.md:295). texture_asset stores one wrap mode, and .ctex has one wrap byte. What 'infinite' should map to is a product choice the design does not make. *Fix:* Options. (A) Keep 10 m and push a warning per light without a range, plus a warning per sampler whose wrap_s != wrap_t (wrap_s is used); SMALL. (B) Derive the range from intensity, range = sqrt(intensity / LIGHT_RANGE_CUTOFF), with a named cutoff constant (for example 0.01 cd/m^2-equivalent), clamped to [0.1, a named maximum]. Directional lights ignore range. Add the same range warning and A's wrap warning. (C) Add an infinite-range representation to light; this needs shader and culling changes. Recommendation: B with A's wrap warning; per-axis wrap stays out of scope because it changes the .ctex format.
- **F395** MINOR shader_asset's program table is a third, unused copy of the permutation list; the README claims the pipeline cache consumes it (`engine/src/renderer/assets/shader_asset.cpp:18`). Still present. Nothing outside shader_asset.cpp and its test calls shader_asset (grep). pipeline_cache keeps its own list (pipeline_cache.cpp:64-117). Yet README.md:29-31 and handbook renderer.md:49-50 say pipeline_cache turns built_in_programs() into pipelines, and the API wiki renderer-assets.md:128-130 says this list is what renderer::create fails with SHADER_NOT_FOUND on. The design keeps shader_asset as the v1 table and the future custom-shader hook (02_renderer.md:570, :938), so deleting it versus wiring it in is the owner's choice. *Fix:* Options. (A) Keep shader_asset and fix the three docs. README.md:29-31 and handbook renderer.md:49-50 should say: 'shader_asset::built_in_programs() mirrors the permutations engine/shaders/CMakeLists.txt compiles and pipeline_cache.cpp loads; test_shader_asset validates it against the compiled index; renderer::create does not read it'. renderer-assets.md:128-130 should drop 'This is what renderer::create fails with SHADER_NOT_FOUND on' and say that renderer::create checks pipeline_cache's own list, while validate(library) is a separate check. Docs only, all renderer-owned, SMALL. (B) Make pipeline_cache::load_shader resolve each variant through shader_asset::find_built_in(name, pixelDefines, vertexDefines) and return its validate() error, so the C++ table is the one list the renderer loads. Add a test that every graphics_variant and compute_variant resolves. This is a moderate refactor of pipeline creation. (C) Delete shader_asset, test_shader_asset.cpp and its API docs; this contradicts the design's future plan. Recommendation: A now; B when custom shaders arrive.
- **F400** MINOR ImGui colours are written as linear values into an sRGB swapchain (UI and colour widgets look washed out) (`engine/shaders/imgui.hlsl:32`). Still present: imgui.hlsl:32 passes ImGui's display-referred vertex colours unchanged, and imgui_renderer.cpp:65 targets RGBA8_SRGB, so the hardware sRGB-encodes them a second time. Any fix visibly changes the editor's established colours and theme, and the alternatives differ in blending space. The design docs do not cover ImGui colour space. *Fix:* Options: (a) Linearise the vertex colours in imgui.hlsl vs_main with srgb_to_linear (colour.hlsli) when the target is sRGB, via a push-constant flag or an SRGB_TARGET permutation. Small, but ImGui then blends in linear space, so anti-aliased edges and text look thinner than ImGui intends. (b) Render ImGui into a UNORM target or view so it blends in sRGB space as designed, and route the scene viewport image through an sRGB-decoding sample. Larger change to swapchain and back-buffer formats. (c) Keep the current output and tune the theme by eye, documenting it. Recommendation: (a), followed by a visual pass over the editor theme with the owner, because every editor colour gets darker and higher contrast. Note F396 deletes srgb_to_linear as unused; (a) would re-add it.
- **F403** MINOR WORLD sprites and debug lines go through exposure, the tonemapper and bloom by default (`engine/src/renderer/passes/sprite_pass.cpp:41`).
